# Lakko — Roadmap

Full phase decomposition — what we build and in what order. The strategy lives in `~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md`; the ADRs in `docs/adr/` are the authoritative source for architectural decisions; this document is the **feature-development tactics**.

---

## Execution context

**This roadmap is not a calendar.** No deliverable is tied to weeks / months / years.

- Code is written by an AI agent (Claude Code) inside sessions started by the maintainer
- A **sprint** = an atomic deliverable that fits into 1-3 coherent sessions and ends with a green CI
- The pace of phases is driven by when the maintainer sits down to work
- **Phase N+1 does not start** until Phase N's exit criteria are green — that is the only dependency
- Stack: Rust core + native UI per platform; MariaDB; Hetzner Cloud as hosting

Decisions about launch / audit / branding / pricing are the maintainer's. This roadmap covers **only what to write, and in what order**.

---

## Phase summary

| Phase | What is added |
|---|---|
| **0** Foundations | Workspace, ADRs, CI, MLS end-to-end in tests, FFI surface, fuzz harnesses |
| **1** 1×1 Messaging | services/auth, services/ds, services/storage, services/discovery, services/push-gw, lakko-ffi-wasm, apps/ios, apps/android, apps/web |
| **2a** Foundation | Encrypted backup in object storage, opt-in WebAuthn passkey, multi-device sync via DeviceGroup substrate |
| **2b** Vanilla-MLS private scale | Private groups + private channels up to ~50k members through vanilla MLS, member-list virtualization, commit batching, KeyPackage refresh schedule, admin/moderator roles |
| **2c** Two-tier substrate | Large private groups + large private channels (50k–1M+ subscribers) through PublisherGroup + sender-key broadcast. Trades backward secrecy on content for scale — documented, aggressive rotation |
| **2d** Public surface | Public groups + public channels, server-mediated plain TLS (no E2EE on content that is public by definition), full-text search, server-side moderation |
| **3** Calls | lakko-calls (SFrame), services/sfu (LiveKit fork), P2P 1×1 + group calls (link and in-chat) up to ~50 participants, CallKit / ConnectionService, coturn |
| **5** Anti-censorship + Scale | REALITY, MASQUE, Shadowsocks-2022, transport-rendezvous, multi-region edge cascade, apps/watchos, localization |

---

## Cross-cutting

### Code quality gates

| Gate | Starting at phase | Type |
|---|---|---|
| `cargo fmt --check` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `cargo test --workspace` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `cargo doc -D warnings` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `cargo deny check` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `reuse lint` | 0 | blocking PR |
| `cargo audit` | 0 | blocking PR (vulnerability) |
| `cargo fuzz` on parsers | 0.4 | continuous (OSS-Fuzz integration) |
| `kani` model checking on the MLS state machine | 2 | nightly |
| Coverage ≥80% (crypto + mls) | 2 | blocking PR |
| Mutation testing on crypto (`cargo-mutants`) | 2 | nightly |
| Hax / F* formal verification of critical paths | 4+ | aspirational |
| SLSA L3 build provenance | 1 | release |
| Reproducible build verifier (dual runner) | 0.4 | release |

### Hosting / infra

Everything self-hosted on Hetzner Cloud. We start with one VPS and split things out only when we hit a ceiling (CPU / RAM / bandwidth). Object storage — Hetzner Storage Box or local disk.

| Phase | Infra requirement |
|---|---|
| 0 | — (no prod) |
| 1 | 1× VPS with all services + MariaDB + nginx + coturn co-located |
| 2a | + Hetzner Storage Box or extra disk volume for the backup-envelope blobs |
| 2b | scale-up VPS, or 2× VPS (backend + DB) once we hit a wall on MariaDB rows / FoundationDB latency for the per-group MLS journal |
| 2c | + dedicated bandwidth allowance for content-key HPKE-envelope fanout (per-subscriber delivery on every rotation). May warrant a second VPS once channels grow past ~100k subscribers each |
| 2d | + full-text search backend (start with MariaDB FULLTEXT, swap to dedicated engine — Meilisearch or similar — when query latency dictates) |
| 3 | + 1× CCX-tier VPS for the SFU (LiveKit fork), 20 TB transfer per month allowance |
| 5 | multi-region edges (for 100k+ viewer broadcasts with low latency globally) |

## Phase 0 — Foundations

### Goal

From an empty repo to a workspace where MLS groups work end-to-end in tests and the FFI surface is stable enough to generate Swift / Kotlin bindings.

### Pre-flight

— (starting phase).

### Sprint breakdown

| Sprint | Contents | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **0.0** | Cargo workspace + ADR-0001…0006 + CI workflow + Nix flake + justfile + REUSE compliance | ✅ done |
| **0.1** | CI gates on GitHub Actions + lakko-proto codegen (protox + prost-build) + lakko-crypto primitives | ✅ done |
| **0.2** | lakko-mls on top of OpenMLS (identity / key_package / group / exporter) + end-to-end Alice ↔ Bob ↔ Charlie | ✅ done |
| **0.3** | lakko-transport (QUIC + in-memory) + lakko-ffi-uniffi (Identity / Group surface) | ✅ done |
| **0.4** | Fuzz harnesses (cargo-fuzz) on parsers (MLS wire, prost); reproducible build verifier (dual runner in CI). OSS-Fuzz upstream PR — when the first real integration lands | ✅ done |
| **0.5** | KT log skeleton — `lakko-transparency` crate (Merkle RFC 6962 + BLAKE3, SignedRoot Ed25519, InclusionProof, InMemoryLog, rollback detection). X.509 credentials + FIPS suite unblock — deferred to Phase 1 (no `openmls_x509_credential` crate in the registry, requires upstream contribution or manual TLS-codec encoding) | ✅ done (KT) / deferred (X.509+FIPS) |

### Exit criteria
- ✅ MLS groups end-to-end in integration tests
- ✅ FFI surface stable (lakko-ffi-uniffi compiles, three-party FFI test green)
- ✅ All CI gates green
- ✅ Fuzz harnesses for every wire-format parser (MLS message, KeyPackage, prost wire/mls_app/media)
- ✅ Reproducible build verifier — CI job exists (continue-on-error while we tune flags for byte identicality)
- ✅ KT log skeleton — `lakko-transparency` crate (production storage in Phase 2)
- 🟡 X.509 credentials + FIPS — deferred to Phase 1; needs either an upstream `openmls_x509_credential` crate or manual TLS-codec encoding of the cert chain

### Top risks
- **OpenMLS upstream churn** — mitigated: fast-follow upgrades (we already did 0.6 → 0.8 when the hpke-rs CVE landed)
- **uniffi 0.28 stale deps** (`paste`, `bincode`) — flagged in `deny.toml` as ignore with a link to upstream

## Phase 1 — 1×1 Messaging

### Goal

The first real functional slice: three devices (iOS, Android, web) exchanging 1×1 messages through a real backend, self-hosted on a single Hetzner VPS. The web client is a parallel track to native mobile, so dogfooding does not depend on Apple / Google accounts and review pipelines.

### Pre-flight
- Phase 0 exit criteria green (including 0.4 fuzz and 0.5 X.509 + KT skeleton)

### Sprint breakdown

#### Backend infra
- **1.0** Hetzner deployment skeleton (✅ done): migration from the lakko_me Node.js stack → the new Rust stack on the SAME VPS. systemd units for 5 services (auth/ds/storage/discovery/push-gw) + group target; system-nginx config with reverse proxy to 127.0.0.1:808X for the existing lakko.me / api.lakko.me domains; system-MariaDB (drop the old `lakko_me` DB, create a fresh `lakko`); `/etc/lakko/lakko.env` for shared config; `infra/scripts/deploy.sh` (git pull → cargo build --release → install → systemctl restart); a step-by-step migration guide in `infra/README.md`. Containerisation (podman / compose) is intentionally deferred — overkill for solo + 1 VPS, we'll add it when horizontal scaling actually matters
- **1.1** `services/auth` — OPAQUE-PAKE password auth (primary), MariaDB-backed signup / login without storing PII; BIP39 mnemonic recovery; PoW (Equix) + Privacy Pass (RFC 9576) anti-spam. WebAuthn passkey is **deferred to Phase 2+** as an opt-in second factor — passkey UX in 2026 is still problematic for the mass market (cross-ecosystem sync issues, recovery friction, mental-model confusion); the password flow covers 100% of users without friction. Sub-sprints: 1.1a (✅ axum + sqlx + probes), 1.1b (✅ OPAQUE registration), 1.1c (✅ OPAQUE login), 1.1d (✅ BIP39 recovery), 1.1e (✅ Equix PoW; Privacy Pass deferred — needs App Attest / Play Integrity issuer)
- **1.2** ✅ `services/storage` — content-addressable ciphertext blob proxy. Filesystem-backed (`LAKKO_BLOB_DIR`, two-level sharded layout `<root>/<hex[0:2]>/<hex[2:4]>/<hex>`). PUT verifies SHA-256 streaming-style; idempotent re-PUT. GET/HEAD streaming download. 50 MB per-blob ceiling. No auth/quota in Phase 1 (server-as-blind-CDN). Owner tracking, quota, DELETE, PoW gating, SeaweedFS/S3 backend — Phase 2+ hardening
- **1.3** ✅ `services/ds` skeleton — MariaDB-backed append-only journals: `ds_group_messages` (per-group fan-out) and `ds_mailbox_messages` (per-device direct, primarily for MLS Welcomes). Cursor-pull with `?after=<id>&limit=<n>`. Server is blind: stores opaque ciphertext keyed by 32-byte pseudonyms, never parses MLS framing. 1 MB per-message ceiling, 30-day retention with periodic pruner. No auth in Phase 1.3 (pseudonyms are random; spam mitigated by nginx rate limit). Long-poll / QUIC stream push and proof-of-membership are Phase 1.4 / Phase 2
- **1.4** ✅ `services/ds` real-time fan-out — GET endpoints accept `?wait=<secs>` (clamped to 60). In-process `tokio::sync::Notify` map keyed by `(queue, owner_id)` wakes long-polls on each POST. Standard double-check pattern (subscribe-then-fetch) plus a 5-second belt-and-suspenders re-tick covers any edge-triggered race. Single-process only — multi-instance fanout via NATS/Redis pub/sub is Phase 2 horizontal scaling; QUIC stream push is Phase 5
- **1.5** ✅ `services/discovery` — username table via OPRF (RFC 9497, Ristretto255-SHA512). Server stores only `OPRF(K, normalize(@username)) → account_id`; never sees plaintext usernames. Endpoints: `/discovery/oprf` (apply server static `K` to a blinded element), `/discovery/register` (insert mapping), `/discovery/lookup` (resolve to `account_id`). PoW-gated. Exact-match only, no prefix search. **Phase-1 known gap**: `/discovery/register` trusts the client's `account_id` claim — Phase 1.5b will gate on auth-issued session tokens. KeyPackage bundle delivery + KT inclusion proofs are 1.5b/Phase 2
- **1.6** ✅ `services/push-gw` scaffold — `(device_pseudonym, provider) → token` registry + `/push/notify` dispatcher behind a `Provider` trait. Payload sent to platforms is strictly `(group_pseudonym, envelope_pointer)` — no plaintext. Phase 1.6 ships only `LogOnlyProvider` (records intent into `tracing`) for all four kinds (APNs / FCM / Web Push / UnifiedPush). Real platform impls land in Phase 1.6b once developer accounts and signing keys are in place — drop-in behind the same trait. PoW-gated. `TokenInvalid` results auto-clean the offending row from `push_tokens`

#### Crates (extension)
- **1.7** ✅ `lakko-storage` — three-layer client-side store: `Store` trait with `InMemoryStore` (tests) and `SqliteStore` (rusqlite, `bundled` libsqlite for cross-compile); `EncryptedStore` wrapper applies application-layer ChaCha20-Poly1305 with per-table HKDF subkeys and AAD bound to `(table, key)` (anti-relocation); high-level facades `MessageStore` (per-group ordered log with versioned wire codec) and `GroupStateStore` (single MLS snapshot per group). SQLCipher full-DB encryption deferred to Phase 2 (app-layer AEAD over opaque values is sufficient for the threat model). IndexedDB backend lands with `lakko-ffi-wasm` (1.13). Hardware-backed root key sourcing is per-platform FFI work (1.14–1.16)
- **1.8** ✅ `lakko-transport` HTTP REST client + SPKI cert pinning. New modules `http` (`HttpTransport` over reqwest with `rustls-tls-manual-roots`: `post_json` / `get_json` / `get_bytes`, 90s read timeout for `?wait=` long polls, `https_only`) and `pinning` (`SpkiPinSet` of SHA-256 hashes of leaf-cert SPKI per RFC 7469 §2.4; `PinnedVerifier` implements `rustls::ServerCertVerifier` and **fully replaces** chain-of-trust validation with the pin check, signature verification on the handshake transcript still runs). Phase 0.3's message-oriented `Transport` / `Connection` traits and the `quic` / `in_memory` backends are unchanged. WebTransport / WebSocket fallback move into 1.13 with the wasm bindings; pluggable obfuscation transports (REALITY, Shadowsocks-2022, MASQUE) into Phase 5. Pin updates via TUF-signed bootstrap config — Phase 5
- **1.9a** ✅ `lakko-contacts` — ContactRequest core + pseudonym lifecycle (ADR-0005 §13.2 + §13.7). Three pseudonym classes (`IdentityPseudonym` BLAKE3-from-pubkey; `StrangerInbox` BLAKE3-keyed; `PerRelationship` BLAKE3-keyed with Outbound/Inbound direction separation against pair-correlation). Sealed-box envelope (HPKE-Base-equivalent: X25519 + HKDF-SHA-256 + ChaCha20-Poly1305) for the intro message. Manual length-prefixed v1 wire codec. Ed25519 signature over the canonical encoding. `compose` / `decode_and_verify` / `open_envelope` API.
- **1.9b** ✅ `lakko-contacts` follow-up — `InviteCapability` URL pointers (scenarios B/C, shipped 1.18b-1.18g), QR safety numbers (scenario D — shipped as **1.9b3**: `safety_number` derivation via BLAKE3 XOF over the lex-sorted pubkey pair, 60-digit Signal-style ASCII output, exposed through UniFFI + wasm-bindgen, with a "Show safety number" toggle on the web Contacts row and parallel helpers on iOS / Android `ContactsRepository`), bootstrap-referral signup (**1.9b4** — `bootstrap_referral=true` `InviteCapability` flag now auto-fires the Accept path on the web invite landing page so the joiner doesn't have to click a second time; iOS / Android already auto-accept every invite by construction), group-invite envelope (scenario E — covered through the existing MLS Welcome ship over the stranger-inbox in 1.16e3 / 1.18g), `ContactAck` reply path (**1.9b1** — `ContactAck` wire struct with `version | accepter_pubkey | accepter_account_id | accepter_x25519_pubkey | ack_to_pseudonym | accepted_at_ms | Ed25519 signature` carried under envelope kind `0x03`; auto-sent on accept across all three clients; signed acks verified on receive and surfaced as `IncomingContactAck`), Privacy Pass tokens carried inside the request (**1.9b2** — `ContactRequest` wire bumped to v3 with a length-prefixed `privacy_pass_token` field, signature covers the token so a man-in-the-middle cannot strip / forge attestation; v1 / v2 envelopes still decode for the rolling-upgrade window; the actual mint / verify against App Attest / Play Integrity stays on the Phase-1.20+ provider sub-sprint). PoW token still deferred — server-side already enforces PoW from 1.1e; the wire-format extension to carry the proof inside the envelope waits for a real client integration sprint.
- **1.10** ✅ `lakko-push` — wake-up payload framing. Compact JSON v1 with three discriminated kinds: `GroupMessage { group_pseudonym, envelope_pointer }`, `MailboxMessage { envelope_pointer }` (device pseudonym omitted — client knows its own), `Wakeup` (generic fallback). Strictly metadata pointers — Apple / Google / Mozilla see only random pseudonyms + integer cursors, no plaintext / preview / sender. Schema-version byte for forward-compat (legacy clients drop unknown versions silently and catch up via long-poll). Server-side payload encryption is Phase 2 hardening (marginal — pseudonyms already carry no identity); Web Push RFC 8291 framing lands with `WebPushProvider` in 1.6b; APNs / FCM platform-specific decode is per-platform FFI work in 1.14 / 1.15
- **1.11** ✅ `lakko-presence` — blinded per-group presence. No global "online" indicator (would leak the social graph). Token = `BLAKE3-keyed(presence_seed, member_pseudonym ‖ epoch_be)[..16]` where `presence_seed` is per-group (rotates with MLS epoch), `member_pseudonym` per-member, `epoch` wall-clock-chunked (default 60s). Members of the same group recompute and recognise each other's tokens; the server and members of *other* groups see uniform 16-byte values that rotate per-epoch. Crate ships `derive_token` + `current_epoch` + `PresenceTracker` (`HashMap<PresenceToken, last_seen_ms>` for the UI list). Real `presence_seed` derivation from MLS exporter lands with the lakko-mls integration; network propagation is apps work
- **1.12** ✅ `lakko-sync` — single-device sync primitives. `HybridLogicalClock` (Kulkarni–Demirbas–Browne 2014): `(physical_ms, logical_counter)` with `tick` + `merge_remote`, monotonicity preserved across wall-clock skew / restart / out-of-order arrival. `EditChain` — flat append-only log of `Update` / `Delete` ops resolved by last-write-wins on `Hlc` (no character-level Yjs/Automerge complexity since only the original sender ever edits their own message in Lakko's model). `Draft` — per-group `(group_id, body, hlc)` with the same LWW rule. Caller-supplied wall-clock source (deterministic tests, wasm32 portability). DeviceGroup multi-device propagation lands in Phase 2.3–2.5
- **1.13a** ✅ `lakko-ffi-wasm` — wasm-bindgen mirror of the UniFFI surface (Phase 0.3): `Identity` (`new` / `signaturePublicKey` getter / `createKeyPackage`); `Group` (constructor = MLS create / `joinFromWelcome` static / `addMember` / `removeMember` / `createApplicationMessage` / `processIncoming` / `exportSecret` / `ownLeafIndex` / `epoch` / `memberCount`); `AddMember` record (commit + welcome bytes); `ProcessedMessage` tagged class with string `kind` discriminator. camelCase JS naming via `js_name` while Rust impl stays snake_case. Errors → `JsError` with the `lakko-mls` message preserved. OpenMLS `js` feature wired through a target-conditional dep so `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-ffi-wasm` works clean. CI gets a `wasm-build` job; justfile gets `just wasm-build`.
- **1.13b** ✅ `lakko-ffi-wasm` extensions. lib.rs split into per-crate modules (`mls`, `sync`, `push`, `presence`, `contacts`, `storage`); 1.13a content moved verbatim to `mls.rs`. New mirrors:
  - `sync` — `Hlc`, `HybridLogicalClock`, `EditOp`, `EditChain`, `EditResolved`, `Draft` (LWW replacement helper).
  - `push` — `PushPayload` discriminated wrapper (`groupMessage` / `mailboxMessage` / `wakeup` constructors + `kind` / `groupPseudonym` / `envelopePointer` getters + JSON encode/decode).
  - `presence` — `PresenceToken`, `derive_token`, `current_epoch`, `PresenceTracker`.
  - `contacts` — pseudonym classes (`IdentityPseudonym` / `StrangerInbox` / `PerRelationship` with `Direction`) plus `ContactRequest::compose` / `decodeAndVerify` / `openEnvelope`.
  - `storage` — `Table`, `InMemoryStore`, `EncryptedStore`, `MessageStore`, `LocalMessage`, `MessageKind`, `GroupStateStore`. **No persistent backend on Web yet** — `SqliteStore` is now host-only (`libsqlite3-sys` won't build for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`); IndexedDB-backed `Store` is 1.13c.
  - `lakko-storage::EncryptedStore` is now `Clone` (every field already `Arc`-wrapped) so multiple high-level facades share the inner KV cheaply.
  - Phase-1 tests in ffi-wasm cover happy paths only — error-path tests panic under host `cargo test` because `JsError::new` is a wasm-bindgen-imported function (host-runtime stub). Error coverage stays in the underlying crates; `wasm-bindgen-test` runner picks up the FFI error paths in 1.16 via apps/web.
- **1.13c** ✅ `lakko-ffi-wasm` follow-up — **scope clarified: the original four-item list (Rust IdbStore + wasm fetch-transport + .d.ts post-processing + wasm-pack publish pipeline) was largely superseded by the apps/web TypeScript-side persistence + HTTP that grew up across 1.16-1.18.** What 1.13c actually ships: **(a) `apps/web/src/auth/session-token-store.ts`** — IndexedDB-backed encrypted store for the Phase 1.5b session token. Wraps the bearer token + expiry under the device root `CryptoKey` (1.16c3c2, non-extractable AES-256-GCM) before persisting; mirror of the `mls-store.ts` / `key-store.ts` envelope pattern. Schema: `db "lakko-session-tokens" v1 / store "tokens" keyPath: "accountId"` with `{ accountId, ciphertext (12-byte IV + AES-GCM ciphertext+tag), expiresAtMs }`. The unencrypted `expiresAtMs` column lets the load path drop stale rows cheaply without spending the AES decrypt cost; the plaintext also carries it (defence in depth — a swapped row with a future expiry can't extend an actually-expired token because the HMAC on the embedded JWT-style format binds the server-side `exp`). Throws on decrypt failure so a device-key rotation surfaces as "please re-enter password" rather than silent continuation. **(b) `AuthContext.tsx` rewired**: `hydrateFromStorage` now returns `{ state, sessionToken }`; `onMount` calls `discovery.setSessionToken(persistedToken)` after the state apply so a returning user with an unchanged device-key chain has the bearer header installed automatically. `signup` / `login` / `recover` each call `putSessionToken(accountIdB64, result.sessionToken, result.sessionExpiresAtMs, deviceKey)` right after the corresponding `putDeviceKey` (best-effort — persistence failure does not block the auth flow). `logout()` fires-and-forgets `deleteSessionToken` so the next page reload doesn't silently re-attach to the same session. `forgetDevice()` drops the token alongside the device key + MLS snapshot + contacts cache. **What 1.13c does NOT ship (and why)**: (i) **Rust IdbStore** — `apps/web` doesn't go through the Rust `lakko-storage` layer at all; MLS snapshots use `apps/web/src/chat/mls-store.ts`, device keys use `apps/web/src/auth/key-store.ts`, contacts cache uses `apps/web/src/contacts/local-store.ts`, and now session tokens use the new `session-token-store.ts`. A Rust-side `IdbStore` would have no consumer. (ii) **wasm fetch-based HTTP transport for `lakko-transport::http`** — `apps/web` uses native browser `fetch` directly via TypeScript (`AuthClient`, `DiscoveryClient`, all the `*Client` modules); the Rust `lakko-transport` is host-only and the Web client never crosses that boundary. (iii) **TypeScript `.d.ts` post-processing** — wasm-pack already emits valid `.d.ts`; `tsc --noEmit` runs clean across the workspace, no post-processing needed. (iv) **wasm-pack publish pipeline** — `apps/web` consumes the wasm-pack output directly from the monorepo (`crates/lakko-ffi-wasm` is a workspace member); no external publish target exists. We re-evaluate when a third-party consumer materialises (Phase 2+). **Gates**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (WASM 1891 KB / 742 KB gzipped — well under the < 2 MB Phase 1.16c target); `cargo test --workspace` 0 failed. **`*/finish` JSDoc gotcha**: the `*/` substring inside a `/** … */` block closes the comment prematurely; caught at first `tsc --noEmit` pass + fixed by rewording.

#### Native apps (iOS + Android)
- **1.14** ✅ `apps/ios` skeleton — SwiftUI: signup / signin (OPAQUE-PAKE), contact list UI, 1×1 chat UI; integration with the XCFramework from lakko-ffi-uniffi. **All sub-items (1.14e1-e11b, 1.14f-f4) shipped — the iOS surface mirrors the Web client for signup, contacts inbox poll / accept / decline, invite-link generate + revoke, invite-link landing screen, the 1×1 chat surface (timeline + composer + manual refresh + continuous polling), and the post-1.14f Welcome forwarding via `ChatRepository`.**
  - **1.14a** ✅ iOS cross-compile + XCFramework recipe — `just build-ios` cargo-builds `lakko-ffi-uniffi` as a `staticlib` for three iOS targets + `xcodebuild -create-xcframework` bundles the per-target archives into a single `LakkoCore.xcframework`. **Targets**: `aarch64-apple-ios` (physical iPhones / iPads — every device since A7, 2013), `aarch64-apple-ios-sim` (Apple-Silicon Mac simulator), `x86_64-apple-ios-sim` (Intel Mac simulator — kept until the Intel-Mac install base drops below ~5%; cheap to keep). 32-bit iOS (armv7 / armv7s) intentionally NOT in the matrix — Apple dropped support with iOS 11. **Simulator slices combined via `lipo -create`** because `xcodebuild -create-xcframework` accepts exactly one binary per `-library` argument + Mac simulators come in both arm64 + x86_64 flavours (the physical-device slice ships standalone). **`module.modulemap` generated inline** in the recipe via a 5-line `printf` template — doing it inline is simpler than maintaining a `tools/ios/modulemap.template` for a single consumer; the framework module name `lakko_ffi_uniffiFFI` matches what the UniFFI-generated `lakko_ffi_uniffi.swift` already `import`s. **`staticlib`, not `cdylib`** — iOS embeds frameworks differently from Android; the codesign + entitlement story for dynamic libs pays off only at app-update scale, and static linking removes App Store reviewer friction. `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/Cargo.toml` already declared `crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]` from 1.15b — cdylib for Android JNI, **staticlib for the iOS XCFramework**, rlib for in-workspace consumers. **`[macos]` recipe attribute** gates `build-ios` to macOS hosts only (same posture as Android-toolchain recipes that need `cargo-ndk`); CI on Linux silently skips, no noise. **Pre-flight check** for `xcodebuild` presence: the recipe's first line is a `command -v xcodebuild` test that fails fast with a pointer to `xcode-select --install` if Xcode Command Line Tools aren't on the host. **Output layout**: `target/ios/LakkoCore.xcframework/` with `Info.plist`, `ios-arm64/` (physical-device slice + headers), `ios-arm64_x86_64-simulator/` (combined-simulator slice + headers). Gitignored under the existing workspace-root `target/` rule. **`apps/ios/README.md`** lays out the rest of the 1.14 phase end-to-end: bootstrap path for the Xcode project / SPM scaffold (1.14b), SwiftUI skeleton + "Hello core" probe (1.14c), feature parity with `apps/android` for auth / contacts / chat (1.14d-f). Each milestone references both the Android sub-sprint AND the web `DevProbe` / ContactsRoute / ChatRoute analogues so the cross-platform work stays in lock-step on UI surface (different SwiftUI / Compose / SolidJS code, same Rust core, same wire format). **What 1.14a does NOT ship**: actual Xcode project (no `apps/ios/Lakko.xcodeproj` exists yet — that's 1.14b's job); SwiftUI source code (1.14c); auth / contacts / chat UI (1.14d-f); CI workflow for the XCFramework build (will batch with the wider quota-conservation note on `ci.yml` once the June 2026 reset lands). **Workspace `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace`** all clean (no Rust touched in 1.14a — it's pure build-pipeline work). **`just build-ios` itself NOT run** from this session — the dev machine has Xcode Command Line Tools but the recipe is the contract; the actual XCFramework artefact lands when 1.14b first needs it. **Open follow-ups**: byte-identical reproducible-build verification across two clean Mac runners (mirror of `apps/android`'s 1.15b posture + `apps/web`'s 1.17b posture); the App Store reviewer reproducible-build gate's exception around App-Store re-signing breaking byte-identicality (we ship the unsigned `.xcframework` as the proof + treat the signed `.ipa` as a separate trust root; same documented compromise that the Phase 0 roadmap §11 calls out).
  - **1.14b** ✅ `apps/ios/Package.swift` — Swift Package wrapping the `LakkoCore.xcframework` from 1.14a into a normal SPM library product the SwiftUI app (Phase 1.14c, separate `.xcodeproj`) imports as a single local-path dependency. Mirror of the contract Android's `apps/android/lakko-core/` Gradle module provides on its side. **Two targets**: `LakkoCoreFFI` as a `.binaryTarget` pointing at `../../target/ios/LakkoCore.xcframework` (relative path resolves from `apps/ios/` to the workspace-root `target/` — same depth as the Android module's resolve to `target/bindings/kotlin/`); `LakkoCore` as a regular `.target` depending on it. **Single library product `LakkoCore`** re-exports the Swift surface; the binary target is wired internally, consumers only see `LakkoCore`. **Platforms pinned**: `.iOS(.v17)` + `.macCatalyst(.v17)`. iOS 17 covers every device the App Store accepts in 2026 (iPhone XS+, iPad 6th-gen+); macCatalyst pre-declared for the future Mac target so adding UI doesn't churn `Package.swift`. **Source layout** under `apps/ios/Sources/LakkoCore/`: committed `LakkoCore.swift` umbrella placeholder (5-line file, declares no public symbols — exists so SPM has a source file to find before the first `just build-ios` lays down the UniFFI-emitted source); the actual `lakko_ffi_uniffi.swift` is **gitignored** + fan'd into the same directory by `just build-ios`'s new copy step. Same posture as Android: build artefact treated as such, source of truth lives in the `#[uniffi::export]` annotations on `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/`. **`just build-ios` extended** with a final `mkdir -p apps/ios/Sources/LakkoCore && cp target/bindings/swift/lakko_ffi_uniffi.swift apps/ios/Sources/LakkoCore/` — mirror of how `regen-bindings` copies the Kotlin tree into `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/kotlin/uniffi/`. **`apps/ios/.gitignore`** covers Xcode + SPM build artefacts: `build/`, `DerivedData/`, xcuserdata directories, `.swiftpm/`, `Package.resolved` (the binary-target pin is trivial; the resolved file would just be noise), `Sources/LakkoCore/lakko_ffi_uniffi.swift`, and the future-Xcode-app-target `Lakko.xcodeproj/` (preserved as an intent marker for 1.14c). **README.md** restructured to surface the Xcode app-creation flow as a numbered 7-step recipe: open Xcode → File → New → Project → iOS → App → Save under `apps/ios/Lakko/` → File → Add Packages → Add Local… → point at `apps/ios/` → Xcode resolves the local SPM package + adds `LakkoCore` as a product the app target depends on. **Critical step** in the README: run `just build-ios` from the workspace root BEFORE Xcode's first Build so the XCFramework exists at the path the binary target resolves to (SPM would otherwise fail to resolve with a "binary target path does not exist" error on Xcode's first attempt). **Why a Swift Package + a separate `.xcodeproj` rather than a single combined Xcode project**: SPM is the modern (2024+) idiomatic path; Package.swift is plain text (clean diffs, reviewable, doesn't leak per-developer paths through Xcode's binary-blob `project.pbxproj` like the legacy shape does); the Xcode app target lands separately in 1.14c via the standard `xcodebuild` New Project wizard. We could have committed a hand-rolled `Lakko.xcodeproj` here but every contributor's first `git diff` would surface dev-machine path drift, which the SPM-first shape avoids. **No host-JVM-style tests** — the Swift Package builds + links against the XCFramework; a `swift build` smoke is the closest analogue but requires Xcode Command Line Tools + the actual XCFramework to exist (so it's gated on `just build-ios` having run first). Until 1.14c's app target ships, the package is a contract artefact — the contract is exercised when the SwiftUI app's first `import LakkoCore` lands in 1.14c. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (no Rust changes in 1.14b — it's pure Apple-build-pipeline work), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14b does NOT ship**: Xcode app target / `Lakko.xcodeproj` (1.14c); SwiftUI source (1.14c); auth / contacts / chat UI (1.14d-f); `just package-ios` recipe for `xcodebuild archive` + `-exportArchive` (1.14c+ when there's a real app to archive); CI workflow (batches with the post-June-2026 quota reset).
  - **1.14c** ✅ SwiftUI "Hello core" smoke app + `xcodegen`-driven project spec — the first SwiftUI surface that actually loads the Rust core through the XCFramework + UniFFI bridge from 1.14a/b. **`apps/ios/project.yml`** is the reviewable text source of truth for the Xcode project; `xcodegen generate` (from `apps/ios/`) produces `Lakko.xcodeproj/` next to it, and the generated `.xcodeproj/` is **gitignored as a build artefact** — same posture Android takes with `app/.cxx/` and the Web with `apps/web/dist/`. **Why xcodegen, not a hand-rolled `project.pbxproj`**: the Xcode project file is a plist-like binary blob with per-developer absolute-path drift; reviewing diffs on it is a permanent papercut. xcodegen has been the de-facto standard for medium-to-large iOS shops + polyglot monorepos since ~2020; the dep is one `brew install xcodegen` + the spec is plain YAML. Mirror of the contract the Android side ships through the Gradle text build script + the Web ships through `vite.config.ts`. **`project.yml` shape**: single `Lakko` app target (`type: application`, `platform: iOS`, sources rooted at `apps/ios/Lakko/`); Info.plist auto-synthesized by xcodegen from the `info.properties` block (`CFBundleDisplayName: Lakko`, `UILaunchScreen: {}` for a blank launch screen, `UISupportedInterfaceOrientations` pinned to portrait); single dependency on the local `LakkoCore` SPM package via `packages: { LakkoCore: { path: "." } }` — same shape Xcode's "File → Add Packages → Add Local…" wizard would emit, just in YAML form. **Build settings**: `SWIFT_VERSION: "5.10"` pinned to match the SPM `Package.swift` `swift-tools-version` from 1.14b (bumping requires lockstep change on both sides); `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER: me.lakko.dev` mirrors the Android `applicationId = "me.lakko.dev"` so debug installs sit in their own bundle namespace next to a future production `me.lakko` build on the same device. **No asset catalog yet** — the default Xcode launcher icon shows on the smoke build; real branding (icon set + accent color + launch screen artwork) lands when 1.14d's signup screen has a place to surface it. **`schemes` block** pins the explicit Build/Run/Archive scheme `Lakko` so a regenerate produces a deterministic scheme file rather than xcodegen's auto-emitted variant (run = Debug, archive = Release). **`options` block** locks `groupSortPosition: top` + `generateEmptyDirectories: true` so a regenerate produces byte-identical `.xcodeproj` content given identical inputs — the reproducible-build posture mirrors the Phase 0 §11 verification gates the Android + Web sides already meet. **`apps/ios/Lakko/LakkoApp.swift`** is the `@main` `App` struct (`WindowGroup { HelloCoreView() }`) — mirror of Android's `MainActivity` from 1.15c + Web's `App.tsx` from 1.16a; the router lands in 1.14d when auth introduces the Anon/Session split, for now every launch shows the same smoke surface. **`apps/ios/Lakko/HelloCoreView.swift`** is the smoke screen: constructs a fresh `Identity` via the UniFFI-generated Swift bindings (`try Identity(name: "lakko-ios-skeleton")`), calls `signaturePublicKey()`, renders the 32-byte Ed25519 verifying key as a monospaced lowercase-hex string in a SwiftUI `Text` with `.textSelection(.enabled)` so the dev can copy-paste the value into a comparison script against the Android/Web smoke probes. **Off-main-thread FFI**: the constructor + accessor run inside `Task.detached(priority: .userInitiated)` awaited from a `.task { await loadIdentity() }` modifier on the view; this keeps the on-first-appear render free of the OpenMLS provider-storage spin-up cost (~tens of ms on a cold launch). Mirror of how the Compose `HelloCoreScreen` from 1.15c puts the same call inside `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)`. **Errors land in the same `status` `@State` slot** so the screen never goes blank — catch block on `loadIdentity` writes `"Error: \(error.localizedDescription)"`; the production paths in 1.14d will hold the `Identity` inside a session manager + explicitly `.close()` it on logout, but for a single-frame smoke we let the UniFFI auto-cleaner the runtime registers handle the release. **`just generate-ios-project`** new `[macos]` justfile recipe wraps `xcodegen generate --spec project.yml` with a pre-flight `command -v xcodegen` test that fails fast with a pointer to `brew install xcodegen` if the dep isn't on the host — same defensive shape as the `[macos] build-ios` recipe's `command -v xcodebuild` check from 1.14a. **First-run sequence** documented in `apps/ios/README.md`: `just regen-bindings` → `just build-ios` → `just generate-ios-project` → `open Lakko.xcodeproj`. Each step has a one-line failure mode + recovery hint. **CLI alternative** (also in the README): `xcodebuild -project Lakko.xcodeproj -scheme Lakko -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 15' build` for contributors who'd rather drive the build from the terminal than Xcode (and for the eventual CI workflow when the macOS runner quota resets in June 2026). **Mirror chain**: this surface is the SwiftUI analogue of `apps/android`'s `MainActivity.HelloCoreScreen` (1.15c) + `apps/web`'s `DevProbe.tsx` (1.16a) — same role (single-screen probe that proves the FFI bridge works on the device), same exit criterion (hex of a freshly-derived Ed25519 verifying key renders on screen), same architectural posture (smoke surface as a discrete sprint exit gate before the auth/contacts/chat work in 1.14d-f starts depending on the bridge). `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (no Rust changes in 1.14c — it's pure SwiftUI + xcodegen-spec work), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14c does NOT ship**: auth / signup / login / recovery UI (1.14d, mirror of Android 1.15d); contacts UI / OPRF lookup wiring / invite consume (1.14e, mirror of 1.15e); 1×1 MLS chat UI / DS long-poll / snapshot restore (1.14f, mirror of 1.15f); `just package-ios` archive recipe driving `xcodebuild archive` + `-exportArchive` against a real signing identity (1.14c+ when there's something worth distributing); CI workflow (batches with the wider quota-conservation note on `ci.yml` once the June 2026 reset lands); App Store Connect metadata / TestFlight pipeline (post-MVP, somewhere in Phase 5 polish).
  - **1.14d1** ✅ Swift auth wire types + `AuthClient` over URLSession — first half of the iOS OPAQUE-PAKE port. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/AccountId.swift`** — UUIDv7 generator (48-bit `unix_ts_ms` || 4-bit version || 12-bit `rand_a` || 2-bit variant || 62-bit `rand_b`, RFC 9562 §5.7) backed by `SecRandomCopyBytes` (Apple's `corecrypto`-backed CSPRNG, same kernel entropy pool `arc4random_buf` reads from) — byte-identical layout to the JVM `SecureRandom` shape the Android side produces; the auth service consumes `account_id` as opaque bytes so cross-platform sign-up + login lands on the same identity. **Base64 helpers**: `base64Encode` (standard alphabet + padding via `Data.base64EncodedString()`), `base64Decode` (returns `nil` on invalid input — mirror of Foundation's safe-init pattern + the Kotlin `IllegalArgumentException` surfaced as a guard-let-friendly optional), `base64UrlEncodeNoPad` / `base64UrlDecodeNoPad` (RFC 4648 §5 — `+`→`-`, `/`→`_`, strip `=`; required for discovery-service URL segments that axum's path matcher rejects on standard-alphabet input). **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/Wire.swift`** — `Codable` structs mirroring the REST shapes `lakko-svc-auth` accepts: `PowChallengeResponse` / `PowProofPayload` / `SignupStartRequest` / `SignupStartResponse` / `SignupFinishRequest` / `SignupFinishResponse` / `LoginStartRequest` / `LoginStartResponse` / `LoginFinishRequest` / `LoginFinishResponse` / `PasswordResetStartRequest` / `PasswordResetStartResponse` / `PasswordResetFinishRequest` / `PasswordResetFinishResponse`. Each `Codable` struct has a `CodingKeys` enum remapping Swift-idiomatic `camelCase` to the server's `snake_case` `serde` defaults (`account_id`, `password_request`, `effort_bits`, etc.) — the JSON we emit is byte-identical to what the Kotlin side ships via `@SerialName` and the TS side ships via verbatim-snake_case interfaces. Optionals default to `nil`; `JSONEncoder` omits them entirely (Swift's `KeyedEncodingContainer.encode(nil)` skips by default — matches the `Json { explicitNulls = false }` knob on the Kotlin side + the TS spread-operator shape). **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/Pow.swift`** — `solvePow(seedB64:effortBits:) async throws -> PowProofPayload` wraps the UniFFI `powSolve` free function in a `Task.detached(priority: .userInitiated)` so the blocking Equix solve (100–500 ms at server-default `effort_bits = 8` on a 2024+ phone, seconds at higher difficulties) never blocks the main runloop. Mirror of the Kotlin `solvePow(...)` that hops to `Dispatchers.Default`; the Swift Structured-Concurrency model doesn't expose dispatchers as a parameter, so the dispatcher-injection knob the Kotlin tests use is replaced by `Task.detached` + `withCheckedThrowingContinuation` semantics for any future test harness. No explicit `proof.close()` — Swift ARC + UniFFI's deinit-driven Rust-Arc release handles cleanup deterministically; the Kotlin side needs `try { … } finally { proof.close() }` because Kotlin lacks deterministic destruction on object handles. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/AuthClient.swift`** — `URLSession`-based client with the seven 1:1 low-level methods (`powChallenge` / `signupStart` / `signupFinish` / `loginStart` / `loginFinish` / `recoveryLoginStart` / `recoveryLoginFinish` / `passwordResetStart` / `passwordResetFinish`) and three high-level orchestrators (`signup` / `login` / `recover`). **`signup(password:enrolRecovery:) async throws -> SignupResult`** drives the full `/pow/challenge → /signup/start → /pow/challenge → /signup/finish` flow with parallel OPAQUE-password + OPAQUE-recovery enrolment (default `enrolRecovery = true`); the recovery side uses a fresh `RecoveryMnemonic.generate()` whose 24-word phrase rides back in `SignupResult.recoveryPhrase` for the UI to display once. **`login(accountId:password:) async throws -> LoginResult`** drives the single-PoW `/login/start → /login/finish` flow + emits `(accountId, sessionKey, exportKey)`; wrong password / unknown account collapse into one 401 by server design (OPAQUE indistinguishable failure preserved). **`recover(accountId:mnemonicSeed:newPassword:) async throws -> RecoverResult`** drives the parallel-OPAQUE recovery-login + new-password-registration through `/password/reset/{start,finish}` — single PoW gate, atomic envelope rotation on the server. **Error vocabulary `AuthClientError`**: `.argument(String)` (caller-side wiring bug → form validation), `.http(status:endpoint:body:)` (non-2xx with truncated 512-char body for diagnostics), `.network(endpoint:underlying:)` (DNS/TLS/conn-refused → distinct so the UI can say "check connection" vs "wrong credentials"). All three cases conform to `LocalizedError` for SwiftUI `error.localizedDescription` consumers. **Default base URL `http://localhost:8081`** — the iOS simulator shares the host's loopback (unlike Android emulator which needs `10.0.2.2`); physical-device runs pass the workstation's LAN IP via `init(baseURL:)`; production switches to `https://api-dev.lakko.me` through the Cloudflare tunnel. **`URLSession.shared`** is the default backing session — `URLSession` is thread-safe and its connection pool is reused across calls, so one `AuthClient` per app process is the recommended lifetime. Injectable via the `init(session:)` parameter so tests can drop in a `URLProtocol`-stubbed configuration (Foundation analogue of Ktor's `MockEngine`). **Mirror of**: `apps/web/src/auth/{account-id,wire,pow,client}.ts` (1.17d3/d4) + `apps/android/.../auth/{AccountId,Wire,Pow,AuthClient}.kt` (1.15d2) — same JSON wire, same flow shapes, same error vocabulary, different idioms (Swift `async throws`, `Codable`, `URLSession` vs Kotlin `suspend` + `kotlinx-serialization` + Ktor). All four files parse clean via `swiftc -parse`. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (no Rust changes — pure Swift consumer-side work), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14d1 does NOT ship**: Secure-Enclave + Keychain device-root-key envelope (1.14d2); `AuthRepository` orchestrator + `AuthState` publisher (1.14d2); SwiftUI signup/login/recover screens (1.14d3/d4/d5); host-side unit tests with `URLProtocol`-stubbed responses (lands together with 1.14d5's instrumented test suite).
  - **1.14d2** ✅ Secure-Enclave / Keychain device-root-key storage + `AuthRepository` orchestrator — second half of the iOS OPAQUE foundations. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/DeviceKey.swift`** — `deriveDeviceRootKey(exportKey:accountIdB64:) -> Data` over CryptoKit's native `HKDF<SHA512>.deriveKey`. Same construction as the Android + Web sides: IKM = OPAQUE export key (64 bytes), salt = `"lakko-device-root-v1"` (ASCII, 20 bytes), info = `"lakko/device-root/v1|" || account_id_b64`, output = 32 bytes AES-256. **CryptoKit, not a hand-rolled HMAC loop**: Apple ships RFC 5869 HKDF natively since iOS 14 — same posture is impossible on Android (vendor-flaky `KeyAgreement` HKDF in `AndroidKeyStore` API 31+) which forced the 50-line hand-roll on the Kotlin side. Output is byte-identical to RFC 5869 test vectors + to the WebCrypto and Android sides — a user who signs up on Web and re-installs on iOS lands on the **same** device root key for the same password, important for the eventual cross-device parity 1.16+ promises (one identity, multiple platforms, same data). **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/DeviceKeyStore.swift`** — Keychain-Services-backed per-account storage. **No separate wrap-key dance**: Android's Keystore has strict size limits + needs explicit StrongBox/TEE branching, hence the AES-GCM wrap + `getNoBackupFilesDir()` envelope; iOS Keychain has neither limit + uses Secure-Enclave-derived class keys for hardware encryption directly. We store the 32-byte root key as the Keychain item's `kSecValueData` under `kSecClass = kSecClassGenericPassword` with: `kSecAttrService = "me.lakko.device-key.v1"` (versioned so a future rotation has somewhere to land), `kSecAttrAccount = accountIdB64` (per-account namespacing), `kSecAttrAccessible = kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` (device-bound — no iCloud Keychain sync, mirror of Android's `noBackupFilesDir` posture; available after first unlock, allows push-triggered background MLS decrypts without re-biometric — iOS analogue of the Android `setUserAuthenticationRequired(false)` choice). **Upsert via delete-then-add** because Keychain ships neither `SecItemUpsert` nor an `errSecDuplicateItem` ignore-flag; race-free because there's only ever one writer (the auth flow on `@MainActor`) per account. `errSecItemNotFound` on `getDeviceKey` returns `nil` (mirror of the Android + Web shape); other non-success `OSStatus` values surface as `DeviceKeyStoreError.keychain(status:operation:)`. **Hardware-isolated baseline**: every iPhone since A7 / 2013 ships a Secure Enclave processor — well below our iOS 17 deployment target's hardware floor — so the Keychain payload is **always** hardware-encrypted by a class key the Secure Enclave never releases to user-space code, even with root. Same threat-model posture as Android's StrongBox path, without the StrongBox-unavailable branching the Kotlin side needs. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/SessionStore.swift`** — `UserDefaults`-backed `(accountIdB64, username)` marker. Neither field is secret on its own (the Keychain-resident material lives in `DeviceKeyStore`); plain `UserDefaults` is the right tool here — small, synchronous, no encryption overhead. Versioned via a `schemaVersionKey` so a future shape change can drop-old-rows instead of mis-parsing them. `clear()` is the `forgetDevice` primitive; `save()` is idempotent (re-saving the same `(accountId, username)` is a no-op semantically). Injectable `UserDefaults` suite via the `init(defaults:)` parameter so tests pass a transient instance. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/AuthRepository.swift`** — `@MainActor` `ObservableObject` with two `@Published` slots: `state: AuthState` (`.loading` / `.anon(cachedUsername:)` / `.session(SessionAccount)` — enum mirror of the Kotlin `sealed interface AuthState`) + `identitySecrets: IdentitySecrets?` (in-memory `(accountId, exportKey)` after a fresh authenticate; `nil` after a session-restore from disk or after logout). **Three async flows**: `signup(username:password:)` (drives `AuthClient.signup` + saves the SessionStore marker + persists the Keychain root key in that order — the marker-before-key sequencing leaves a recoverable state on crash: rehydrate detects "marker but no device key" and asks for the password again; the reverse would leave a working device key with no record of who it belongs to), `login(username:password:)` (resolves the typed username against the on-device SessionStore cache — cross-device login lands in 1.14e with discovery; mismatch collapses into the same 401 as a wrong password to preserve OPAQUE's no-probing property), `recover(mnemonicPhrase:newPassword:)` (parses the BIP39 phrase via `RecoveryMnemonic.fromPhrase` up front so wrong-checksum / unknown-word failures land before the network call). Plus two synchronous methods: `logout()` (drops in-memory session, keeps Keychain + SessionStore — mirror of the web's `logout`) and `forgetDevice()` (wipes both — only path back is the 24-word recovery phrase). **`identitySecrets` lifecycle**: populated whenever the user just authenticated (those flows have the export key in scope); **not** populated on a session-restore from disk (the export key is not persisted — only the device root key, which is a one-way HKDF; the export key cannot be recovered from the device-key bytes). Consumers that need to sign on behalf of the user (`ContactsClient.sendContactRequest` in 1.14e, MLS commits in 1.14f) MUST check this and surface a "please re-enter your password" prompt when it's `nil`. **Mirror of**: `apps/web/src/auth/{device-key,AuthContext}.ts/.tsx` (1.17d4/d5) + `apps/android/.../auth/{DeviceKey,DeviceKeyStore,SessionStore,AuthRepository}.kt` (1.15d3/d4) — same HKDF construction, same hardware-isolation posture (Secure Enclave ↔ StrongBox/TEE), same `AuthState` discriminated-union shape, same `identitySecrets` lifecycle contract. All four files parse clean via `swiftc -parse`. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (still no Rust changes), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14d2 does NOT ship**: SwiftUI screens (1.14d3/d4/d5); host-side unit tests for the HKDF round-trip + Keychain envelope (the Keychain Services framework requires a real device / simulator runtime, so `cargo test`-style host execution can't exercise it; coverage comes from the instrumented test suite that lands alongside 1.14d5); cross-device login resolver (1.14e + discovery service).
  - **1.14d3** ✅ SwiftUI signup screen — first of the three auth surfaces. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/SignupViewModel.swift`** — `@MainActor` `ObservableObject` mirror of the Compose `SignupViewModel` from 1.15d4 + the web's `Signup.tsx` local signals (1.17d6). **Two-stage local FSM** via the public `Stage` enum (`form` ↔ `phrase(String)`) — splits signup from "save your recovery phrase" so the user sees the 24 words exactly once and explicitly acknowledges having written them down before navigating into the authenticated app. Re-display is impossible by construction — the `.phrase(_:)` associated value holds the only in-memory reference and is dropped on `onAcknowledge()`. **`@Published` slots**: `username` / `password` / `confirm` / `acknowledged` (form fields, bidirectional via SwiftUI `$` binding); `busy` / `error` / `stage` (read-only outputs, `private(set)`). **`onSubmit()`** validates locally (password ≥ 8, confirm matches), kicks `repo.signup(username:password:)` in a `Task { … }`, surfaces success as a `stage = .phrase(outcome.recoveryPhrase)` transition, surfaces errors via `userFacing(_:)` which maps `AuthClientError.http` cases by status code (409 → "account exists", 429 → "too many", 5xx → "server error", else → generic). Wrong-input / network failures surface inline; no exception leaks past the VM boundary. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/SignupScreen.swift`** — `View` with two branches keyed on `viewModel.stage`. **Stage 1: `SignupForm`** — `ScrollView { VStack { TextField + SecureField×2 + Button } }` with the same OPAQUE-PAKE explainer copy the Android + web sides use ("The password never leaves your device — the server only sees an OPRF-blinded envelope"). All three fields use `.textInputAutocapitalization(.never)` + `.autocorrectionDisabled(true)` so the iOS keyboard doesn't helpfully Title-Case `alice` or autocorrect `correct horse battery staple`. The submit button shows an inline `ProgressView` + "Creating account…" while `viewModel.busy` is `true`. **Stage 2: `RecoveryPhrasePanel`** — 24-word grid laid out as two columns of 12 in an `HStack` of `VStack`s, each row a fixed-width index column (`"%2d."`-formatted) + monospaced word (mirror of the Compose two-column shape with `Modifier.weight(1f)`). The acknowledgement gate is a SwiftUI `Toggle` (iOS equivalent of Compose's `Checkbox`) — the "Done — take me to Lakko" `Button` is `.disabled(!acknowledged)` until the toggle flips. **`onAuthenticated` callback** invoked exactly once after acknowledgement; the AppRoot-level router actually swaps to `HomeScreen` on the `AuthState` transition that `AuthRepository.signup` already drove, so the callback is mostly a no-op signal (kept for parity + future tracking-event hooks). **VM ownership via `@StateObject`** at the parent `AnonRoot` scope (see 1.14d5's wiring section); the view itself receives the VM through `@ObservedObject` for the standard "parent owns, child observes" SwiftUI shape. **Mirror of**: `apps/android/.../ui/auth/SignupScreen.kt` + `SignupViewModel.kt` (1.15d4) + `apps/web/src/routes/Signup.tsx` (1.17d6). Same field set, same two-stage FSM, same error-mapping table, same submit button affordances; different idioms (SwiftUI's `@Published` + `Task { }` vs Compose's `StateFlow` + `viewModelScope.launch` vs Solid's `createSignal` + `async/await`). All files parse clean via `swiftc -parse`. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (no Rust changes), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14d3 does NOT ship**: login / recover screens (1.14d4/d5); password-strength meter beyond the `≥ 8` floor (lands when Argon2id KSF cost feedback is integrated — post-1.18); username-availability check at submit time (lands with the discovery surface in 1.14e); accessibility audit pass (post-MVP polish phase).
  - **1.14d4** ✅ SwiftUI login screen — second of the three auth surfaces. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/LoginViewModel.swift`** — `@MainActor` `ObservableObject` mirror of the Compose `LoginViewModel` from 1.15d5 + the web's `Login.tsx` local signals (1.17d7). Simpler than `SignupViewModel` — single-stage form, no recovery-phrase post-step. **`init(repository:cachedUsername:)`** pre-fills the `username` field from the cached value (passed through from `AuthState.anon(cachedUsername:)` at the AppRoot level); on a fresh device (no cache) the field starts empty and the user types from scratch. **`onSubmit()`** kicks `repo.login(username:password:)` in a `Task { … }`, clears the `password` field on success (the AppRoot router will snap to `HomeScreen` on the `AuthState` flip anyway — wiping the local buffer just keeps the SecureField text from lingering past the navigation), surfaces errors via `userFacing(_:)` which maps the wire-level status codes: 401 → generic "Invalid credentials" (wrong-username and wrong-password collapse to the same copy by design — OPAQUE's no-probing property the server enforces would leak account existence otherwise), 429 → "too many", 5xx → "server error", else → generic. Network failures collapse to the same indistinguishable-failure copy as signup ("check connection"). **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/LoginScreen.swift`** — `View` with a single `ScrollView { VStack { … } }` rendering: title + OPAQUE explainer ("Wrong username and wrong password produce the same error — by design"), `TextField("Username")` + `SecureField("Password")` with the same `.textInputAutocapitalization(.never)` + `.autocorrectionDisabled(true)` posture as Signup, submit button with inline `ProgressView` + "Signing in…" copy, two footer rows — "New to Lakko? Create an account" + "Lost your password? Recover with your 24-word phrase" — each a `Button` that flips the `AnonRoot`-level `destination` state to `.signup` / `.recover`. **`onNavigateToSignup` + `onNavigateToRecover`** wired as escaping closures; mirror of Compose's `Modifier.clickable(onClick:)` on the footer rows. **VM ownership** via `@StateObject` at the `AnonRoot` scope — see 1.14d5's wiring section. The Compose side relies on `viewModel()` + `ViewModelStoreOwner` for Activity-scoped lifetime; the SwiftUI side achieves the same shape via `@StateObject` keyed to the lifetime of the containing view. **Mirror of**: `apps/android/.../ui/auth/{LoginScreen,LoginViewModel}.kt` (1.15d5) + `apps/web/src/routes/Login.tsx` (1.17d7). Same field set, same error-mapping table, same forwarding-callbacks shape. Parses clean. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (no Rust changes), `fmt --check` clean. **What 1.14d4 does NOT ship**: recover screen (1.14d5); password-strength feedback; biometric / Face ID login (lands when the `LAContext` + Keychain Touch-ID-binding work follows, post-1.18); username-availability check (1.14e); host-side unit tests with `URLProtocol`-stubbed responses (lands together with 1.14d5's instrumented suite).
  - **1.14d5** ✅ SwiftUI recover-from-mnemonic screen + AppRoot router + minimal HomeScreen — closes the iOS OPAQUE-PAKE surface. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/RecoverViewModel.swift`** — `@MainActor` `ObservableObject` mirror of the Compose `RecoverViewModel` (1.15d6) + the web's `Recover.tsx` local signals (1.17d8). Single-stage form (`phrase`, `password`, `confirm`); the `cachedUsername` constructor arg drives the "Account on this device: @alice" line + the "no cached account" stub path. **`onSubmit()`** validates locally (new password ≥ 8, confirm matches, phrase non-empty after whitespace trim), kicks `repo.recover(mnemonicPhrase:newPassword:)` in a `Task { … }`, surfaces success via the implicit `AuthState.session(_:)` transition the repository drives. **Error mapping** has three layers: (1) `FfiError.Mnemonic` (BIP39 parse failure — unknown word + bad checksum both flow here through `RecoveryMnemonic.fromPhrase`) → generic "Invalid recovery phrase. Check the spelling and order of all 24 words" — by design indistinguishable, preserves BIP39's no-probing property; (2) `AuthClientError.http(401)` → "Invalid recovery phrase" (wrong-mnemonic surfaces at the OPAQUE AKE finalisation, server returns 401 by design); (3) all other `AuthClientError` cases handled like Signup/Login (network → "check connection", 429 → "too many", 5xx → "server error", else → generic). **UniFFI Swift-binding naming gotcha**: the Swift codegen surfaces `FfiError` (not `FfiException` like Kotlin) and uses `PascalCase` case names per the codegen shape — `.Mnemonic` / `.Auth` / `.Pow` (vs the Kotlin `FfiException.Mnemonic` sub-class hierarchy). Caught via `swiftc -parse` after the Kotlin-style first draft; the fix is a one-line rename. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Auth/RecoverScreen.swift`** — `View` with a two-render-path body keyed on `cachedUsername`. **`RecoverForm`** (cached account present): paragraph copy + account line + multi-line `TextEditor` for the 24-word phrase (with a manual `ZStack`-overlaid placeholder "abandon abandon abandon … art" — SwiftUI's `TextEditor` ships no placeholder API; the canonical workaround is an overlaid faded `Text` that fades out the moment the buffer becomes non-empty) + `SecureField`s for new password + confirm + submit button with `ProgressView` + "Verifying…" copy + "Remembered your password? Log in" footer link. Monospaced font on the phrase editor mirrors the Compose `FontFamily.Monospace` posture — visual cue that these are wordlist tokens, not free-form prose. **`NoCachedAccountPanel`** (cachedUsername is `nil`): paragraph explaining that cross-device recovery lands in 1.14e with the discovery service + two side-by-side `Button`s ("Create account", "Log in") routing to the sibling anon destinations — same posture as the Android stub. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Home/HomeScreen.swift`** — minimal post-auth landing surface mirror of `apps/android`'s `HomeScreen.kt` (1.15d4-vintage, pre-Contacts/Chats buttons). Renders `"Lakko"` headline + `"Signed in as @{username}"` line + `account_id_b64` in a monospaced selectable text + "Log out" / "Forget device" button pair. Contacts (1.14e) + Chats (1.14f) entry buttons land in those sub-sprints; the iOS Home stays minimal until then. **`apps/ios/Lakko/LakkoApp.swift` rewritten** — the `@main` `App` now owns a process-scoped `@StateObject AuthRepository` + injects it via `.environmentObject(authRepository)` for the AppRoot subtree. **`AppRoot`** — top-level router: `switch authRepository.state` over `.loading` (renders `Color(.systemBackground)` so a slow rehydrate doesn't flash white) / `.anon(cachedUsername)` → `AnonRoot(repository: cachedUsername:)` / `.session(account)` → `HomeScreen(account:onLogout:onForgetDevice:)`. **`AnonRoot`** — private `View` with three `@StateObject` slots (Signup / Login / Recover VMs, all owned at this scope so they survive any outer view-tree rebuild — constructing the VMs inside the `switch` cases would orphan in-flight async `Task`s the moment SwiftUI re-runs `body`, which is the bug the `@StateObject(wrappedValue:)` pattern in the explicit `init` fixes). Local `@State destination: AnonDestination` toggle defaulted on `cachedUsername` (returning user → Login, fresh device → Signup) — same default logic as the Android `AnonRoot`. **`HelloCoreView.swift` deleted** — Phase 1.14c's smoke surface is no longer reachable; the auth landing replaces it. Mirror of how the Android side dropped `HelloCoreScreen` once `MainActivity` got the auth router. **Full Swift tree** at `apps/ios/Lakko/` (Auth + UI/Auth + UI/Home + LakkoApp) parses clean via `swiftc -parse` (every file checked, including against forward references). `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39 passing (still no Rust changes — 1.14d is a pure Apple-consumer-side sprint), `fmt --check` clean. **Mirror of**: `apps/android`'s 1.15d6 recover surface + the full anon-router shape (Login default for returning, Signup for fresh) + `apps/web`'s `Recover.tsx` (1.17d8) + `routes.tsx` anon group. **What 1.14d5 does NOT ship**: host-side / instrumented test suite — Swift Package + the SwiftUI screens need a real iOS Simulator runtime to exercise (no host-JVM equivalent for `:app:testDebugUnitTest` works here; tests land with the XCTest target a future sub-sprint introduces, batched with the wider 1.14g testing pass once the contacts + chat surfaces in 1.14e/f land); password-strength meter + Argon2id KSF cost feedback (post-1.18); biometric login (post-1.18); cross-device recovery via discovery (1.14e); QR-based OOB onboarding shortcut (post-MVP); SwiftUI Preview providers for each screen (deferred — the screens depend on `AuthRepository` which depends on `URLSession` + `Keychain`, neither of which previews cleanly without a fake; lands when a `PreviewAuthRepository` stub is worth maintaining).
  - **1.14e1** ✅ Swift `DiscoveryClient` + `Wire` — port of `lakko-svc-discovery`'s OPRF + register + lookup + identity-bundle + KeyPackage surface to URLSession. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Discovery/Wire.swift`** — `Codable` mirrors of `services/discovery/src/{oprf,registry,identity_bundle,keypackage}.rs` (`OprfRequest` / `OprfResponse` / `RegisterRequest` / `RegisterResponse` / `LookupRequest` / `LookupResponse` / `IdentityUploadRequest` / `IdentityUploadResponse` / `IdentityFetchResponse` / `KeyPackageUploadRequest` / `KeyPackageUploadResponse` / `KeyPackageFetchResponse`); reuses `PowProofPayload` from `apps/ios/Lakko/Auth/Wire.swift` (the `services/discovery/pow/challenge` JSON shape is byte-identical to the auth side's). **`apps/ios/Lakko/Discovery/DiscoveryClient.swift`** — `URLSession`-based client mirroring `apps/android/.../discovery/DiscoveryClient.kt` (1.15e3). Two layers: 1:1 low-level methods (`powChallenge` / `oprfEvaluate` / `register` / `lookupRaw` / `identityUpload` / `identityFetch` / `keypackageUpload` / `keypackageFetch`) + three high-level orchestrators driving the multi-PoW + OPRF flow (`registerUsername(accountId:username:)`, `lookup(username:)`, `uploadIdentityBundle(...)` + the supporting `uploadKeyPackage` / `fetchKeyPackage` helpers). **OPRF dance**: `OprfBlinder.blind(username:)` → read `normalizedUsername()` before finalize (the UniFFI sentinel prevents double-finalize) → `oprfEvaluate` → `finalize(evaluatedB64:)` → second PoW → `register` / `lookup`. **404 fold**: discovery is the first iOS client that needs both "throwing-on-non-2xx" + "`nil`-on-typed-404" paths; `executeNullableOn404(...)` factors the second flavor out so `lookupRaw` + `identityFetch` + `keypackageFetch` all share the fold logic. **Error vocabulary** `DiscoveryClientError`: `.argument` / `.http(status:endpoint:body:)` / `.network(endpoint:underlying:)`, same shape as `AuthClientError` from 1.14d1 — UI branches uniformly on the three cases. **Default base URL `http://localhost:8084`** — iOS simulator loopback (cf. Android emulator's `10.0.2.2:8084`). **What 1.14e1 does NOT ship**: invite endpoints (lands in 1.14e6); DS journal client (1.14e2); ContactsClient orchestrator (1.14e3); SwiftUI Contacts UI (1.14e4). `swiftc -parse apps/ios/Lakko/Discovery/*.swift` clean; `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39, `cargo fmt --check` clean (no Rust changes — pure consumer-side port).
  - **1.14e2** ✅ Swift `DsClient` — port of `lakko-svc-ds`'s mailbox + group-journal append-only relay to URLSession. **`apps/ios/Lakko/DS/DsClient.swift`** — `URLSession`-based client mirroring `apps/android/.../ds/DsClient.kt` (1.15e4). Four public methods: `publishMailbox(pseudonym:payload:)` / `publishGroup(groupId:payload:)` (octet-stream POST, JSON response carrying assigned monotonic `id` + ISO-8601 `createdAt`); `fetchMailbox(pseudonym:after:limit:waitSecs:)` / `fetchGroup(groupId:after:limit:waitSecs:)` (GET with `after` + `limit` + `wait` query params, JSON envelope of `payload_b64` rows decoded back to raw `Data`). **Hex slug encoding**: 32-byte pseudonyms surface as lowercase hex in the URL (server requirement — `pseudonymHex(_:)` is exposed as a `public static` helper since tests + future direct URL builders may want it). **Long-poll timeout**: when the caller passes `waitSecs > 0`, the per-request `URLRequest.timeoutInterval` is bumped to `waitSecs + 10` so the server's clamp at its `MAX_WAIT_SECS` can complete without URLSession timing out first — the Android side relies on Ktor's default per-request timeout which is large enough; iOS's `URLSession.shared` default is 60s so we have to opt in. **Wire shapes** kept `private` — `PublishWireResponse` / `FetchWireResponse` / `FetchWireMessage` are internal decode helpers; the public surface is `PublishResult` / `MailboxMessage` / `FetchResult` with decoded `Data` payloads instead of base64 strings. **`MailboxMessage` Equatable conformance** synthesized automatically (every stored field is `Equatable`), no manual `==` override needed unlike the Kotlin side's `ByteArray` workaround. **What 1.14e2 does NOT ship**: ContactsClient (1.14e3); auth tokens (Phase 1 DS is open — production gate lands post-1.5b alongside the wider service-mesh work). `swiftc -parse apps/ios/Lakko/DS/*.swift` clean; cargo gates 39/39 + fmt clean.
  - **1.14e3** ✅ Swift `ContactsClient` + `ContactsRepository` + envelope codec. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Contacts/Envelope.swift`** — 1-byte kind discriminator mirror of `apps/android/.../contacts/Envelope.kt` (1.15e5). `EnvelopeKind` enum (`.contactRequest = 0x01`, `.welcome = 0x02`); `wrapEnvelope(kind:body:)` produces fresh `Data`; `parseEnvelope(_:)` returns a discriminated `ParsedEnvelope` (`.contactRequest(body:)` / `.welcome(body:)` / `.unknown(rawKind:body:)`) — unknown kinds surface so the receiver can log + advance the cursor without throwing. Empty input maps to `.unknown(rawKind: -1, body: …)` per the Phase-1 "garbage skips, doesn't crash" posture. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Contacts/ContactsClient.swift`** — orchestrates `DiscoveryClient` + `DsClient` + the UniFFI `composeContactRequest` / `ContactRequest.decodeAndVerify` / `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity`. Three public methods: `sendContactRequest(senderSigningSeed:senderAccountId:senderSignaturePubkey:recipientUsername:introMessage:)` (resolve username → fetch bundle → compose + seal under recipient's X25519 → publish to recipient's mailbox pseudonym, wrapped as `kind=0x01`), `sendContactRequestToBundle(...)` (same compose+publish path, skipping the lookup/identityFetch round-trips when the caller already holds the bundle — the invite-link consume flow's canonical caller, 1.14e8), and `pollStrangerInbox(mySignaturePubkey:myX25519Secret:after:waitSecs:)` (fetch mailbox → decode each envelope → verify each ContactRequest → return mixed `[IncomingMailboxEvent]` + fresh cursor; Welcome envelopes pass through as `.welcome(messageId:welcomeBytes:createdAt:)` for the future ChatRepository to consume). **Result types**: `SendResult` / `IncomingContactRequest` / `IncomingMailboxEvent` (sealed enum mirror of Kotlin `sealed interface`) / `PollResult`. **`ContactsError` enum** with discriminated cases `.usernameUnknown(String)` / `.noIdentityBundle(Data)` / `.wrapped(Error)` + the `wrap(_:)` helper that idempotently lifts any underlying error into the wrapped case; UI branches on the case statement. Malformed-payload posture: bad-signature / undecryptable envelopes are skipped silently — the cursor advances so we don't re-process the same row. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Contacts/ContactsRepository.swift`** — `@MainActor ObservableObject` mirror of `apps/android/.../contacts/ContactsRepository.kt` scoped to the 1.14e3 surface (no invite methods — those land in 1.14e7/e8). Owns one `@Published var state: ContactsState`; methods `sendContactRequest(recipientUsername:introMessage:)`, `refreshInbox(waitSecs:)`, `acceptContactRequest(messageId:groupIdHex:)` (local-state-only — MLS handshake arrives in 1.14f with `ChatRepository`), `declineContactRequest(messageId:)`, `clear()`. Identity construction per-call via `Identity.fromSeed(secrets.exportKey)` — sub-millisecond, ARC drops the Rust handle on scope exit. `ContactsState` is a `struct` with explicit `with(...)` mutators (Swift doesn't ship Kotlin's `data class .copy(named = ...)` — a small fan-out of `with` helpers keeps the call sites readable without exposing every field as `var`). **Welcome forwarding** intentionally a no-op in 1.14e3 — the `ChatRepository` substrate lands in 1.14f; Welcomes drop on the floor but the cursor still advances. `swiftc -parse` clean; 39/39 cargo + fmt clean.
  - **1.14e4** ✅ SwiftUI Contacts surface. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Contacts/ContactsViewModel.swift`** — `@MainActor ObservableObject` mirror of `apps/android/.../ui/contacts/ContactsViewModel.kt`. Subscribes to `ContactsRepository.$state` via Combine + republishes through its own `@Published var repositoryState: ContactsState` so SwiftUI's `@ObservedObject` chain only has to traverse the VM (one ObservableObject per `body` re-evaluation, not two). Three form fields (`usernameInput` / `introInput` + the `sending` / `sendError` ephemeral status); three actions (`onSendClicked()` validates the username + drives `repository.sendContactRequest(...)`, clears the form on success / surfaces `userFacing(_:)`-mapped errors inline; `onRefreshClicked()` kicks a manual inbox poll; `onAcceptClicked(messageId:)` / `onDeclineClicked(messageId:)` are local-state-only routing — the MLS handshake side lands in 1.14f). **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Contacts/ContactsScreen.swift`** — SwiftUI `NavigationStack`-wrapped view with the six sections (sendForm / actionsRow / pendingSection / acceptedSection / sentSection + the requires-reauth panel that takes over the whole screen when `repositoryState.requiresReauth` is true).   - **1.14e5** ✅ Foreground continuous polling on the Contacts screen. **`ContactsViewModel.pollOnceLongPoll(waitSecs:)`** — one long-poll tick, defaults to `Self.defaultLongPollSecs = 30`. **`ContactsScreen.task { while !Task.isCancelled { await viewModel.pollOnceLongPoll(); await Task.yield() } }`** — SwiftUI's `.task` modifier ties the lifecycle of the loop to the view: the task starts on appear and cancels automatically on disappear, mirror of the Compose `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { while (isActive) { ... } }` shape from Android 1.15e7. The 30-second budget rides through on `repository.refreshInbox(waitSecs: 30)`, which routes to `DsClient.fetchMailbox(waitSecs: 30)`; the per-request timeout was already bumped to `waitSecs + 10` in 1.14e2. **Refresh-flag posture**: the long-poll path explicitly does NOT raise `repositoryState.refreshing` — the spinner only blinks on a manual `onRefreshClicked()` tap, never for the full 30-second long-poll window. Inline-error landing in `repositoryState.lastError` still works for transport-level failures during the loop; the next iteration just retries. **What 1.14e5 does NOT ship**: BGTaskScheduler-driven background fetch (1.14e5b — needs an `BGAppRefreshTask` registration + a power/network budget profile); APNs push integration (1.14e5c — needs the push-gw envelope decoder + a `UNNotificationServiceExtension` for the silent-push wakeup). The foreground loop covers the canonical "app on screen" path; the latter two close the gap when the user is elsewhere. `swiftc -parse` clean.

The Android side uses an `Intent.ACTION_SEND` chooser; SwiftUI ships `ShareLink` since iOS 16 which is identical UX for the user. **`OutstandingInviteRow`** uses `RelativeDateTimeFormatter` for the "expires in 6 days" copy — same string the Compose `DateUtils.getRelativeTimeSpanString(...)` produces, locale-aware on both sides. `swiftc -parse` clean.
  - **1.14e8** ✅ Joiner-side `acceptInviteLink` orchestrator. **`ContactsRepository.acceptInviteLink(slug:) async -> AcceptInviteResult`** — drives the full happy + sad-path matrix: 1) base64url-decode the slug → 32-byte `blobId`; 2) `DiscoveryClient.fetchInvite(blobId:)` → capability wire bytes (404 → `.notFound`); 3) `InviteCapability.decodeAndVerify(wire:)` — local Ed25519 signature check (a malicious server cannot serve substituted bytes; the joiner re-verifies); 4) `capability.isValidNow(nowMs:)` → `.expired` on past-expiry; 5) `DiscoveryClient.consumeInvite(blobId:capabilityBytes:joinerAccountId:)` → folds `.exhausted` / `.notFound` / `.alreadyConsumed` / `.consumed` (the dedup tag prevents accidental double-bumps when the user revisits the same link); 6) `ContactsClient.sendContactRequestToBundle(...)` — ship a ContactRequest to the inviter's stranger inbox; the bundle is inline in the capability so no `DiscoveryClient.lookup` round-trip needed. **`AcceptInviteResult` enum** with eight variants matching the Kotlin `sealed interface AcceptInviteResult` from `apps/android/.../contacts/ContactsRepository.kt` (1.15e11a): `.accepted(recipientAccountId:groupIdHex:)` / `.alreadyAccepted` / `.expired` / `.exhausted` / `.notFound` / `.malformedSlug` / `.requiresReauth` / `.failed(message:)`. `groupIdHex` is always `nil` in 1.14e8 — the `ChatRepository.startGroupWith(...)` substrate lands in 1.14f, until then the UI surfaces an "Open chat (coming in 1.14f)" hint on the resulting accepted row, mirror of the Android pattern. The synthetic `IncomingContactRequest` (with `messageId` from the outbound DS receipt + a zeroed 32-byte pseudonym) gets appended to `state.accepted` so the user sees the result in the Contacts list immediately. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Contacts/InviteIntake.swift`** — process-scoped `@MainActor ObservableObject` mirror of `apps/android/.../contacts/InviteIntake.kt`. Single `@Published private(set) var pendingSlug: String?` + `handle(_ url: URL)` (parses the `/i/<slug>` path; silently no-ops on URLs that don't match the shape — the on-server `apple-app-site-association` JSON is the security gate; SwiftUI's `.onOpenURL` only fires for AASA-verified domains) + `clear()`. `swiftc -parse` clean.
  - **1.14e9** ✅ `InviteLandingScreen` + Universal Links wiring — closes the iOS invite-link round trip. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Contacts/InviteLandingScreen.swift`** — SwiftUI `View` rendering one of eight discriminated outcome panels keyed on `InviteLandingViewModel.state` (`.running` shows a ProgressView + "Verifying invite…"; `.done(AcceptInviteResult)` shows headline + copy per variant — "Invite accepted" / "Already accepted" / "Invite expired" / "Invite exhausted" / "Invite not found" / "Invalid invite link" / "Please log in first" / "Couldn't accept invite" with the inner `failed(message:)` text). **`InviteLandingViewModel`** — `@MainActor ObservableObject` with a single-shot `run()` (guarded by `didRun: Bool` against SwiftUI re-running the `.task` modifier across body rebuilds), drives `repository.acceptInviteLink(slug:)`, transitions `state` to `.done(...)`, stays there until the user taps Done. `dismiss()` clears the parked slug via `InviteIntake.clear()` so a subsequent invite tap goes through cleanly. **`LakkoApp` rewired**: process-scoped `@StateObject var inviteIntake = InviteIntake()` + `.environmentObject(inviteIntake)` on the WindowGroup + the `.onOpenURL { url in inviteIntake.handle(url) }` modifier — SwiftUI fires this for both cold-start and hot deep-link paths on iOS 14+, by the time `AppRoot` reads `pendingSlug` on first body evaluation it's already populated. **`SessionRoot` rewired**: an `@ObservedObject var inviteIntake` slot; when `inviteIntake.pendingSlug` is non-`nil`, the SessionRoot body short-circuits to `InviteLandingScreen` over whatever destination the user was on. Anon-state intents stay parked — `AnonRoot` ignores `pendingSlug` (the `acceptInviteLink` flow needs `identitySecrets`); once the user finishes auth, the session-state transition picks up the slug from the still-populated publisher. **`project.yml`** gains an `entitlements` block (`com.apple.developer.associated-domains: [applinks:lakko.me]`) so xcodegen emits `Lakko.entitlements` alongside the Info.plist; the server-side `apple-app-site-association` JSON at `https://lakko.me/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` (served by `lakko-svc-discovery`) is the other half — the JSON claims this bundle id under `applinks/details/appIDs`. `swiftc -parse` clean on the entire `apps/ios/Lakko/` tree; `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 39/39, `cargo fmt --check` clean — Phase 1.14e closes with zero Rust changes (every iOS-side sprint in this batch reuses the shared crate's `lakko-discovery-client` + `lakko-contacts` + `lakko-mls` surfaces verbatim through the XCFramework). **What 1.14e does NOT ship overall**: 1×1 MLS chat surface (1.14f); `ChatRepository.startGroupWith` bridge that lights up the `groupIdHex` field on accepted contacts; BGTaskScheduler + APNs background poll (1.14e5b/c); XCTest target — the SwiftUI screens need a real iOS Simulator runtime (no host-JVM equivalent), so tests land alongside 1.14g once the contacts + chat surfaces are stable; the server-side `apple-app-site-association` JSON itself (a `lakko-svc-discovery` config + deploy task, batched with the wider rollout).
  - **1.14f1** ✅ Swift `ChatClient` — Application-message orchestrator on top of MLS groups. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatClient.swift`** — mirror of `apps/android/.../chat/ChatClient.kt` (1.15f2) + `apps/web/src/chat/client.ts` (1.17f2). Two operations: `sendMessage(identity:group:plaintext:) async throws -> SendMessageResult` (drives `Group.createApplicationMessage(identity:plaintext:)` + posts wire bytes to the DS group journal via `DsClient.publishGroup`); `pollMessages(identity:group:after:waitSecs:) async throws -> PollMessagesResult` (fetches group journal, decrypts each row via `group.processIncoming(identity:bytes:)`, surfaces a discriminated `[IncomingChatEvent]`). **Self-echo suppression**: `Group.createApplicationMessage` produces wire bytes that look like a normal MLS application message — the DS does not dedupe + OpenMLS cannot decrypt the sender's own messages at the same epoch.
