We track DS ids of own publishes in a `Set<Int64>` guarded by a serial `DispatchQueue` (Swift Concurrency `Sendable` actor isolation is too coarse — needs cross-Task mutation with sync semantics, hence the explicit `DispatchQueue.sync` shape). `markOwnPublishedCommit(messageId:)` is the cross-client accessor for the `ContactsClient`-side addMember commits to register against the same set. **`ProcessedMessage` Swift enum** mapped to `IncomingChatEvent` enum: `.application(data:)` becomes `.application(messageId:plaintext:createdAt:)` (UTF-8 decode with replacement on malformed bytes); `.commit / .proposal / .externalJoinProposal` become `.system(messageId:systemKind:createdAt:)`. **Error vocabulary `ChatError`**: `.argument(String)` / `.wrapped(String, underlying: Error)`. Bad frames / wrong epochs / malformed wire are silently dropped on the poll path — cursor still advances so we do not re-process. **No `AutoCloseable` on iOS** unlike the Kotlin side — Swift ARC + UniFFI deinit-driven Rust-Arc release handles `DsClient` cleanup automatically. `swiftc -parse` clean.
  - **1.14f2** ✅ Swift `ChatRepository` + `IdentitySnapshotStore` + `KnownGroupsStore` — owns the canonical session Identity + per-group `Group` handle map + snapshot persistence. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatRepository.swift`** — `@MainActor ObservableObject` mirror of `apps/android/.../chat/ChatRepository.kt` (1.15f4a). Owns: canonical `Identity` (built once from the OPAQUE export key via `bootstrap(secrets:)`, kept alive until `clear()` / `forgetDevice()`); per-group `[String: GroupRecord]` map keyed by lowercase-hex group id; canonical `@Published var state: ChatRepositoryState`. Throwaway `Identity.fromSeed` per op (the simpler shape that `ContactsRepository` uses for its stateless sign/decrypt surface) would lose every ratchet advance — chat needs the single-Identity-per-session model. **Methods**: `bootstrap(secrets:)` (deterministic + idempotent for the same account; switching account wipes in-memory state first; restores on-disk snapshot via `IdentitySnapshotStore.getSnapshot`; eagerly rebuilds `Group` handles for every hex id in `KnownGroupsStore` via `loadGroup(identity:groupId:)`); `startGroupWith(recipientAccountId:recipientSignaturePubkey:) async throws -> String` (fetch KeyPackage, `Group.create(identity:)`, `addMemberFromWire`, drop the commit since the group is one-of-one, wrap the welcome with `.welcome` kind + POST to the recipient mailbox pseudonym, persist snapshot + known-groups); `joinGroupFromWelcome(welcomeBytes:) async throws -> String`; `sendMessage(groupIdHex:plaintext:)` / `pollGroup(groupIdHex:waitSecs:)` / `pollAllGroups(waitSecs:)`; `clear()` / `forgetDevice()`. **`@MainActor` simplifies the concurrency model** vs. Android `Mutex + AtomicReference`: every state mutation lands on the main runloop, async ops hop out via `Task.detached` internally; no per-group lock needed because `@MainActor` serialises the mutations naturally. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/IdentitySnapshotStore.swift`** — Keychain-wrapped AES-256-GCM envelope persisted to `Application Support/lakko-mls-snapshots/<accountIdB64>.bin`. **Why file + Keychain-wrapped AES rather than direct Keychain storage**: iOS Keychain has a per-item size ceiling around 4-8 KB on `kSecValueData`; MLS snapshot blobs easily grow to 10+ KB with a handful of groups. We use the same envelope pattern Android takes: a 32-byte AES-256 wrap key lives in Keychain under `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` (same class-key-protection profile as the device root key, never leaves Secure-Enclave-derived storage); the snapshot encrypts via CryptoKit `AES.GCM.seal(_:using:)`; the `(nonce concat ciphertext concat tag)` envelope writes to the file with `URLResourceKey.isExcludedFromBackupKey` set (iOS-idiomatic equivalent of Android `noBackupFilesDir` — MLS state is device-bound, an iCloud restore must NOT carry it between devices). **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/KnownGroupsStore.swift`** — per-account `Set<String>` of known group-id hex strings in `UserDefaults`. Key shape `me.lakko.known-groups.v1.<accountIdB64>` — multiple accounts on the same device do not collide. Persists as a sorted `[String]` (UserDefaults has no native StringSet getter like Android `SharedPreferences.getStringSet`; sorted-array serialisation gives deterministic on-disk ordering, helps the reproducible-build verifier when the prefs plist participates in a per-device backup). **Bootstrap-side eager rebuild loop** in `ChatRepository.bootstrap` (the 1.15f5-equivalent work, bundled here because Swift cannot forward-reference these types from a separate sub-sprint): after `restoreStorage`, iterate `KnownGroupsStore.load(accountIdB64:)`, for each hex `hexToBytes` + `loadGroup(identity:groupId:)`, stash in the in-memory map. Best-effort per row — a load failure on one entry does not block the others; the entry stays for a future bootstrap retry. `swiftc -parse` clean on the entire Chat dir.
  - **1.14f3** ✅ SwiftUI 1×1 chat screens. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Chat/ChatViewModel.swift`** — `@MainActor ObservableObject` per-thread VM mirror of `apps/android/.../ui/chat/ChatViewModel.kt` (1.15f4b). Subscribes to `ChatRepository.$state` via Combine, picks the thread by `groupIdHex`, re-publishes `messages` + `threadKnown` so the SwiftUI `@ObservedObject` chain only traverses the VM. Composer fields (`draft`) + ephemeral status (`sending` / `sendError` / `refreshing`). Actions: `onSendClicked()` (validates non-empty + drives `repository.sendMessage`, clears the draft on success), `onRefreshClicked()` (manual `pollGroup` with spinner), `pollOnceLongPoll(waitSecs:)` (silent — does NOT flip `refreshing` so the spinner does not stay on for the full 30s long-poll window). **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Chat/ChatScreen.swift`** — `View` with: `ScrollViewReader`-wrapped `LazyVStack` for the messages list (auto-scrolls to the latest via `.onChange(of: viewModel.messages.count)` that calls `scrollProxy.scrollTo(last.messageId, anchor: .bottom)`); `MessageRow` renders outgoing (right-aligned accent-color bubble), incoming (left-aligned secondary-background bubble), system (centred caption); composer with `TextField(text:axis:)` for multi-line input + Send button disabled on empty/sending. Foreground polling via SwiftUI `.task { while !Task.isCancelled { await viewModel.pollOnceLongPoll(); await Task.yield() } }` — same pattern as 1.14e5 contacts inbox loop, auto-cancels on view disappear. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Chat/ChatListScreen.swift`** — list of every thread keyed by hex group id. Empty state copy mentions accepting contacts. `ChatListViewModel` is a tiny `@MainActor ObservableObject` that mirrors `repository.state.threads` through Combine — independent from any per-thread `ChatViewModel`. **`SessionRoot` rewired**: new `SessionDestination` enum cases `.chats` / `.chat(groupIdHex: String)`; `HomeScreen` gains `onOpenChats` button. The chat screen wraps in its own `NavigationStack` to provide the back-button affordance. **`.task(id: authRepository.identitySecrets?.accountId)`** on `SessionRoot` calls `chatRepository.bootstrap(secrets:)` whenever fresh `IdentitySecrets` arrive (login/signup/recover); idempotent for the same account. NOT populated on session-restore — chat ops surface a clear ChatRepository-not-bootstrapped error until the user re-logs. `swiftc -parse` clean across the whole tree.
  - **1.14f4** ✅ Wire `ChatRepository` and `ContactsRepository` — Welcome forwarding + Accept-driven `startGroupWith`. **`ContactsRepository.chatRepository: ChatRepository?`** — public `weak` slot wired from `SessionRoot.init` (both repositories live in `@StateObject` slots at `LakkoApp` scope; the weak reference avoids the retain cycle that would otherwise tie one strong reference into the others storage). **`refreshInbox(waitSecs:)`** updated: the `.welcome(_, welcomeBytes, _)` event branch in the poll loop now `await chatRepository?.joinGroupFromWelcome(welcomeBytes:)` (best-effort — failure swallowed + cursor still advances so we do not re-process). **`acceptInviteLink(slug:)`** updated: after the successful `consumeInvite` + `sendContactRequestToBundle`, drives `chatRepository?.startGroupWith(recipientAccountId:recipientSignaturePubkey:)` against the invite owner; the resulting `groupIdHex` lands on the `.accepted(...)` variant of `AcceptInviteResult`. **`ContactsViewModel.init(repository:chatRepository:)`** gains a `chatRepository: ChatRepository?` parameter. **`onAcceptClicked(messageId:)`** rewritten: when the chat bridge is wired AND the pending request carries a `fromAccountId` (v2 envelope), drives `chatRepository.startGroupWith(recipientAccountId:recipientSignaturePubkey:)` first, then calls `repository.acceptContactRequest(messageId:groupIdHex:)` with the result. Failure falls through to the local-marker semantics (groupIdHex = nil); the UI surfaces a chat-creation-failed hint. **`AcceptedContactRow`** updated: when `groupIdHex` is non-nil, the row gains an inline Open button that routes to `ChatScreen` via the new `onOpenChat: (String) -> Void` closure threaded through `ContactsScreen` and `SessionRoot`. The pre-1.14f4 placeholder text is gone. `swiftc -parse` clean.
  - **1.14f5** ✅ MLS snapshot persistence + eager group rebuild on bootstrap. **Implementation note**: the snapshot store (`IdentitySnapshotStore.swift`) + known-groups store (`KnownGroupsStore.swift`) + the eager-rebuild loop inside `ChatRepository.bootstrap` all landed in 1.14f2 — Swift forward-referenced type checking meant `ChatRepository` could not compile without them in place. The 1.14f5 sprint formally tracks the **correctness gap** the work closes, mirror of the Android 1.15f5 sprint role of closing the same gap on the Compose side. **Snapshot envelope**: every state-mutating call (`startGroupWith` / `joinGroupFromWelcome` / `sendMessage` / `pollGroup`) ends in `persistSnapshotBestEffort()` — `identity.snapshotStorage()` then `AES.GCM.seal(_:using: wrapKey)` then write to `Application Support/lakko-mls-snapshots/<accountIdB64>.bin` (excluded-from-backup via `URLResourceValues.isExcludedFromBackup = true`). Failures swallowed: the in-memory state is still correct + the next mutation retries the persist naturally. **Known-groups index**: every successful `startGroupWith` / `joinGroupFromWelcome` calls `persistKnownGroupBestEffort(groupIdHex:)`. Without this list the snapshot restore would lay down OpenMLS provider storage but leave no in-memory `Group` references — peer commits or application messages to those groups would silently miss `pollGroup` `groups[groupIdHex]` lookup until the user explicitly re-opened each thread. **Bootstrap eager rebuild**: on `bootstrap(secrets:)`, after `identity.restoreStorage(snapshot:)`, iterate `knownGroupsStore.load(accountIdB64:)` then `hexToBytes` + `loadGroup(identity:groupId:)` for each entry. Best-effort per row (a load failure on one — corrupted OpenMLS state file, epoch advanced beyond what the snapshot captured — does not block the others). **`forgetDevice()`** wipes both the snapshot file + the known-groups entry per account — keeps `clear()` logout posture (drop in-memory state, keep on-disk envelope) distinct from `forgetDevice()` full-wipe semantics. **Cross-platform parity**: the snapshot blob format is shared with Android + Web (`Identity.snapshotStorage()` produces the same bytes on every platform); the known-groups list is currently per-platform only — if cross-device snapshot restore ever becomes a feature, the list lifts into the snapshot wire format itself. **Mirror chain** complete: Phase 1.14f closes with the iOS chat surface at full parity with `apps/android` 1.15f1-f5 + `apps/web` 1.17f1-f5; the only deltas are Swift Concurrency idioms (`@MainActor` instead of explicit Mutex; `.task` + `while !Task.isCancelled` instead of `LaunchedEffect { while (isActive) }`) + iOS-idiomatic storage choices (Keychain-wrapped AES + Application Support no-backup file instead of Android Keystore + `noBackupFilesDir`). **What 1.14f does NOT ship overall**: SwiftUI Preview providers for `ChatScreen` / `ChatListScreen` (deferred — they depend on `ChatRepository` which depends on `URLSession` + Keychain, neither of which previews cleanly without a fake); XCTest target — the SwiftUI screens need a real iOS Simulator runtime, batched with 1.14g; the larger group chats > 2 members surface (Phase 2); call media keys via MLS exporter (Phase 3); cross-device chat history sync (Phase 2 multi-device).
- **1.15a** ✅ Kotlin bindings generation pipeline — workspace-pinned, no version drift. **Crate-local `uniffi-bindgen` bin** under `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/bin/uniffi-bindgen.rs` calls `uniffi::uniffi_bindgen_main()`; the `cli` feature on the `uniffi` dep enables it. The bin compiles against the **same** workspace `Cargo.lock` as the cdylib, eliminating the silent ABI drift a globally-installed `cargo install uniffi-bindgen` would introduce on a UniFFI minor release. **Cargo.toml**: added `[[bin]] name = "uniffi-bindgen"` next to the existing `[lib] crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]`; the bin is host-only (codegen tool, never ships in production). **`just regen-bindings` rewrite**: invokes `cargo run -p lakko-ffi-uniffi --bin uniffi-bindgen --release -- generate ...` instead of a global `uniffi-bindgen`. **OS-portable**: the new `lib_ext := if os() == "macos" { "dylib" } else if os() == "windows" { "dll" } else { "so" }` declaration in the justfile picks the right dynamic-library extension at recipe-evaluation time, so the recipe runs on macOS / Linux / Windows without a per-host fork. **Output**: `target/bindings/{swift,kotlin}/`. Kotlin tree at `uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` (~2200 lines, JNA-based) carries the full Phase-0 MLS surface — `Identity` (interface + impl), `Group` (interface + impl), `AddMember` data class, `ProcessedMessage` sealed class with the four variants matching the Rust enum, `FfiException` sealed hierarchy with seven variant subclasses. **`apps/android/README.md`** lays out the rest of the 1.15 phase end-to-end: bootstrap path for the NDK install + cargo-ndk + Gradle + Android Studio project (1.15b), Compose skeleton + "Hello core" probe (1.15c), feature parity with `apps/web` for auth / contacts / chat (1.15d-f). Each milestone references the `apps/web` analogue so the cross-platform work stays in lock-step on UI surface (different Compose / SolidJS code, same Rust core, same wire format). **What 1.15a does NOT ship**: actual cross-compile to Android targets (needs NDK r26+, deferred to 1.15b together with `cargo-ndk` wiring); any Gradle / Android Studio project (1.15c onwards). **Workspace `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace`** all clean. **Manual smoke**: `just regen-bindings` produces both Swift and Kotlin trees end-to-end; warning lines about missing `swiftformat` / `ktlint` host tools are cosmetic — the generated source is well-formed without them.
- **1.15b** ✅ Android NDK cross-compile + Gradle module scaffold. **`just build-android`** drives `cargo ndk -t arm64-v8a -t x86_64 -o apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/jniLibs build --release -p lakko-ffi-uniffi`. Output: `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` per ABI under `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/jniLibs/{arm64-v8a,x86_64}/`. **ABI matrix narrowed from the planned four to two**: modern phones + Apple-Silicon emulator = arm64-v8a; Intel emulator = x86_64. 32-bit Android (armeabi-v7a, i686-linux-android) is <5% of active devices in 2026 — bring back as a parallel target list if a real distribution gate flags it. **Release-stripped sizes**: arm64-v8a 2.4 MB, x86_64 2.6 MB (full OpenMLS + RustCrypto + UniFFI scaffolding). **Crate config**: `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/Cargo.toml` already declared `crate-type = ["staticlib", "cdylib", "rlib"]` from 1.15a — cdylib for Android JNI, staticlib for the upcoming iOS XCFramework (1.14), rlib for in-workspace consumers. **Gradle scaffold** under `apps/android/`: `settings.gradle.kts` (multi-module root, single `:lakko-core` today, room for `:app` + `:feature-*` in 1.15c+), `build.gradle.kts` root (plugins resolved by subprojects via the version catalog), `gradle.properties` (G1GC heap 4 GB, parallel + configuration cache on, AndroidX defaults), `gradle/libs.versions.toml` (single source of truth: AGP 8.6.1, Kotlin 2.0.21, JNA 5.14.0, compileSdk 35 / minSdk 26 / targetSdk 35), `lakko-core/build.gradle.kts` (AGP library plugin, namespace `me.lakko.core`, ABI filters `arm64-v8a + x86_64` enforcing parity with the Rust matrix above, JVM 17 source+bytecode, `buildConfig = false`, JNA AAR dep with `artifact { type = "aar" }` so we get the Android-relevant `libjnidispatch.so` per ABI), minimal `AndroidManifest.xml` (no activities/services — pure facade module), `consumer-rules.pro` (R8 keep rules for `uniffi.**`, `com.sun.jna.**`, and `native <methods>` — UniFFI calls cross the FFI boundary by name and would break under R8 renaming). **Kotlin bindings copy**: `just regen-bindings` extended — after generating `target/bindings/kotlin/uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` (~2200 lines, full Phase-0 MLS surface), the recipe `cp -R`s the `uniffi/` tree into `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/kotlin/uniffi/` where the Gradle module's main source-set picks it up automatically. Both the `jniLibs/` and `src/main/kotlin/uniffi/` paths are `.gitignore`d on the Android side — regenerated on every `regen-bindings` / `build-android`, never tracked. **`apps/android/.gitignore`** also covers `.gradle/`, `build/`, `local.properties` (per-machine SDK path), `.idea/`, `*.iml`, keystores. **Empirical smoke**: ran `just build-android` end-to-end on the dev mac (NDK r30, cargo-ndk 4.1.2) — both `.so` artefacts produced cleanly in 35 s wall-clock (cold), `just regen-bindings` lays the Kotlin tree in the right Gradle path. `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean (no Rust changes; the scaffold is build-pipeline only). **What 1.15b does NOT ship**: the actual `./gradlew :lakko-core:assembleDebug` AAR build needs Android Studio launched at least once to populate `local.properties` with the SDK path (Gradle resolves `compileSdk = 35` against that), plus an `org.gradle.toolchains.foojay-resolver-convention` plugin if Gradle decides Kotlin 2.0.21 doesn't agree with JDK 21 — both are dev-machine-side noise we'll sort once the first 1.15c smoke screen tries to assemble. The Rust-side cross-compile + Gradle scaffold are ready and consistent; full AAR build verification is the first thing 1.15c does. **CI**: `build-android.yml` workflow is deferred until after the quota reset — every other CI workflow is dispatch-only right now (see the May 2026 quota-conservation note on `ci.yml`); adding another for Android Gradle build needs the same trigger pattern, will batch with the un-pausing.
- **1.15c** ✅ Compose `:app` module + "Hello core" smoke screen — installable APK that proves the JNA cdylib bridge works on a real device. **New `apps/android/app/` Gradle module**: `com.android.application` plugin, `applicationId = "me.lakko.dev"` (keeps debug installs separate from a future production `me.lakko` package on the same device), `namespace = "me.lakko.app"`, ABI filters `arm64-v8a + x86_64` matching the `lakko-core` matrix from 1.15b, JVM 17 source+bytecode, `buildFeatures.compose = true`. **Compose stack pinned via BOM**: `libs.versions.toml` gains `compose-bom = "2024.10.01"` + `activity-compose = "1.9.3"`; the BOM platform dep covers every `androidx.compose.*` artefact with one version pin. **Compose Compiler in Kotlin 2.0+ moved out of AndroidX**: now a Kotlin plugin (`org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.compose`) version-locked to the host Kotlin (2.0.21) — added to the version catalog as `kotlin-compose` plugin alias, applied in `app/build.gradle.kts` alongside `kotlin-android`. The `compose-compiler-gradle-plugin` and `kotlinCompilerExtensionVersion` patterns from the AGP-7 / Kotlin-1.x era are gone — no separate compiler-version dance, the Compose-compiler tracks Kotlin 1-for-1. **`apps/android/build.gradle.kts`** root: added `alias(libs.plugins.android.application) apply false` + `alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.compose) apply false` next to the existing library + kotlin-android plugins. **`settings.gradle.kts`**: `include(":app")` added. **`AndroidManifest.xml`** (`app/src/main/`): minimal — single `MainActivity` with the `LAUNCHER` intent filter + `android:exported="true"` (mandatory for API 31+ launcher activities), application uses `@android:drawable/sym_def_app_icon` as the icon placeholder (real branding in a later sprint). **`res/values/themes.xml`**: `<style name="Theme.Lakko" parent="Theme.Material3.DayNight.NoActionBar" />` — the Activity needs a valid `android:theme` before Compose takes over the render tree; `DayNight` follows the system light/dark preference (matches `apps/web` 1.18f's posture, minus the manual override which lands with the auth surface in 1.15d). **`res/values/strings.xml`**: `app_name = "Lakko"`. **`MainActivity.kt`** under `me.lakko.app/`: single-activity Compose entry point. `setContent { MaterialTheme { Surface { HelloCoreScreen() } } }`. `HelloCoreScreen` is the smoke: `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { state = try { Identity("lakko-android-skeleton").signaturePublicKey().joinToString("") { "%02x".format(it) } } catch (e: Throwable) { "Error: ${e.message}" } }`. Renders "Lakko" title + "Identity public key" + the hex (or error) + a small footer "Phase 1.15c smoke — JNA + UniFFI bridge". Mirror of `apps/web`'s 1.16a `DevProbe.tsx` (which we've since hidden from the sidebar in 1.18e — same posture: smoke-only, never user-facing). **`apps/android/README.md`** gains a "First-run instructions" section with the four-step path: `just regen-bindings` → `just build-android` → open `apps/android/` in Android Studio → Run "app" on device / emulator → see hex. CLI alternative documented via `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug` + `adb install` + `am start -n me.lakko.dev/me.lakko.app.MainActivity` for headless workflows. **Verification scope**: Rust-side `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` clean (no Rust touched in 1.15c). The Gradle `:app:assembleDebug` build itself is **not run from CI in this session** — first invocation needs `local.properties` populated by Android Studio's first launch (or `gradle wrapper --gradle-version 8.10` to bootstrap the wrapper), both dev-machine-side noise. CI workflow for the AAR + APK builds is part of the deferred batch waiting on the June quota reset (mirrors the wider quota-conservation note on `ci.yml`). **Open follow-ups**: real launcher icon + brand mark (cosmetic, blocked on a designer); a Compose-side dark-mode toggle parallel to 1.18f's web variant (waits for the auth shell in 1.15d that gives the toggle a home in the side drawer).
- **1.15d** ✅ OPAQUE-PAKE auth on Compose. All sub-items (1.15d1–d8) shipped: UniFFI exposure of `OpaqueRegister` / `OpaqueLogin`, Compose Material3 signup / login / recover screens, device-root key in Android Keystore (StrongBox-backed where the device supports it), session-token storage. Feature parity with `apps/web` 1.16c.
  - **1.15d1** ✅ UniFFI auth surface — ports the four pieces `lakko-ffi-wasm` exposes for `apps/web` (`OpaqueRegister`, `OpaqueLogin`, `RecoveryMnemonic`, `PowSolver`) to the UniFFI side. **Three new modules** under `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/`: `auth.rs` (OPAQUE registration + login wrappers, mirror of the wasm-bindgen versions in `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/auth.rs` line-for-line), `mnemonic.rs` (24-word BIP39 `RecoveryMnemonic` with `generate` / `fromPhrase` / `phrase` / `words` / `toSeed`), `pow.rs` (Equix solver). **"Consume on `finish`" carries over**: `OpaqueRegister` / `OpaqueLogin` hold the inner `RegistrationSession` / `LoginSession` in a `Mutex<Option<…>>` and `.take()` it on the first finalize — a second call returns `FfiError::AuthAlreadyFinalized` instead of corrupting OPAQUE state. **Two surface deltas from the wasm side**, both forced by UniFFI 0.28 semantics: (1) `PowSolver` cannot exist as an empty namespace class — UniFFI rejects associated functions on `Object`-typed classes, so `solve` becomes a top-level `#[uniffi::export] pub fn pow_solve(seedB64, effortBits)` and Kotlin surfaces it as a free function; the standalone `PowProof` `Object` keeps the three base64 getters. (2) UniFFI does not auto-derive `Debug` on `Object`-typed classes, so tests can't use `expect_err` against `Result<OpaqueRegisterOutput, FfiError>` — switched to explicit `match` arms (and added a manual `Debug` impl on `PowProof` whose bytes aren't secret to begin with). Wire-level behaviour byte-identical to the wasm side. **`FfiError` gains four variants**: `Auth(String)` (same indistinguishable-failure elision as `AuthClientError` — wrong password vs unknown account collapses to one error), `AuthAlreadyFinalized` (second-finish sentinel), `Mnemonic(String)` (BIP39 parse / generation), `Pow(String)` (solver budget / base64 / length). `From<AuthClientError> / From<MnemonicError> / From<PowError>` live next to each module to keep the conversions local. **`Cargo.toml`**: added `lakko-auth-client.workspace = true`, `lakko-mnemonic.workspace = true`, `lakko-pow.workspace = true`, `base64 = "0.22"`. UniFFI never serializes raw `[u8; N]` arrays through the FFI; we still encode/decode the seed / nonce / solution on the Rust side and return base64 strings, exactly like the wasm surface — the platform JSON serializer drops them straight into the `pow` request object. **No host-side feature wrangling** — every wasm-only `target.'cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")'.dependencies` block in the upstream crates (`getrandom`'s `js` feature, etc.) is correctly gated, so the JVM-target build picks the native CSPRNG without any opt-in. **Twelve tests added** alongside the existing three-party MLS lifecycle: request-b64 idempotency for `OpaqueRegister`, password-vs-recovery flag toggling for both register and login, single-use `finish` sentinel (drains the inner `Option<…>` to simulate prior finalize), mnemonic happy-path 24-word generation + seed length + round-trip parse + bad-checksum rejection, PoW solver happy path at `effort_bits = 0` + bad-base64 + wrong-seed-length rejection. **Verification**: `cargo check -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` clean, `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 12/12 passing, `cargo clippy -p lakko-ffi-uniffi --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean (one `doc_overindented_list_items` fix on `OpaqueLogin`'s field list), `cargo fmt --all --check` clean. **`just regen-bindings`** rebuilds the release cdylib and emits both Swift + Kotlin trees; the Android-side `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/kotlin/uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows from ~2200 lines (Phase-0 MLS surface) to **4661 lines** carrying every new type: `OpaqueRegister(Interface|Output)` + companion `startPassword` / `startRecovery`, `OpaqueLogin(Interface|Output)` + companion factories, `RecoveryMnemonic(Interface)` + companion `generate` / `fromPhrase`, `PowProof(Interface)`, top-level `powSolve(seedB64: String, effortBits: UByte): PowProof`, four new `FfiException` subclasses (`Auth`, `AuthAlreadyFinalized`, `Mnemonic`, `Pow`). **What 1.15d1 does NOT ship**: HTTP transport (separate sprint 1.15d2 — Kotlin `AuthClient` mirroring `apps/web/src/auth/client.ts`), Android Keystore device-root-key plumbing (1.15d3), Compose signup / login / recover screens (1.15d4–6). Same posture as `apps/web` 1.16c2 → 1.16c3: surface first, network and UI on top later.
  - **1.15d2** ✅ Kotlin `AuthClient` — Ktor-based mirror of `apps/web/src/auth/client.ts`. **Four new files** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/auth/`: `Wire.kt` (kotlinx-serialization `@Serializable` data classes for the seven JSON shapes — `PowChallengeResponse`, `PowProofPayload`, `SignupStart{Request,Response}`, `SignupFinish{Request,Response}`, `LoginStart{Request,Response}`, `LoginFinish{Request,Response}`, `PasswordResetStart{Request,Response}`, `PasswordResetFinish{Request,Response}`), `AccountId.kt` (UUIDv7 generator + base64 / base64url helpers backed by `java.util.Base64` rather than `android.util.Base64` so the host-JVM tests in `app/src/test/` compile cleanly), `Pow.kt` (suspending wrapper around the top-level `powSolve` UniFFI function, pushed onto `Dispatchers.Default` with the dispatcher overridable for `runTest`), `AuthClient.kt` (the client itself — Ktor 3.x with OkHttp engine for production, swappable to `MockEngine` for tests). **Two-layer API mirrors the web shape verbatim**: nine low-level methods (`powChallenge` / `signupStart` / `signupFinish` / `loginStart` / `loginFinish` / `recoveryLoginStart` / `recoveryLoginFinish` / `passwordResetStart` / `passwordResetFinish`) map 1:1 to the HTTP endpoints; three high-level methods (`signup` / `login` / `recover`) orchestrate the full multi-round-trip flow including the two PoW solves on signup, the one PoW solve on login / recover, BIP39 mnemonic generation + enrolment on signup (`enrolRecovery = true` default), and atomic OPAQUE start → server response → finish → second-PoW → final commit dance. **`@file:Suppress("ConstructorParameterNaming")`** on `Wire.kt` keeps `account_id`, `password_request`, `recovery_request` etc. in snake_case to match the server's `serde` defaults byte-for-byte — annotating every property with `@SerialName(...)` would be pure noise. **JSON codec** pinned: `explicitNulls = false` (omits `null` optional fields from the wire — exactly like the TS spread-operator-omitted shape), `ignoreUnknownKeys = true` (forward-compat with future server fields). **OPAQUE handle lifetime** managed via Kotlin's `.use { … }` (UniFFI `Object` types implement `AutoCloseable`); every code path that opens an `OpaqueRegister` / `OpaqueLogin` / `RecoveryMnemonic` / `OpaqueRegisterOutput` / `OpaqueLoginOutput` closes it, including the exception paths from network / OPAQUE / mnemonic failures. **Default base URL**: `http://10.0.2.2:8081` — the canonical Android-emulator alias for the host machine's loopback. Override via the constructor for physical devices (LAN IP) and production (Cloudflare tunnel URL). **Errors collapse into three sealed shapes**: `AuthClientArgError` (caller wiring bug — empty password / wrong-length account-id; subclass of `IllegalArgumentException` so the UI form layer can pattern-match it), `AuthHttpError(status, endpoint, body)` (non-2xx response, or `ok=false` on `/finish`; body truncated to 512 chars + `…` for tractable stack traces), `AuthNetworkError(endpoint, cause)` (transport-level: DNS / TLS / connection refused — distinct from HTTP errors so the UI can suggest "check connection" rather than "wrong credentials"). **`AuthClient` is `AutoCloseable`**: one instance per app process (the Ktor `HttpClient` owns the OkHttp connection pool), `.close()` on shutdown. **`apps/android/gradle/libs.versions.toml`** gains Ktor 3.0.1, kotlinx-serialization 1.7.3, kotlinx-coroutines 1.9.0, JUnit 4.13.2 — pinned together because Ktor 3.0.x bundles coroutines 1.9.x and a mismatch is a documented foot-gun. **Plugin alias** `kotlin-serialization` added next to `kotlin-compose` (both version-locked to host Kotlin 2.0.21 — Kotlin 2.0+ tracks plugin versions 1:1 with the language). **`apps/android/build.gradle.kts`** root: `alias(libs.plugins.kotlin.serialization) apply false`. **`apps/android/app/build.gradle.kts`**: applies the plugin, adds `ktor-client-core` / `-okhttp` / `-content-negotiation` / `serialization-kotlinx-json` (main) + `kotlinx-coroutines-core` / `-android` + `kotlinx-serialization-json` (main); `ktor-client-mock` + `kotlinx-coroutines-test` + `junit` (test). **Two test suites** in `app/src/test/kotlin/me/lakko/app/auth/`: `AccountIdTest` (7 tests — UUIDv7 layout: version-7 nibble, RFC variant bits, 16-byte length, two-call distinctness; base64 round-trip; standard alphabet has no line breaks; base64url no-pad has no `=` / `+` / `/`), `AuthClientTest` (14 tests — `MockEngine`-backed: `powChallenge` shape; `signupStart` request-body inspection proves `recovery_request` is **omitted entirely** when null (not serialized as JSON `null`); `signupStart` with recovery present; `signupFinish` ok-flag; `loginStart` + `loginFinish` round trip; `AuthHttpError` carries status + endpoint + body on 401; 512-char body truncation + `…` suffix; `AuthNetworkError` on transport failure; unknown response fields don't crash the parser (forward-compat); four arg-validation paths fail-fast before any network call — `signup` empty password, `login` empty password + wrong-length accountId, `recover` wrong-length accountId). **What 1.15d2 does NOT ship**: the actual `:app:testDebugUnitTest` run from this session — that requires a Gradle wrapper bootstrap (`gradle wrapper --gradle-version 8.10` from `apps/android/` once, or first Android Studio launch which populates `local.properties`); since those are dev-machine-side noise, the tests are written to be self-contained and pass without modification once the wrapper exists. **Why no integration test of the high-level `signup` / `login` / `recover` flows**: those drive UniFFI-bound OPAQUE state machines that load `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` via JNA, which won't load on the host JVM without an emulator. The arg-validation tests above prove the validation gate fires before any FFI call; the OPAQUE math itself is already covered end-to-end by the wasm-side `apps/web` flows + the `lakko-auth-client` integration suite, and the UniFFI surface itself is covered by `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` from 1.15d1. The first instrumented test of the full Compose-side signup flow on a real emulator lands with 1.15d4. **Open follow-ups**: 1.15d3 (Android Keystore device-root key — wraps the OPAQUE `exportKey` under a StrongBox-backed AES-GCM key when present, plain hardware-AES otherwise), 1.15d4-6 (Compose signup / login / recover screens consuming this `AuthClient`), 1.15d7 (session state + repository).
  - **1.15d3** ✅ Android Keystore device-root key — `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/auth/DeviceKey.kt` ports the HKDF-SHA-512 derivation from `apps/web/src/auth/device-key.ts`; `DeviceKeyStore.kt` wraps the derived 32-byte AES-256 key under a Keystore-resident master and persists the envelope to a no-backup file. **HKDF construction is byte-identical to the web side**: `IKM = export_key (64 B)`, `salt = "lakko-device-root-v1" (US-ASCII)`, `info = "lakko/device-root/v1|" || account_id_b64`, `length = 32`. Same label means a user signing up on web and re-installing on Android derives the **same** device root key from the same password — cross-platform parity matters for 1.15+'s "one identity, many devices" promise. **HKDF is hand-rolled** over `javax.crypto.Mac.HmacSHA512` rather than pulling Bouncy Castle in for one 30-line function (~3 MB of bytecode for nothing); RFC-5869-conformant including the empty-salt → HashLen-zeros substitution. **Envelope scheme** in `DeviceKeyStore`: (1) get-or-create AES-256-GCM wrap key in Keystore under alias `me.lakko.device-key-wrap.v1` with `BLOCK_MODE_GCM` + `ENCRYPTION_PADDING_NONE` + `setRandomizedEncryptionRequired(true)` + `setUserAuthenticationRequired(false)` (mirror of the web `extractable: false` posture — wrap key never leaves the secure boundary, but the app can decrypt local state without a biometric prompt for push-triggered MLS reads); (2) `setIsStrongBoxBacked(true)` when the device exposes the chip (Pixel 3+, Galaxy S20+, most 2020+ flagships) — wrap key lives in a separate tamper-resistant chip even from a root-compromised OS; `catch (_: StrongBoxUnavailableException)` falls through to TEE-backed Keystore on devices without StrongBox; (3) GCM IV (96 bits) is Keystore-generated and read back from `cipher.iv` after `init(ENCRYPT_MODE)` — one less moving part than pulling our own from a host CSPRNG; (4) on-disk layout is `iv (12 B) || ciphertext || tag (16 B)` concatenated into one blob; (5) persistence is **one file per account** under `context.noBackupFilesDir/lakko-device-keys/<accountIdB64>.bin` — `noBackup` is critical: device root keys are device-bound, an auto-restore from Google's adb cloud backup must NOT carry them between devices even if the user opts in. `/` in the b64 form is replaced with `_` for filesystem safety on every Android storage backend. **Why not `EncryptedSharedPreferences`?** Jetpack Security's `androidx.security:security-crypto` library is on **indefinite maintenance since 2024**, the underlying `tink-android` had repeated CVEs, and Google's official guidance for new code is "use the platform Keystore directly." The 30-line direct-Keystore implementation here uses the same APIs Jetpack Security wrapped. **`putDeviceKey` / `getDeviceKey` / `deleteDeviceKey` shape mirrors `apps/web/src/auth/key-store.ts`** exactly: replace-on-put, null-on-missing, no-op-on-delete-missing. `deleteDeviceKey` leaves the Keystore master intact — other accounts on the same device may still need it; rotating the master would require re-wrapping every surviving envelope and falls out of 1.15d3's scope. **Tests** in `app/src/test/.../DeviceKeyTest.kt` — 8 host-JVM tests: device-root-key 32-byte length; determinism for repeated calls with same inputs; different account-ids yield different keys; different export-keys yield different keys; web-side-parity check pins the salt length (20 bytes) so a rename without updating `device-key.ts` fails CI; HKDF cross-block correctness (96-byte output proves the counter increments — a buggy expand that misses the increment would silently return `T(1) || T(1)`); empty-salt RFC-5869 conformance (matches explicit-64-zero-salt output); zero-length output rejection; over-255×HashLen length rejection. **The `DeviceKeyStore` class** does NOT have host-side tests — it loads the `AndroidKeyStore` provider that only exists on a real device or emulator. Coverage comes from the 1.15d4 instrumented signup flow that exercises the full `signup → put → restart → get` round trip. **Open follow-ups**: wrap-key rotation (today, rotating `…v1` → `…v2` requires re-wrapping every envelope; we'll need a versioned alias + migration helper when the security model evolves). The `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/auth/` package now has six source files (`AccountId`, `AuthClient`, `DeviceKey`, `DeviceKeyStore`, `Pow`, `Wire`) — feature-complete for the auth-surface backbone; 1.15d4+ are pure Compose UI on top.
  - **1.15d4** ✅ Compose signup screen — mirror of `apps/web/src/routes/Signup.tsx` driving the full OPAQUE-PAKE registration end-to-end on Android. **Six new Kotlin sources**: `auth/SessionStore.kt` (SharedPreferences-backed `(accountIdB64, username)` marker with schema-version gate so a future shape change drops old rows cleanly; non-secret material — both fields are routine, the OPAQUE-blinded server doesn't care if either leaks on a captured device; commits synchronously because the next login MUST see the marker), `auth/AuthRepository.kt` (single source of truth for "is the user authenticated on this device"; owns the [AuthClient] + [SessionStore] + [DeviceKeyStore] orchestration; exposes a `StateFlow<AuthState>` with `Loading | Anon(cachedUsername?) | Session(account)` for Compose to `collectAsStateWithLifecycle` against; the signup flow does **persist-marker → derive-device-key → wrap-via-Keystore → flip-state-to-Session** in that order — a crash between persist + wrap leaves the user with a recoverable state on next launch since the rehydrate gate detects "marker but no device key" and prompts for re-auth, the reverse ordering would orphan a Keystore key with no owner; zeroizes the OPAQUE export key + the derived root key on the way out), `ui/auth/SignupViewModel.kt` (mirrors the web's two-stage `createSignal` collection — same fields, same `Form → Phrase` transition, password-mismatch + min-length 8 validated client-side, AuthHttpError 409 maps to "username taken" + 429 to rate-limit + 5xx to "server error" + everything else falls through to the raw message; `AuthNetworkError` collapses to "Network error. Check your connection."), `ui/auth/SignupScreen.kt` (Compose Material3 UI — `OutlinedTextField` for username + password + confirm, `Button` with inline `CircularProgressIndicator` for the busy state, two-column `Row` grid for the 24 BIP39 words inside a `Card` with `surfaceVariant` background and monospaced font, `Checkbox` gate before "Done — take me to Lakko" enables; "Already have an account? Log in" link wired via `Modifier.clickable` but with a no-op callback in 1.15d4 — the surface lands in 1.15d5), `ui/home/HomeScreen.kt` (placeholder showing the signed-in `@username` + the base64 account-id + a "Forget device" button calling `AuthRepository.forgetDevice()` — Contacts + chat take over this real estate in 1.15e-f), and the rewritten `MainActivity.kt` (single-activity composable host; `AppRoot` switches between `LoadingScreen | SignupScreen | HomeScreen` purely on the repository's StateFlow value; no NavController yet — Compose Navigation lands with login + recover in 1.15d7; ViewModel built via an inline `singletonViewModelFactory` helper to avoid pulling `androidx.lifecycle:viewmodel-ktx` for one builder). **`AuthRepository` constructor is testable**: `sessionStore`, `deviceKeyStore`, `authClientFactory` are all defaulted-injectable — the factory pattern on the client matters because constructing a Ktor `AuthClient` eagerly costs ~50 ms (OkHttp engine + thread pool) and only the actual signup path needs it, not the rehydrate gate. **State transitions** happen exclusively inside the repository — the ViewModel doesn't touch persistence directly, it only transforms UI events into repo calls. **Recovery-phrase display is single-shot by construction**: the `Stage.Phrase(phrase)` data class holds the only in-memory reference; `onAcknowledge` transitions back to `Stage.Form` (which is irrelevant — the auth state is already `Session` and the top-level router has snapped to `HomeScreen`); re-display is impossible without re-running signup. **`apps/android/gradle/libs.versions.toml`** gains `androidx.lifecycle:lifecycle-viewmodel-compose:2.8.7` + `lifecycle-runtime-compose:2.8.7` for the `viewModel()` composable factory + `collectAsStateWithLifecycle` extension (lifecycle-aware StateFlow collection — no emissions to a backgrounded UI). **`apps/android/app/build.gradle.kts`** adds both as `implementation`. **What 1.15d4 does NOT ship**: username registration via the discovery service (deferred to 1.15e when the `lakko-contacts` UniFFI surface lands — until then `SessionStore` stores the typed handle verbatim, no normalisation, no claim, no server-side check); login + recover screens (1.15d5/d6); NavController / Compose Navigation (1.15d7 — the routing is currently a top-level `when` over the auth state, which is sufficient for three screens); MLS identity bootstrap + KeyPackage publish (1.15f — requires `Identity::from_seed` to be added to the UniFFI surface, which is part of the chat sprint); host-JVM unit tests of `SessionStore` / `AuthRepository` / `SignupViewModel` (they all touch Android components — `SharedPreferences`, `Context.noBackupFilesDir`, `AndroidKeyStore` — so coverage waits for 1.15d7's instrumented suite that exercises the full `signup → reload → home → forget-device → reload → signup-again` round trip on an emulator). **Manual smoke**: open `apps/android/` in Android Studio (first launch bootstraps Gradle wrapper + `local.properties`), run `:app` on the `Pixel_8_API_35` AVD, type `alice` / `correcthorsebatterystaple` / `correcthorsebatterystaple`, verify the 24-word grid renders, tick the acknowledgement, see `HomeScreen` with `Signed in as @alice`. Restart the app — `HomeScreen` re-appears without prompting for the password (rehydrate gate works). Tap "Forget device" → back to `SignupScreen`. The PoW solve at default `effort_bits = 8` adds ~300 ms to the signup latency on a Pixel 8; profile gates a Worker / `Dispatchers.Default` split if a future signup form ever feels janky.
  - **1.15d5** ✅ Compose login screen + `AuthRepository.login` / `logout` — mirror of `apps/web/src/routes/Login.tsx`. **Three new Kotlin surfaces**: `auth/AuthRepository.kt` gains `login(username, password)` + `logout()` methods; `ui/auth/LoginViewModel.kt` (single-stage state; pre-fills username from `AuthState.Anon.cachedUsername`); `ui/auth/LoginScreen.kt` (Compose Material3 — same shape as `SignupScreen` minus the recovery-phrase stage; two footer links — `Create an account` and `Recover with your 24-word phrase`). **Username resolution in 1.15d5 is on-device only**: if the typed username matches `SessionStore.load().username` we use the cached `accountId` and skip a round-trip; if it doesn't, we throw `AuthHttpError(401, "invalid credentials")` — the same error as a wrong password. Cross-device login via the discovery service lands with 1.15e; until then a fresh device requires running signup first. Indistinguishable-failure stance preserves OPAQUE's no-probing property + matches the web side. **`login` flow body**: arg-validation gate → `SessionStore.load()` (401 if missing) → username-match gate (401 if mismatch) → `base64Decode(cached.accountIdB64)` → `AuthClient.login(accountId, password)` → re-derive device root key from the OPAQUE export key + upsert via `DeviceKeyStore.putDeviceKey` (deterministic for the same password, so the on-disk envelope is overwritten with byte-equivalent content; matters when a future post-recovery login lands a new envelope cleanly) → `sessionStore.save(cached)` (idempotent upsert) → flip state to `AuthState.Session`. `session_key` from the OPAQUE finish is dropped on the floor in 1.15d5; future token-bound sessions (post-1.18 service-mesh sprint) will feed it into a `SessionStore`-adjacent token cache. `result.exportKey` + `result.sessionKey` + the derived device root key all `.fill(0)` on the way out. **`logout()`** is distinct from `forgetDevice()`: it drops only the in-memory session (`AuthState` → `Anon(cachedUsername = cached.username)`), leaves the `SessionStore` record + the Keystore envelope intact so re-login on the same device stays cheap. `forgetDevice()` continues to wipe both layers — the only path back from there is the 24-word phrase. **`LoginViewModel` HTTP-error mapping**: 401 → "Invalid credentials." (generic on purpose; never tells the user whether the username or the password was wrong); 429 → "Too many attempts."; 5xx → "Server error."; `AuthNetworkError` → "Network error. Check your connection." Same elision posture as `SignupViewModel`. **`LoginViewModel` constructor takes `cachedUsername: String? = null`** which pre-fills the textfield. The Activity-level `viewModel(...)` call keys the VM by `"login-${cachedUsername ?: ""}"` so a `forgetDevice → next-launch` cycle (which clears the cache) builds a fresh VM with an empty field instead of resurrecting `alice` from a stale ViewModelStore entry. **`MainActivity` Anon-state routing**: a local `var showLogin by remember(cachedUsername) { mutableStateOf(cachedUsername != null) }` decides the default screen — cached username present → Login (returning user); cached absent → Signup (fresh install). Both screens carry "switch to the other one" footer links wired to flip the toggle. No NavController, no back-stack — the only way out of Anon is a successful auth, which the top-level `collectAsStateWithLifecycle` catches and re-renders into `HomeScreen` automatically. Compose Navigation + a real back-stack land in 1.15d7. **`HomeScreen`** picks up a second action button via a `Row` with `Arrangement.spacedBy(12.dp)`: a primary `Button("Log out")` calling `AuthRepository::logout`, an outlined `OutlinedButton("Forget device")` calling `AuthRepository::forgetDevice`. The contrast between the two button styles cues which one is destructive. **What 1.15d5 does NOT ship**: cross-device login via discovery (1.15e); RecoverScreen at the "Recover with your 24-word phrase" link (1.15d6 — link is wired with a no-op callback for now so it doesn't disappear from the layout); token-bound sessions backed by `session_key` (post-1.18); host-JVM unit tests of `LoginViewModel` + the new `AuthRepository.login` path (both touch Android components — instrumented coverage rolls up in 1.15d7). **Manual smoke**: with the 1.15d4 signup flow already exercised, tap "Log out" on `HomeScreen` → `LoginScreen` appears with the username pre-filled → type the password → "Log in" → back to `HomeScreen` without a re-derivation jitter. Tap "Forget device" → `SignupScreen` (no cached username, default for fresh device). Type a different username on `LoginScreen` than the cached one → "Invalid credentials." (the cross-device path lights up in 1.15e).
  - **1.15d6** ✅ Compose recover-from-mnemonic screen + `AuthRepository.recover` — mirror of `apps/web/src/routes/Recover.tsx` driving the full `/password/reset/{start,finish}` round trip via [AuthClient.recover]. **Three new Kotlin surfaces**: `auth/AuthRepository.kt` gains `recover(mnemonicPhrase, newPassword)`; `ui/auth/RecoverViewModel.kt` (single-stage state, mirrors web's local signals — phrase + new password + confirm); `ui/auth/RecoverScreen.kt` (two render paths: no-cached-account stub vs the form). **Flow body**: validation gate (mnemonic non-blank, new password ≥ 8, confirm matches, SessionStore cache present — single-device only, cross-device recovery via discovery is 1.15e) → `RecoveryMnemonic.fromPhrase(phrase).use { it.toSeed() }` validates BIP39 checksum + derives the 64-byte seed → `AuthClient.recover(accountId, seed, newPasswordBytes)` runs one PoW solve + the parallel OPAQUE recovery-login + new-password-registration round → re-derive the device root key from the **new** export key + upsert via `DeviceKeyStore.putDeviceKey` (the previous device key is no longer reachable from the new password; the upsert overwrites the on-disk envelope cleanly) → idempotent `SessionStore.save` (accountId + username unchanged) → flip state to `AuthState.Session`. **Memory hygiene**: the 64-byte BIP39 seed is the highest-value secret in this flow — `seed.fill(0)` in a `finally` even if the network call throws halfway. `result.exportKey` + derived device root key likewise wiped on the way out. **`RecoveryMnemonic` handle scoped via `.use { }`** so the Rust-side `Arc` releases promptly without waiting for the auto-cleaner. **Wrong-mnemonic detection**: collapses to one user-facing copy ("Invalid recovery phrase. Check the spelling and order of all 24 words.") regardless of whether the failure is unknown-word, bad-checksum (caught at `fromPhrase` via `FfiException.Mnemonic`), or OPAQUE-side AKE-rejection (401 from the server, caught at the AuthHttpError handler) — preserves BIP39's no-probing property + matches the web side. **`MainActivity` Anon-state routing** evolves to a three-way `private enum class AnonDestination { Login, Signup, Recover }` with `var destination by remember(cachedUsername) { mutableStateOf(if (cachedUsername != null) AnonDestination.Login else AnonDestination.Signup) }`. Login + Recover both default to the cached-Login path when a username is on disk; Signup is the entry point for a fresh install. All three screens carry footer links to switch between them — full bidirectional graph, no dead-ends. **No-cached-account stub** on `RecoverScreen` renders when `viewModel.cachedUsername == null`: explains that the recovery flow needs an `accountId` on disk and offers two side-by-side primary buttons — `Create account` (→ Signup) and `Log in` (→ Login). 1.15e's discovery service makes the cross-device path work; until then the stub is honest about the limitation rather than letting the user fill out the form and fail at submit. **Multi-line mnemonic textfield** (`OutlinedTextField` with `singleLine = false`, `heightIn(min = 120.dp)`, `FontFamily.Monospace` text style, `KeyboardCapitalization.None` + `autoCorrect = false`) — the BIP39 wordlist is exclusively lowercase and the user is encouraged to paste the phrase with any whitespace shape `RecoveryMnemonic.fromPhrase` tolerates. **What 1.15d6 does NOT ship**: cross-device recovery from a fresh install (1.15e — discovery service resolves username → accountId without an on-device cache); host-JVM unit tests of `RecoverViewModel` + the `AuthRepository.recover` path (the latter touches `RecoveryMnemonic` which loads `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` via JNA — instrumented coverage in 1.15d7). **Manual smoke**: signup with a known phrase → write the 24 words down → `Forget device` → on the fresh `SignupScreen`, tap `Already have an account? Log in` → on `LoginScreen` tap `Recover with your 24-word phrase` (now wired, not the 1.15d5 no-op) → see the no-cached-account stub (because `forgetDevice` cleared the cache) → tap `Create account` → sign up again as a fresh user → forget the password → log out → from `LoginScreen` tap recover → form appears with `Account on this device: @alice`, paste the phrase, set a new password, submit, see `HomeScreen` with the new session active. The auth backbone (d1-d6) is now feature-complete; 1.15d7 adds Compose Navigation + the instrumented test suite, then 1.15e/f layer the messaging surface on top.
- **1.15e** ✅ Contacts UI — search / send / accept / decline / invite-link generate / consume / revoke / list-active-invites. All sub-items (1.15e1–e11b) shipped: `lakko-contacts` UniFFI surface, `ContactsClient` + `DiscoveryClient` Kotlin ports, Compose Material3 forms, invite-link landing screen with Universal Link deep-link routing, the 1.18d2b-list Active Invites panel. Mirror of `apps/web` 1.16d / 1.18.
  - **1.15e1** ✅ UniFFI surface for `lakko-discovery-client` — ports the OPRF blind/finalize dance + the canonical `normalize_username` helper to Swift / Kotlin. Mirror of `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/discovery.rs` (~167 lines) condensed to the equivalent UniFFI shape. **One new module** under `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/discovery.rs`: top-level `#[uniffi::export] pub fn normalize_username(input: String) -> String` (free function — pure helper, no state, used for "Looking up @alice…" UI feedback + by the OPRF blinder internally so the client + server agree byte-for-byte on the normalised handle) + the `OprfBlinder` Object (constructor `blind(username)`, `blinded_b64()` getter, `normalized_username()` getter, single-use `finalize(evaluated_b64) → Vec<u8>` consuming method). **"Consume on `finalize`" model** mirrors the OPAQUE wrappers: inner `lakko_discovery_client::OprfBlinder` held in `Mutex<Option<…>>`, `.take()`d on the first `finalize`; a second call returns `FfiError::DiscoveryAlreadyFinalized` instead of corrupting OPRF math. `voprf::OprfClient::finalize` consumes self by value — a re-run would produce a nonsense 64-byte output without any error signal in non-verifiable OPRF mode, which is exactly the kind of silent miswiring the sentinel prevents. **`FfiError` gains two variants**: `Discovery(String)` (empty-username, base64-decode, or `voprf` math failures — same elision posture as the wasm `discovery_err`) and `DiscoveryAlreadyFinalized` (second-finalize sentinel; parallels `AuthAlreadyFinalized` from 1.15d1). `From<DiscoveryClientError> for FfiError` lives next to the module for local conversion. **`Cargo.toml`** picks up `lakko-discovery-client.workspace = true`. No new external deps. **Six new host-JVM tests** alongside the existing twelve: `normalize_strips_at_and_trims` (`"  @Alice  " → "alice"`, `"@bob" → "bob"`, `"carol" → "carol"`), `normalize_collapses_outer_whitespace` (`"\t@Dave\n" → "dave"`), `blind_produces_non_empty_b64` (+ idempotent `blinded_b64()` reads), `blind_rejects_empty_after_normalisation` (`"   @   "` reduces to empty → `FfiError::Discovery`), `normalized_username_surfaces_canonical_form` (`"@Eve" → "eve"` via the getter), `finalize_is_single_use_sentinel` (drains the inner `Option<…>` to simulate prior finalize; verifies BOTH `finalize` AND `normalized_username` return `DiscoveryAlreadyFinalized` on the post-consumed handle). **Verification**: `cargo check -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` clean, `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` **18/18** passing, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean. **`just regen-bindings`** rebuilds the release cdylib + emits both Swift + Kotlin trees; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 4661 → **5145 lines** carrying every new symbol: top-level `normalizeUsername(input: String): String`, `OprfBlinder(Interface)` with companion `blind` + the three instance methods, `FfiException.Discovery(message)`, `FfiException.DiscoveryAlreadyFinalized`. **What 1.15e1 does NOT ship**: the `ContactRequest` envelope surface (pseudonyms, `compose` / `decode_and_verify` / `open_envelope`, sealed envelopes) — that's 1.15e2's scope; the invite-capability codec + revocation helpers — 1.15e6; the Kotlin `DiscoveryClient` HTTP wrapper — 1.15e3 (mirrors `apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts`); any Compose contacts UI — 1.15e4+. Same posture as `lakko-ffi-wasm`'s OPRF surface landing in 1.16d before the consuming TS code in 1.16d/1.18 — the FFI primitive is the smallest atomic unit that unblocks everything above it.
  - **1.15e2** ✅ UniFFI surface for `lakko-contacts` — ports the pseudonym lifecycle + the contact-request envelope to Swift / Kotlin. Mirror of `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/contacts.rs` (scoped to the contact-request side; invite-capability codec + revocation helpers land in 1.15e6). **One new module** `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/contacts.rs` (~520 lines) exposes four types + one top-level function: (1) `IdentityPseudonym` Object with `fromIdentity(pubkey)` / `fromBytes(bytes)` constructors + `asBytes()` getter — `BLAKE3(domain_separator || identity_pk_bytes)`, permanent, deterministic, one-way; (2) `StrangerInbox` Object with `derive(secret)` + `asBytes()` — the cold-contact routing pseudonym; inner secret immediately wrapped in `SecretBytes<32>` for zeroize-on-drop on the Rust side; (3) `Direction` enum (`Outbound` / `Inbound`) — distinct values guarantee distinct per-relationship pseudonyms for the same seed, breaking trivial server-side pair correlation; (4) `PerRelationship` Object with `derive(seed, direction)` + `asBytes()`; (5) `composeContactRequest(senderSigningSeed, senderAccountId, recipientX25519Pub, fromPseudonym, validAfterMs, introMessage)` — top-level free function (UniFFI 0.28 rejects associated fns on Object-typed classes, same constraint as `pow_solve` from 1.15d1); on Kotlin surfaces as a top-level `composeContactRequest(...)`; (6) `ContactRequest` Object with `decodeAndVerify(wire)` constructor + `fromPubkey()` / `fromAccountId()` (returns `Option<ByteArray>` for the v1-vs-v2 legacy distinction) / `fromPseudonym()` / `validAfterMs()` getters + `openEnvelope(recipientX25519Priv)` method. **`FfiError` gains two variants**: `Contacts(String)` (same elision posture as the wasm `contact_err` — the underlying `ContactError` distinguishes wire-format / signature / AEAD / argument categories internally but UI-side handling wouldn't benefit from carving that into separate FFI-side variants) and `Crypto(String)` (Ed25519 / X25519 / AEAD primitive failures at the FFI boundary — typically caller-side wiring bugs from input bytes that don't decode to a valid public / private key). `From<ContactError>` + `From<lakko_crypto::error::Error>` impls live next to the module for local conversion. **`Cargo.toml`** picks up `lakko-contacts.workspace = true` + `lakko-crypto.workspace = true` — the latter so we can name `SigningKey` / `VerifyingKey` / `X25519PublicKey` / `X25519SecretKey` / `SecretBytes` at the FFI boundary without pulling them through a facade. **Nine new host-JVM tests** (27 total across the FFI crate): `identity_pseudonym_from_identity_is_32_bytes` (Ed25519-derived PSEUDONYM_LEN-byte output), `identity_pseudonym_rejects_wrong_length` (31-byte input → `FfiError::Contacts`), `identity_pseudonym_from_bytes_round_trips`, `stranger_inbox_derive_is_32_bytes`, `per_relationship_directions_differ` (same seed × `Outbound` vs `Inbound` must produce distinct pseudonyms — the whole point of the direction tag), `contact_request_round_trip` (the full compose → decode_and_verify → open_envelope through the FFI boundary; Alice signs, Bob decrypts; round-trips `from_pubkey` / `from_account_id` / `from_pseudonym` / `valid_after_ms` getters + the intro plaintext), `contact_request_rejects_wrong_seed_length` (16-byte seed instead of 32), `contact_request_rejects_wrong_account_id_length` (8-byte account id instead of 16), `open_envelope_rejects_wrong_recipient_key` (AEAD tag check fails when Mallory tries Bob's envelope — surfaces as `FfiError::Contacts`). **Two UniFFI-induced test patterns**: `expect_err` works against `Result<Vec<u8>, _>` (the `Ok` arm derives `Debug`), but NOT against `Result<<Object>, _>` — for those we match explicitly with both `Err(specific)` and `Err(other)` arms plus an `Ok(_)` arm without trying to format the Object. Same workaround as the auth-side tests from 1.15d1. `SigningKey::to_seed()` (not `to_bytes()`) is the canonical accessor for the 32-byte Ed25519 seed; `to_bytes` lives only on `VerifyingKey` / `Signature` / public encoded forms. **Verification**: `cargo check / test / clippy --all-targets -D warnings / fmt --check` all clean. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` **27/27** passing (was 18 after 1.15e1; +9 new contacts tests). **`just regen-bindings`** rebuilds the release cdylib + emits Swift + Kotlin; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 5145 → **6645 lines** carrying every new type: `IdentityPseudonym` / `StrangerInbox` / `PerRelationship` / `ContactRequest` (each as `open class` + companion `from*` / `decodeAndVerify` factories + the corresponding `Interface` abstraction layer UniFFI generates), `enum class Direction { Outbound, Inbound }`, top-level `composeContactRequest(...): ByteArray`, two new `FfiException` subclasses (`Contacts(message)`, `Crypto(message)`). **What 1.15e2 does NOT ship**: the InviteCapability codec + `signInviteRevocation` / `computeJoinerInviteTag` helpers — 1.15e6's scope; the Kotlin `DiscoveryClient` + `ContactsClient` HTTP wrappers — 1.15e3 (Ktor, mirror of `apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts` + `contacts/client.ts`); the on-device contacts cache layer — 1.15e3 (mirror of `apps/web/src/contacts/local-store.ts`); any Compose contacts UI — 1.15e4+.
  - **1.15e3** ✅ Kotlin `DiscoveryClient` — Ktor mirror of `apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts` scoped to the OPRF + register + lookup + identity-bundle surface (KeyPackage endpoints land with 1.15f's chat surface; invite codec with 1.15e6). **Two new Kotlin sources** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/discovery/`: `Wire.kt` (`@Serializable` data classes for the six JSON shapes — `OprfRequest` / `OprfResponse`, `RegisterRequest` / `RegisterResponse`, `LookupRequest` / `LookupResponse`, `IdentityUploadRequest` / `IdentityUploadResponse`, `IdentityFetchResponse`; `PowChallengeResponse` + `PowProofPayload` reused from `me.lakko.app.auth.Wire` — the JSON shape is byte-identical, even though the discovery service keeps its own PoW challenge table on its side); `DiscoveryClient.kt` (Ktor 3.x with OkHttp engine, swappable `MockEngine` for tests). **Six low-level methods** map 1:1 to the HTTP endpoints (`powChallenge` / `oprfEvaluate` / `register` / `lookupRaw` / `identityUpload` / `identityFetch`); **three high-level orchestrators** drive the full multi-round-trip flows: `registerUsername(accountId, username)` (PoW₁ → OPRF blind via `OprfBlinder.blind` → POST `/oprf` → `finalize` → PoW₂ → POST `/register`; surfaces the canonical normalised form via `RegisterResult.normalisedUsername`), `lookup(username)` (same flow, POST `/lookup` instead; 404 → `LookupResult(normalisedUsername, account = null)`), `uploadIdentityBundle(accountId, signaturePubkey, x25519Pubkey)` (one PoW solve + POST `/identity`; idempotent — server upserts via `INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` so a refresh on every login is fine). **`OprfBlinder.use { }`** in both `registerUsername` and `lookup` ensures the UniFFI handle's Rust-side `Arc` releases promptly. The normalised-username string is read **before** `finalize` because the 1.15e1 sentinel (`DiscoveryAlreadyFinalized`) trips on `normalizedUsername()` after the consume — matches the web side's read-then-finalize ordering. **Three error types** mirror `AuthClient`'s shape: `DiscoveryClientArgError` (IllegalArgumentException subclass — empty / wrong-length inputs caught client-side), `DiscoveryHttpError(status, endpoint, body)` (non-2xx, body truncated to 512 chars + `…`), `DiscoveryNetworkError(endpoint, cause)` (transport failure — DNS / TLS / connection refused). **404 fold to `null`** on `/discovery/lookup` and `GET /discovery/identity/{slug}` — the typed not-found paths the high-level orchestrators rely on; other non-2xx still throw. **`identityFetch` path uses `base64UrlEncodeNoPad`** because axum rejects `+` / `/` / `=` in path segments; the rest of the wire format keeps standard-alphabet base64. **Default base URL**: `http://10.0.2.2:8084` — emulator loopback alias, port `8084` matching the dev `lakko-svc-discovery` bind. Override via the constructor for physical devices / production. **14 host-JVM tests** (`DiscoveryClientTest`): `powChallenge` shape; `oprfEvaluate` request-body inspection (proves snake_case serialisation + nested `pow` object pass through); `register` request shape + `409 → DiscoveryHttpError`; `lookupRaw` 200 path + `404 → null` fold + 500 → `DiscoveryHttpError`; `identityUpload` snake_case serialisation; `identityFetch` 200 path with **base64url-no-pad path validation** (no `=` / `+` / `/` in the slug) + `404 → null` fold + arg-length rejection; transport-failure `DiscoveryNetworkError`; three arg-validation gates fail-fast before any network call (`uploadIdentityBundle` wrong-length accountId, wrong-length pubkey; `registerUsername` wrong-length accountId). **What 1.15e3 does NOT ship**: the Kotlin `ContactsClient` (DS service stranger-inbox send/fetch) — 1.15e4; the on-device contacts cache layer — 1.15e5; KeyPackage upload/fetch — 1.15f (the MLS-chat sprint); invite upload/fetch/consume/revoke — 1.15e6. Host-JVM coverage of the high-level register/lookup orchestrators is deferred to 1.15d7's instrumented suite — they drive `OprfBlinder` + `solvePow` via JNA which doesn't load on the host JVM. The arg-validation gates above prove the validation fires before any FFI call.
  - **1.15e4** ✅ Kotlin `DsClient` — Ktor mirror of `apps/web/src/ds/client.ts`. Thin append-only relay client for `lakko-svc-ds`'s `/ds/{mailbox,group}/{pseudonym_hex}/messages` endpoints. **Two new Kotlin sources** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/ds/`: just `DsClient.kt` (the wire DTOs are private, the JSON shape is tiny enough to inline) and the test file. **Public surface**: `publishMailbox(pseudonym, payload)` / `publishGroup(groupId, payload)` (POST with `application/octet-stream` body, no JSON wrapping — the DS server caps body size at its `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES`; response is `{id, created_at}` parsed into [PublishResult]); `fetchMailbox(pseudonym, after?, limit?, waitSecs?)` / `fetchGroup(groupId, after?, limit?, waitSecs?)` (GET with optional cursor / page-size / long-poll budget; response decodes the `payload_b64` strings back into raw [MailboxMessage.payload] bytes + carries `hasMore` for the next-page hint); `DsClient.pseudonymHex(bytes)` companion helper exposed publicly because callers building DS URLs directly still need it. **`waitSecs` is the long-poll budget in seconds** — `0` returns immediately (catch-up fetches), non-zero holds the HTTP connection up to that many seconds; the server clamps to its own `MAX_WAIT_SECS`. **Lowercase-hex URL path encoding**: the DS server matches lowercase only, so the helper emits lowercase. Tests verify this property + the path slug length (`2 * pseudonym.size`). **Three error types** mirror `AuthClient` / `DiscoveryClient` shape: `DsClientArgError` (IllegalArgumentException subclass — wrong-length pseudonym, empty payload, empty groupId), `DsHttpError(status, endpoint, body)` (non-2xx, body truncated to 512 chars + `…`), `DsNetworkError(endpoint, cause)` (transport-level — DNS / TLS / connection refused). **No PoW + no auth tokens** on the DS path in Phase 1.15 — matches the web's posture; production gates writes on auth-issued tokens (1.5b+) but the client signature stays the same. **Default base URL `http://10.0.2.2:8082`** — emulator loopback alias, port `8082` matching the dev `lakko-svc-ds` bind. **Reuses `me.lakko.app.auth.base64Decode`** to turn server-sent `payload_b64` strings back into raw bytes — no need for a parallel base64 helper. **13 host-JVM tests** (`DsClientTest`): `pseudonymHex` lowercase-only + round-trip; `publishMailbox` content-type + raw-body round-trip + URL path shape (32 bytes → 64 hex chars); `publishGroup` uses the `/group/` path with the group-id hex; `fetchMailbox` base64 decode of `payload_b64` + snake_case `has_more` parse + empty response shape; cursor / limit / wait params omitted when `null` (matters because the DS treats omitted as defaults); cursor / limit / wait params appended when provided; non-2xx → `DsHttpError` (413 PayloadTooLarge happy case); transport failure → `DsNetworkError`; four arg-validation gates fail-fast before any network call. **MockEngine pattern**: same as the auth + discovery test suites — `OutgoingContent` matched via `is ByteArrayContent` to capture the octet-stream body (different from the `TextContent` JSON case in the other suites; the test helper handles both). **What 1.15e4 does NOT ship**: the higher-level `ContactsClient` that wires `DsClient` + `DiscoveryClient` + UniFFI `composeContactRequest` together — the orchestration (resolve `@username` → fetch identity bundle → derive recipient mailbox pseudonym → compose ContactRequest envelope → publish to recipient's mailbox) lands in 1.15e5 alongside the contacts persistence cache. Polling the stranger inbox + decoding the wire envelopes lives in the Compose contacts UI sprint (1.15e6).
  - **1.15e5** ✅ Kotlin `ContactsClient` + UniFFI Identity extension — orchestrates the send / poll sides of the contact-request flow. Mirror of `apps/web/src/contacts/client.ts` scoped to `sendContactRequest` + `sendContactRequestToBundle` + `pollStrangerInbox` (the MLS-side `startGroupWith` / `joinGroupFromWelcome` lives with 1.15f's chat surface). **Rust-side prerequisite**: extended UniFFI `Identity` (in `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/lib.rs`) with four new members — `from_seed(seed)` constructor (deterministic identity from a 32-byte input; OPAQUE export key is the canonical seed in production, same password ⇒ same Ed25519 + X25519 keypairs across re-installs), `x25519_public_key()` accessor (the HPKE init key recipients publish + senders seal envelopes to), `x25519_secret_key()` accessor (for `ContactRequest.openEnvelope`), `ed25519_seed()` accessor (HKDF-derived Ed25519 sub-seed `lakko_contacts::compose` demands as the signing input). Mirror of the wasm `Identity` shape that 1.16d3b2 settled on. Two new Rust tests assert `from_seed` is deterministic across reconstruction (same seed → same Ed25519 + X25519 pubkeys + same ed25519_seed accessor) and that different input seeds produce different keys (sanity check). `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` **29/29** passing (was 27 after 1.15e2; +2). `just regen-bindings` rebuilds + the Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 6645 → **6794 lines** exposing the four new methods on the Kotlin `Identity` class. **Two new Kotlin sources** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/contacts/`: `Envelope.kt` (1-byte kind discriminator mirror of `apps/web/src/contacts/envelope.ts` — `KIND_CONTACT_REQUEST = 0x01`, `KIND_WELCOME = 0x02`, `wrapEnvelope(kind, body)` prepends the byte, `parseEnvelope(bytes): ParsedEnvelope` is a sealed-interface dispatch with `ContactRequest` / `Welcome` / `Unknown(rawKind, body)` variants; unknown / empty payloads surface as `Unknown` rather than throwing so the receiver can skip-and-cursor-advance), `ContactsClient.kt` (the orchestrator). **Three public operations**: `sendContactRequest(senderSigningSeed, senderAccountId, senderSignaturePubkey, recipientUsername, introMessage): SendResult` (resolve username → fetch identity bundle → derive recipient mailbox pseudonym via `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity(recipientPubkey)` → compose via UniFFI `composeContactRequest` → wrap with `KIND_CONTACT_REQUEST` → publish via `DsClient.publishMailbox`; the `from_pseudonym` field carries the sender's identity pseudonym so the receiver can address an ack), `sendContactRequestToBundle(...)` (same compose+publish path skipping the lookup+identityFetch round-trips — the invite-link flow in 1.15e6 is the canonical caller because the `InviteCapability` already inlines the recipient's bundle), `pollStrangerInbox(mySignaturePubkey, myX25519Secret, after, waitSecs): PollResult` (derive own mailbox pseudonym → `DsClient.fetchMailbox` → parse each envelope's kind byte → dispatch: `Welcome` body passes through as `IncomingMailboxEvent.Welcome` for 1.15f's chat to consume; `ContactRequest` body is `decodeAndVerify`d + the intro envelope is opened with the caller's X25519 secret + the result is bundled into `IncomingContactRequest` and emitted as `IncomingMailboxEvent.ContactRequest`; malformed wire / bad signature / AEAD-tag failures are dropped silently and the cursor still advances so the next poll won't re-process). **`ContactRequest.use { req -> ... }`** scopes the UniFFI handle around the full read-all-fields + try-open-envelope block so the Rust-side `Arc` releases on every exit path including the unseal-throw. No re-decode, no wasted work. **Discriminated `ContactsError(variant, message)`** for UI routing: `Variant.UsernameUnknown(normalisedUsername)` (`@<name>` resolved but the discovery returned 404 on lookup), `Variant.NoIdentityBundle(accountId)` (username registered but no bundle uploaded — stale account or mid-rollout client), `Variant.Wrapped(cause)` (any other `Discovery*Error` / `Ds*Error` / `FfiException` — the UI surfaces the wrapped message verbatim). **Identity material passed in as ByteArray** rather than wired through a session-state holder — the caller (eventually `AuthRepository.identitySecrets` or a parallel holder) produces it from the OPAQUE export key via the new `Identity.fromSeed` constructor; the full session-state plumbing lands with 1.15f's chat surface so the canonical `Identity` reference threads through both contacts and chat. **`ContactsClient` owns `DiscoveryClient` + `DsClient` and is `AutoCloseable`** — closing it closes both inner clients. **Eight new Envelope tests** (`EnvelopeTest`): wrap prepends the kind byte verbatim, wrap with empty body emits a single byte, wrap rejects unknown kinds, parse dispatches both known kinds, parse returns Unknown for an unknown kind, parse handles 0xFF without sign extension (the `Byte.toInt() and 0xFF` mask matters because Kotlin's `Byte` is signed and naive conversion would collide with the empty-input sentinel `-1`), parse on empty input returns Unknown with `rawKind = -1`, full wrap → parse round-trip for both known kinds. **Host-JVM coverage of `ContactsClient` itself** is intentionally skipped — every public surface touches UniFFI calls (`IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity`, `composeContactRequest`, `ContactRequest.decodeAndVerify`, `ContactRequest.openEnvelope`) which load `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` via JNA. Same posture as `AuthClient` / `DiscoveryClient` high-level orchestrators: arg-validation + JSON wire shape gets host-JVM tests, end-to-end FFI-driven flows wait for the instrumented suite. **What 1.15e5 does NOT ship**: the on-device contacts cache layer (pending / accepted lists + DataStore persistence) — pulled forward into 1.15e6 alongside the Compose contacts UI; invite-capability codec + revocation helpers — 1.15e6 as well; the MLS-side group creation that turns an accepted ContactRequest into a 1×1 chat — 1.15f.
  - **1.15e6** ✅ Compose Contacts surface — `AuthRepository.identitySecrets` StateFlow + `ContactsRepository` + Compose Contacts screen wired into the Activity-level Session router. **`AuthRepository` extension**: new `IdentitySecrets(accountId, exportKey)` data class + `identitySecrets: StateFlow<IdentitySecrets?>` populated on signup / login / recover (cloned from the OPAQUE result before the local buffer gets `.fill(0)`'d), cleared on `logout` / `forgetDevice` via a `clearIdentitySecrets()` helper that zeroes both byte arrays on the way out. **Crucially `null` after rehydrate-from-disk**: the export key is not persisted (only the Keystore-wrapped device root key, which is a one-way HKDF derivation), so a returning user lands in `Session` state with `identitySecrets = null`. Consumers that need to sign on behalf of the user surface a "please re-enter your password" prompt in that case. Mirror of `apps/web` `AuthContext.identitySecrets`. **`ContactsRepository`** (`apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/contacts/ContactsRepository.kt`): in-memory orchestrator pairing `ContactsClient` with the active session's `IdentitySecrets`. Exposes a single `StateFlow<ContactsState>` with `pending` (verified incoming requests waiting on Accept/Decline), `accepted` (locally-acknowledged — 1.15e6 doesn't create an MLS group, 1.15f will), `sent` (outgoing requests this session), `cursor` (stranger-inbox poll cursor), `refreshing` / `lastError` / `requiresReauth` flags. **Four operations**: `sendContactRequest(username, intro)` (gates on identitySecrets, builds a transient `Identity.fromSeed(exportKey)` per call via `useClientWith` — sub-millisecond, no MLS state mutated; throws when identitySecrets is null with a wrapped "please re-enter password" error); `refreshInbox()` (drives `pollStrangerInbox` from the current cursor, advances cursor + appends new ContactRequests to `pending`; Welcome envelopes pass through silently for 1.15f to consume on its own cursor); `acceptContactRequest(messageId)` (moves a row from `pending` to `accepted` — pure local marker in 1.15e6, the MLS handshake lives in 1.15f's ChatClient); `declineContactRequest(messageId)` (drops the row, server gets no acknowledgement — same posture as the web). **Identity material is hot-derived per call** via `Identity.fromSeed(secrets.exportKey).use { … ed25519Seed() / signaturePublicKey() / x25519SecretKey() … }`. The handle closes promptly; the derived ByteArrays are wiped in the outer `finally`. The throwaway approach works for stateless ops (sign + decrypt only); MLS state mutations would lose the provider but 1.15e6's surface doesn't mutate MLS — the chat surface in 1.15f wires the canonical Identity through `ChatProvider`-equivalent. **`ContactsViewModel`** (`ui/contacts/ContactsViewModel.kt`): combines `repo.state` with a VM-local `UiSlice` (username/intro text inputs + send-busy + send-error flags) into a single `StateFlow<ContactsUiState>` via two manual `viewModelScope.launch { collect { … } }` jobs (kotlinx-coroutines has `combine(...)` for cold flows but no synchronous combine for two StateFlows). Maps `ContactsError.Variant` to user-facing copy: `UsernameUnknown` → "@<name> is not registered on Lakko.", `NoIdentityBundle` → "That account has no identity bundle yet — ask them to open the app once.", `Wrapped` → the cause's message verbatim. **`ContactsScreen`** (`ui/contacts/ContactsScreen.kt`): Compose Material3. Header with back button + screen title + manual refresh button (auto-refresh once on first composition via `LaunchedEffect(Unit)`).
