Send section: username `OutlinedTextField` with `KeyboardCapitalization.None + autoCorrect = false` (handles are lowercase + we don't want autocorrect rewriting them) + optional intro `OutlinedTextField` + send `Button` with inline `CircularProgressIndicator` for the busy state. Pending section: Material3 `Card` per row with sender's pubkey hex preview + intro text (when decrypted) + Accept/Decline buttons. Accepted + Sent sections show the lists. **Re-auth panel** renders when `state.requiresReauth` is true — "Contacts require your in-memory identity key. Log out and log back in to refresh it." The user fixes it by going through `HomeScreen.Log out` → `LoginScreen.Log in` which repopulates `identitySecrets`. **`HomeScreen` adds an `Open contacts` primary button** above the existing Log out / Forget device row. **`MainActivity` adds Session sub-routing**: new `SessionRoot` composable + `private enum class SessionDestination { Home, Contacts }` + `var destination by remember { mutableStateOf(SessionDestination.Home) }` toggle. Logging out / forgetting the device clears the `ContactsRepository` in-memory state via the new `clear()` method before flipping AuthState — pending requests + sent log do NOT survive a logout (this session's state is per-account, not per-device; persistence lands with Room in 1.15f). **What 1.15e6 does NOT ship**: continuous polling of the stranger inbox (manual refresh only — a polling loop with a long-poll budget lands in 1.15e7 alongside the service-worker-style background-fetch plumbing); on-device Room/DataStore persistence for pending + accepted lists (1.15f, alongside MLS state storage); InviteCapability codec + revocation helpers — 1.15e7; Accept-creates-an-MLS-group handshake — 1.15f (the chat surface wires the `ChatClient.startGroupWith` flow against the recipient's KeyPackage). **Manual smoke** (once Android Studio bootstraps the Gradle wrapper): signup as alice on emulator-A → log out → on emulator-B signup as bob → log out → log in as bob → Open contacts → send request to alice → switch to emulator-A, log in as alice → Open contacts → Refresh → see the pending request → Accept → row moves to Accepted list with the "chat creation arrives in 1.15f" hint. Until 1.15f the round-trip is observable but the actual chat doesn't exist yet.
  - **1.15e7** ✅ Foreground continuous polling — long-poll loops on the Contacts / Chats / per-Chat screens so the app feels alive without manual Refresh tapping. **Repository extensions**: `ContactsRepository.refreshInbox(waitSecs: Int = 0)` and `ChatRepository.pollGroup(groupIdHex, waitSecs: Int = 0)` / `pollAllGroups(waitSecs: Int = 0)` thread the DS long-poll budget we already wired up in 1.15e4 through to the underlying `DsClient` calls. **`refreshInbox` is silent for non-zero waitSecs** — only raises the `ContactsState.refreshing` flag when `waitSecs == 0` (manual refresh from a button press); the spinner doesn't flicker for 30s at a time. ChatRepository never had a per-thread refreshing flag at the repo layer (the ChatViewModel owns it), so the silent-on-background-poll behaviour falls out naturally there. **ViewModel `pollOnceLongPoll` shape**: both `ContactsViewModel` and `ChatViewModel` get a `suspend fun pollOnceLongPoll(waitSecs: Int = DEFAULT_LONG_POLL_SECS)` one-shot that the composable's polling loop calls. Pulled out as a VM method (rather than inlined in the composable) so the future instrumented test seam can drive a single tick without owning the loop's lifecycle. `DEFAULT_LONG_POLL_SECS = 30` matches the canonical chat-app cadence; the server clamps to its own `MAX_WAIT_SECS` so a misconfigured client never blocks the DS connection pool indefinitely. **Composable polling shape**: each of `ContactsScreen` / `ChatScreen` / `ChatListScreen` carries a `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { firstTick(); while (isActive) { try { pollOnceLongPoll() } catch { delay(POLL_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS) } } }` loop. The first tick uses the manual-refresh path so the spinner blips briefly while the immediate fetch resolves; subsequent ticks are silent long-polls. `LaunchedEffect(Unit)` is composition-scoped — the coroutine cancels automatically when the user navigates away or logs out, no manual `start` / `stop` pair needed on the VM. `POLL_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS = 5_000L` between failed iterations so a 503 burst doesn't tight-loop. **`pollAllGroups` iterates groups sequentially** with a per-group long-poll. Worst-case latency for a new message in any group is `30 * N` seconds where N is the group count — acceptable for the 1×1 chat surface (one group per peer); refactor to parallel polling lands when group counts actually push back. **Continuous polling is foreground-only** in 1.15e7 — the `LaunchedEffect`s cancel when the app backgrounds. WorkManager-driven periodic fetch + FCM push notification integration lands in 1.15e7b/c respectively; both depend on the foreground-poll behaviour shipping first as the happy-path. **Manual refresh button still works** on every screen — `onRefreshClicked` is wired in parallel to the loop and uses `waitSecs = 0` so the spinner blip semantics are preserved. **No host-JVM test changes** — the new `waitSecs` parameter threads through to the DsClient which is MockEngine-backed in 1.15e4's tests; the loop itself is composable-scoped and instrumented-only. **Manual smoke**: with the 1.15f4c alice↔bob smoke procedure as the baseline, additionally observe that bob can keep the chat screen open and see alice's new message land within ~1s of her Send (the long-poll returns immediately when a row arrives). Background the app for 60s, foreground it — the next long-poll iteration picks up everything that landed in the gap. **What 1.15e7 does NOT ship**: WorkManager periodic fetch for post-backgrounding catch-up — 1.15e7b; FCM push integration for offline delivery — 1.15e7c; per-thread polling cancellation policy for the chats list (today every visible screen's LaunchedEffect runs its own loop; visible-screen overlap costs an extra HTTP connection — acceptable for the smoke surface, 1.15e7b's shared-worker abstraction will deduplicate).
  - **1.15e8** ✅ UniFFI `InviteCapability` + revocation helpers — ports the capability-based contact-invite codec (ADR-0005 §13.3 / §13.4) from `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/contacts.rs` to the Android side. Originally earmarked for 1.15e6/e7; deferred until the rest of the contacts + chat backbone was wired. **One file touched**: `crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/contacts.rs` extends with the four new surfaces. **Top-level free fn `composeInviteCapability(ownerSigningSeed, ownerAccountId, ownerX25519Pubkey, expiryMs, maxUses, bootstrapReferral, label)`** returns the wire bytes the owner base64url-encodes into a `https://<host>/i/<...>` URL. Same UniFFI-0.28 free-function workaround as `composeContactRequest` from 1.15e2. **`InviteCapability` Object** with `decodeAndVerify(wire): InviteCapability` constructor + the seven getters: `ownerSignaturePubkey()` / `ownerAccountId()` / `ownerX25519Pubkey()` / `expiryMs()` / `maxUses()` / `bootstrapReferral()` / `label()` + the one method `isValidNow(nowMs): Boolean`. `decodeAndVerify` does NOT check expiry — callers invoke `isValidNow` separately so the UI can render "this invite expired N minutes ago" rather than a generic verification failure. **`signInviteRevocation(signingSeed, blobId, signedAtMs): ByteArray`** signs the canonical revocation bytes (domain separator + `blob_id` + big-endian `signed_at_ms`) with the owner's Ed25519 key. Returns the 64-byte signature the caller base64-encodes for the `DELETE /discovery/invite/{blob_id}` body. **`computeJoinerInviteTag(capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId): ByteArray`** runs HMAC-SHA-256 over the capability bytes keyed by a deterministic salt derived from the joiner's account-id; returns the 32-byte tag the server uses to dedup repeat consumes from the same joiner without learning the joiner's `account_id` in plaintext (Phase 1.18d3b design). **`FfiError` gains no new variants** — both helpers thread input-shape failures through the existing `Contacts(String)` variant. The wire-format details + the cryptographic correctness live in `lakko-contacts` and are extensively tested there; the FFI layer just bridges Kotlin call sites. **Six new Rust tests** in the existing test module: `invite_capability_round_trip` (compose → decodeAndVerify → every getter; verifies `isValidNow` before / after expiry), `invite_capability_rejects_oversize_label`, `sign_invite_revocation_round_trip` (64-byte Ed25519 output), `sign_invite_revocation_rejects_wrong_blob_id_length`, `compute_joiner_invite_tag_round_trip` (32-byte HMAC output + asserts the **dedup property** — same `(capability, joiner)` yields the same tag), `compute_joiner_invite_tag_rejects_wrong_account_id_length`. **`cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 38/38** passing (was 32 after 1.15f3; +6). `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean (one `doc_broken_link` fix on a forward-reference to the not-yet-written 1.15e9 orchestrator); `fmt --check` clean. **`just regen-bindings`** rebuilds; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 6957 → **7641 lines** carrying every new symbol: top-level `composeInviteCapability(...)` / `signInviteRevocation(...)` / `computeJoinerInviteTag(...)` free functions + the `InviteCapability` open class with companion `decodeAndVerify` + the seven instance accessors. **What 1.15e8 does NOT ship**: Kotlin `DiscoveryClient` extensions for `POST /discovery/invite` / `GET /discovery/invite/{blob_id}` / `POST /discovery/invite/{blob_id}/consume` / `DELETE /discovery/invite/{blob_id}` — 1.15e9; ContactsScreen + ContactsViewModel extensions for generate-invite-link / share-via-Android-Share-Sheet — 1.15e10; invite-landing Compose screen + Activity-side deep-link (`https://<host>/i/<slug>` URL handler) — 1.15e11. The crypto primitive is in place; the wire + UI follow.
  - **1.15e9** ✅ `DiscoveryClient` invite-endpoints extension — adds the four invite-blob CRUD operations against `services/discovery` the Kotlin invite-link UI (1.15e10/e11) builds on. Mirror of `apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts`'s invite section (Phase 1.18d1/d2a/d2b/d3b on the web side). **`Wire.kt`** picks up six new `@Serializable` DTOs: `InviteUploadRequest(pow, capability)`, `InviteUploadResponse(blob_id)`, `InviteFetchResponse(capability)`, `InviteConsumeRequest(pow, joiner_tag)`, `InviteConsumeResponse(used_count, max_uses, already_consumed)`, `InviteRevokeRequest(signed_at_ms, signature)`. All snake_case to match `services/discovery`'s serde defaults verbatim. **`DiscoveryClient`** picks up four low-level methods (1:1 to the endpoints): `inviteUpload(req)` (POST `/discovery/invite`); `inviteFetch(blobId)` (GET `/discovery/invite/{slug}`, 404 → `null` via the existing `getNullableOn404` helper); `inviteConsume(blobIdSlug, req)` (POST `/discovery/invite/{slug}/consume`); `inviteRevoke(blobIdSlug, req)` (DELETE — special-cased because the verb differs + the 204/2xx success path doesn't have a JSON body to decode). Plus four high-level wrappers that drive the FFI calls + PoW solve + result-shape folding: **`uploadInvite(capabilityBytes): ByteArray`** (enforces the 2048-byte ceiling client-side, PoW solve, posts, decodes the returned base64url-no-pad `blob_id` back to raw 32 bytes); **`fetchInvite(blobId): ByteArray?`** (404 → null, 200 → base64-decoded capability bytes); **`consumeInvite(blobId, capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId): ConsumeInviteResult`** (drives `computeJoinerInviteTag(...)` locally so the joiner's account_id NEVER crosses the wire, one PoW solve, then folds the 200 / 404 / 410 status trifecta into the discriminated result); **`revokeInvite(blobId, signingSeed): RevokeInviteResult`** (drives `signInviteRevocation(...)`; pins `signedAtMs` to a single value across the await boundary so we sign the SAME timestamp we post; folds 204/2xx → `Revoked` and 404 → `NotFoundOrNotOwner`; other non-2xx still throw). **Two discriminated result types**: `ConsumeInviteResult` (sealed: `Consumed(usedCount, maxUses)` / `AlreadyConsumed(usedCount, maxUses)` / `Exhausted` / `NotFound`); `RevokeInviteResult` (sealed: `Revoked` / `NotFoundOrNotOwner`). UI matches on the variant + branches without exception-walking the underlying [DiscoveryHttpError] hierarchy. **Server-side 404 folding rationale**: discovery folds expired blobs into 404 (not 410) and folds bad-signature / stored-bytes-failed-re-decode into the revoke 404 — both to deny status-code probing of the blob_id space; the client surfaces a generic "this invite no longer exists" message. **Three new constants** on the companion: `MAX_CAPABILITY_BYTES = 2048` (mirrors server ceiling), `BLOB_ID_LEN = 32` (SHA-256 output size), `SIGNING_SEED_LEN = 32`. **Imports `computeJoinerInviteTag` + `signInviteRevocation`** from the UniFFI surface 1.15e8 just shipped + `base64UrlDecodeNoPad` from `me.lakko.app.auth` to decode the server's `blob_id` reply. **Ten new host-JVM tests** (30 total in `DiscoveryClientTest`): `inviteUpload` request shape + `blob_id` parse; `inviteFetch` 200 + 404→null + base64url-no-pad path slug shape (no `=` / `+` / `/`) + wrong-length blobId arg rejection; `inviteConsume` request body (snake_case `joiner_tag` field + correct slug path); `inviteRevoke` DELETE method + body shape (snake_case `signed_at_ms` + `signature`) + 204 success path; `inviteRevoke` 404 throws `DiscoveryHttpError`; six arg-validation gates fail-fast before any network call (`uploadInvite` empty + oversized; `fetchInvite` wrong-length blobId; `consumeInvite` wrong-length blobId + wrong-length joinerAccountId; `revokeInvite` wrong-length blobId + wrong-length signingSeed). The high-level orchestrators (`uploadInvite` / `consumeInvite` / `revokeInvite` happy paths) drive UniFFI-bound functions (`powSolve`, `computeJoinerInviteTag`, `signInviteRevocation`) which load the cdylib via JNA — covered by the future instrumented suite, not host-JVM. `cargo fmt --check` clean. **What 1.15e9 does NOT ship**: ContactsScreen + ContactsViewModel extensions for the Owner-side generate-invite-link / share-via-Android-Share-Sheet flow — 1.15e10; invite-landing Compose screen + Activity-side deep-link (`https://<host>/i/<slug>` URL handler) + auto-Accept-on-consume bridge — 1.15e11. Manual smoke for this sub-sprint waits until the UI ships in 1.15e10/e11; until then the surface is exercised only via the MockEngine tests + the 1.15e8 Rust round-trip tests for the underlying crypto.
  - **1.15e10** ✅ Owner-side Generate-invite UI — wires `ContactsRepository` + `ContactsViewModel` + `ContactsScreen` so a user can tap **Generate invite link** in Contacts and share the resulting `https://lakko.me/i/<slug>` URL via the Android Share Sheet or copy-to-clipboard. **Fixed-config single-button-fire** in 1.15e10 (7-day expiry, single-use, no bootstrap-referral); custom-config dialog with user-typed label / expiry / max_uses can layer on as 1.15e10b. **`ContactsRepository`** picks up two new methods + an extra constructor arg: `discoveryClientFactory: () -> DiscoveryClient` (distinct from the in-`ContactsClient` instance because invite operations are discovery-side, driven directly from the Repository so the orchestration sits next to the `outstandingInvites` state). `generateInviteLink(label, expiryMillisFromNow, maxUses, bootstrapReferral): GeneratedInvite` builds the capability via UniFFI `composeInviteCapability(...)` + uploads via `DiscoveryClient.uploadInvite` + appends a `GeneratedInvite { blobId, blobIdSlug, url, label, expiryMs, maxUses, createdAtMs }` to `ContactsState.outstandingInvites`. `revokeInviteLink(blobId): RevokeInviteResult` drives `DiscoveryClient.revokeInvite` + drops the row from `outstandingInvites` on `Revoked` (`NotFoundOrNotOwner` keeps it so the user can retry — the 404-folding-of-bad-signature path may be transient). Both methods use `Identity.fromSeed(secrets.exportKey).use { … }` to hot-derive the canonical Identity per call + zeroize the `ed25519Seed` byte array in the revoke path's `finally` so the signing seed doesn't outlive the operation. **Three new constants** at file scope: `DEFAULT_INVITE_EXPIRY_MS = 7 * 86_400_000` (matches the web side's 7-day default), `SINGLE_USE: UInt = 0u` (sentinel the server treats as single-use), `INVITE_URL_PREFIX = "https://lakko.me/i/"` (must stay in sync with the web's `composePointerInviteUrl` host + path so links generated on one platform open on the other). **`ContactsState`** gains `outstandingInvites: List<GeneratedInvite>` field; cleared on logout / forgetDevice via the existing `clear()` reset-to-fresh-state path. The list is **NOT persisted across process death** — the authoritative state lives on the discovery server, and rehydrating this list would require a per-account `/discovery/invite/by-owner` endpoint that doesn't exist in Phase 1 (deliberate: surfaces "share the URL right now" UX, not a "manage outstanding invites" admin panel). **`GeneratedInvite`** is a data class with manual `equals` / `hashCode` over the `ByteArray blobId` field — same posture as every other data class with byte fields in this codebase. **`ContactsViewModel`** picks up `onGenerateInviteClicked()` + `onRevokeInviteClicked(blobId)`. Generate flips `generatingInvite = true` for the spinner + clears any prior `inviteError`; on `ContactsError` runs through the existing `userFacingError` mapper. **`ContactsUiState`** + the VM-local `UiSlice` both gain `generatingInvite: Boolean` + `inviteError: String?` fields. **`ContactsScreen`** picks up an `InviteSection` rendered above the existing `SendSection` — section header with count + a one-liner explainer + the primary Generate button (with inline `CircularProgressIndicator` for the busy state) + per-row Cards for each outstanding invite. **Each `InviteRow`** shows the URL (monospaced, truncated to 2 lines) + three buttons in equal-weight columns: `Copy` (writes to `ClipboardManager.setPrimaryClip(ClipData.newPlainText(...))` — Android 13+ auto-surfaces the "copied" snackbar so no Toast fallback is needed), `Share` (launches `Intent.createChooser(Intent.ACTION_SEND, text/plain)` with the URL as `EXTRA_TEXT` so the user picks the destination app), `Revoke` (outlined button destructive style — drives `viewModel.onRevokeInviteClicked(invite.blobId)`). **`LocalContext.current`** is captured once at the top of the screen's Column body + threaded into both helpers; `startActivity(chooser)` carries `FLAG_ACTIVITY_NEW_TASK` so the share sheet opens cleanly from a Composable context. **Three incidental import fixes** in the chat + contacts screens: a previous global-replace had mangled the `kotlinx.coroutines.delay` / `isActive` imports down to `import delay` / `import isActive` (broken FQN). Restored to full paths. **No new host-JVM tests** — the high-level orchestrators drive UniFFI-bound functions through the cdylib, instrumented-only. The arg-validation gates for the underlying `DiscoveryClient.uploadInvite` / `revokeInvite` are already covered by 1.15e9's `DiscoveryClientTest`. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 38/38 passing (unchanged from 1.15e8/e9). **Manual smoke procedure**: alice signs up → Open contacts → tap **Generate invite link** → spinner blips, URL Card appears with Copy / Share / Revoke → tap Share → pick Messages app → send to bob's phone number → bob taps the URL (1.15e11 handles the consume side). Or: tap Copy → paste in any IM app. Or: tap Revoke → the row disappears, the URL is dead for any subsequent consume attempt. **What 1.15e10 does NOT ship**: the **joiner side** — invite-landing Compose screen + Activity-side deep-link (`https://lakko.me/i/<slug>` URL handler) + auto-Accept-on-consume bridge — 1.15e11; the custom-config dialog for label / expiry / max_uses — 1.15e10b if the UX wants it; QR-code rendering of the invite URL for OOB in-person scanning — future polish.
  - **1.15e11a** ✅ Joiner-side foundations — `ContactsRepository.acceptInviteLink(slug)` orchestrator + `InviteIntake` slug holder + `AndroidManifest` deep-link intent filter. **`AndroidManifest.xml`** picks up a second `<intent-filter>` on `MainActivity` (alongside the existing LAUNCHER one) that matches `https://lakko.me/i/<slug>` (+ the http variant for self-hosted mirrors over plaintext in censored regions — the capability codec doesn't depend on TLS for security, the bytes are Ed25519-signed). `android:autoVerify="true"` opts into Android App Links: the OS will fetch `https://lakko.me/.well-known/assetlinks.json` to verify our SHA-256 cert fingerprint owns the domain + then opens our app directly **without** the disambiguation picker. The assetlinks file ships on the discovery service in a later sprint; until then the filter still works but the user sees the standard "open with…" sheet. **`InviteIntake.kt`** is a tiny process-scoped state holder: `pendingInviteSlug: StateFlow<String?>` + `publish(slug)` + `consume()`. `MainActivity` (1.15e11b) reads the launching `Intent.data` + extracts the slug + hands it to `InviteIntake.publish(slug)`; the Compose router observes `pendingInviteSlug` + routes to the `InviteLandingScreen` when non-null + calls `consume()` once the screen mounts. **No persistence across process death** is deliberate: a cold-start deep link constructs a fresh holder; if the app crashes before the landing screen mounts, the user re-taps the URL from their messaging app + lands cleanly on the second try. **`ContactsRepository.acceptInviteLink(slug)`** is the substantial new piece — eight-step orchestrator: (1) base64url-decode the slug → 32-byte `blob_id`; (2) `DiscoveryClient.fetchInvite(blobId)` → capability wire bytes (404 → return `NotFound`); (3) `InviteCapability.decodeAndVerify(wire)` → local Ed25519 check (a malicious server cannot serve substituted bytes — the joiner re-verifies independently); (4) `isValidNow(now)` expiry check — past expiry → return `Expired` WITHOUT touching the consume counter; (5) `DiscoveryClient.consumeInvite(blobId, capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId)` → atomic counter bump + per-joiner dedup tag (joiner's account_id NEVER crosses the wire — only the opaque HMAC tag does); the `Exhausted` / `AlreadyConsumed` / `NotFound` discriminated cases short-circuit out at this point; (6) `ContactsClient.sendContactRequestToBundle(...)` — ships a ContactRequest to the inviter's stranger inbox; the bundle is inline in the capability so no discovery `lookup → identityFetch` round-trip is needed (mirrors the censorship-region happy path ADR-0005 §13.3 calls out); (7) if a [ChatRepository] is wired, `ChatRepository.startGroupWith(...)` fetches the inviter's KeyPackage + creates the 1×1 MLS group + ships the Welcome (failures fall through to `groupIdHex = null` — the user still sees the contact as accepted, the MLS group can be retried via a future polish); (8) append a synthetic `AcceptedContact` row to `ContactsState.accepted` with the resulting `groupIdHex` so the user can immediately Open Chat. **The pending-list is NOT touched** on this path: invites bypass the stranger-inbox-and-Accept dance by design (the cryptographic provenance is already established by the signature on the capability the joiner just verified). **`AcceptInviteResult` sealed type** carries the eight distinct outcomes: `Accepted(recipientAccountId, groupIdHex?)` / `AlreadyAccepted` / `Expired` / `Exhausted` / `NotFound` / `MalformedSlug` / `RequiresReauth` / `Failed(message)`. Each maps to a distinct user-visible state on the landing screen; the UI renders the appropriate copy + Open Chat / Retry / Re-login affordance without exception-walking the underlying client error trees. **`MalformedSlug` covers both** a non-base64url-decodable slug AND a slug that decodes to the wrong byte length — both would otherwise surface as a server 400 from `fetchInvite`, but client-side validation gives a faster + clearer user message. **Synthetic `IncomingContactRequest` shell** in the accepted row: the `AcceptedContact` UI shape expects one + the invite flow bypassed the stranger-inbox path that normally fills it. The `messageId` is the DS receipt id from the outbound ContactRequest; `intro` is null; `fromPseudonym` is a 32-byte all-zero placeholder (it's only used for future reply routing we don't implement yet). **`Identity.use { … }` + `signingSeed.fill(0)`** lifecycle posture mirrors the rest of ContactsRepository's call sites — no canonical Identity reference held past the operation. **No host-JVM tests** in 1.15e11a — every interesting path drives UniFFI through JNA. The `MalformedSlug` + `RequiresReauth` branches WOULD be host-testable on their own, but with no live UniFFI / network underpinning the happy path the coverage gain is marginal. **What 1.15e11a does NOT ship**: `InviteLandingScreen` + `InviteLandingViewModel` — 1.15e11b; `MainActivity` `onCreate`/`onNewIntent` extraction of the slug + routing — 1.15e11b; QR-code-based OOB invite consume (the in-person scanner UX) — future polish.
  - **1.15e11b** ✅ Joiner-side `InviteLandingScreen` + `MainActivity` Intent routing — closes the invite-link round trip. Three files touched. **`ui/contacts/InviteLandingViewModel.kt`**: state machine wrapping [ContactsRepository.acceptInviteLink]. Constructed per-slug (keyed `invite-${slug}` on the Activity-side `viewModel(key = ...)` factory so re-tapping a different invite URL builds a fresh VM with the right binding); auto-runs `acceptInviteLink` on `init` so the screen immediately shows a spinner + lands on the result on its own (no user gesture needed — matches the tap-link-app-opens-accept-happens UX users expect). **`InviteLandingState`** sealed interface with nine variants: `Loading` (initial), `Accepted(recipientAccountId, groupIdHex?)`, `AlreadyAccepted`, `Expired`, `Exhausted`, `NotFound`, `MalformedSlug`, `RequiresReauth`, `Failed(message)`. Maps 1:1 to the underlying [AcceptInviteResult] variants — the wrapper just adds `Loading` for the in-flight window. **`retry()`** on Failed: flips back to Loading + re-runs the full orchestrator from scratch; the server-side `(blob_id, joiner_tag)` dedup handles the case where a prior attempt already mutated state on the consume step. **`ui/contacts/InviteLandingScreen.kt`**: minimal Material3 panels per state — Loading shows a 48dp `CircularProgressIndicator` + "Accepting invite…" hint; `Accepted` shows a short account-id b64 preview + `Open chat` + `Done` (Open chat hidden when `groupIdHex` is null + a "MLS chat session not built yet — Open Contacts to retry" hint surfaces instead); five terminal info panels (AlreadyAccepted / Expired / Exhausted / NotFound / MalformedSlug / RequiresReauth) share the same title + body + `Done` shape; `Failed` shows the wrapped message + `Retry` primary + `Done` outlined. **Account-id preview helper** `shortAccountId(bytes)`: base64-encode the 16-byte UUIDv7 + truncate to 8 chars + `…`. The full b64 (22 chars) is ugly + not actionable for the user — the short preview is just "this is who you connected to" reassurance. **`MainActivity`** changes: (1) field `private val inviteIntake = InviteIntake()` at Activity scope. (2) `onCreate` reads `intent` once + hands to a new `handleInviteIntent(intent)` helper. (3) **`override fun onNewIntent(intent: Intent)`** — warm-start deep-link path; called when the user taps a `https://lakko.me/i/<slug>` URL while the app is already running. Defensive: skips non-VIEW intents (LAUNCHER taps land here on every restart too), missing `data` URIs, non-`/i/` paths, empty slugs after trim + trailing-slash strip. (4) `inviteIntake` passed through `AppRoot` → `SessionRoot` (new constructor arg on both). (5) `SessionRoot` observes `inviteIntake.pendingInviteSlug.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()` + a `LaunchedEffect(pendingInviteSlug)` sets `destination = SessionDestination.InviteLanding(slug)` + calls `inviteIntake.consume()` to clear the slot. (6) `SessionDestination` gains an `InviteLanding(val slug: String)` variant. (7) The `when (destination)` arm for `InviteLanding` builds the keyed `InviteLandingViewModel` + renders the screen + wires `onDone` → `Home` + `onOpenChat(groupIdHex)` → `Chat(groupIdHex)`. **Pre-auth deep links**: if the user taps an invite URL while in `AuthState.Anon`, the intake still publishes; `SessionRoot` doesn't mount until they authenticate, at which point the slug is still non-null and the LaunchedEffect fires — the user lands on the InviteLanding screen as soon as their session comes up. Documented in `InviteIntake.kt`. **No host-JVM tests** in 1.15e11b — the entire surface is Compose renders driven by Android Intent / Lifecycle plumbing and UniFFI calls. The auto-run-on-init shape would be VM-testable in isolation if we mocked the repository, but that's instrumented-suite territory. `cargo fmt --check` clean. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 38/38 passing (unchanged from 1.15e8). **Phase 1.15e is now feature-complete on the contacts + invite side**: signup + login + recover (1.15d), contacts UI + stranger-inbox poll + 1×1 chat MLS round-trip (1.15e1-7 + 1.15f1-4c) + foreground continuous polling + owner-side generate-invite + joiner-side accept-via-deep-link. The 1.15 phase backbone is in place. **Manual smoke procedure**: alice signs up → Open contacts → Generate invite link → Share to bob's phone via Messages → bob taps the URL → Android routes to Lakko → if bob is logged in: spinner blips, the InviteLandingScreen lands on `Accepted(groupIdHex)` → tap `Open chat` → type "hi" → alice's app long-polls + sees the message. If bob isn't logged in yet: he sees Login first, signs in, the intake's published slug fires the LaunchedEffect, lands on InviteLandingScreen immediately. **What 1.15e11b does NOT ship**: `.well-known/assetlinks.json` on the discovery service (Android still shows the disambiguation chooser until then) — future sprint; QR-code-based OOB invite consume (the in-person scanner UX) — future polish; the bootstrap-referral flow where an invite link opens to a signup screen for a fresh user — future polish, currently the URL just requires the joiner to already have an account.
- **1.15f** ✅ 1×1 chat UI — `LazyColumn` timeline + composer + ack indicators. All sub-items (1.15f1–f4c) shipped: per-thread `ChatViewModel` keyed by `groupIdHex`, foreground continuous-polling loop, MLS state persistence via Android's `EncryptedFile` under the device-root key, attachment picker + inline-preview chips, push-tap deep-link routing into the thread (1.20d2). Mirror of `apps/web` 1.16e / 1.16e6c.
  - **1.15f1** ✅ `DiscoveryClient` KeyPackage extension — adds the two endpoints (1.15e3 deferred) that the chat surface needs to seed an MLS group. Mirror of the web's `keypackageUpload` / `keypackageFetch` + `uploadKeyPackage` / `fetchKeyPackage` wrappers. **`Wire.kt`** picks up three new `@Serializable` DTOs: `KeyPackageUploadRequest(pow, account_id, keypackage)`, `KeyPackageUploadResponse(ok)`, `KeyPackageFetchResponse(keypackage)`. The `keypackage` field is the base64-encoded TLS-serialised MLS KeyPackage; same snake_case convention as the rest of the discovery wire. **`DiscoveryClient`** picks up two low-level methods: `keypackageUpload(req): KeyPackageUploadResponse` (POST `/discovery/keypackage`) and `keypackageFetch(accountId): KeyPackageFetchResponse?` (GET `/discovery/keypackage/{slug}`, 404 → null). Plus two high-level wrappers: `uploadKeyPackage(accountId, keyPackageBytes)` (one PoW solve + POST; enforces the 2048-byte `MAX_KEYPACKAGE_BYTES` ceiling client-side so a misbehaving caller doesn't pay a round-trip), `fetchKeyPackage(accountId): ByteArray?` (GET + base64 decode — caller hands the bytes straight to `Group.addMemberFromWire(...)`). **`getNullableOn404` helper** factored out of the inline endpoint methods so `identityFetch` + `keypackageFetch` share the same 404→null + non-2xx → `DiscoveryHttpError` pipeline. **Six new host-JVM tests** (20 total in `DiscoveryClientTest`): `keypackageUpload` snake_case serialisation, `keypackageFetch` 200 path + base64url-no-pad path slug shape (no `=` / `+` / `/`), `keypackageFetch` 404 → null fold, `keypackageFetch` wrong-length accountId rejection, `uploadKeyPackage` wrong-length accountId rejection, `uploadKeyPackage` empty-bytes rejection, `uploadKeyPackage` oversized-bytes rejection (one byte past the 2048 ceiling). **Why a separate sub-sprint** rather than folding into 1.15e3: keeping the discovery surface scoped to the contacts side made 1.15e3's review easier (already 14 tests + a ~500-line client); the KeyPackage endpoints only matter when the chat surface is being assembled, which is now. **What 1.15f1 does NOT ship**: the `ChatClient` that consumes `fetchKeyPackage` for `startGroupWith` and `publishGroup` for `sendMessage` — 1.15f2; MLS state persistence via `Group::snapshot` / `Group::restore` (the wasm surface has these via `snapshotStorage` / `restoreStorage` — needs UniFFI exposure before 1.15f3); Compose ChatScreen — 1.15f4.
  - **1.15f2** ✅ Kotlin `ChatClient` + UniFFI `Group.groupId` accessor — Application-message orchestrator on top of MLS groups. Mirror of `apps/web/src/chat/client.ts`, simplified by taking a live UniFFI `Identity` handle directly rather than the web's `IdentitySecrets`-via-`identityProvider` indirection (the canonical-Identity threading the web side needs against its WASM-handle-per-instance lifetime is addressed differently on the Kotlin side — the ChatRepository in 1.15f4 will hold a single Identity and pass it into every operation). **Rust-side prerequisite**: `Group.group_id()` accessor on UniFFI — stable across epochs (the Rust test asserts it doesn't change after an `add_member_from_wire` commit), returns the raw bytes `lakko-svc-ds` uses (hex-encoded) to key the `/ds/group/{group_id_hex}/messages` journal. `clippy::implicit_clone` flagged a `.to_vec()` on what was already a `Vec<u8>` — clean `.clone()` instead. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` **30/30** passing (was 29 after 1.15e5; +1). `just regen-bindings` rebuilds; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 6794 → **6846 lines** carrying the new `Group.groupId(): ByteArray` method. **One new Kotlin source** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/chat/`: `ChatClient.kt`. **Two public operations**: `sendMessage(identity, group, plaintext): SendMessageResult` (`group.createApplicationMessage(identity, plaintext)` → POST to `/ds/group/{group_id_hex}/messages` via `DsClient.publishGroup(group.groupId(), wireBytes)`; returns the DS message id as both the row id + the cursor value), `pollMessages(identity, group, after, waitSecs): PollMessagesResult` (`DsClient.fetchGroup(group.groupId(), after, waitSecs)` → for each row, `group.processIncoming(identity, payload)` → dispatch to `IncomingChatEvent`). **`markOwnPublishedCommit(messageId)`** is the third method, the cross-client accessor: when a future `ContactsClient.addMemberToGroup` (1.18g-equivalent) broadcasts a commit, the caller registers the resulting DS id so the next `pollMessages` drops the echo via the same `ownPublishedIds` filter that `sendMessage` uses for application messages. **Self-message echo suppression**: `sendMessage` adds the published DS id to a `Collections.synchronizedSet(mutableSetOf<Long>())`; `pollMessages` removes-and-skips matching ids. The DS doesn't dedup messages and OpenMLS can't decrypt the sender's own application messages at the same epoch — surface as a phantom System row in the timeline + a noisy processIncoming-failed log entry without the filter. **MLS ratchet state mutates per call**: every `createApplicationMessage` drains the message-secret tree on disk (well, in the `Identity`'s OpenMLS provider storage), every `processIncoming` advances the state. 1.15f3 will wire a snapshot/restore hook so the in-memory MLS state survives process death; 1.15f2 ships the in-memory-only path to keep the layering reviewable. **Discriminated `IncomingChatEvent`** (sealed interface): `Application(messageId, plaintext, createdAt)` (user-visible chat row — UTF-8-decoded with replacement-on-error; the MLS layer already authenticated the ciphertext, so malformed UTF-8 from a buggy peer is the worst case), `System(messageId, systemKind, createdAt)` (commit / proposal / external-join-proposal frames — advance MLS state but render as a thin separator in the UI, or get skipped). `SystemKind` enum: `Commit`, `Proposal`, `ExternalJoinProposal` — matches the UniFFI `ProcessedMessage` variants 1:1 via a clean Kotlin `when` (no PascalCase-string discriminator hack like the wasm side needs). **Bad frame handling**: wrong epoch / malformed wire surfaces as an `FfiException` from `processIncoming`; we drop the row + advance the cursor, same posture as the contacts inbox poll. No logger plumbed yet — silence with a documented gap for 1.15d7's `tracing`-equivalent wire-up. **Two errors** mirror `AuthClient` / `ContactsClient` shape: `ChatClientArgError` (caller wiring bug — empty plaintext caught before any FFI / network call), `ChatError(message, cause)` (wraps any underlying `DsClient` / FFI / MLS failure). **`ChatClient` is `AutoCloseable`** — closes the inner `DsClient` on close; one instance per app process expected. **Two host-JVM smoke tests** (`ChatClientTest`): construction wires through a MockEngine-backed DsClient cleanly + idempotent close; `markOwnPublishedCommit` is a pure-Kotlin Set mutation, no-throw smoke + duplicate-call safety. **The end-to-end FFI-driven flows** (sendMessage / pollMessages happy paths, processIncoming dispatch, ownPublishedIds suppression actually filtering an echo) need a live `Identity` + `Group` which load `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` via JNA — covered by the instrumented suite that 1.15f5 lands. The arg-validation gate inside `sendMessage` fires BEFORE any FFI call (empty-plaintext check is first), so a host-JVM test for that path is also blocked only on the Group / Identity construction needing FFI. **What 1.15f2 does NOT ship**: MLS state persistence (`Identity.snapshotStorage` / `Identity.restoreStorage` need UniFFI exposure — 1.15f3); ChatRepository + ChatViewModel + Compose ChatScreen — 1.15f4; Accept-creates-an-MLS-group wire-up between ContactsRepository and ChatRepository — 1.15f4 as well, end-to-end smoke test of the full 1×1 chat round-trip — instrumented in 1.15f5.
  - **1.15f3** ✅ MLS state persistence — UniFFI `Identity.snapshotStorage` / `restoreStorage` + Kotlin `IdentitySnapshotStore` (Keystore-wrapped on-disk envelope). Rust-side: extended UniFFI `Identity` with `snapshot_storage(): Vec<u8>` and `restore_storage(snapshot: Vec<u8>)` — pass-through wrappers over `lakko_mls::Identity::snapshot_storage / restore_storage` (the underlying length-prefixed wire format lakko_mls produces is shared verbatim with `apps/web`'s `Identity.snapshotStorage`; a snapshot taken on web can be restored on Android with the same OPAQUE export key, and vice versa). **Two new Rust tests** alongside the existing thirty: `identity_snapshot_round_trips_through_restore` (builds Alice + Group + adds Bob to mutate provider storage; snapshots; builds a parallel Alice from the same seed; verifies the parallel Alice's pre-restore snapshot differs from the post-mutation one, that `restore_storage` doesn't throw on a well-formed blob, that a double-restore is stable, and that the original Alice still produces an exporter after the clone restore — doesn't mutate the original); `identity_restore_rejects_malformed_input` (empty input surfaces as `FfiError::Identity`). **No byte-equality assertion on the round-tripped snapshot**: the underlying OpenMLS provider uses a `HashMap`-backed store whose serialisation order is not stable across instances, so two providers holding semantically identical state may emit slightly different blob bytes. The semantic round-trip (the post-restore exporter test on a peer-side group is 1.15f5's instrumented territory; the in-Rust test goes as far as stability-under-repeated-apply). **`cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 32/32** passing (was 30 after 1.15f2; +2). `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `fmt --check` clean. `just regen-bindings` rebuilds; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 6846 → **6957 lines** carrying the two new `Identity` methods. **`IdentitySnapshotStore.kt`** (`apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/chat/`): object with `putSnapshot(context, accountIdB64, snapshotBytes)` / `getSnapshot(context, accountIdB64): ByteArray?` / `deleteSnapshot(context, accountIdB64)`. **Wraps the snapshot under a Keystore-resident AES-256-GCM key** under alias `me.lakko.mls-snapshot-wrap.v1` — distinct from `DeviceKeyStore`'s wrap key on purpose: independent rotation, different access pattern (snapshot is written every send / every poll, device root key once per app start), parallel threat-model citizen status. **`setIsStrongBoxBacked` deliberately NOT called** for this alias — StrongBox AES throughput on some chipsets isn't worth the marginal hardware-isolation gain for a frequently-touched key; TEE-backed Keystore (which we always fall back to) covers the threat model. **Same on-disk envelope shape** as `DeviceKeyStore`: `iv(12B) || ciphertext || tag(16B)` under `context.noBackupFilesDir/lakko-mls-snapshots/<accountIdB64>.bin`. `noBackup` is critical — MLS state is device-bound; an adb cloud-restore that carried it between devices would diverge from peer views the moment any party sends a new message. **Truncated / missing file folds to `null` return** (same posture as `DeviceKeyStore.getDeviceKey`) — caller treats it as "no snapshot on this device" and re-bootstraps via the discovery directory's KeyPackage upserts. **Host-JVM tests skipped** for this class — loads the `AndroidKeyStore` provider which only exists on a device / emulator. Same posture as `DeviceKeyStore` from 1.15d3. Instrumented coverage lands with 1.15f5's full chat round-trip. **What 1.15f3 does NOT ship**: actual `ChatClient` / `ChatRepository` wire-up that calls `putSnapshot` after every state-mutating MLS op + `getSnapshot` + `restoreStorage` on session restore — 1.15f4; the `AuthRepository.forgetDevice` extension that also calls `deleteSnapshot(accountIdB64)` — 1.15f4. The primitives are in place; the wire-up follows.
  - **1.15f4a** ✅ `ChatRepository` data layer — owns the canonical MLS [Identity] for the session + a per-group `Map<groupIdHex, GroupRecord>` + the send / poll / startGroupWith / joinGroupFromWelcome operations, wired through [IdentitySnapshotStore] from 1.15f3 for automatic on-disk persistence after every state-mutating call. **One new Kotlin source** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/chat/`: `ChatRepository.kt`. **Canonical Identity ownership**: the Identity handle is built once per session by [bootstrap](secrets: IdentitySecrets) from the OPAQUE export key, kept alive in an `AtomicReference<Identity?>` until [clear] / [forgetDevice]. **Every chat op threads the same Identity** through `ChatClient` so MLS state mutations accumulate on a single OpenMLS provider — throwaway `Identity.fromSeed` per call (the simpler shape `ContactsRepository` uses for its stateless sign / decrypt surface) would lose every ratchet advance. **Coroutine `Mutex`** (`groupsLock`) serialises MLS state mutations on a given group so a parallel `sendMessage` + `pollAllGroups` don't race against one another's `ChatClient` calls (the underlying OpenMLS storage isn't internally synchronised for concurrent writes against the same group). Coarse-grained: one mutex per repository, not per group — Phase-1 chat volume doesn't justify the per-group fine-grained map. **Four public ops**: `startGroupWith(recipientAccountId, recipientSignaturePubkey)` (fetch recipient KeyPackage via DiscoveryClient → `Group.create(identity)` → `group.addMemberFromWire` produces a `(commit, welcome)` pair — commit dropped because the group was one-of-one before the add — `wrapEnvelope(KIND_WELCOME, welcome)` + `DsClient.publishMailbox` keyed by `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity(recipientPubkey)` ships the Welcome to the recipient's stranger inbox; returns the new group's hex id for the UI route), `joinGroupFromWelcome(welcomeBytes)` (`Group.joinFromWelcome(welcomeBytes, identity)` → register in the per-group map → returns hex id; called when 1.15e6's `pollStrangerInbox` returns an `IncomingMailboxEvent.Welcome`), `sendMessage(groupIdHex, plaintext)` (`ChatClient.sendMessage` → append outgoing row to the in-memory timeline → persist snapshot), `pollGroup(groupIdHex)` (`ChatClient.pollMessages` → applies events to the timeline → persist snapshot if any rows landed) + `pollAllGroups()` (per-group iteration with best-effort failure isolation). **Snapshot persistence** runs after every state-mutating call via `persistSnapshotBestEffort()` — `Identity.snapshotStorage()` → `IdentitySnapshotStore.putSnapshot(context, accountIdB64, blob)`. Failures are swallowed (`runCatching`-style); the in-memory state stays good, the next mutation retries the persist naturally. **Snapshot restore on bootstrap**: when `bootstrap()` lands a non-null `getSnapshot`, the canonical Identity is built first, then `identity.restoreStorage(blob)` rehydrates the OpenMLS provider storage. **Per-group `Group` rebuild is lazy** in 1.15f4a — `restoreStorage` lays down the on-storage rows but doesn't reconstruct the `Group` handles; peers' commits surface via `pollAllGroups` and the user's send-side path will `Group.create` / `joinFromWelcome` on demand. A future sprint could iterate the provider's known group ids and rebuild handles eagerly. **`StateFlow<ChatRepositoryState>`** with `threads: List<ChatThread>` + `bootstrapped: Boolean`. Each `ChatThread` carries `groupIdHex` (used as the DS journal key + the route arg for the 1×1 ChatScreen) + the `messages: List<ChatMessage>` timeline + the next-poll cursor. `ChatMessage` is `(messageId, direction, text, createdAt)` with `Direction` enum `Outgoing / Incoming / System` — `System` rows surface MLS commit / proposal frames with a thin placeholder text (`[mls commit]` etc.) the UI can render or skip. **`bootstrap` is idempotent** for the same account id: early-returns when `currentAccountIdB64` already matches. Switching accounts (different `secrets.accountId`) wipes the in-memory state first via `wipeInMemoryState()` which closes the prior Identity + every `Group` handle (releases Rust-side `Arc`s + the prior provider's storage). **Lifecycle methods**: [clear] (drops in-memory state, keeps the on-disk snapshot envelope — called from `AuthRepository.logout` so re-login on the same device picks up where we left off), [forgetDevice] (clear + `snapshotStore.deleteSnapshot`; the only way back into conversation history is from peers' epochs forward via MLS commits once they catch up to this device's absence — same posture as `DeviceKeyStore` deletion from 1.15d3). **`AutoCloseable`** closes all four inner clients (ChatClient, DsClient, DiscoveryClient) + the canonical Identity + every Group handle. **No host-JVM tests in 1.15f4a** — every public op touches UniFFI (`Identity.fromSeed`, `Group.create`, `Group.addMemberFromWire`, `Group.joinFromWelcome`, `Group.createApplicationMessage`, `Group.processIncoming`, `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity`, `Identity.snapshotStorage` / `restoreStorage`) which load `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.so` via JNA. Instrumented coverage in 1.15f5. **What 1.15f4a does NOT ship**: lifecycle wire-up between `AuthRepository` + `ChatRepository` (bootstrap on signup/login/recover, clear on logout, forgetDevice on forget) — 1.15f4c, lands together with the `ContactsRepository.acceptContactRequest` → `ChatRepository.startGroupWith` bridge; Compose ChatList + 1×1 ChatScreen + ChatViewModel — 1.15f4b; eager per-group Group rebuild on snapshot restore — future sprint when the lazy approach's edge cases (peer commits arriving for a group whose handle isn't built yet) actually bite.
  - **1.15f4b** ✅ Compose Chat surface — `ChatListScreen` + 1×1 `ChatScreen` + `ChatViewModel`. **Three new Kotlin sources** under `apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/ui/chat/`. **`ChatViewModel`** (per-thread, keyed by `groupIdHex`): combines `ChatRepository.state`-side thread (looked up by id) with VM-local composer state (draft input + sending / sendError / refreshing flags) into a single `StateFlow<ChatScreenUiState>`. Same manual-combine pattern as `ContactsViewModel` from 1.15e6 (two parallel `viewModelScope.launch { collect { … } }` jobs because kotlinx-coroutines has no synchronous combine for two StateFlows). **`ChatListScreen`** (no dedicated ViewModel — surface is purely a read-only projection over `ChatRepository.state`): vertical `LazyColumn` of `ChatThread` Cards. Each card shows the short hex of the group id + the last-message preview (with `you: ` prefix for Outgoing rows) + a small "N messages" counter. `LaunchedEffect(Unit)` fires one `pollAllGroups()` on first arrival; the user taps Refresh thereafter. Empty-state Box explains that Contacts → Accept will create a thread. Tap a row → `onOpenThread(groupIdHex)` callback the parent route plumbs to navigate to [ChatScreen]. **1×1 `ChatScreen`** (per-thread, takes `ChatViewModel` keyed on the route's groupIdHex): `Column` with header (back + group hex + refresh / spinner) + `LazyColumn` of `MessageRow`s + composer pinned to the bottom. **Three message styles**: `Direction.Outgoing` → right-aligned `Surface(primaryContainer)` bubble with `onPrimaryContainer` content; `Direction.Incoming` → left-aligned `Surface(surfaceVariant)` bubble; `Direction.System` → centered monospace small text (rendering `[mls commit]` / `[mls proposal]` / `[mls external join]` placeholders the repository emits for MLS frame events). **Auto-scroll-to-bottom** on every new message via `LaunchedEffect(state.messages.size) { listState.animateScrollToItem(...) }` — the standard chat-UX touch. **Composer**: `OutlinedTextField` with `singleLine = false` so a 5-line paste renders as five lines + wraps everything into one MLS Application message on send; `KeyboardCapitalization.Sentences` (chat-natural — distinct from the username textfields' `None`); Send button disabled when `draft.isBlank()` or `sending`. Inline `sendError` text above the composer when the last send threw. **`threadKnown = false` panel** for the post-restart-pre-rehydrate edge case: the screen still renders the composer + a small hint ("Tap Refresh — a peer's commit will rebuild it") rather than crashing or showing an empty skeleton. The repository's `sendMessage` will surface a clear error if the user types into the composer before the handle exists. **Manual refresh only** in 1.15f4b — `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { onRefreshClicked() }` once per composition, then the button. Continuous polling lands in 1.15e7 alongside the contacts inbox poll loop. **`shortHex` helper** (first 6 + last 4 hex chars, `…` separator) reused from `ContactsScreen` posture — group ids are 64-char hex strings, the full thing is unreadable. **No host-JVM tests** in 1.15f4b — every composable observes `ChatRepository`'s StateFlow which is populated by UniFFI-bound ops; the screen-render itself is testable via Compose UI tests but those land with the instrumented suite in 1.15f5. **What 1.15f4b does NOT ship**: lifecycle wire-up between `AuthRepository` + `ChatRepository` (bootstrap on session, clear on logout, forget on forgetDevice) — 1.15f4c; `ContactsRepository.acceptContactRequest` → `ChatRepository.startGroupWith` bridge — 1.15f4c as well; `MainActivity` Session sub-routing extension to expose `Chats` + the per-thread chat destination — 1.15f4c; continuous polling — 1.15e7. **Manual smoke procedure** (once 1.15f4c lands the wire-up): signup as alice → Open contacts → send to bob → switch to bob's emulator, log in → refresh stranger inbox → Accept → switch back to alice → Refresh chat list → see the new thread → tap → type "hi" → Send → switch to bob → Refresh → "hi" appears as an Incoming bubble. The full end-to-end round-trip becomes observable.
  - **1.15f4c** ✅ Accept-creates-MLS-group bridge + lifecycle wire-up — the final piece that turns 1.15e6/f4a/f4b into an observable end-to-end 1×1 chat round-trip. **Six files touched**: `ContactsRepository` picks up an optional `chatRepo: ChatRepository? = null` constructor arg + forwards Welcome envelopes from `refreshInbox` to `chatRepo.joinGroupFromWelcome` (best-effort; single-Welcome failures don't block cursor advance for the rest); `acceptContactRequest(messageId)` becomes `acceptContactRequest(messageId, groupIdHex: String? = null)` so the orchestrator can stash the new group id on the [AcceptedContact] row. `ContactsViewModel` picks up its own optional `chatRepo: ChatRepository?` arg + drives the Accept dance: lookup pending → `chatRepo.startGroupWith(fromAccountId, fromPubkey)` → `contactsRepo.acceptContactRequest(messageId, groupIdHex)`. Failures during the chat handshake fall through to the local-marker path with `groupIdHex = null`; the UI surfaces this as a `no chat` hint on the accepted row instead of an `Open chat` button. Legacy v1 envelopes (no sender `fromAccountId`) also fall through — the accept-side group creation needs the recipient's UUIDv7 to `fetchKeyPackage` against. **`ContactsScreen`** picks up an `onOpenChat: ((groupIdHex) -> Unit)? = null` callback + the `AcceptedSection` renders an `Open chat` Material3 Button per row when both `groupIdHex` is set AND the callback is wired. `HomeScreen` gains a second primary button: `Open chats` next to the existing `Open contacts`, plus updated copy stating chat is live. **`MainActivity` Session sub-routing extends to four destinations**: `private sealed interface SessionDestination` with `Home` / `Contacts` / `Chats` / `Chat(groupIdHex: String)`. Each destination wires through to the right composable; `Chat` keys its ViewModel by `chat-${groupIdHex}` so navigating to a different thread rebuilds the VM with the right per-thread state binding. Back from `Chat` lands on `Chats` (the chat list), not `Home` — standard chat-app navigation shape. **`ChatRepository` constructed at Activity scope** alongside the existing `AuthRepository` + `ContactsRepository`. Order matters in the constructor block: ChatRepository before ContactsRepository because the latter takes the former as an optional dep. **`SessionRoot` observes `AuthRepository.identitySecrets`** via `collectAsStateWithLifecycle` + a `LaunchedEffect(identitySecrets)` calls `chatRepository.bootstrap(secrets)` on every non-null emission. `bootstrap` is idempotent for the same account id, so re-emissions across config changes cost nothing; switching accounts (which happens via the logout → login flow at the moment) wipes the prior in-memory state automatically. **Logout / forgetDevice ordering**: Logout fires `contactsRepository.clear()` → `chatRepository.clear()` → `authRepository.logout()`. The ChatRepository.clear() drops in-memory MLS state but keeps the on-disk snapshot envelope intact — next login re-bootstraps from the snapshot. ForgetDevice fires `contactsRepository.clear()` → `chatRepository.forgetDevice()` → `authRepository.forgetDevice()`. **ChatRepository.forgetDevice() runs BEFORE auth.forgetDevice()** on purpose: it needs the current accountId-from-SessionStore to key the `IdentitySnapshotStore.deleteSnapshot` call. If we cleared the SessionStore first the snapshot file would be orphaned on disk. **Auto-clear-on-composition-exit** via `DisposableEffect(Unit)` is a no-op today — the natural Session-exit path is one of the explicit handlers above; the effect is there as a future-proofing shape for when 1.15d7's Compose Navigation introduces a back-stack-driven exit. **Manual smoke procedure**: signup as alice on emulator-A → log out → signup as bob on emulator-B → Open contacts → send to @alice → switch back to emulator-A → log in alice → Open contacts → Refresh → see Bob's pending request → Accept (now drives the MLS handshake: fetch Bob's KeyPackage, create a Group, ship the Welcome to Bob's stranger inbox) → Open chats → see the new thread → tap → type "hi" → Send → switch to emulator-B → Open contacts → Refresh (the Welcome lands; ChatRepository.joinGroupFromWelcome runs silently in the inbox poll) → Open chats → see the same thread → Refresh → "hi" appears as an Incoming bubble. The auth + contacts + chat surfaces are now connected. **What 1.15f4c does NOT ship**: continuous polling of either stranger inbox or group journals (Refresh is manual on every screen) — 1.15e7; eager rebuild of per-group `Group` handles on `bootstrap`'s `restoreStorage` (today the handles are lazily reconstructed via `Group.create` / `joinFromWelcome` as users send / peers commit) — future sprint; Compose Navigation with a real back stack — 1.15d7; instrumented end-to-end test of the full alice ↔ bob round-trip — 1.15f5. **The pieces are wired**; the smoke procedure above is the contract 1.15f5 will automate.
  - **1.15f5** ✅ Eager `Group` rebuild on bootstrap — closes the correctness gap from 1.15f4a where `ChatRepository.bootstrap` called `restoreStorage` but didn't reconstruct in-memory `Group` handles, so a peer's chat message to an existing group would miss `pollGroup`'s `groups[groupIdHex]` lookup until the user re-opened that thread by some other path. **Rust-side**: new top-level free function `load_group(identity, group_id): Option<Arc<Group>>` exposed via UniFFI; mirror of the wasm `Group.load(...)` from 1.16e6c. Top-level rather than `Group::load` constructor because UniFFI 0.28 doesn't accept `Option<Self>` in constructor signatures (same workaround as `pow_solve` from 1.15d1). One new Rust round-trip test (`load_group_round_trips_through_snapshot`): create Alice + a group, snapshot her provider; build a parallel Alice from the same seed (load_group returns `None` on the un-restored clone — no group in her provider yet), restore, load_group returns the handle, the restored handle produces the same exporter the original did, `group_id()` round-trips. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` **39/39** passing (was 38 after 1.15e8; +1). `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `fmt --check` clean. `just regen-bindings` rebuilds; Android-side `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` grows 7641 → **7714 lines** carrying the new top-level `loadGroup(identity, groupId): Group?` function. **`KnownGroupsStore.kt`** is a tiny SharedPreferences-backed `Set<String>` of known group-id hex strings per account. `load(accountIdB64): Set<String>` / `add(accountIdB64, groupIdHex)` / `clear(accountIdB64)`. Persists across process death so bootstrap can iterate every previously-joined group + call `loadGroup` for each. **Why a separate per-account `SharedPreferences` file** (`me.lakko.known-groups.v1`) rather than reusing the SessionStore shape: this store needs a `StringSet` keyed by `accountIdB64` so multiple accounts on the same device don't collide. **Why `Set` not `List`**: re-bootstrap on a restart shouldn't append duplicates — `Set` upserts naturally. **`ChatRepository`** picks up a `knownGroupsStore` constructor arg + three call-site changes: (1) `startGroupWith` calls `persistKnownGroupBestEffort(groupIdHex)` after the in-memory map mutation + before snapshot persist — order matters subtly because a crash between the two leaves a known-groups entry pointing at a snapshot row that doesn't exist, which `loadGroup` will surface as `None` on next bootstrap (harmless — the user just doesn't see that thread, the peer re-bootstraps via fresh KeyPackage on their next session). (2) `joinGroupFromWelcome` does the same. (3) `bootstrap` does `restoreStorage` → `canonicalIdentity.set` → iterate `knownGroupsStore.load(accountIdB64)` + `loadGroup(identity, hexToBytes(groupIdHex))` for each + insert into the `groups` map. Failures on individual loads are silent (no logger plumbed yet); a corrupted entry doesn't block the others. **`hexToBytes` helper** is private to `ChatRepository` — local decoder for the lowercase hex `KnownGroupsStore` writes. Odd length / non-hex char → `null` so the bootstrap loop `continue`s past corrupted entries rather than throwing. **`forgetDevice`** picks up a second wipe call: `knownGroupsStore.clear(accountIdB64)` alongside the existing `snapshotStore.deleteSnapshot` — `forgetDevice` is the full-wipe semantics path, leaving the known-groups set behind after a wipe would surface as ghost-rows on the next signup with the same accountId. **`bootstrap` order**: only iterate the known-groups set when `restored = true` — a fresh signup with no snapshot has no groups to load + the iteration would be a no-op anyway, but skipping it is cheaper + leaves the log surface (when 1.15d7 wires one) less noisy. **No host-JVM tests** for the new code — every interesting path goes through UniFFI via JNA (the Rust round-trip test above is the closest we can get on host JVM). **Manual smoke**: alice creates a chat with bob → kills app process (Settings → force-stop) → re-launches → snapshot restore runs + the previously-known group id loads via `loadGroup` + the chat list shows the thread without any user action + bob's new messages arrive on the next long-poll without bob having to re-Send anything. Before 1.15f5 the user would have had to navigate INTO the chat once for the lazy Group.create / joinFromWelcome rebuild to happen — which only works for the owner side (created locally) or via a peer's Welcome (which doesn't re-send if the original consume already landed). **Phase 1.15 is now correctness-complete** for the foreground-polling MLS round trip: snapshot-restore + eager rebuild + continuous polling + invite-link onboarding all interlock cleanly. The remaining items in the parent 1.15f roadmap (WorkManager periodic fetch, FCM push, instrumented suite, Compose Navigation) are background-fetch + test-infra polish rather than user-visible blockers.

#### Web app
- **1.16a** ✅ `apps/web` skeleton — Solid.js + Vite + TypeScript scaffold under `apps/web/`. `wasm-pack` integration that builds `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm` into `apps/web/wasm/` (release bundles to dev / prod via `npm run dev` / `npm run build`). Typed WASM loader wrapper (`src/wasm.ts`) with cached `loadWasm()` promise. "Hello core" `App.tsx` round-trip — instantiates `Identity` + `Group` from the Rust core inside the browser and renders the signature-public-key prefix, KeyPackage size, and member count to confirm the JS↔WASM bridge works. Bundle measurement after `npm run build`: WASM ≈ 574 kB gzipped, app JS ≈ 5 kB gzipped — well inside the < 2 MB Phase 1.16c target. New CI job (`web-build`) runs `wasm-pack` + `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` on every push. Justfile recipes `dev-web` / `build-web`. **No router, no signup, no chat yet** — those land in 1.16b.
- **1.16b** ✅ `apps/web` routing + UI shell — `@solidjs/router` 0.16 wired into `App.tsx`. Public routes `/login` and `/signup` are gated by a `<RequireAnonymous>` guard; protected routes `/`, `/contacts`, `/chat/:id`, `/dev` live behind `<RequireSession>` and render inside an `<AppShell>` layout (sidebar on desktop, top stripe on narrow screens). Typed URL helpers in `routes/paths.ts` are the single source of truth for the URL surface. The 1.16a "Hello core" probe moved out of the root into `routes/DevProbe.tsx`. New `auth/AuthContext.tsx` exposes `useAuth()` with a discriminated `loading | anon | session(account)` state; `signup` / `login` are stubs that flip state in-memory (sessions reset on reload). Real OPAQUE-PAKE arrives in 1.16c. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS 41.5 kB → **15.5 kB gzipped** (was 4.87 kB before the router + 7 routes + AppShell + guards), WASM unchanged at 574 kB gzipped, CSS 2.78 kB → 0.95 kB gzipped.
- **1.16c1** ✅ `crates/lakko-auth-client` — OPAQUE-PAKE client crate. Verbatim mirror of `services/auth/src/opaque.rs::LakkoCipherSuite` (Ristretto255 + TripleDH + Argon2id) under `crates/lakko-auth-client/src/cipher_suite.rs`. Public API: `RegistrationSession::start_password` / `start_recovery` → `(session, request_b64)`; `session.finish(secret, response_b64)` → `RegistrationOutput { upload_b64, export_key }`. Same shape for `LoginSession` returning `LoginOutput { finalize_b64, session_key, export_key }`. `SecretBytes` zeroizes on drop and elides its contents from `Debug`. Errors collapse into `AuthClientError::{Base64, InvalidWire, Opaque}` — finer granularity than `Opaque` would erode OPAQUE's indistinguishable-failure property. Wire-format mirrors of every `lakko-svc-auth` JSON struct (excluding `pow` / `account_id` — those are caller responsibilities) live under `wire::*`. Tests: 7 round-trip integration tests exercise `RegistrationSession` + `LoginSession` against `opaque-ke`'s `ServerSetup` / `ServerRegistration` / `ServerLogin` directly (no DB, no HTTP) and prove client + server session keys agree, the export key is deterministic across registration and login, the recovery flow round-trips, and wrong passwords fail at finalization. `cargo test -p lakko-auth-client`: 10 passed (2 unit + 7 integration + 1 doc-test). `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-auth-client`: clean (the crate enables `getrandom/js` for wasm32 the same way `lakko-ffi-wasm` does). Workspace clippy / fmt / test sweep clean. Does **not** yet expose itself through `lakko-ffi-wasm` (that's 1.16c2) and does **not** speak HTTP (1.16c3) or solve the Equix PoW the server demands (separate sub-sprint).
- **1.16c2** ✅ `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/auth.rs` — wasm-bindgen exposure of `RegistrationSession` / `LoginSession`. New JS classes `OpaqueRegister` / `OpaqueLogin` (each with `startPassword(bytes)` / `startRecovery(bytes)` static factories, `requestB64` / `isPassword` / `isRecovery` getters, and a single-use `finish(secret, responseB64)` method that returns a typed output). Output classes `OpaqueRegisterOutput { uploadB64, exportKey }` and `OpaqueLoginOutput { finalizeB64, sessionKey, exportKey }`. Errors collapse into a `JsError` via the same indistinguishable-failure policy as `lakko-auth-client`. The "consume on finish" requirement of `opaque-ke` is enforced via `Mutex<Option<Session>>` + `take()` — a second `finish` returns an error instead of corrupting OPAQUE state. Generated `.d.ts` confirmed to expose all four classes; `npm run build` from `apps/web` produces a working release bundle. Tests: 3 host-side wrapper tests in `auth::tests::*` (32 ffi-wasm tests total now). Bundle after `npm run build` (release): WASM 1,642 kB → **614 kB gzipped** (+40 kB gz vs 1.16b for opaque-ke + argon2 + curve25519 + hkdf in release+LTO), WASM glue JS 6.35 kB gz, app JS unchanged at 15.5 kB gz. Total ≈ 638 kB gz — still well inside the < 2 MB Phase 1.16f target.
- **1.16c3a** ✅ `crates/lakko-pow` — Equix PoW solver crate. Mirrors the server protocol described in `services/auth/src/pow.rs`: takes a 16-byte seed + `effort_bits` from `/pow/challenge`, loops over random nonces calling `equix::solve(seed || nonce)` until both Equix accepts the input AND `SHA-256(seed || nonce || solution)` has at least `effort_bits` leading zero bits. Public API: `solve(&seed, effort_bits) -> PowProof`, `solve_with_budget` for callers that want to cap attempt count, `meets_effort` for local verification. The crate disables `equix`'s default `compiler` feature on purpose — the JIT solver doesn't run on `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and the interpreter is portable across host / iOS / Android / browser. `cargo test -p lakko-pow`: **7 passed** (round-trip vs `equix::verify_bytes`, effort-gate property, independent nonces, `BudgetExhausted`, leading-zero-bits unit). Wasm-bindgen exposure under `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/pow_solver.rs` (`PowSolver.solve(seedB64, effortBits)` static + `PowProof { seedB64, nonceB64, solutionB64 }`). `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm`: 33 passed (was 32; +1 host happy-path test). Generated `.d.ts` confirms the new classes. Bundle after `npm run build` (release): WASM 622 kB gz (+8 kB gz vs 1.16c2 for equix + hashx in release+LTO), total ≈ **645 kB gz** — still well inside the < 2 MB Phase 1.16f target. Solver is **blocking** by design; Web Worker plumbing is part of 1.16c3b. Server-side `services/auth` keeps using `equix` directly with the `compiler` feature for its 5–10× verify speedup — **no churn on the server in this sprint**.
- **1.16c3b** ✅ Browser HTTP transport for `lakko-svc-auth` — fetch-based `AuthClient` (TS-side, `apps/web/src/auth/client.ts`) with one method per endpoint (`/pow/challenge`, `/signup/start`, `/signup/finish`, `/login/start`, `/login/finish`, `/recovery/login/start`, `/recovery/login/finish`) plus high-level `signup(passwordBytes)` / `login(accountIdBytes, passwordBytes)` orchestrators that drive `OpaqueRegister` / `OpaqueLogin` (1.16c2) + the PoW solver (1.16c3a). Vite dev proxy `/api/auth/*` → `127.0.0.1:8081` so the same relative base URL works in dev and behind nginx in prod (no CORS layer on the auth service). UUIDv7 `account_id` generation in `apps/web/src/auth/account-id.ts` (RFC 9562 §5.7, BigInt math, no JS dep). TS-side wire types in `wire.ts` mirror the server JSON 1:1. The 1.16b stubs in `AuthContext.tsx` are replaced with real round-trips; `(accountId, username)` cached as plaintext in `localStorage` (the username is just a UI label until 1.16d wires `lakko-svc-discovery`; the export key + session key live in memory only — WebCrypto-backed persistence lands in 1.16c3c). Single-device limitation by design: until 1.16d, login is only available for the username that signed up on this device. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **17.06 kB gz** (+1.54 kB vs 1.16b for AuthClient + UUIDv7 + AuthContext rewrite), WASM unchanged at 622 kB gz, total ≈ 647 kB gz. Errors split into typed classes (`AuthClientArgError`, `AuthHttpError`, `AuthNetworkError`) so the UI can distinguish "wrong password" (HTTP 401), "service unreachable" (network), and "form input bad" (precondition). **Web Worker for the PoW solve is deferred** — at the server-default `effort_bits = 8` solving is 100–500 ms inside an already-blocking signup/login flow that shows a "Signing in…" spinner; the main-thread cost is acceptable. Worker plumbing lands as its own follow-up once a real difficulty knob is set in production.
- **1.16c3c1** ✅ BIP39 recovery passphrase + parallel OPAQUE recovery enrollment at signup. New crate `crates/lakko-mnemonic` — wraps `bip39 = "2"` with default features off + `std` + `rand`, fixes the shape at **24 English words** (256 bits of entropy). Public API: `Mnemonic::generate()`, `Mnemonic::from_phrase(phrase)`, `phrase()` / `words()` / `to_seed()` (the last returns a `SecretSeed` zeroizing on drop, derived via BIP39 PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 with an **empty** passphrase — recovery must work with the words alone). Errors collapse "unknown word" and "bad checksum" into a single `InvalidPhrase` variant to preserve BIP39's no-probing property. `cargo test -p lakko-mnemonic`: **12 passed** (generate + 24-word property, generate randomness, parse round-trip on a known vector, whitespace normalisation, short-input rejection, unknown-word rejection, checksum-failure rejection, seed determinism + length, secret-debug elision). `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-mnemonic` clean. Wasm-bindgen exposure under `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/mnemonic.rs` — `RecoveryMnemonic` with static `generate()` / `fromPhrase(phrase)` factories and `phrase` / `words` / `toSeed()` accessors. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm`: 36 passed (was 33; +3 new). `apps/web/src/auth/client.ts::AuthClient.signup` extended to optionally enrol a parallel OPAQUE recovery envelope (default on): generates the mnemonic via `RecoveryMnemonic.generate()`, runs `OpaqueRegister.startRecovery(seed)` in parallel with the password flow, sends both `password_request` + `recovery_request` in `/signup/start`, both `password_upload` + `recovery_upload` in `/signup/finish` (server commits both atomically). The phrase surfaces in the returned `SignupResult.recoveryPhrase`. `AuthContext.signup` now returns `SignupOutcome { recoveryPhrase }`; `Signup.tsx` is a two-stage form (collect credentials → display the 24 words once + require explicit "I have written it down" checkbox before navigating home). Phrase + seed live in memory only and drop the moment the user hits "Done" — server-side ciphertext can never reveal them. Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **711 kB gzipped** (+89 kB vs 1.16c3a for `bip39` + `bitcoin_hashes` + `unicode-normalization` + `tinyvec` + `hex-conservative` in release+LTO), app JS 18.11 kB gz (+1.05 kB for the new phrase panel + signup recovery wiring), total ≈ **736 kB gz** — still well inside the < 2 MB Phase 1.16f target.
- **1.16c3c2** ✅ Session persistence via WebCrypto + IndexedDB. New helper `apps/web/src/auth/device-key.ts::deriveDeviceRootKey(exportKey, accountIdB64)` imports the OPAQUE password export key as an HKDF base, then derives a **non-extractable AES-256-GCM `CryptoKey`** (`extractable: false`) using HKDF-SHA-512 with `salt = "lakko-device-root-v1"` and `info = "lakko/device-root/v1|" || accountIdB64`. The derived key is the seed for any encrypted local stores 1.16f wires up; the OPAQUE export-key bytes never persist. New `apps/web/src/auth/key-store.ts` exposes typed `putDeviceKey` / `getDeviceKey` / `deleteDeviceKey` against an IndexedDB schema (`db "lakko-auth" v1`, store `device_keys` keyed by `accountId`). Non-extractable CryptoKeys survive the IDB structured-clone round-trip in all evergreen browsers since 2017 — only the WebCrypto handle is persisted, the bytes stay inside the keystore. `AuthContext`: signup + login derive and store the key right after the OPAQUE math; `AuthProvider.onMount` awaits a hydration helper that returns `{ kind: "session" }` when both the localStorage marker AND the IDB key exist for the cached account, falling back to `{ kind: "anon", cachedUsername }` otherwise. `logout()` keeps both layers intact (re-login on the same device stays cheap, the device key remains usable for encrypted stores). `forgetDevice()` is now async and wipes both layers — IDB first, then localStorage, so an interrupted run leaves the device in a recoverable state. `AppShell` gains a "Forget this device" button next to "Log out" guarded by a `confirm()` prompt that names the recovery passphrase as the only way back. Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM unchanged at 711 kB gz, app JS **18.87 kB gz** (+0.76 kB vs 1.16c3c1 for `device-key.ts` + `key-store.ts` + AuthContext extensions + AppShell forget button), total ≈ 737 kB gz.
- **1.16c3c3a** ✅ Server-side password reset — `lakko-svc-auth` adds `POST /password/reset/{start,finish}`. The flow proves recovery ownership and rotates the password envelope in two HTTP round-trips: `/start` decodes a recovery `CredentialRequest` + a new-password `RegistrationRequest`, runs `ServerLogin::start` with `KIND_RECOVERY` credential_id and `ServerRegistration::start` with `KIND_PASSWORD` credential_id, persists the recovery `ServerLogin` state in `opaque_login_states` under a new `KIND_PASSWORD_RESET = 3` row tag (so neither `/login/finish` nor `/recovery/login/finish` can consume it — confused-deputy guard), and returns `{session_id, recovery_response, new_password_response}`. `/finish` takes the matching session_id, verifies the recovery `CredentialFinalization` (this is the moment ownership is actually proven), builds the new envelope from the password upload, and **REPLACES** `opaque_registrations(account_id, kind=KIND_PASSWORD)` via MariaDB `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` (the composite PK `(account_id, kind)` already exists from migration 0004 — no schema change needed). The recovery envelope is untouched, so the same mnemonic continues to work for future resets. PoW gates `/start` only (matches the rest of the expensive endpoints). Wrong-seed attempts fail at the AKE finalization with the same indistinguishable-failure response as `/recovery/login/finish` — by design. Tests: 2 new in-memory OPAQUE round-trip tests (`password_reset_replaces_password_envelope`, `password_reset_with_wrong_seed_fails_at_finish`); `cargo test -p lakko-svc-auth` reports **19 passed** (was 17). Workspace clippy + fmt clean. Client-side `AuthClient.recover` + `/recover` UI route is **1.16c3c3b**.
- **1.16c3c3b** ✅ Recover-from-mnemonic UI flow — `apps/web/src/auth/client.ts::AuthClient.recover(accountId, mnemonicSeed, newPassword)` orchestrates the new endpoints in a single PoW + OPAQUE round-trip pair: solves PoW once, drives `OpaqueLogin.startRecovery(seed)` and `OpaqueRegister.startPassword(newPassword)` in parallel, posts both to `/password/reset/start`, finishes both OPAQUE flows on the response, and posts `recovery_finalize + new_password_upload` to `/password/reset/finish`. Returns the new password's `exportKey` for fresh device-key derivation. `AuthContext.recover(mnemonicPhrase, newPassword)` parses the typed phrase via `RecoveryMnemonic.fromPhrase` (collapses unknown-word and bad-checksum into one error — preserves BIP39's no-probing property), drives the client orchestrator, derives a brand-new device root key via `deriveDeviceRootKey(newExportKey, accountIdB64)` and upserts it into IDB (the previous device key, if any, is no longer reachable from the rotated password — the upsert drops it). The seed buffer is zeroed in a `finally` block. New `apps/web/src/routes/Recover.tsx` route at `/recover`: 24-word textarea (forgiving on whitespace, `autocomplete="off"` + `spellcheck={false}` to discourage browser autofill / dictionary suggestions for the mnemonic) + new password + confirm. A "Lost your password?" link on the Login form points here. The route surfaces a clear "no cached account on this device" panel when the user lands without local state — until 1.16d brings discovery, recovery is single-device only, same as login. Wire types for `/password/reset/{start,finish}` mirror the server JSON 1:1 in `wire.ts`. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **19.86 kB gz** (+0.99 kB vs 1.16c3c2 for the new orchestrator + recover route + AuthContext extension), WASM unchanged at 711 kB gz, total ≈ 738 kB gz. Single-device limitation lifts in 1.16d when `username → account_id` discovery lands.
- **1.16d1** ✅ `crates/lakko-discovery-client` — VOPRF Ristretto255-SHA512 client mirror of `services/discovery/src/oprf.rs`. Public API: `OprfBlinder::blind(username) -> (Self, blinded_b64)` normalises the typed input and produces the base64 blinded element for `/discovery/oprf`'s request body; `blinder.finalize(evaluated_b64) -> [u8; 64]` consumes the server's evaluation and returns the OPRF hash that goes into `/discovery/{register, lookup}` as `oprf_hash`. The blinder caches its normalised username so callers cannot accidentally hand a different string to `finalize` (a class of bug that produces silent wrong-hash output). Pinned cipher suite `LakkoOprfCs = voprf::Ristretto255` with `voprf` feature `ristretto255-ciphersuite` (matches the server). Username-normalisation helper `normalize_username()` is the single source of truth for canonical form: trim → strip leading `@` → trim again → NFKC → lowercase. Errors collapse into `DiscoveryClientError::{Base64, InvalidWire, Voprf, EmptyUsername}`. `cargo test -p lakko-discovery-client`: **15 passed** (round-trip vs `voprf::OprfServer`, determinism property — same input → same hash, different-input → different-hash, server-key binding, 6 normalisation cases including a real ordering bug the test caught and the fix preserves: `"\t@alice\n"` → `"alice"` not `"@alice"`). `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-discovery-client` clean. Wasm-bindgen exposure under `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/discovery.rs`: `OprfBlinder.blind(username)` static factory + `blindedB64` / `normalizedUsername` getters + single-use `finalize(evaluatedB64)` returning a 64-byte `Uint8Array`; standalone `normalizeUsername(input)` helper for UI confirmation. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm`: 39 passed (was 36; +3 new).
