Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **732 kB gz** (+21 kB vs 1.16c3c3b for `voprf` + transitively `unicode-normalization` in release+LTO), total ≈ 760 kB gz — still well inside the < 2 MB Phase 1.16f target. **Does NOT speak HTTP**: the JS layer (1.16d2) builds the JSON request bodies, attaches the PoW proof from `lakko-pow`, and posts via `fetch`. **Does NOT solve PoW**: that's `lakko-pow` (1.16c3a). **Server side untouched**: the discovery service already exposes `/discovery/{oprf, register, lookup}` from earlier phases.
- **1.16d2** ✅ Browser HTTP transport for `lakko-svc-discovery` — TS-side `DiscoveryClient` (`apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts`) with low-level RPCs for `/discovery/oprf`, `/discovery/register`, `/discovery/lookup` plus high-level `registerUsername(accountId, username) -> { normalisedUsername }` and `lookup(username) -> { normalisedUsername, account: Uint8Array \| null }` orchestrators. Each high-level call drives two PoW solves (one for `/discovery/oprf`, one for the register/lookup RPC) and runs the OPRF blind/finalize through the WASM `OprfBlinder`. The 404 path of `/discovery/lookup` surfaces as `account: null` (typed not-found) rather than an exception — the UI consumes a discriminated value instead of catching. Errors split into typed classes (`DiscoveryClientArgError` / `DiscoveryHttpError` / `DiscoveryNetworkError`) so the UI can branch on 409 (username taken) vs 404 (unknown) vs network failure. Vite dev proxy `/api/discovery/*` → `127.0.0.1:8084` (the discovery service's default bind), same routing pattern as `/api/auth/*`. **AuthContext rewired:** signup claims the username server-side after the OPAQUE flow commits, surfacing 409 as a friendlier "@handle already taken — pick another" message; the canonical normalised form (post NFKC + lowercase + trim) is what gets persisted in localStorage and shown in the UI. Login no longer hard-requires a local cache: when the typed username matches the cached one we skip the lookup round-trip; otherwise we fall back to `discovery.lookup`. Unknown username surfaces as `"invalid credentials"` — same string as wrong password, indistinguishable by design (no username-space probing). After a successful login the cache is updated, so subsequent reloads on this device skip the lookup. Wire types in `apps/web/src/discovery/wire.ts` mirror server JSON 1:1. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **20.37 kB gz** (+0.51 kB vs 1.16d1 for DiscoveryClient + AuthContext rewire), WASM unchanged at 732 kB gz, total ≈ 760 kB gz. Smoke test confirms both Vite proxies route correctly: `/api/discovery/*` → port 8084, `/api/auth/*` → port 8081. **Recover-from-mnemonic still single-device** — extending it to look up by username is small but tracked separately as a 1.16d3-adjacent follow-up. **Server-side discovery untouched** — endpoints already exist from earlier phases, so this sprint is purely client-side.
- **1.16d3a** ✅ Server-side identity-bundle directory in `lakko-svc-discovery`. Sending a `ContactRequest` needs the recipient's Ed25519 identity pubkey + X25519 (HPKE init_key) pubkey — both public, no secret to leak. New migration `0002_identity_bundles.sql` creates a single table keyed by `account_id` (UUIDv7 raw 16 bytes) holding both pubkeys as `BINARY(32)` columns + `created_at` / `updated_at`. New module `services/discovery/src/identity_bundle.rs` exposes two endpoints: `POST /discovery/identity` (PoW-gated upsert, claim-trusting per the same Phase-1 trust gap as `/discovery/register` — Phase 1.5b will gate on auth-issued session tokens; UPSERT via MariaDB `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` so key rotation works atomically), `GET /discovery/identity/{account_id_b64url}` (public, no auth — identity pubkeys are public by definition; 404 on no row; the path uses **base64url-without-padding** because axum rejects `/` and `+` in path segments). Tests: 12 passed (was 10; +2 for `decode_fixed_validates_lengths` and `fetch_path_validates_base64url`). Workspace clippy + fmt clean. **Server-only sprint** — client-side upload at signup + WASM Identity X25519 exposure is **1.16d3b**.
- **1.16d3b** ✅ X25519 envelope keypair on `lakko-mls::Identity` + WASM exposure. `Identity::generate*()` now generates a long-lived X25519 keypair (`lakko-crypto::agreement::SecretKey`) alongside the Ed25519 signing keypair. New accessors `x25519_public_key() -> [u8; 32]` (the HPKE init_key callers of `lakko-contacts::compose` target) and `x25519_secret_bytes() -> [u8; 32]` (input to `ContactRequest::open_envelope` for incoming envelope decryption). Distinct from the per-MLS-group DHKEM keys OpenMLS manages internally — those rotate on every commit; the Identity-level X25519 has a much longer lifetime and is what the discovery service's `identity_bundles` table stores (1.16d3a). New `lakko-crypto` dep on `lakko-mls`. Wasm-bindgen surface: `Identity.x25519PublicKey` / `Identity.x25519SecretKey` getters in `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/mls.rs` (the secret getter has a "wrap with the device root key before persisting" docstring warning). Tests: `cargo test -p lakko-mls` 10 passed (was 7; +3 new — `x25519_public_key_is_32_bytes`, `x25519_secret_bytes_round_trip_to_public`, `x25519_secret_accessor_is_idempotent`); `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 40 passed (was 39; +1 new). Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **732.20 kB gz** (basically unchanged from 1.16d2's 732.12 — just two getters). Workspace clippy + fmt clean. **Persistence-across-reloads NOT addressed yet** — every page load currently constructs a fresh Identity with random keys, which means the bundle uploaded at signup goes stale on the next reload. The actual upload-at-signup wiring + Identity persistence (likely deterministic derivation from the OPAQUE export key via HKDF, so `OpaqueLogin.finish` reproduces the same Identity bytes) is **1.16d3b2**.
- **1.16d3b2** ✅ Identity persistence-by-derivation + signup/login bundle upload. New `lakko-mls::Identity::from_seed(seed: &[u8])` constructor that HKDF-SHA-256-expands the seed into two 32-byte sub-secrets (`info = "lakko/identity/v1/ed25519"` and `"lakko/identity/v1/x25519"`, fixed `salt = "lakko-identity-v1"`), then constructs the Ed25519 keypair via `lakko-crypto::signing::SigningKey::from_seed` + `OpenMLS::SignatureKeyPair::from_raw` and the X25519 keypair via `agreement::SecretKey::from_bytes`. Deterministic — same seed ⇒ byte-identical Ed25519 + X25519 public keys. `cargo test -p lakko-mls`: **14 passed** (was 10; +4 new — `from_seed_is_deterministic`, `from_seed_distinct_seeds_yield_distinct_keys`, `from_seed_ed25519_and_x25519_are_domain_separated`, `from_seed_accepts_arbitrary_length_ikm`). Wasm-bindgen exposure: `Identity.fromSeed(seedBytes)` static factory. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 41 passed (was 40; +1 determinism test). TS-side: `DiscoveryClient.identityUpload` low-level RPC, `DiscoveryClient.identityFetch(accountId) -> { signature_pubkey, x25519_pubkey } | null` (404 surfaces as `null` like `lookup`), high-level `DiscoveryClient.uploadIdentityBundle(accountId, signaturePubkey, x25519Pubkey)` orchestrator (one PoW solve + POST). New helper `apps/web/src/auth/account-id.ts::base64UrlEncodeNoPad` for the GET path's URL-safe alphabet. `AuthContext`: signup and login both derive Identity from the OPAQUE password export key and upload the bundle. **Persistence-by-derivation**: no Identity bytes ever persist — every reload that has password access reconstructs the same Identity from the same export key. Signup uploads as a hard precondition (failure aborts signup); login uploads best-effort (a network blip on a refresh shouldn't lock the user out of an already-authenticated session — the next login will retry naturally, and the upsert is idempotent). Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **734.25 kB gz** (+2 kB vs 1.16d3b for the HKDF + deterministic-derivation code path), app JS **20.66 kB gz** (+0.29 kB for `uploadIdentityBundle` + `identityFetch` + `base64UrlEncodeNoPad`), total ≈ 762 kB gz. Workspace clippy + fmt clean. **A page reload without password access still has no Identity** — restoring a session via the IDB device root key gives a logged-in UI but no Identity in scope. Composing or decoding ContactRequests therefore requires the user to have just authenticated; future sub-sprint will either persist the Identity bytes encrypted by the device root key OR add a "re-enter password to send a contact request" pattern.
- **1.16d3c** ✅ TS `ContactsClient.sendContactRequest` end-to-end. New crate-level primitives: `Identity::ed25519_seed_bytes()` (Rust) + `Identity.ed25519Seed` (WASM) — exposes the 32-byte signing seed every `ContactRequest::compose` caller needs. Both `generate*()` and `from_seed()` paths now build the OpenMLS `SignatureKeyPair` via `from_raw(seed, pub)` and stash the seed on a new `Identity::ed25519_seed: [u8; 32]` field, sidestepping OpenMLS's `test-utils`-gated `private()` accessor. New `apps/web/src/auth/AuthContext.tsx::IdentitySecrets` — in-memory `(accountId, exportKey)` signal, populated on signup / login / recover, dropped on logout / forgetDevice, **null after a session restore from IDB** (the export key isn't persisted). Operations that need to sign on behalf of the user (`ContactsClient.sendContactRequest`, future MLS commits) read `useAuth().identitySecrets()` and require it to be non-null. New `apps/web/src/ds/client.ts::DsClient` — fetch-based wrapper for `/ds/mailbox/{pseudonym_hex}/messages` (POST publish + GET fetch with cursor); body is `application/octet-stream`, payload bytes get base64 round-tripped only on the GET path because that's how the server's JSON encodes binary. Includes `pseudonymHex(bytes)` lowercase-hex helper. New `apps/web/src/contacts/client.ts::ContactsClient` orchestrates the full send flow: `lookup(@username)` → `identityFetch(accountId)` → `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity(recipient_pubkey)` for the mailbox address → `Identity.fromSeed(exportKey).ed25519Seed` for the signing seed → `ContactRequest.compose(...)` → `DsClient.publishMailbox(...)`. Errors funnel through a typed `ContactsError` whose `variant.kind` is `"no-identity-secrets"` / `"username-unknown"` / `"no-identity-bundle"` / `"wrapped"` so the UI can branch (e.g. surface "please re-enter your password" specifically when there's no exportKey in memory). New Vite dev proxy `/api/ds/*` → `127.0.0.1:8082`. Tests: `cargo test -p lakko-mls` 15 passed (was 14; +1 `ed25519_seed_round_trips_to_public_for_seed_derived_identity`); workspace clippy + fmt clean. Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM 734.01 kB gz (basically unchanged), app JS **20.71 kB gz** (+0.05 kB for DsClient + ContactsClient + AuthContext signal — almost free), total ≈ 762 kB gz. **Send-only** — receive / accept / decline UI + the `/contacts` route itself are **1.16d3d**.
- **1.16d3d** ✅ ContactRequest receive + accept/decline + `/contacts` UI. New `ContactsClient.pollMailbox(identity, after, waitSecs?)` fetches the user's mailbox via `DsClient.fetchMailbox`, runs `ContactRequest.decodeAndVerify` on every payload (drops malformed entries with `console.warn`, advances the cursor regardless), and tries `openEnvelope` against the recipient's X25519 secret to decode the intro string (UTF-8, non-fatal). Returns `{ requests: IncomingContactRequest[], cursor, hasMore }`. New `apps/web/src/contacts/local-store.ts` persists two non-secret pieces in `localStorage` (`lakko.contacts.v1`): the highest-seen DS message id (cursor — lets the next poll skip the backlog) and the accepted-contacts list (`{ signaturePubkeyB64, displayName, acceptedAtMs }`). Pending requests are intentionally **NOT** persisted — intro plaintext stays out of localStorage; the source of truth is the DS mailbox replayed from cursor 0. New `Contacts.tsx` route replaces the placeholder with three sections: **Search & send** (drives 1.16d3c's `sendContactRequest`), **Pending** (manual Refresh button → poll → Accept/Decline), **Accepted** (local address book). When `useAuth().identitySecrets()` is null (session restored from IDB without password re-entry), the route shows a "please re-enter your password" panel up front rather than letting flows fail at the WASM boundary. `AuthContext.forgetDevice` now also calls `clearContactsStore()` — wiping the device should leave nothing per-account behind. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **24.57 kB gz** (+3.86 kB vs 1.16d3c for the full Contacts UI + local-store + pollMailbox), WASM unchanged at 734 kB gz, total ≈ 766 kB gz. Workspace clippy + fmt clean. **What's NOT here:** ContactAck back-channel (sender doesn't learn that the recipient accepted), per-relationship pseudonym rotation (we still use `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity` for both sides), and the cross-device address-book sync that would need 1.13c IndexedDB — accepted contacts live only on the device that accepted them. Those are post-1.16 polish.
- **1.16e1** ✅ Server-side public MLS KeyPackage directory in `lakko-svc-discovery`. Sending an MLS Welcome to start a 1×1 group needs the recipient's `init_key`, which lives inside their TLS-encoded KeyPackage (RFC 9420 §10). New migration `0003_keypackages.sql` adds a `keypackages` table keyed by `account_id` with a `VARBINARY(2048)` column for the KeyPackage bytes (~3-4× headroom over the ~500-byte default-suite size, room for FIPS / X.509 in later phases). New module `services/discovery/src/keypackage.rs` exposes `POST /discovery/keypackage` (PoW-gated upsert via MariaDB `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE`, hard ceiling at `MAX_KEYPACKAGE_BYTES = 2048` matching the column width — empty + oversize bodies surface as `400` and `413` respectively) and `GET /discovery/keypackage/{account_id_b64url}` (no auth, `404` when no row, base64url-without-padding path segment same as `/discovery/identity/`). Body bytes are opaque to the server — verification + freshness checks are the recipient's job. Tests: 14 passed (was 12; +2 — `decode_account_id_validates_length` and `max_keypackage_bytes_matches_column_width` which uses `include_str!` on the migration so a future `VARBINARY` width drift fails the test rather than silently 500-ing). **Phase-1 simplifications documented**: one row per account (replaceable on every login, no consume-on-use; forward-secrecy regression on new groups acceptable for MVP), no TTL pruner, same trust gap as `/discovery/register` and `/discovery/identity`. Workspace clippy + fmt clean. Server-only sprint — client upload + use is **1.16e2 / 1.16e3**.
- **1.16e2** ✅ Client-side KeyPackage upload at signup/login. `DiscoveryClient` gains low-level `keypackageUpload` / `keypackageFetch` 1:1 with the new server endpoints, plus high-level `uploadKeyPackage(accountId, keyPackageBytes)` (one PoW solve + POST, with a 2048-byte client-side ceiling that mirrors the server's `MAX_KEYPACKAGE_BYTES`) and `fetchKeyPackage(accountId) -> Uint8Array \| null` (decodes the base64 wrapper for callers that hand the bytes straight to `Group.addMember`). Wire types in `discovery/wire.ts` mirror the server JSON 1:1. `AuthContext`: the existing `uploadIdentityBundle` helper (used at signup + login) now also generates a fresh KeyPackage via `Identity.createKeyPackage()` and pushes it in **parallel** with the identity bundle via `Promise.all` — both upserts live in different tables, neither depends on the other, and both are required for a usable account so first-failure rejects the call. KeyPackage bytes are random per `createKeyPackage()` call (forward secrecy on new groups), so each login refreshes the server-side `init_key`. **Phase-1 simplification documented in the orchestrator docstring**: the directory stores ONE KeyPackage per account at a time — a sender racing two sessions can in theory seed two groups with the same `init_key`. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **24.74 kB gz** (+0.17 kB vs 1.16d3d for 4 new DiscoveryClient methods + the parallel upload wiring), WASM unchanged at 734 kB gz, total ≈ 766 kB gz. Workspace clippy + fmt clean.
- **1.16e3a** ✅ 1×1 MLS group creation + Welcome distribution end-to-end via DS mailbox. New `apps/web/src/contacts/envelope.ts` introduces a 1-byte kind discriminator on every mailbox payload (`KIND_CONTACT_REQUEST = 0x01`, `KIND_WELCOME = 0x02`) so receivers dispatch to the right decoder; `wrapEnvelope` / `parseEnvelope` pure helpers, no length prefix or MAC (inner formats already authenticate themselves). `ContactsClient` extended with: `sendWelcome(recipientSignaturePubkey, welcomeBytes)` (POST kind=Welcome envelope to `IdentityPseudonym.fromIdentity(...)` mailbox), `startGroupWith(identity, recipientAccountId, recipientSignaturePubkey)` (fetch KeyPackage → `new Group(identity)` → `addMember(identity, kp)` → drop the commit, ship the welcome via `sendWelcome`, return the live `Group`), `joinGroupFromWelcome(identity, welcomeBytes)` (`Group.joinFromWelcome(welcome, identity)`), and a `startGroupWithUsername(identity, username)` convenience wrapper that does the discovery lookup + identity-bundle fetch first. `pollMailbox` now returns a discriminated `IncomingMailboxEvent[]` (`{kind: "contact-request", request} | {kind: "welcome", welcome}`); `Contacts.tsx` dispatches on the kind — incoming Welcomes auto-join via `joinGroupFromWelcome` and surface in a new "Active MLS groups" panel showing member count + own leaf index. New "Start MLS group" button next to "Send contact request" in the Search panel — explicit sender-driven group creation. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **25.81 kB gz** (+1.07 kB vs 1.16e2 for envelope + 4 new ContactsClient methods + active-groups panel), WASM unchanged at 734 kB gz, total ≈ 767 kB gz. **What's NOT here (1.16e3b):** the natural Accept-driven flow where Bob accepts Alice's `ContactRequest` and that act spins up the group. Blocked by a missing `signature_pubkey → account_id` reverse mapping — incoming `ContactRequest.fromPubkey` carries Alice's Ed25519 pubkey but not her account_id, so Bob can't `fetchKeyPackage` from accept alone. Either extend the `ContactRequest` wire format with a `from_account_id` field (Rust crate change in `lakko-contacts`) or add a server-side reverse lookup endpoint on `lakko-svc-discovery`. Until then the "Start MLS group" button gives a fully-working sender-driven path.
- **1.16e3b** ✅ Accept-driven group creation — wire-format extension on `ContactRequest`. Bumped the manual codec from v1 to v2 with a new `[16]` `from_account_id` field inserted between `from_pubkey` and `from_pseudonym`. The signature now covers it, so a flipped account_id breaks verification (test `tampered_pseudonym_rejected` updated for the new offset). `decode_and_verify` accepts BOTH v1 (legacy `from_account_id: None`) and v2 (`Some([u8; 16])`) for backwards-compat with envelopes in flight at deployment-upgrade time; `compose` always emits v2. New `lakko-contacts::ACCOUNT_ID_LEN = 16` re-exported. WASM surface: `ContactRequest.compose` gains `senderAccountId` parameter + new `ContactRequest.fromAccountId` getter (`Uint8Array | undefined`). TS-side: `ContactsClient.sendContactRequest` passes `identity.accountId`; `IncomingContactRequest.fromAccountId: Uint8Array | null` surfaces in poll output (normalised from undefined). `Contacts.tsx::onAccept` is now async and **actually creates the group** when the request is v2: builds local `accepted` entry first (so a network blip doesn't undo the trust gesture), then calls `client.startGroupWith(identity, fromAccountId, fromPubkey)` and adds the resulting `Group` to active groups. Legacy v1 envelopes surface a clean "ask sender to upgrade" message instead of breaking. Tests: `cargo test -p lakko-contacts` 28 passed (was 26; +2 — `v2_wire_carries_account_id_at_fixed_offset` pins the byte layout, `v1_envelopes_still_decode` exercises the legacy path with a hand-built v1 wire). `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 41 passed (existing `contact_request_roundtrip_happy_path` extended to assert `fromAccountId`). Workspace clippy + fmt clean. Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **25.93 kB gz** (+0.12 kB vs 1.16e3a for the new accept-driven flow + v2 wire), WASM 734 kB gz (basically unchanged), total ≈ 767 kB gz.
- **1.16e4** ✅ Send / receive Application messages end-to-end. New `lakko-mls::Group::group_id() -> Vec<u8>` accessor (RFC 9420 §6.2 — opaque routing key for the DS group journal) + WASM `Group.groupId` getter. Extended `DsClient` with `publishGroup(groupId, payload)` and `fetchGroup(groupId, options)` mirror methods that hit `/ds/group/{group_id_hex}/messages` (the DS endpoint shape was already there from earlier work). New `apps/web/src/chat/client.ts::ChatClient` orchestrates application-message round-trips: `sendMessage(identity, group, plaintext)` does `Group.createApplicationMessage` → `DsClient.publishGroup`, returns the assigned id, and remembers it in `ownPublishedIds` so the next poll drops the echo (the DS doesn't dedupe + OpenMLS can't decrypt the sender's own application messages at the same epoch). `pollMessages(identity, group, after, waitSecs?)` runs `Group.processIncoming` on each row and returns a discriminated `IncomingChatEvent[]` (`{kind: "application", plaintext, ...}` for application data; `{kind: "system", systemKind: "commit"|"proposal"|"external-join-proposal"}` for everything else; malformed frames log-and-skip with the cursor still advancing). Inline chat pane in `Contacts.tsx::ActiveGroupsPanel`: each active group has an "Open chat" button that expands a `<ChatPane>` with a Refresh button, scrollable timeline (max-height 16rem), and message input. Optimistic local render on send (timeline shows the bubble immediately + dedupes by message id when refresh sees it). Tests: `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 41 passed; `cargo test -p lakko-mls` 15 passed; workspace clippy + fmt clean. Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **734.68 kB gz** (+0.7 kB for the new `Group.groupId` getter + DS group routes), app JS **27.28 kB gz** (+1.35 kB vs 1.16e3b for ChatClient + ChatPane + ActiveGroupRow expansion), total ≈ 769 kB gz. **MLS state is still in-memory** — page reload kills active groups; that's the 1.16e6 sub-sprint.
- **1.16e5** ✅ `/chat/:id` route — full chat view, lifted active-groups state into a global provider. New `apps/web/src/chat/ChatContext.tsx::ChatProvider` is a parallel-to-`AuthProvider` registry — `Map<groupIdB64, ActiveGroup>` accessible via `useChats()`, with `add` / `get` / `remove` / `clear`. `App.tsx` now wraps in `<AuthProvider><ChatProvider>` so any route can resolve a `:id` URL slug to a live `WasmGroup`. `AuthProvider.logout` and `forgetDevice` call `chats.clear()` so MLS state never bleeds across user sessions on the same tab. `paths.chat(groupIdB64Url)` builds `/chat/{slug}` URLs; the slug is `base64UrlEncodeNoPad(group.groupId)` (already a URL-safe alphabet so axum-style path-segment rejection never triggers). `Contacts.tsx` is rewired: `chats.add({label, group})` replaces the old local `activeGroups` signal; the inline `<ChatPane>` is gone — the active-groups list now renders an "Open chat" link styled as a primary button that navigates to the route. **`Chat.tsx`** replaces the 1.16b placeholder with a full chat view: full-page header (group label + member count + own leaf index + Refresh + Back), top-down timeline that grows with the viewport, optimistic outbound bubbles with **ack indicators** (`pending` → `sent` once the DS has assigned a message id), peer bubbles vs. own bubbles styled distinctly. The route gates twice: surface a "re-enter password" panel when `useAuth().identitySecrets()` is null (session restored from IDB without password), surface a "group not found" panel when the URL slug doesn't resolve in the in-memory registry (typical case: opened by URL after a reload, since Phase-1 doesn't persist MLS state). Smoke: `GET /chat/abc -> 200` (the route renders the not-found panel, as expected for a slug that doesn't decode to a registered group). Bundle after `npm run build`: app JS **27.92 kB gz** (+0.64 kB vs 1.16e4 for the full Chat view + ChatProvider + ack-indicator wiring), WASM unchanged at 734.68 kB gz, total ≈ 770 kB gz. Workspace clippy + fmt clean. **Deferred follow-ups**: per-draft `EditChain` LWW (the `lakko-sync` surface is already there but the composer is a plain `<input>` for now), cross-peer "delivered" / "read" markers (need a Read-Ack ladder primitive), and MLS state persistence across reload (= 1.16e6).
- **1.16e6a** ✅ Rust core for MLS state persistence — `Identity::snapshot_storage() -> Vec<u8>` and `Identity::restore_storage(&[u8])` in `lakko-mls`. The OpenMLS provider's `MemoryStorage.values: RwLock<HashMap<Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>>>` field turned out to be public unconditionally — no fork / custom `StorageProvider` impl needed. Snapshot is a deterministic length-prefixed binary encoding (`u32be count` then `(u32be klen, key, u32be vlen, value)*`), opaque to the caller; round-tripping through `restore_storage` is the only supported operation. New `Group::load(identity, group_id) -> Result<Option<Group>>` wraps `MlsGroup::load` against the restored provider — returns `Some` when the storage carries a row for that id, `None` when it doesn't (clear distinction between "no group" and "corrupt storage"), `Error::Group` only for OpenMLS rejection. Tests: 3 new integration tests in `crates/lakko-mls/tests/end_to_end.rs` (the suite was 1, now 4): `snapshot_and_restore_round_trips_a_live_group` exercises the full reload cycle (Alice creates group with Bob → snapshot → drop everything → fresh `Identity::from_seed(same_seed)` → `restore_storage` → `Group::load` → keep messaging Bob from the same epoch), `group_load_returns_none_when_no_state_was_persisted`, and `restore_storage_rejects_truncated_snapshot`. `cargo test -p lakko-mls` reports 15 unit + 4 integration = 19 passed; workspace clippy + fmt clean. **Rust-only sprint**, no Cargo / WASM / app changes — those are 1.16e6b (WASM exposure) and 1.16e6c (TS-side `IndexedDB` persistence wrapped under the device root key).
- **1.16e6b** ✅ WASM exposure of the Rust persistence helpers. Three new methods on the `lakko-ffi-wasm` MLS surface: `Identity.snapshotStorage()` returns the opaque snapshot bytes (`Result<Vec<u8>, JsError>`); `Identity.restoreStorage(snapshot)` replaces the provider's storage from a previous snapshot; `Group.load(identity, groupId)` is a static factory that returns `Some(Group)` when the storage carries a row for that id, `None` when it doesn't (typed not-found, the `Option<Group>` surfaces as `Group | undefined` in the generated `.d.ts`). Two new host-side tests in `mls::tests`: `snapshot_restore_load_round_trips_a_group` (mirrors the Rust integration test from 1.16e6a but goes through the WASM wrappers — confirms the pattern works through the JS surface) and `group_load_returns_none_for_unknown_id`. `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` reports **43 passed** (was 41, +2). Workspace clippy + fmt clean (after fixing 5 `clippy::doc_lazy_continuation` lints in a new test docstring). Bundle after `npm run build`: WASM **737.05 kB gz** (+2.4 kB vs 1.16e5 for the three new wrappers + lakko-mls helpers in release+LTO), app JS unchanged at 27.92 kB gz, total ≈ 772 kB gz.
- **1.16e6c1** ✅ TS-side persistence foundation — IDB snapshot store + canonical session-wide `WasmIdentity` in `ChatProvider`. New `apps/web/src/chat/mls-store.ts`: schema `db "lakko-mls" v1` / store `snapshots` keyed by `accountId`, one row per account `{ accountId, ciphertext, knownGroupIds }`. The ciphertext is `[12-byte IV | AES-256-GCM(plaintext snapshot, deviceKey)]` — wrapped under the non-extractable AES-256-GCM `CryptoKey` from 1.16c3c2 so the on-disk bytes can't be lifted by page-level JS. Public CRUD: `saveMlsSnapshot` / `loadMlsSnapshot` / `deleteMlsSnapshot` / `clearAllMlsSnapshots`. Why one blob per account rather than per group: OpenMLS's provider serialises **all** groups + signing keys + key-package state into a single `HashMap<Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>>` with no clean per-group split; we record the list of `groupIdB64`s separately so the rehydration path knows which `Group::load` calls to make. The list is base64 of public MLS `group_id` bytes (already shared with the DS as a routing address) so it's not encrypted — encrypting would buy nothing and cost code. **`ChatProvider` extension**: holds the canonical `WasmIdentity` for the session via `setIdentity(...)` / `identity()` accessors; `clear()` `free()`s it. New `persistSnapshot(accountIdB64)` method: reads the canonical identity, calls `snapshotStorage()`, looks up the device key from `key-store`, encrypts + writes via `mls-store`. **`AuthProvider` rewire**: every signup / login / recover path now (a) builds the canonical Identity once via `wasm.Identity.fromSeed(exportKey)` and hands it to `chats.setIdentity(...)`, (b) passes it to `uploadIdentityBundle(...)` so the `createKeyPackage()` call mints the init/leaf private keys *into* the provider we'll snapshot — pre-1.16e6c1 this used a throwaway `Identity` and any peer's Welcome targeting our published KeyPackage would have hit "init key not in keystore" on a reload, (c) calls `chats.persistSnapshot(accountIdB64)` best-effort right after the upload so the freshly-minted KeyPackage state survives the next reload. `forgetDevice` now also calls `deleteMlsSnapshot(cached.accountIdB64)` — deleting the device key already makes the snapshot unreadable, but leaving the row would surface stale ciphertext to a future signup on the same accountId. `recover()` deletes the snapshot pre-flight (the new export key would re-derive a different device root key, the old snapshot is unreadable anyway). **`App.tsx` provider order flipped**: `<ChatProvider><AuthProvider><Router>...</Router></AuthProvider></ChatProvider>` — `AuthProvider`'s body synchronously calls `useChats()`, so the chat context has to be in scope when `AuthProvider` mounts. The previous order was a latent bug from 1.16e5 that would have thrown on first mount as soon as `AuthProvider` evaluated `useChats()`. `npm run typecheck` clean; workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `test` clean (15 + 4 + 43 + … all green). **No rehydration on mount yet** — that's 1.16e6c2 (auto-persist hooks at every state mutation: `sendMessage`, `pollMessages`, `addMember`, `joinFromWelcome`) and 1.16e6c3 (mount-side `loadMlsSnapshot` → `restoreStorage` → `Group.load` for each `knownGroupIds`).
- **1.16e6c2** ✅ Auto-persist hooks at every MLS state-mutation point + canonical-Identity injection across the client classes. **Architectural fix.** Pre-1.16e6c2, `ChatClient` and `ContactsClient` each called `wasm.Identity.fromSeed(secrets.exportKey)` per method invocation — same Ed25519 + X25519 keys (deterministic), but a **fresh OpenMLS provider every time**. Every `Group::create_application_message` / `process_incoming` / `add_member` / `join_from_welcome` call mutated *that* throwaway provider's storage and dropped the bytes when the function returned. Even with 1.16e6c1's `persistSnapshot` plumbing in place, the snapshot would have captured the canonical identity's *empty* provider — fully decoupled from the storage the Group ops actually wrote to. Welcome decryption after a reload would have failed on "init key not in keystore". This sprint plumbs the canonical Identity through both clients via a constructor-time injection point and adds a post-mutation persist callback. **`ChatClient`**: new `ChatClientOptions { identityProvider, onPersist }`; private `resolveIdentity(secrets)` returns the canonical when wired (production), falls back to one-shot `Identity.fromSeed` for tests / standalone callers. `sendMessage` fires `onPersist` after a successful DS publish; `pollMessages` fires once at the end if at least one row was processed (each `processIncoming` mutates provider storage even when it's a no-op for the UI — application messages drain message keys, commits replace the ratchet tree, proposals queue). **`ContactsClient`**: same shape — `ContactsClientOptions`, `resolveIdentity`, `persistBestEffort`. `sendContactRequest` and `pollMailbox` switch to the canonical for key-material consistency but don't fire persist (they don't touch MLS state). `startGroupWith` and `joinGroupFromWelcome` fire persist after group construction. Compose-side `account_id` plumbing in `sendContactRequest` adjusted: the canonical Identity getter returns a `WasmIdentity` with deterministic keys but no `accountId` field — we keep the `IdentitySecrets` parameter purely as the source of `accountId`, with an early-return if it's null. **Route wiring**: `Contacts.tsx` and `Chat.tsx` instantiate their respective clients with `{ identityProvider: () => chats.identity(), onPersist: () => chats.persistSnapshot(s.account.accountId) }` closures; both lazy-read the auth + chat contexts so a logout/login on the same page session is observed without re-instantiating the client. Failures inside `onPersist` are logged and swallowed — the in-memory MLS state stays good and the next mutation retries the snapshot naturally. **`exactOptionalPropertyTypes`** required spelling the optional fields as `(() => …) | undefined` rather than the `?:` shorthand — no behavioural diff, just a TS strictness quirk on private-field declarations. `npm run typecheck` clean; workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `test` clean. After this sprint the IDB snapshot stays continuously in sync with the canonical provider; **mount-side rehydration** (load snapshot → `restoreStorage` → `Group::load` for each `knownGroupIds`) is the closing 1.16e6c3 sub-sprint.
- **1.16e6c3** ✅ Mount-side MLS rehydration — closing piece of the persistence chain. New `apps/web/src/chat/rehydrate.ts::rehydrateMlsState(chats, accountIdB64)`: looks up the device root key from `key-store`, calls `loadMlsSnapshot(...)`, `restoreStorage(bytes)` on the canonical Identity, then `Group.load(identity, base64Decode(groupIdB64))` for every entry in `knownGroupIds`. Each successfully-loaded group is `chats.add(...)`-ed with a stand-in label (`"Group <6-char-prefix>"` from the base64 group_id) — Phase-1 doesn't carry per-group display metadata across reloads, the contacts cache from 1.16d3d will let us look up real labels in a follow-up. **Failure isolation**: the helper is best-effort top to bottom — corrupt blob, missing device key, dangling group_id, OpenMLS rejection on a single id — every failure logs and bails on its own arm without taking down the rest of the restore. The user always ends up with a working authenticated session even if MLS state was unreadable; the next persist overwrites the bad blob. **AuthContext wiring**: `signup` / `login` / `recover` now call `rehydrateMlsState(chats, accountIdB64)` between `chats.setIdentity(canonical)` and `uploadIdentityBundle(...)` — order matters because the freshly-minted KeyPackage from `uploadIdentityBundle` must land in the **already-restored** provider, not into a fresh empty one. Per flow: signup is a no-op (no prior snapshot); login is the load case that justifies the entire 1.16e6 sub-sprint (same accountId, same device → groups come back); recover is a uniform no-op because `AuthContext.recover` deletes the snapshot pre-flight (the new export key would yield a different device root key, the old ciphertext is unreadable). **Bonus fix**: renamed `MlsSnapshotRecord.ciphertext` → `MlsSnapshotRecord.snapshotBytes` — `loadMlsSnapshot` was returning *decrypted* bytes in a field labelled "Encrypted MLS provider snapshot", a trap for the next reader. The on-disk row shape (`PersistedRow.ciphertext`) keeps the original name because that one really does carry `[IV | AES-GCM]`. `npm run typecheck` clean; workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `test` clean. **End-to-end loop closes here**: a page reload restores the active-groups registry, the canonical Identity's provider has every prior group's ratchet state, and `Chat.tsx` opens the timeline at the same epoch the user left it.
- **1.16f1** ✅ Bundle baseline + first WASM size pass. New `apps/web/scripts/bundle-report.mjs`: zero-dep Node script that walks `dist/` after a production build and prints raw / gzip-9 / brotli-Q11 sizes per artifact + a totals summary, with an advisory budget gate (< 2 MB gz target, hard cap 4 MB before non-zero exit). New npm scripts `report` / `report:no-build` and `just bundle-report` / `just bundle-report-only` recipes wrap it. **`wasm-opt -Oz` switch**: added `[package.metadata.wasm-pack.profile.release] wasm-opt = ['-Oz', '--enable-mutable-globals']` to `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/Cargo.toml` — `wasm-pack` defaults to Binaryen `-O` (balanced); `-Oz` is size-only optimisation. **Honest measurement**: pre-`-Oz` baseline 1,941,754 raw → 735,271 gz / 554,281 brotli; post-`-Oz` 1,877,880 raw (−63,874, −3.3 %) → 735,885 gz (+614, +0.08 %) / 556,206 brotli (+1,925, +0.35 %). The classic `-Oz` paradox — Binaryen replaces repeated patterns with shorter-but-less-compressible alternatives, so the uncompressed shrinks but the compressed stays flat (or marginally worse on brotli). On the wire we win nothing; the raw bytes are 64 kB smaller, which the browser still has to *parse* before instantiating the module — so the win is parse-time on cold loads, not transfer-time. Decision: keep `-Oz`; the parse-time payoff is genuinely there for slow mobile devices in the deployment-target geographies, and the compressed tax is rounding noise. **Total bundle today**: 1.97 MB raw → 755 kB gz / 590 kB brotli (`just bundle-report`). 1.3 MB of headroom under the 2 MB gz budget — no further pressure to chase wins inside 1.16f. Workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `test` clean. Subsequent sub-sprints in 1.16f wire the **service worker** (offline shell, app-shell precache, push-event handler — 1.16f2) and the **`wasm-bindgen-test` browser runner** for FFI error-path coverage (1.16f3).
- **1.16f2** ✅ Service worker + offline shell. Vanilla SW — no Workbox, no third-party deps; the whole flow is ~150 lines and stays auditable. **`apps/web/public/sw.js`** ships with two placeholders (`__BUILD_VERSION__` / `__PRECACHE_MANIFEST__`) that the post-build script rewrites; routing logic discriminates on URL: hashed assets under `/assets/*` → **cache-first** (filenames carry content hashes, immutable per name); navigation / `mode === "navigate"` → **network-first** with cache fallback (so a fresh deploy of `index.html` always brings in the latest hashed asset references); `/api/{auth,discovery,ds}` → **network-only** (a cached OPAQUE round / message journal / KeyPackage list would be an active footgun); everything else → browser default. Update lifecycle: `install` precaches everything atomically + calls `skipWaiting()`, `activate` sweeps every prior `lakko-app-shell-*` cache that doesn't match `BUILD_VERSION`. **`apps/web/scripts/build-sw.mjs`** is a zero-dep Node post-build pass — walks `dist/`, computes a deterministic short SHA-256 prefix over the precache content (deterministic for reproducible builds — no wall-clock time), substitutes both placeholders. Runs as the last step of `npm run build` (added after `vite build`). **`apps/web/src/sw/register.ts`** registers the SW from `main.tsx` after the app mounts: dev-build gate via `import.meta.env.DEV` (Vite HMR + SW conflict on `/api/*` proxy), capability gate (`"serviceWorker" in navigator`), `updatefound` listener that posts `{ type: "SKIP_WAITING" }` to the new worker so a refresh picks up new bytes without waiting for every Lakko tab to close. **PWA surface**: `apps/web/public/manifest.webmanifest` (minimal — name, theme colour, standalone display; icons stay empty until a branding pass), `index.html` gets `<link rel="manifest">` + Apple's `apple-mobile-web-app-*` equivalents. **Push event handler is a no-op stub** — 1.18's encrypted-push receive path is where it gets wired. **TS gotcha**: `import.meta.env.DEV` needs `"types": ["vite/client"]` in `tsconfig.json` (was `[]`); added that one line, no runtime change. **Bundle delta**: 759.5 kB gz (+4 kB vs 1.16f1) — `sw.js` 3.4 kB gz, `manifest.webmanifest` 0.3 kB gz, `index.html` +0.16 kB for the manifest + Apple metas, `main.tsx` +0.26 kB for the register call. Cheap for an offline-capable app shell + the receive surface for 1.18 push. `npm run build` clean, post-pass logs `BUILD_VERSION=cd22d52228b7` + 7 precache entries; `cargo check` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `test` all green; `npm run typecheck` clean. **Deferred to 1.16f3+**: a "new version available" banner UI (currently the SW silently activates on the next page load), and the `wasm-bindgen-test` browser runner so we can write a real "offline cold-start serves the app shell" test instead of relying on devtools.
- **1.16f3** ✅ `wasm-bindgen-test` smoke suite under Node. Added `wasm-bindgen-test = "0.3"` as a wasm32-conditional dev-dep on `lakko-ffi-wasm` (`[target.'cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")'.dev-dependencies]`); new `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/wasm_tests.rs` gated on `cfg(all(test, target_arch = "wasm32"))`. Three smoke cases that the host `cargo test` cannot reach: (a) `identity_new_completes_in_wasm_runtime` — `Identity::new("smoke")` calls `getrandom` under the hood; a missing `js` feature on `getrandom` / `openmls` would surface as an unimplemented-panic in the WASM runtime, invisible to host tests; (b) `identity_snapshot_round_trips_through_wasm` — the full `Identity::from_seed` → `snapshot_storage` → fresh `from_seed(same seed)` → `restore_storage` → `snapshot_storage` cycle through the actual WASM bridge, with byte-identical output asserted; (c) `restore_storage_returns_helpful_error_for_truncated_snapshot` — `JsError` on truncated input must surface as a real JS `Error` with a non-empty `.message` (the rehydrate path in `apps/web/src/chat/rehydrate.ts` logs that string when a corrupt blob hits a hydrate). New `just test-wasm` recipe → `wasm-pack test --node crates/lakko-ffi-wasm`. Picked **Node V8** over a browser driver because (i) zero install overhead — both the dev mac and ubuntu-latest already ship Node, and (ii) all three target properties are WASM-runtime properties, not browser-API properties — IDB / structured-clone / SW-context tests want a real headless browser and arrive in a follow-up sub-sprint together with 1.13c's IDB-backed `Store`. **wasm-bindgen-test 0.3.70 grammar quirk**: the `wasm_bindgen_test_configure!` macro no longer accepts `run_in_node`; the runner picks the host from CLI flags (`--node` vs `--headless --chrome` etc.), so the file does not invoke `configure!` at all. **Cfg gate trick**: `mod wasm_tests` lives behind `cfg(all(test, target_arch = "wasm32"))` so a regular `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-ffi-wasm` (the `wasm-build` CI job, the `apps/web` production build) never tries to pull in the test-only dev-dep — without that double-gate the lib build fails on a missing `wasm_bindgen_test` import. **CI**: new `wasm-test` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (Linux-only, no browser driver). Local: `just test-wasm` shows `3 passed; 0 failed`. Host-side `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` unchanged at 43 tests — the wasm32 module is invisible on host. Workspace `cargo check` / `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` / `fmt --check` clean. **Closes 1.16f**: bundle baseline (f1) + service worker / offline shell (f2) + WASM-runtime smoke (f3) all done; `apps/web` Phase-1 acceptance gates are green and the next surfaces are 1.17 (static-host pipeline + SRI + reproducible artefacts) and 1.14 / 1.15 (`apps/ios` + `apps/android` skeletons).
- **1.17a** ✅ SRI hashes + strict CSP meta + nginx vhost sample. New `apps/web/scripts/post-build.mjs`: zero-dep Node post-build pass that walks `dist/index.html`, finds every `<script src="/assets/...">` / `<link href="/assets/...">` reference, computes SHA-384 over the referenced file content, and injects `integrity="sha384-<base64>"` next to the existing `crossorigin` attribute. Also injects a strict `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy" content="...">` right after the `X-Content-Type-Options` meta as a defense-in-depth fallback for the directives a meta tag CAN carry — `script-src`, `style-src`, `connect-src`, `worker-src`, `manifest-src`, `object-src`, etc. **Idempotent**: a second run on an already-SRI'd HTML reports `(idempotent re-run)` and does not duplicate or break anything (the `matched` vs `injected` distinction inside `injectIntegrity` is the load-bearing piece). Wired into `npm run build` BEFORE `build-sw.mjs` so the SW precaches the post-SRI / post-CSP HTML; that way a cache-fallback navigation still serves the hardened shell. **`apps/web/nginx.conf.example`**: drop-in vhost template for the Hetzner deploy. Ships the full HTTP-header policy that meta tags can't carry: `Content-Security-Policy` (with `frame-ancestors 'none'` — only available via header), `Strict-Transport-Security` (max-age 2 years + includeSubDomains + preload), `X-Frame-Options DENY` (belt-and-suspenders for middleboxes that strip CSP), `Cross-Origin-{Opener,Resource,Embedder}-Policy`, `Permissions-Policy` denying every sensor / autoplay / payment surface we don't use. Routing: hashed `/assets/*` cached forever (`max-age=31536000, immutable`), `/sw.js` `no-store` (browsers re-fetch on every navigation; an intermediary stale-cache deadlocks deploys), `/api/{auth,ds,discovery}/` proxy to the local services (matches the Vite dev proxy in `vite.config.ts` so the JS layer's relative URLs work in both environments), SPA fallback `try_files $uri /index.html` for client-side routing on full reload. **CSP directive notes**: `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` is mandatory — `WebAssembly.instantiate` counts as eval under CSP, and the `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` keyword (Chrome 95+ / Firefox 100+ / Safari 16+) lets us run WASM without unlocking JS eval. `'unsafe-inline'` on `style-src` is the cost of Solid.js' inline `style={...}` props — eliminating it requires hashing every inline style or moving everything to classes; deferred. `connect-src 'self'` covers `/api/*`. **Bundle delta**: index.html 734 → 1036 bytes gz (+302 B for the three SHA-384 hashes + CSP meta); total 759.5 → 759.8 kB gz, rounding noise. Workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `test` clean; `npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run build` chains `vite build` → `post-build.mjs` → `build-sw.mjs` end-to-end. **Open follow-ups in 1.17b/c**: (b) reproducible-build verifier specifically for `apps/web` (two clean builds → byte-identical `dist/`), (c) actual nginx + systemd integration on the VPS to serve `dist/`.
- **1.17b** ✅ Reproducible-build verifier for `apps/web/dist/`. Added `web-repro` job to `.github/workflows/repro.yml` (sibling of the existing Rust `repro` job, same weekly cron `0 6 * * 0` + `workflow_dispatch`). Two clean builds back-to-back: `rm -rf dist wasm node_modules` → `npm ci` → `npm run build` → `sha256sum` over the `dist/` tree → wipe → repeat → `diff`. Pinned toolchain everywhere: Rust stable + `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, Node 20, `wasm-pack ^0.13` (same pin as the per-push `web-build` / `wasm-test` jobs), `npm ci` (not `npm install`) so `package-lock.json` is the binding contract. **Empirically verified locally** before shipping: two clean builds on the dev mac produced **byte-identical** `dist/` (zero diff across all 7 files: index.html, sw.js, manifest, three hashed assets, the WASM blob). Promoted to a real CI gate (no `continue-on-error`) — unlike the Rust repro which keeps `continue-on-error: true` because uniffi 0.28's proc-macros leave `liblakko_ffi_uniffi.rlib` flaky, the web pipeline has zero known non-determinism today. If a future Vite reshape / dep bump breaks reproducibility, the weekly cron surfaces it within seven days and we either fix the regression or add a `KNOWN_FLAKY` filter (mirroring the Rust pattern). **Why this matters**: SRI hashes from 1.17a only protect against tampering between *deploy time* and *user fetch*. Reproducibility extends the trust chain *to the source* — a third party can `git checkout` the same commit, run `npm run build`, and verify the SHA-256s of every byte we ship match the public release artefacts. Combined with cosign-signed Sigstore manifests (Phase 0/SLSA L3) this is the foundation for "you don't have to trust the maintainer's build environment, only the source".
- **1.17c** ✅ Two-path apps/web deploy to the Hetzner VPS — **CI primary**, dev-box fallback. The 1.17a/b artefacts (SRI-injected `index.html`, `sw.js` with substituted placeholders, byte-identical `dist/`) are now actually shipped. **Primary path** — new `.github/workflows/deploy-web.yml`: `workflow_dispatch` trigger (continuous on `push: branches: [main]` is a flag flip when we're confident in the cadence), pinned toolchain (Rust stable + wasm32 + wasm-pack `^0.13` + Node 20), `npm ci && npm run build` in CI, sanity-check `dist/index.html` carries `integrity="sha384-` + `dist/sw.js` no longer carries the post-build placeholders (refusing to deploy a half-baked tree saves a same-day rollback), then ssh-rsync into `/tmp/lakko-web-stage/` on the VPS, atomic `sudo rsync` from staging into `/var/www/lakko-web/`, `sudo systemctl reload nginx`, smoke-probe `https://lakko.me/health`. Two GH secrets carry the auth: `LAKKO_DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` (Ed25519 private half, dedicated key — not reuse of the maintainer's personal key) + `LAKKO_DEPLOY_KNOWN_HOSTS` (`ssh-keyscan` output, pinned so the runner doesn't TOFU-pin a substituted host key). `concurrency.group: deploy-web` serialises overlapping runs. **Fallback path** — `just deploy-web` recipe in `justfile`: same end-state via local `npm ci && npm run build` and the maintainer's own ssh agent. Use cases: CI down, plane Wi-Fi, shipping an unmerged branch fast. Both paths converge on the **identical VPS-side contract**: rsync staging → live + nginx reload, gated by a tightly-scoped sudoers entry (`/etc/sudoers.d/lakko-deploy-web` — NOPASSWD only for the exact `rsync` invocation and `systemctl reload nginx`). **`infra/nginx/sites-available/lakko.conf` updates**: activated the `/var/www/lakko-web` root that was a placeholder since Phase 1.0 (commented-out lines 61-64); ported the full security-header set from 1.17a's `apps/web/nginx.conf.example` (CSP with `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, `'unsafe-inline'` for style attrs, COOP/CORP `same-origin`, COEP `unsafe-none`, full Permissions-Policy denylist, `frame-ancestors 'none'`); added `/api/{auth,ds,discovery}/` proxy paths to lakko.me parallel to the existing api.lakko.me routes (the web client uses relative `/api/*` URLs to keep CSP `connect-src 'self'` valid); added immutable cache for `/assets/*`, `no-store` for `/sw.js`, short cache for manifest/robots/favicon, SPA `try_files $uri /index.html` fallback for client-side routing on full reload. **Cleanup**: dropped `apps/web/nginx.conf.example` — superseded; `infra/nginx/sites-available/lakko.conf` is the canonical config, single source of truth. **`infra/README.md`** gained two new sections — "Deploy — apps/web" with primary/fallback paths spelled out, plus full one-time bootstrap (web-root creation, sudoers config with the literal commands, GH secret generation flow with `ssh-keygen` + `ssh-keyscan` + secret upload + local-copy `shred`). Workspace `cargo` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `npm typecheck` all clean. **Closes 1.17**: SRI + CSP (1.17a) + reproducibility verifier (1.17b) + actual deploy plumbing (1.17c) — the `apps/web` static-host pipeline is end-to-end ready. Next sprint by maintainer's chosen order is **1.18** (ContactRequest E2E + invite-link flow).

#### Cross-platform features
- **1.18a** ✅ Rust core: `lakko-contacts::invite` — capability-based invite struct + signed wire codec. New `crates/lakko-contacts/src/invite.rs` mirrors `request.rs`'s manual length-prefixed-binary pattern for predictability. **`InviteCapability` v1 wire format**: `u8 schema (=1)` + `[32] owner_signature_pubkey` (Ed25519) + `[16] owner_account_id` + `[32] owner_x25519_pubkey` (long-term HPKE init key) + `u64be expiry_ms` (`NEVER_EXPIRES = 0` sentinel) + `u32be max_uses` (`SINGLE_USE = 0` sentinel) + `u8 bootstrap_referral` (0/1, signup deep-link flag) + `u16be label_len` + `[label_len] label_bytes` (UTF-8, ≤ `MAX_LABEL_LEN = 64`) + `[64] signature` (Ed25519 over everything else). Public surface: `compose(...) -> Result<Vec<u8>>`, `decode_and_verify(wire) -> Result<InviteCapability>`, `InviteCapability::is_valid_now(now_ms) -> bool`. **What V1 ships vs the ADR-0005 §13.3 spec**: dropped `owner_transparency_proof` (lakko-transparency doesn't yet expose inclusion proofs end-to-end — TOFU on the inviter's pubkey until that lands), dropped `consumed_pubkey_log: HmacBloom` (server-side max-uses enforcement is 1.18d's job; the field is preserved as a UI hint), dropped `blob_id` storage indirection (V1 puts capability bytes **directly in the URL** as base64url; URL grows from ~30 to ~150-220 chars but no server dependency and no extra round-trip — swap to indirection later without a wire-format break), dropped `ephemeral_prekey` (the joiner generates its own per-envelope ephemeral via the existing `crate::envelope::seal`, and our flow has joiner→owner as the first sealed message, so the owner-side prekey is unnecessary). **Tests** (15 new, total `cargo test -p lakko-contacts` 30 → 45): round-trip, empty-label round-trip, `NEVER_EXPIRES` always-valid, expiry boundary (equal = expired), `bootstrap_referral` round-trip, label at exact `MAX_LABEL_LEN` round-trip, label over max rejected at `compose`, tampered-signature `BadSignature`, tampered-label `BadSignature` (signature is over the label, so even a one-byte change surfaces as signature failure not UTF-8 error), truncated-wire `InvalidEncoding`, empty-wire `InvalidEncoding`, unknown-schema-version `InvalidEncoding`, invalid `bootstrap_referral` byte (non-0/1) `InvalidEncoding`, trailing bytes after signature `InvalidEncoding`, wire-starts-with-schema-version-byte (regression guard), wrong-signing-key fails verify (hand-built wire with attacker's signature), label-with-invalid-UTF-8 `InvalidEncoding` (hand-built wire to bypass `&str` typing — preserves `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` invariant on the crate). **Subsequent sub-sprints**: 1.18b (wasm-bindgen exposure of compose/decode_and_verify + an `InviteCapability` JS class), 1.18c (web UI: "Generate invite link" button + "Open invite URL" landing page that turns a fresh-from-URL capability into a ContactRequest seed), 1.18d (server-side `services/discovery /invite/{blob_id}` endpoint + HmacBloom max-uses enforcement, switching from inline-bytes URLs to short-pointer URLs).
- **1.18b** ✅ wasm-bindgen exposure — new `InviteCapability` JS class on the `lakko-ffi-wasm` surface, added to `crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/contacts.rs` next to `ContactRequest` (mirror of the same import + wrapper-struct + static-factory pattern). Static `compose(ownerSigningSeed, ownerAccountId, ownerX25519Pubkey, expiryMs, max_uses, bootstrapReferral, label) -> Uint8Array` + static `decodeAndVerify(wire) -> InviteCapability`; getters `ownerSignaturePubkey` / `ownerAccountId` / `ownerX25519Pubkey` / `expiryMs` / `maxUses` / `bootstrapReferral` / `label`; instance method `isValidNow(nowMs) -> bool`. Argument-shape validation at the WASM boundary surfaces as `JsError` (32-byte seed, 16-byte account id, 32-byte X25519, label ≤ MAX_LABEL_LEN); deeper failures (signature verify, malformed wire, invalid UTF-8 label, unknown schema) bubble up from the underlying Rust codec via `contact_err`. **Tests**: 2 new host-side cases in `contacts::tests` (happy-path round-trip + `NEVER_EXPIRES` / `SINGLE_USE` / `bootstrap_referral=true` / empty label sentinel coverage); 1 new wasm-bindgen-test smoke in `wasm_tests.rs::invite_capability_round_trips_through_wasm` confirming the WASM bridge survives the round-trip through V8 — exercises the Vec<u8> ↔ Uint8Array, String ↔ JS string, u64 ↔ BigInt, and bool ↔ boolean conversions in one shot. **Counts**: `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 43 → 45, `wasm-pack test --node` 3 → 4. **Bundle delta**: WASM 737 → 739 kB gz (+0.3 kB for the new wasm-bindgen exports + the 7 getters + the static factories in release+LTO). Workspace `cargo check` / `clippy` / `fmt` / `npm typecheck` all clean. **Deferred**: the `MAX_LABEL_LEN` constant isn't yet exposed as a JS-visible value — the Web UI in 1.18c will hardcode the 64-byte cap until we decide whether to surface it (probably as a top-level `INVITE_LABEL_MAX_LEN` export, fold-in is trivial when the call sites land).
- **1.18c** ✅ Web UI for invite links — owner-side generation + joiner-side landing. **`apps/web/src/contacts/invite.ts`** (new): URL plumbing `composeInviteUrl(bytes, origin?) -> string` / `parseInviteSlug(slug) -> Uint8Array` (built on `base64UrlEncodeNoPad` / new sibling `base64UrlDecodeNoPad` in `auth/account-id.ts` — folds in the inline base64url-decode that `Chat.tsx` had been carrying since 1.16e5), preset enums `ExpiryPreset = "1d" | "7d" | "30d" | "never"` + `MaxUsesPreset = "single" | "ten" | "unlimited"` with their `*ToMs` / `*ToValue` translators (single = 0 sentinel, unlimited = u32::MAX, never = 0n sentinel), describer helpers `describeExpiry(ms, now)` ("in 6 days" / "expired 3 hours ago" / "never expires") + `describeMaxUses(n)` ("single-use" / "up to 10 uses" / "unlimited"). `INVITE_LABEL_MAX_LEN = 64` mirrors the Rust constant for client-side form validation (cleaner UX than waiting for a `JsError` round-trip). **`apps/web/src/contacts/client.ts`** refactor: extracted the inner compose-and-publish logic from `sendContactRequest` into a private `composeAndPublishContactRequest(...)` helper so a new public `sendContactRequestToBundle(identity, recipientAccountId, recipientSignaturePubkey, recipientX25519Pubkey, intro)` can call it without re-walking the discovery `lookup → identityFetch` round-trip. Trade-off: `recipientUsername` in the returned `SendResult` is `""` for the bundle path — the joiner doesn't have one (no discovery hop happened). Useful both as a happy-path optimisation and as a censored-region path (`/discovery/*` may be blocked while the link still works because the bundle is inline). **`apps/web/src/routes/Invite.tsx`** (new): `/i/:slug` landing page. Three states the page resolves to via a `createResource` driven on the slug signal — bad-slug / decode-failure (generic "this invite is invalid" panel; we deliberately do NOT surface codec internals to avoid leaking wire-format detail), anonymous user ("sign up or log in to accept" gate; deep-link return after auth is a follow-up — visitor manually re-opens the link for now), authenticated user (inviter card with fingerprint / expiry / max-uses / `bootstrap_referral` flag + Accept / Decline pair). Accept flips through `ContactsClient.sendContactRequestToBundle(...)` and navigates to `/contacts` after 1.5 s on success. Expired capabilities show a banner + disable Accept. **`apps/web/src/routes/Contacts.tsx`** gains an `InvitePanel` between SendPanel and PendingPanel: form fields `label` / `expiry` (preset dropdown — "Never" carries an explicit "stalking risk — see ADR-0005" hint) / `maxUses` / `bootstrap_referral` checkbox; Generate fires `wasm.InviteCapability.compose(...)` with the canonical Identity from `<ChatProvider>` (1.16e6c2 coherence — same Ed25519 seed + X25519 pubkey every other contact-flow stamps) plus the `accountId` from `auth.identitySecrets()`; result panel surfaces the URL with a Copy-to-clipboard button + "Generate another" reset. **Routes**: `/i/:slug` registered as a top-level public route in `routes.tsx` (anyone can open an invite, including anons) — explicitly NOT wrapped in `<RequireSession>` or `<RequireAnonymous>`. `paths.invite(slug)` builder + `inviteRoutePattern = "/i/:slug"` added. **Bundle delta**: app JS 29.4 → 32.6 kB gz (+3.1 kB for `InvitePanel` / `Invite` route / `invite.ts` helpers); WASM 737.5 → 740.2 kB gz (+2.7 kB for the `InviteCapability` 1.18b exports — same +0.3 kB delta from earlier counted twice because release+LTO has been re-run after a few intermediate sprints); total 762.7 → 765.8 kB gz, 1.28 MB headroom under the 2 MB budget. **Gates**: `cargo check` / `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` / `fmt --check` clean, `cargo test --workspace` clean, `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite → post-build SRI/CSP → build-sw with the 7 precache entries unchanged in count). **Open follow-ups inside 1.18c**: post-auth deep-link return for the anonymous-user case (Vite's `<A>` link drops you on /signup but doesn't restore the original `/i/:slug` URL on session resume — needs either a localStorage stash or a `?return=` query parameter convention); the bootstrap_referral auto-send-on-signup path mentioned in ADR-0005 §13.4 (just lights up the UI flag in V1, no automation). 1.18d closes the rest with the server-side max-uses enforcement.
- **1.18d1a** ✅ Server-side capability blob storage — POST + GET endpoints, content-addressed by SHA-256, idempotent uploads. New `services/discovery/migrations/0004_invites.sql`: `invites` table keyed by `blob_id BINARY(32)` (PRIMARY KEY) with `capability_bytes VARBINARY(2048)` + denormalised `owner_account_id BINARY(16)` + `expiry_ms BIGINT UNSIGNED` + `used_count INT UNSIGNED` (reserved for d2's `max_uses` enforcement, not consulted in d1) + indexes on `(owner_account_id)` and `(expiry_ms)`. New `services/discovery/src/invite.rs`: **POST `/discovery/invite`** validates the PoW token, base64-decodes the capability, runs `lakko_contacts::invite::decode_and_verify` server-side (junk uploads rejected at ingress + denormalised columns are sourced from the verified struct so a malicious uploader can't lie about owner_account_id), derives `blob_id = SHA-256(bytes)`, upserts via `INSERT … ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` (same bytes ⇒ same id ⇒ no-op retry), returns `{ blob_id_b64url }`. **GET `/discovery/invite/{blob_id}`** decodes the slug, fetches `(capability_bytes, expiry_ms)`, applies a server-side expiry filter (`expiry_ms != 0 && now_ms >= expiry_ms` ⇒ 404, indistinguishable from "never issued" so an attacker can't probe the id space by status code), returns `{ capability_b64 }`. **Why content-addressed**: the same capability uploaded twice gets the same id (idempotency baked in), and the 32-byte hash doubles as a tamper detector — a malicious server can't substitute bytes without changing the id, and the joiner re-hashes locally before consuming (the locally-recomputed `blob_id` check is a 1.18d2 follow-up). **Why server-side `decode_and_verify`**: rejects forged signatures / version-skewed wire / oversized labels at ingress; provides the verified struct fields for index columns; capability bytes are public-by-design so coupling the server to `lakko-contacts` adds no privacy risk. **`services/discovery/Cargo.toml`** gains `lakko-contacts.workspace = true` as a regular dep + `lakko-crypto.workspace = true` as a dev-dep (the round-trip test mints a real Ed25519/X25519 pair). **Tests**: 5 new in `invite::tests` (`cargo test -p lakko-svc-discovery` 13 → 20): SHA-256-of-bytes, same-bytes-yield-same-id, distinct-bytes-yield-distinct-id, two pin tests against the migration's `VARBINARY(2048)` + `BINARY(32)` widths so a future schema bump that misses the constants surfaces as a unit-test failure rather than a production 500, and a `decode_and_verify` round-trip + tampered-signature rejection that pins the contract this handler relies on (a future change to `lakko-contacts` that loosens signature checking surfaces in the discovery test suite, not just in the client). **Gates**: `cargo build -p lakko-svc-discovery` clean, `cargo test -p lakko-svc-discovery` 20 passed, workspace `cargo check` / `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` / `fmt --check` / `cargo test --workspace` all clean. **What 1.18d1a leaves for d1b/d2/d3**: (d1b) wire the web client side — `DiscoveryClient.uploadInvite(bytes)` / `fetchInvite(blob_id)`, switch `composeInviteUrl` to emit pointer URLs by default with inline-bytes back-compat, switch `Invite.tsx` to recognise both URL forms; (d2) `max_uses` enforcement + owner-side revocation (`DELETE`) + expiry pruner; (d3) HmacBloom side table per blob for per-joiner de-dup.
- **1.18d1b** ✅ Web client switch — pointer URL by default + inline-bytes back-compat. **`DiscoveryClient` extension** (`apps/web/src/discovery/client.ts`): low-level `inviteUpload(req)` mirroring the keypackage pattern + high-level `uploadInvite(capabilityBytes) -> Uint8Array` (drives one PoW solve + `POST /discovery/invite`, returns the raw 32-byte SHA-256 ready for the pointer URL — server returns base64url-no-pad, client decodes back to bytes through `base64UrlDecodeNoPad`) + `fetchInvite(blobId) -> Uint8Array | null` (`GET /discovery/invite/{slug}`, 404 surfaces as `null` per existing convention). Sanity check on the response shape: server returning a non-32-byte blob_id throws `DiscoveryHttpError(200, "/discovery/invite", "...")` rather than corrupting the URL silently. **New wire types** in `discovery/wire.ts`: `InviteUploadRequest` / `InviteUploadResponse` / `InviteFetchResponse`. **`apps/web/src/contacts/invite.ts` refactor**: split `composeInviteUrl` into the auto-pick base + named variants `composePointerInviteUrl(blobId)` / `composeInlineInviteUrl(capabilityBytes)` for explicit call sites. **Discriminated `parseInviteSlug`** returns a tagged union — `{ kind: "pointer", blobId }` for 32-byte slugs, `{ kind: "inline", capabilityBytes }` for ≥160-byte slugs (the 1.18a wire-format minimum, an empty-label invite). The 32-vs-160 gap is the discriminator: no overlap by construction, no ambiguity at parse time. New `INVITE_BLOB_ID_LEN = 32` and `INVITE_INLINE_MIN_LEN = 160` constants make the relationship explicit. **`Contacts.tsx::InvitePanel`** rewires `onGenerate`: tries `discovery.uploadInvite(bytes)` first; on `DiscoveryNetworkError` (transport failure — Vite dev without the service running, censored region path), falls back to `composeInlineInviteUrl(bytes)` and surfaces an explicit "discovery service was unreachable, falling back to inline-bytes URL" warning in the result panel. **Crucially does NOT fall back on `DiscoveryHttpError`** (PoW reject / decode_and_verify reject / oversize) because those are real bugs the server caught — smuggling the same bytes inline would just mean the joiner re-rejects locally. **`Invite.tsx`** dispatches via `parseInviteSlug` inside the `createResource` driver: pointer → `discovery.fetchInvite(blobId)` → re-runs `decodeAndVerify` on returned bytes (catches a malicious server that serves substituted bytes — though the SHA-256 cross-check is a 1.18d3 follow-up); inline → run `decodeAndVerify` directly. Both paths converge on the same downstream `ConnectPanel`. **Bundle delta**: app JS 32.6 → 33.2 kB gz (+0.65 kB for `DiscoveryClient` upload/fetch helpers + the `parseInviteSlug` discriminated union + `InvitePanel`'s pointer-flow logic + the back-compat copy in `GeneratedUrlPanel`); total 765.8 → 766.4 kB gz, well under the 2 MB budget. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + post-build SRI/CSP + sw post-build), `cargo check` / `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` / `fmt --check` / `cargo test --workspace` all clean. **Closes the inline → pointer URL transition** end-to-end: a 1.18c client opening a 1.18d1b pointer URL still parses (it'll see a 32-byte slug, and the slug-length test the 1.18c `parseInviteSlug` does was a single `base64UrlDecodeNoPad` — the new discriminated union surfaces a typed parse instead, but both branches reach `decodeAndVerify` eventually). 1.18d2 next wires `max_uses` enforcement + owner-side revocation + the periodic expiry pruner.
- **1.18d2a-server** ✅ `max_uses` enforcement — new migration + denormalised column + atomic consume endpoint. New `services/discovery/migrations/0005_invite_max_uses.sql`: `ALTER TABLE invites ADD COLUMN max_uses INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (positioned `AFTER expiry_ms` to keep the schema's "denormalised-from-capability" columns clustered). Default `0` is the `SINGLE_USE` sentinel — most-conservative interpretation of any 1.18d1 row that pre-dates the column (academic in practice — 1.18d1 hasn't reached production). **Sentinel mapping** (matches `lakko_contacts::invite::SINGLE_USE` / `MAX_USES_UNLIMITED`): `0` → effective ceiling 1, `0xFFFFFFFF` → unlimited (u32::MAX), any other N → N. Mapping lives at the **SQL layer** (CASE expression in the consume UPDATE) rather than at row-write time so the table stays a faithful reflection of the wire format and a future `lakko_contacts` change to sentinel semantics doesn't require a data migration. **`upload` handler updated** to populate `max_uses` from the verified `cap.max_uses` (added `, max_uses` to the INSERT column list + the upsert clause; same idempotency contract holds — same bytes ⇒ same row ⇒ on-conflict updates the `capability_bytes` and `max_uses` together so a re-upload after a `lakko-contacts` bump that changes the wire-encoded `max_uses` interpretation is consistent). **New `POST /discovery/invite/{blob_id}/consume` endpoint** — body `{ pow }`. Atomic single-shot UPDATE: `SET used_count = used_count + 1 WHERE blob_id = ? AND (expiry_ms = 0 OR expiry_ms > now) AND used_count < CASE WHEN max_uses = 0 THEN 1 WHEN max_uses = 0xFFFFFFFF THEN 0xFFFFFFFF ELSE max_uses END`. Result rows-affected disambiguates: 1 ⇒ success, read-back `(used_count, max_uses)` for the response body; 0 ⇒ second SELECT to tease apart "no row" / "expired" / "exhausted". HTTP outcomes: **200 OK** with the post-increment counter, **404 Not Found** for absent or expired (folded into one status to deny status-code probing of valid blob_ids — same posture as `fetch`), **410 Gone** for budget exhausted (distinct from 404 because the joiner already peeked via GET — the UI surfaces "this invite has been used up" rather than a generic "not found"). **Decoupled from GET**: a peek does not burn through the counter — refresh, back/forward, etc. cost nothing. The Accept button on the joiner side calls `/consume` exactly once before sending the ContactRequest. **Refactor**: extracted `current_unix_ms()` helper, replaced the inline derivation in `fetch`, kept the same saturating-on-`SystemTime`-error semantics. **Tests** (3 new in `invite::tests`, total `cargo test -p lakko-svc-discovery` 20 → 23): `effective_max_uses_maps_sentinels` pins the host-side mirror of the SQL CASE — drift between Rust and SQL surfaces here, not as per-row enforcement mismatch in production; `max_uses_unlimited_matches_u32_max` pins the constant the SQL CASE binds; `max_uses_column_type_is_int_unsigned` reads `0005_invite_max_uses.sql` and asserts `INT UNSIGNED` + `DEFAULT 0` survive (default is load-bearing — bumping to a more permissive value silently would loosen enforcement on legacy rows). The `effective_max_uses` helper is `#[cfg(test)]`-gated since SQL is the actual enforcement; clippy `-D warnings` would have caught a non-gated dead function. **Gates**: `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean. **Subsequent sub-sprints**: 1.18d2a-web (Accept button calls `/consume` before sending ContactRequest, surfaces 410 as "this invite has been used up"); 1.18d2b (DELETE endpoint with Ed25519-signed revocation request — same pubkey that signed the capability has the right to revoke, no `services/auth` session token needed); 1.18d2c (background expiry pruner mirroring `pow::spawn_pruner`).
- **1.18d2a-web** ✅ Joiner-side `/consume` wiring with discriminated 200/404/410 handling. **`apps/web/src/discovery/wire.ts`** gains `InviteConsumeRequest` (just `pow` — 1.18d3 will add `joiner_account_id` for HmacBloom de-dup) + `InviteConsumeResponse { used_count, max_uses }`. **`DiscoveryClient.consumeInvite(blobId) -> ConsumeInviteResult`** drives one PoW solve over `services/discovery`'s `/pow/challenge` + the atomic POST, then folds the trifecta of HTTP responses into a discriminated union: `{ kind: "consumed", usedCount, maxUses }` on 200, `{ kind: "exhausted" }` on 410 (max_uses ceiling hit), `{ kind: "not-found" }` on 404 (never-issued OR expired — server folds, client surfaces "expired or revoked" copy). Other non-2xx still throw `DiscoveryHttpError` — discriminated path reserved for the three response shapes the joiner is actually supposed to act on. Low-level `inviteConsume(slug, req)` mirror added for symmetry with `inviteUpload`. **`apps/web/src/routes/Invite.tsx`** rewires the Accept flow: `DecodedInvite` now also carries `blobId: Uint8Array | null` (non-null only on the pointer path; inline-bytes URLs from 1.18c back-compat carry `null` and skip /consume entirely — back-compat compromise documented). `ConnectPanel.onAccept` calls `discovery.consumeInvite(blobId)` first when the blobId is non-null; on `"exhausted"` flips the panel into a locked state with a "this invite has been used up — `max_uses` ceiling reached" banner and disables both the intro input and the Accept button (Decline still works), on `"not-found"` shows the "no longer valid — revoked or expired between page load and click" banner, on `"consumed"` proceeds to `sendContactRequestToBundle(...)`. **Last-use copy**: when `consumed` and `usedCount >= effectiveMaxUses(maxUses)`, surfaces "Last use claimed — sending ContactRequest…" before the regular DS-id notice. **`effectiveMaxUses(maxUses)`** helper at file-bottom mirrors the server's `effective_max_uses` Rust mirror of the SQL CASE expression — three-layer redundancy (SQL = source of truth, Rust mirror is `#[cfg(test)]`, JS mirror is UI-only) but each layer's drift surfaces in the right place. **Bundle delta**: app JS 33.2 → 33.6 kB gz (+0.45 kB for the `consumeInvite` plumbing + the discriminated `ConsumeInviteResult` + the three new banners + the Invite.tsx Accept-flow refactor); WASM unchanged; total 766.4 → 766.8 kB gz, well under the 2 MB budget. **Inline-bytes URLs bypass enforcement by design** — no server row to bump. A 1.18c client opening an old inline link, or a 1.18d2 client opening one (network-error fallback), gets the same "no max_uses ceiling" behaviour. Documented compromise; closing it would require either retiring inline URLs (breaks the censored-region path) or a hash-based proof-of-bytes the server records — both bigger than this sub-sprint. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean, `cargo check / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean. **Closes the max_uses enforcement loop** end-to-end: server stores ceiling (1.18d2a-server), atomic UPDATE enforces (1.18d2a-server), joiner consumes before send (1.18d2a-web), 410 Gone surfaces in UI (1.18d2a-web). Next: 1.18d2b (owner revocation via Ed25519-signed DELETE), 1.18d2c (expiry pruner).
- **1.18d2b-server** ✅ Owner-signed revocation — new `DELETE /discovery/invite/{blob_id}` endpoint. Body shape `{ signed_at_ms: u64, signature: base64 }`. Server flow: decode `blob_id` from path, gate on the replay window before any DB work (`signed_at_ms` must be `±REVOKE_REPLAY_WINDOW_MS` of `now_ms`; symmetric tolerance for client clock skew), `SELECT capability_bytes` for the row, run `decode_and_verify` to recover `owner_signature_pubkey` (the row went through the same verify at upload time, so a re-decode here is purely "extract the embedded pubkey" — should never fail; if it does we fold into 404), build the canonical bytes the owner signs (`b"lakko-invite-revoke/v1\x00" || blob_id || signed_at_ms.to_be_bytes()` — 63 bytes, domain-separated + version-bound + replay-bound) and verify the request's Ed25519 signature against the recovered pubkey, then `DELETE` the row on success. **Response model**: `204 No Content` on success, `404 Not Found` over the union of "no row" / "decode_and_verify failed" / "signature did not verify" (folded so attacker probing the blob_id space cannot distinguish a never-issued id from one they don't own — the only signal a prober gets is the same as for a random unknown id), `400 Bad Request` only for body-shape failures (bad base64, wrong-sized signature, `signed_at_ms` outside the replay window — these ARE distinct from 404 because they indicate a buggy client, not a probing attacker). **No PoW gate on DELETE**: the cryptographic gate (Ed25519 verify against owner pubkey, ~50 µs) is much stronger than PoW, and a malformed request costs the server one indexed SELECT + one signature verify before being rejected — cheap enough that anti-DoS work isn't worth the round-trip. **`services/discovery/Cargo.toml`** promotes `lakko-crypto.workspace = true` from dev-dep to regular dep — the verify call site needs `signing::Signature::from_bytes` and `SIGNATURE_LEN` directly. **Tests** (4 new in `invite::tests`, `cargo test -p lakko-svc-discovery` 23 → 27): `revoke_canonical_bytes_layout` pins the byte-for-byte shape (prefix + blob_id + big-endian u64 = 63 bytes total) so a future refactor that drifts the layout surfaces in this suite, not as a server-side reject of every web client's revocation attempt; `revoke_replay_window_is_10_minutes` pins the constant the web client builds against — tightening or loosening here without bumping the client surfaces as silent revocation failures with no distinguishable error code; `within_replay_window_is_symmetric` covers the boundary cases (±10 min accepted including exactly at the boundary, ±10 min + 1 ms rejected); `revoke_signature_round_trip` exercises sign + verify + four tamper paths (signed_at_ms / blob_id / different signing key — each must reject). **Gates**: `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean (one `manual_abs_diff` clippy lint caught on `within_replay_window` and fixed via `u64::abs_diff`). **Subsequent sub-sprints**: 1.18d2b-web (`DiscoveryClient.revokeInvite(blobId, signingSeed) -> RevokeInviteResult` + a "Revoke this invite" button in the same-session generation flow); 1.18d2b-list (deferred — owner index endpoint needs an auth design decision the rest of the discovery surface doesn't have yet, cf. ROADMAP design notes); 1.18d2c (expiry pruner background task).
- **1.18d2b-web** ✅ Owner-side `revokeInvite` end-to-end + same-session UI. **Protocol-layer move**: the canonical-bytes layout (`REVOKE_DOMAIN_V1`, `REVOKE_REPLAY_WINDOW_MS`, `BLOB_ID_LEN`) and the `revoke_canonical_bytes(...)` builder migrated from `services/discovery/src/invite.rs` to `crates/lakko-contacts/src/invite.rs` — single source of truth for the wire detail both sides drive sign / verify against, eliminates the silent-drift risk a JS mirror would introduce. Server-side just imports + uses; web side reaches the same bytes via the new `wasm.signInviteRevocation`. **New `lakko_contacts::invite::sign_revocation(signing, blob_id, signed_at_ms) -> [u8; 64]`** helper wraps the canonical-bytes build + Ed25519 sign in one call. **`wasm.signInviteRevocation(signingSeed, blobId, signedAtMs) -> Uint8Array`** exposed in `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/contacts.rs` — argument-shape gates (32-byte seed, 32-byte blob_id) at the WASM boundary; under the hood `SigningKey::from_seed` + `rust_sign_revocation` + `to_vec()`. **`apps/web/src/discovery/wire.ts`** gains `InviteRevokeRequest { signed_at_ms, signature }`. **`DiscoveryClient`**: low-level `inviteRevoke(slug, req)` (DELETE verb — not factored through `post()` because the body shape is distinct enough that going via the JSON helper would obscure more than it shares; throws on any non-204) + high-level `revokeInvite(blobId, signingSeed) -> RevokeInviteResult` that pins `signed_at_ms = Date.now()` once across the await boundary so we sign exactly the value we POST, base64-encodes the signature, surfaces 204 → `{ kind: "revoked" }` and 404 → `{ kind: "not-found-or-not-owner" }` (server-side fold over "no row" / "bad signature" / "stored bytes failed re-decode" — same response model as the consume path).
