Other non-2xx still throw `DiscoveryHttpError`. **UI**: `GeneratedUrlPanel` gains a "Revoke this invite" button on the pointer-form path (`canRevoke = generatedBlobId() !== null`); on the inline-bytes fallback path the button is replaced with a muted "(Revoke unavailable on the inline-bytes fallback — no server row to delete)" footer line so the asymmetry is visible to the operator. `InvitePanel` tracks `generatedBlobId` (`null` on inline-fallback, `Uint8Array` on pointer) alongside the existing URL state; `onRevoke` calls `discovery.revokeInvite(...)` with the owner's `chats.identity().ed25519Seed`, on either result resets the panel and surfaces a one-line notice through the existing `error` channel (a dedicated success banner is overkill for a terminal action that re-renders the form). **Tests** (4 net new, +1 host wasm-bindgen test): `lakko-contacts` 45 → 48 (`revoke_canonical_bytes_layout` / `revoke_replay_window_is_10_minutes` / `sign_revocation_round_trip` — moved + adapted from the discovery suite, since the layout is now protocol-owned); `lakko-ffi-wasm` 45 → 46 (`sign_invite_revocation_round_trip` — exercises the wasm-bindgen wrapper and verifies the signature against the same canonical bytes the server-side path will rebuild on the row); `lakko-svc-discovery` 27 → 24 (3 tests dropped because they moved to `lakko-contacts`; remaining `within_replay_window_is_symmetric` stays here — that helper is server-side only). Net workspace test count: roughly flat, but each test is now homed in the crate that owns the invariant. **Bundle delta**: app JS 33.6 → 34.1 kB gz (+0.5 kB for `revokeInvite` plumbing + `RevokeInviteResult` + the panel button + the busy/canRevoke wiring); WASM 740.2 → 741.0 kB gz (+0.7 kB for the new `signInviteRevocation` export + helper); total 766.8 → 767.8 kB gz, 1.28 MB headroom. **Gates**: `cargo test --workspace` clean, `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` clean, `fmt --check` clean, `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + post-build SRI/CSP + sw post-pass), `wasm-pack test --node` 4 passed (existing smokes still green; the round-trip pin is on the host side). **Closes the owner-revocation loop on the same-session path.** 1.18d2b-list (cross-session "active invites" enumeration) stays deferred behind the auth-design choice noted below.
- **1.18d2b-list** ✅ Owner-side index endpoint + "Active invites" panel in `/contacts`. Picked design (b) — signed-query endpoint. `lakko-contacts::invite` gets `OWNER_LIST_DOMAIN_V1` ("lakko-invite-owner-list/v1\0", distinct from `REVOKE_DOMAIN_V1` so a captured revoke signature can't be replayed against the list endpoint) + `owner_list_canonical_bytes(owner_account_id, signed_at_ms)` + `sign_owner_list(signing_key, ...)`. `services/discovery` gets `POST /discovery/invite/by-owner`: decode body → enforce the same ±10 min replay window as revoke → resolve owner's verifying key from `identity_bundles.signature_pubkey` for the supplied `account_id` → verify Ed25519 against the canonical bytes → SELECT live invites (filter `expiry_ms = 0 OR expiry_ms > now`) → decode each `capability_bytes` to extract `label` + `bootstrap_referral` (neither denormalised onto a column) → return JSON. 400 on body-shape failures, 404 fold over "no identity_bundle for `account_id`" / "signature mismatch" / "stored pubkey malformed" (same attacker-probing-resistant fold as revoke). Route sits under the existing session-token middleware as a DoS gate, not the ownership proof. UniFFI + wasm-bindgen expose `signOwnerListQuery`. Each client has the full pipeline: `DiscoveryClient.listInvitesByOwner(accountId, signingSeed) -> ListInvitesResult` + a TS-parity `ActiveInvite` row shape carrying `blobIdSlug` / `expiryMs` / `maxUses` / `usedCount` / `label` / `bootstrapReferral` / `createdAt`. The `<ActiveInvitesPanel>` in `/contacts` (web Solid, iOS SwiftUI, Android Compose) renders the list with per-row Revoke (re-uses the existing 1.18d2b-web `revokeInvite` surface). Refresh is **explicit user gesture** — not auto-loaded — so the timing-correlation side-channel (server learns `account_id` per list query) stays under user control. Threat-model trade-off accepted: server already learns `owner_account_id` at upload time (signature verify needs the bound pubkey from `identity_bundles`); list queries add one more timing-correlation channel of the same kind, not a new identity leak. OPRF-blinding the query would require re-keying `invites` + `identity_bundles` by `f(account_id)` across the whole discovery surface — Phase 2+ rewrite, not Phase-1 polish. Per-row signed receipts so the client can detect a malicious server omitting rows: deferred to Phase 2 transparency. Tests: 4 new unit tests in `lakko-contacts` (layout pin, distinct-from-revoke-domain, sig round-trip, signed_at-binds-into-signature), 2 in `lakko-ffi-uniffi` (round-trip + wrong-account_id-length rejection). Web `tsc --noEmit`, all-target clippy `-D warnings`, full workspace tests — clean.
- **1.18d2c** ✅ Expiry pruner — background task spawned at startup. New `services/discovery/src/invite.rs::spawn_pruner` mirrors the existing `pow::spawn_pruner` shape (interval + loop + log + carry on) with a different cadence (1 h vs 60 s) — invite blobs typically live for hours/days while PoW challenges live for ~minute, so an hourly sweep is proportionate. Wakes via `tokio::time::interval(Duration::from_secs(3600))`, skips the immediate-fire first tick (avoid racing with first upload), then per loop runs `DELETE FROM invites WHERE expiry_ms != 0 AND expiry_ms < ?` against the current `current_unix_ms()` (helper extracted in 1.18d2a, shared with `fetch` / `consume`). The `idx_expiry` index from `0004_invites.sql` is the load-bearing piece — without it the recurring sweep would do a full-table seq scan each tick. **`expiry_ms = 0` rows preserved by design** — the `NEVER_EXPIRES` sentinel from `lakko_contacts::invite` is an explicit opt-in to a stalking-risk shape per ADR-0005 §13.3; the codec layer surfaces a UI warning, the storage layer doesn't second-guess. Wired in `main.rs` next to `_pow_pruner` as `_invite_pruner = invite::spawn_pruner(pool.clone())`; tokio task is aborted on service shutdown via the JoinHandle ownership pattern. **Tests**: 1 new `prune_interval_is_one_hour` constant pin (a future bump to 5 min on a hot table needs to be a deliberate edit); `cargo test -p lakko-svc-discovery` 24 → 25. **Bonus clippy fix** (`unnecessary_trailing_comma` in `lakko-ffi-wasm`'s revocation arg-shape error path) caught + resolved during the sweep. **Gates**: `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean. **Closes 1.18d2 entirely**: max_uses enforcement (d2a) + owner-side revocation (d2b) + expiry pruner (d2c). The full ADR-0005 §13.3 spec on the server side now ships everything except (a) per-joiner HmacBloom de-dup (1.18d3) and (b) cross-session "active invites" enumeration for owners (1.18d2b-list, deferred behind auth-design choice). Together with the web-side flow (1.18a wire format, 1.18b wasm-bindgen surface, 1.18c UI, 1.18d1b client switch, 1.18d2a-web consume, 1.18d2b-web revoke), invite-link-driven contact onboarding is **end-to-end functional** for the same-session web flow. Mobile parity (1.14 / 1.15) reads the same surface.
- **1.18d3a** ✅ Per-joiner consume-dedup tag — protocol foundation. New `lakko_contacts::invite::compute_joiner_tag(capability_bytes, joiner_account_id) -> [u8; 32]`. Two-stage construction: salt `= SHA-256(JOINER_TAG_SALT_DOMAIN_V1 || capability_bytes)` (per-blob unique, derived deterministically from the capability so no wire-format bump), tag `= HMAC-SHA-256(salt, joiner_account_id)` (server-blind — server never sees plaintext `joiner_account_id`, only opaque 32-byte tags per blob). Domain prefix `b"lakko-invite-bloom-salt/v1\x00"` is versioned for future scheme bumps (e.g. switching to HMAC-BLAKE3). **Properties**: per-blob unique (same joiner across two invites yields two unrelated tags — server cannot link consume events), server-blind (tag is one-way HMAC, no reverse to `joiner_account_id`), deterministic (re-consume from the same joiner conflicts on the side-table PK in 1.18d3b), no wire-format bump (salt derives from already-public `capability_bytes`). **Privacy ceiling documented**: an attacker holding the capability AND a candidate `joiner_account_id` can recompute the tag and submit it; the server's PK conflict gives a probing oracle. PoW + rate limits mitigate at scale; full privacy against a determined attacker requires a server-blind OPRF step deferred per ROADMAP. **`lakko-crypto::hash::hmac_sha256`** added as a new pub helper next to `sha256` / `blake3` / `blake3_keyed`; takes `&[u8]` keys per RFC 2104 (HMAC accepts arbitrary key lengths — pre-hashed if longer than block, zero-padded if shorter). Three new tests in `lakko_crypto::hash::tests` (RFC 4231 test case 1 known vector, key-injection, message-injection); 32 → 35 tests in lakko-crypto. **`lakko-contacts::invite`** gains the helper + four new tests (`joiner_tag_is_deterministic`, `joiner_tag_changes_with_capability`, `joiner_tag_changes_with_account_id`, `joiner_tag_layout_pin` — pins domain prefix bytes + length + JOINER_TAG_LEN constant); 48 → 52 tests in lakko-contacts. **`wasm.computeJoinerInviteTag(capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId) -> Uint8Array`** exposed in `lakko-ffi-wasm/src/contacts.rs`; argument-shape gate (16-byte joiner account_id required, capability slice unbounded — server-side ceiling enforces actual cap). Host smoke test (`compute_joiner_invite_tag_round_trip`) covers determinism + capability-injection + account-id-injection through the WASM wrapper; 46 → 47 tests in lakko-ffi-wasm. **Cross-implementation pin deferred**: a hex-encoded known-vector test (useful for future `apps/ios` / `apps/android` parity verification) needs `hex` as a workspace dep — adding it just for one test isn't worth the dep churn yet. Determinism + injection tests give strong drift-detection in the meantime. **Gates**: `cargo test --workspace` clean (lakko-crypto 35 / lakko-contacts 52 / lakko-ffi-wasm 47, +8 net), `clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` clean, `fmt --check` clean. **Subsequent sub-sprints**: 1.18d3b (server-side: migration `0006_invite_consumers.sql` for the side table, transactional `consume` handler with `INSERT INTO invite_consumers ... ON CONFLICT → already_consumed` branch + idempotent return), 1.18d3c (web client: `consumeInvite` accepts `capabilityBytes` + `joinerAccountId`, computes tag via WASM, surfaces a new `"already-consumed"` discriminated variant in the UI).
- **1.18d3b** ✅ Server-side per-joiner consume-dedup. New migration `services/discovery/migrations/0006_invite_consumers.sql`: `invite_consumers (blob_id BINARY(32), joiner_tag BINARY(32), consumed_at TIMESTAMP, PRIMARY KEY (blob_id, joiner_tag), FOREIGN KEY (blob_id) REFERENCES invites(blob_id) ON DELETE CASCADE)`. The PK shape is the dedup primitive — `INSERT IGNORE` against `(blob_id, joiner_tag)` returns `rows_affected == 0` for repeats. **FK CASCADE** is what gives free cleanup on owner-revoke (1.18d2b) or expiry-prune (1.18d2c) — consumer rows die with the invite, no extra sweeper logic needed. **Why side-table not bloom**: ADR-0005 §13.3 calls for an `HmacBloom`, but for Phase-1 invites with `max_uses ≤ 10` typical, a small exact-dedup side table is simpler than a bloom and avoids false-positive rejection of innocent joiners. Switching to bloom-on-disk later is a non-breaking storage swap; the wire protocol stays unchanged because the joiner-side helper already produces a 32-byte opaque tag (1.18d3a). **Wire format updates**: `ConsumeRequest` gains `joiner_tag: String` (base64 of 32 bytes); `ConsumeResponse` gains `already_consumed: bool`. **Handler rewrite**: single transaction wrapping `INSERT IGNORE INTO invite_consumers` + the existing 1.18d2a atomic UPDATE. Three terminal paths: (a) `rows_affected == 0` from the insert ⇒ joiner has consumed before ⇒ SELECT current `(used_count, max_uses)`, COMMIT, return 200 with `already_consumed = true` (counter NOT bumped); (b) insert succeeded + UPDATE bumped one row ⇒ COMMIT, return 200 with `already_consumed = false`; (c) insert succeeded but UPDATE bumped zero rows (concurrent exhaustion) ⇒ disambiguate via SELECT (404 on absent / expired, 410 on exhausted), ROLLBACK so the consumer-row from step (a) doesn't persist for an exhausted invite. **FK violation handling**: `INSERT IGNORE` on a non-existent parent `blob_id` returns the FK-constraint error directly (not silenced by IGNORE); we string-match `"foreign key constraint"` to fold into the same 404 the handler returns elsewhere — keeps the response model consistent with the rest of the surface (no row vs not-yours-to-consume vs expired all surface as 404). **Clippy `too_many_lines`**: the consume handler crossed the pedantic 100-line ceiling at 169 lines; extracting the dedup-insert / bump / disambiguation paths into helpers would scatter the open `tx` across functions and obscure rollback ordering, so an `#[allow(clippy::too_many_lines)]` with a clear-rationale comment is the better trade-off. **Tests** (1 new in `invite::tests`): `invite_consumers_migration_shape` pins the four migration invariants the consume handler relies on (table exists, `joiner_tag BINARY(32)` matches `JOINER_TAG_LEN`, PK on `(blob_id, joiner_tag)`, FK CASCADE on `invites.blob_id`); 25 → 26 in lakko-svc-discovery. **Coordinated wire-format break with 1.18d3c**: `joiner_tag` is now mandatory on the consume request — a 1.18d2a-web client (no tag in the body) hitting a 1.18d3b server gets a 400 BAD_REQUEST. Documented compromise; web client update lands in d3c immediately. **Gates**: `cargo build / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean. **Subsequent**: 1.18d3c (web client extends `consumeInvite` signature, computes the tag via WASM, surfaces a new `"already-consumed"` discriminated variant in the UI).
- **1.18d3c** ✅ Web client + UI — closes the per-joiner consume-dedup loop end-to-end. **Wire types** in `discovery/wire.ts`: `InviteConsumeRequest` gains `joiner_tag: string` (mandatory base64 of 32 bytes); `InviteConsumeResponse` gains `already_consumed: boolean`. **`DiscoveryClient.consumeInvite` signature breaking change**: `(blobId, capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId) -> ConsumeInviteResult`. The web client now computes the dedup tag locally before any network work via `wasm.computeJoinerInviteTag(capabilityBytes, joinerAccountId)` (1.18d3a), POSTs `{ pow, joiner_tag }` to the server, and folds the new `already_consumed` flag into the discriminated `ConsumeInviteResult` as a fourth variant: `{ kind: "consumed" | "already-consumed", usedCount, maxUses }` plus the existing `{ kind: "exhausted" }` and `{ kind: "not-found" }`. Argument-shape gates: 32-byte blob_id + 16-byte joiner account_id (server-side ceiling enforces the capability size). The `joinerAccountId` never crosses the wire — only the opaque HMAC tag does. **`Invite.tsx::ConnectPanel`** rewires Accept: pulls `auth.identitySecrets()?.accountId` (with a defensive null-check that the outer Show wrapper already guarantees), passes `decoded.capabilityBytes` (new field on `DecodedInvite` — captured from either the server's `fetchInvite` body for the pointer path or the slug-decoded bytes for the inline back-compat path) plus the joiner's account_id into `consumeInvite`. New `"already-consumed"` terminal state on the existing `exhausted()` signal — reuses the locked-panel pattern but with a `form-notice` banner ("You've already accepted this invite — your ContactRequest is on its way to **{label}**") instead of `form-error`, and crucially **skips the `sendContactRequestToBundle` call** entirely so the joiner doesn't accidentally re-spam the inviter's stranger inbox with a duplicate envelope. Decline still works on all four locked states. **Bundle delta**: app JS 34.1 → 34.4 kB gz (+0.3 kB for the new variant + banner + accountId threading); WASM 741.0 → 741.5 kB gz (+0.5 kB for `computeJoinerInviteTag` re-export, already counted at d3a — re-checked after rebuild); total 767.8 → 769.1 kB gz, 1.28 MB headroom under the 2 MB budget. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + post-build SRI/CSP + sw post-pass; new BUILD_VERSION = `29c06bc7a7e4`), `cargo check / clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` all clean. **Closes 1.18 entirely.** End-to-end invite-link flow now ships: capability codec (1.18a) → wasm-bindgen surface (1.18b) → web UI for generation + landing (1.18c) → server blob storage with content-addressing (1.18d1a) → pointer URLs with inline back-compat (1.18d1b) → `max_uses` enforcement (1.18d2a) → owner-signed revocation (1.18d2b) → expiry pruner (1.18d2c) → per-joiner HMAC dedup with server-blind `joiner_tag` (1.18d3). What ADR-0005 §13.3 specifies but Phase-1 still defers: `owner_transparency_proof` (KT-log inclusion — blocked on `lakko-transparency` end-to-end), cross-session "active invites" enumeration (1.18d2b-list — blocked on auth-design choice between session token vs signed query), an OPRF-based server-blind variant of the consume-probing oracle the current `joiner_tag` design admits (deferred — current PoW + rate-limit mitigation is acceptable for Phase 1). **Mobile parity** (1.14 / 1.15) reads the same surface — the wire format, the WASM-shape helpers, and the discriminated client API all stay byte-identical when iOS/Android wire up via UniFFI.
- **1.18i** ✅ Contacts-cache integration for member display — hex fingerprints in the 1.18h members panel get replaced with real `displayName`s when the leaf's signature pubkey matches an `AcceptedContact` in the 1.16d3d local store. New `acceptedByPubkey` `createMemo` in `Chat.tsx::ChatView`: subscribes to the existing `membersVersion` signal (re-evaluates on the same cadence as `members` — every refresh, addMember, removeMember), reads `loadAccepted()` from `contacts/local-store`, builds a `Map<signaturePubkeyB64, displayName>`. Member-row render: lookup `base64Encode(m.sigPubkey)` against the map; render the `displayName` when found, fall back to `shortFingerprint(m.sigPubkey)` otherwise. The lookup key matches `local-store.ts`'s canonical `signaturePubkeyB64` (standard-alphabet base64 of the 32-byte Ed25519 verifying key) — both sides converge on the same encoding so no normalisation drift. **Cost**: one localStorage read per `membersVersion` bump (sub-millisecond). **Coverage**: only accepted-via-ContactRequest contacts get a name — somebody who joined a group we're in but never went through our accept flow still gets a hex fingerprint (intentional — we don't auto-trust everyone in a group we share). Cross-tab safety: if the user accepts a ContactRequest in a sibling tab on `/contacts`, the next refresh in this tab re-reads localStorage and surfaces the new name without a hard reload. **Bundle delta**: app JS 36.2 → 36.3 kB gz (+0.06 kB — single memo + Map build + lookup expression); total 772.0 → 772.1 kB gz, 1.27 MB headroom under 2 MB budget. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` / `npm run build` / `cargo workspace check / clippy -D warnings / fmt-check / test --workspace` all clean (no Rust touched). **Open follow-ups in the same arc**: chat-label updates (currently `props.entry.label` is set at group-create time as "Group with @bob" and never refreshes; once the group becomes 3-person via addMember the label is misleading — fix wants the same accepted-contacts lookup at the header level, deferred to a polish pass once we have a real "rename group" UX); resolving members against the discovery-side `identity_bundle` for not-yet-accepted leaves (would require a network round-trip per unknown leaf and a server-side reverse lookup `sig_pubkey -> account_id` — wider sprint, blocked on the auth-design decision behind the deferred 1.18d2b-list).
- **1.18h** ✅ Member management — list + remove. Closes the open follow-ups noted in 1.18g (the "Remove member" + "member list display" items). **New `lakko-mls::MemberRecord`** struct: `{ leaf_index: u32, signature_public_key: Vec<u8> }`. **New `Group::members() -> Vec<MemberRecord>`**: one-shot allocation over OpenMLS's `inner.members()` iterator (member sets bounded by application, ≤ 1k Phase-1 groups per ROADMAP — Vec is cheaper than streaming protocol through wasm-bindgen). Ordering matches OpenMLS internal traversal (left-to-right over leaves, stable inside epoch); callers MUST NOT cache leaf_index past a `process_incoming` Commit — remove/add cycle renumbers leaves. **New integration test** `members_enumerates_leaves_at_current_epoch`: one-of-one → length 1, Alice at index 0; after `add_member_from_wire` → length 2, Alice 0 + Bob 1. `cargo test -p lakko-mls` 19 → 20. **New `wasm.GroupMember` class**: `leafIndex: u32` + `signaturePublicKey: Uint8Array` getters. **`Group.members() -> any[]`**: returns `Vec<JsValue>` of `GroupMember` instances (wasm-bindgen doesn't let getters return `Vec<CustomStruct>` directly through the macro, so it's a regular method `members()` not a getter). **New wasm-bindgen-test** (1.16f3 Node runner) `group_members_returns_one_entry_for_a_fresh_group`: pins the array-shape contract (`.len() === 1`, non-null/undefined entries, `members.len()` agrees with `memberCount`). The full per-member field round-trip stays on the apps/web side — inside the test target wasm-bindgen doesn't auto-impl `JsCast` for the inner struct, so `dyn_into::<GroupMember>()` doesn't compile under `cargo build --tests`; the JS layer can dyn_into at runtime. `wasm-pack test --node` 4 → 5. **New `ContactsClient.removeMemberFromGroup(identity, group, leafIndex) -> { commitMessageId }`**: symmetric to `addMemberToGroup` minus the Welcome step (the kicked member has no Welcome to receive — their leaf is gone; they learn they're out by failing to decrypt the next commit on their own poll). Resolves canonical Identity, calls `group.removeMember(identity, leafIndex)`, broadcasts the commit via `ds.publishGroup`, `persistBestEffort()`s the new state. Caller registers `commitMessageId` with `chat.markOwnPublishedCommit` to suppress the echo (same pattern as 1.18g). **`Chat.tsx::ChatView`** gains: `membersOpen` toggle signal + `membersVersion` counter, `members()` createMemo that re-reads `props.entry.group.members()` whenever the version bumps (Solid signal-tracking trick — JSX expressions only subscribe to signal reads, not WASM property accesses); a "Members" / "Hide members" header button next to "Add member"; a collapsible `<section aria-label="Members">` panel listing every leaf with a `#leafIndex · <12-hex-prefix-fingerprint>` label + per-row Remove button (own-leaf row shows "(you)" instead — can't kick yourself); an `onRemoveMember(leafIndex)` handler with a `window.confirm` gate (destructive operation, deserves a one-click pause), drives `contacts.removeMemberFromGroup` + `markOwnPublishedCommit` + memberCount/membersVersion bump + `refresh()`. Version counter also bumps on every `refresh()` success so a remove/add commit from another member updates the displayed leaf set on the next poll. New `shortFingerprint(pubkey)` helper at file-bottom — 12-hex-char prefix, mirror of the same helper in `Invite.tsx::shortFingerprintFor`. If a third call site shows up, fold both into `contacts/local-store.ts`'s `fingerprintShort` (currently base64-based; this one is hex-based for the leaf-list typeface). **Receive side unchanged**: `pollMessages` already processes incoming Commits (1.16e4); the post-poll `setMemberCount` + `setMembersVersion(v+1)` propagate the new state to the UI without any new ChatClient plumbing. **Bundle delta**: app JS 35.6 → 36.2 kB gz (+0.6 kB — `removeMemberFromGroup` + members signal + Remove handler + members panel rendering + `shortFingerprint`); WASM 7.47 → 7.55 kB gz (+0.08 kB for `Group.members` + `GroupMember` exports in release+LTO); total 770.7 → 772.0 kB gz (+1.3 kB; 1.27 MB headroom under the 2 MB budget). **Tests**: `cargo test --workspace` clean (lakko-mls 19→20, lakko-ffi-wasm host count unchanged), `wasm-pack test --node` 4→5. `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean, `just lint -D warnings / fmt-check` clean. **Open follow-ups**: "Create group with N from scratch" (multi-recipient form on `/contacts` — currently only 1-on-1 helper; needs sequential addMember calls + Welcome bundling; separate sprint), self-removal / "leave group" (kicking yourself isn't supported by `removeMember` — caller would need a separate `MlsGroup::leave` proposal which isn't yet wired through `lakko-mls`; deferred), member-list integration with the 1.16d3d contacts cache so accepted contacts display by their stored `displayName` instead of a hex fingerprint (improves UX once a real "Bob" handle exists for the leaf, currently we only have the pubkey).
- **1.18g** ✅ Multi-user groups UI — exposed the N-participant MLS capability the Rust core has supported since 1.13a. **Code state pre-1.18g**: `lakko-mls::Group::add_member` / `remove_member` worked end-to-end (`ffi_three_party_lifecycle` integration test had been green since the start of Phase 1); `wasm.Group.addMember` / `wasm.Group.removeMember` were exposed on the wasm-bindgen surface; **but** the only call site in `apps/web` was `ContactsClient.startGroupWith` which intentionally dropped the commit (`void added.commit`) because at create-time the group is one-of-one with no existing members to fan out to. Multi-user groups therefore existed in the Rust core but couldn't be created or extended through the web UI. **New `ContactsClient.addMemberToGroup(identity, group, accountId, sigPubkey)`**: fetches the new member's KeyPackage from `/discovery/keypackage`, resolves the canonical Identity (1.16e6c2 — same instance every Group on this device shares), runs `group.addMember(myIdentity, keyPackageBytes)` against the **existing** group, **broadcasts the commit** via `ds.publishGroup(group.groupId, commitBytes)` (the step `startGroupWith` skips), ships the Welcome to the new member's stranger-inbox via the existing `sendWelcome`, then `persistBestEffort()`s the post-add provider state (new leaf added to the ratchet tree, epoch keys rotated, the consumed KeyPackage's init key pruned from local storage). Returns `{ commitMessageId, welcomeMessageId }`. **Convenience wrapper** `addMemberToGroupByUsername` resolves `@username` via discovery first (mirror of `startGroupWithUsername`). **New `ChatClient.markOwnPublishedCommit(messageId)`**: registers a DS id with the existing `ownPublishedIds` filter so the sender's own next `pollMessages` drops the echo. Without this the sender would hit `processIncoming` on a commit they've already applied locally, log a noisy "cannot process own outgoing commit at the same epoch" warning, and render a phantom system-row in the timeline. Crosses the client boundary cleanly: ContactsClient owns the addMember flow (discovery + Welcome), ChatClient owns the dedup registry — a thin accessor keeps the surfaces independent. **`Chat.tsx::ChatView`** gains: a `ContactsClient` instance wired with the same canonical-Identity + persist plumbing as ChatClient (both call `chats.persistSnapshot` after every mutation); `addMemberOpen` / `addMemberInput` signals for the inline form; a new "Add member" button in the chat header (cycles open / cancel); an inline form (`@username` input + Add submit) that appears when toggled and collapses after a successful add or explicit cancel; an `onAddMember` handler that drives `addMemberToGroupByUsername` + `markOwnPublishedCommit` + `setMemberCount(group.memberCount)` + post-add `refresh()` to flush the cursor past the suppressed commit; a `memberCount` reactive signal that mirrors `props.entry.group.memberCount` so the header re-renders after every add (Solid's fine-grained reactivity only tracks signal accesses, not arbitrary object property reads — the WASM-side memberCount getter had to be lifted into a signal). The signal also updates on `refresh()` success so an incoming commit from a different sender (someone else added a third party) advances the displayed count on the next poll. **Receive side unchanged**: existing `pollMailbox` → `joinGroupFromWelcome` (1.16e3) already handles Welcomes for groups of any size — the new member just lands at the higher epoch. Existing members' `pollMessages` already processes Commits via `group.processIncoming` (1.16e4) and surfaces them as `{kind:"system", systemKind:"commit"}` rows in the timeline. No new ChatClient receive plumbing needed. **Auto-persist coverage**: `addMemberToGroup` calls `persistBestEffort()` after the network publishes, same shape as `startGroupWith`. The post-add canonical-Identity state (new leaf, rotated epoch keys, pruned KeyPackage init) gets written to IndexedDB; the next reload's `rehydrateMlsState` brings the group back at the same epoch on the sender's device. **Bundle delta**: app JS 35.0 → 35.6 kB gz (+0.6 kB — `addMemberToGroup` + `addMemberToGroupByUsername` + `markOwnPublishedCommit` + inline Add-member form + handler + `memberCount` signal); WASM unchanged (no new wasm-bindgen exports); total 770.2 → 770.7 kB gz, 1.28 MB headroom under the 2 MB budget. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean, `cargo workspace check / clippy -D warnings / fmt --check / test --workspace` clean (no Rust touched). **Open follow-ups**: a "Remove member" UI (Rust core has it; UI doesn't — `Group.removeMember` returns the wire-encoded commit, plumbing is symmetric to addMember and ~30 LoC of UI; deferred until a real "kick from group" UX shows up in user testing), a real member list display in the chat sidebar (currently the header just shows the count + own leaf index; rendering the full leaf table needs a per-leaf identity lookup against the contacts cache from 1.16d3d), a "Create group with multiple participants from scratch" flow on `/contacts` (currently only "Start group with @bob" 1-on-1; "Start group with @bob, @charlie, @dave" needs a multi-input form + sequential addMember calls + bundling the Welcomes — straightforward but a different sprint).
- **1.18f** ✅ Web polish pass 2 — SW update prompt + dark mode (bootstrap-referral auto-send deferred). **(1) SW "new version available" banner**. Pre-1.18f the SW silently auto-skipped-waiting via `self.skipWaiting()` in the install handler + an unconditional `postMessage({type:"SKIP_WAITING"})` from `register.ts` on the `installed` statechange. That was a footgun: the still-running page kept executing prior JS while the new SW served new bytes — mismatched-version invariants on cache reads. **Fix**: removed `skipWaiting()` from `public/sw.js` install (first install still progresses to active naturally because no prior worker; updates stay in `waiting`); rewrote `src/sw/register.ts` to dispatch a `lakko:sw-update-available` `CustomEvent<ServiceWorker>` on `window` instead of postMessaging directly; new `SW_UPDATE_EVENT` constant is the source-of-truth for the event name. Also covers the edge case where a prior load left `registration.waiting` set (user navigated away mid-update) — surfaces the banner on mount. **New `src/sw/SwUpdateBanner.tsx`** is a Solid component mounted once at the App.tsx root (overlays every route — auth shell, app shell, invite landing). Listens for `SW_UPDATE_EVENT`, renders a fixed-position toast at the bottom of the viewport with "A new version of Lakko is ready" + Reload button. On click: subscribes to `controllerchange` on `navigator.serviceWorker` (`{once: true}`), posts `SKIP_WAITING` to the waiting worker; SW activates → `controllerchange` fires → `window.location.reload()`. **(2) Dark mode**. `apps/web/src/styles.css` reshape: light tokens stay under `:root` + `:root[data-theme="light"]`; new dark token set under `:root[data-theme="dark"]` (`--surface-bg: #0e1014`, `--surface-fg: #e6e8eb`, WCAG-AA contrast against bg, `--accent` brightens to `#6a8cff` for AA against `--surface-elevated`, shadows step opacity up to 0.45). **System-preference fallback** via `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root:not([data-theme]) { ... } }` — fires only when the user hasn't set an explicit override. **Manual toggle** in `apps/web/src/ui/theme.ts`: three-state cycle `system → dark → light → system`, persisted to `localStorage["lakko.theme"]`, applied to `<html data-theme="...">`. `bootstrapTheme()` runs **before** Solid renders (in `main.tsx`) so the first frame doesn't flash the wrong theme. Cycle button in `AppShell`'s nav-footer renders `Theme: <current>` so the user sees the active state. **Token-clean hover overlay**: the previously-hardcoded `rgba(0,0,0,0.04)` on sidebar-link hover became `color-mix(in srgb, var(--surface-bg) 92%, var(--surface-fg))` — adapts to both themes (light: faint grey, dark: faint lighter-than-bg). `color-mix` lands in all browsers since 2023, ~96% support in 2026. **(3) Bootstrap-referral auto-send-on-signup — deferred**: would need to (a) plumb `?auto-accept=1` through the auth `?return=` chain, (b) re-fetch the capability after auth (joiner's `accountId` only exists post-signup), (c) auto-fire `consumeInvite` + `sendContactRequestToBundle` on the post-auth invite landing mount. Total ~50-70 LoC across 3 files + a "just-authed" timestamp signal in AuthContext to time-window the auto-accept. Defers cleanly to post-Phase-2 multi-device when session tokens exist anyway — at that point `?auto-accept=1` becomes "you have a fresh session token; redeem it" instead of an awkward time-window heuristic. **Bundle delta**: app JS 34.4 → 35.0 kB gz (+0.6 kB — SwUpdateBanner + theme.ts + AppShell toggle); CSS 0.95 → 1.06 kB gz (+0.13 kB — dark tokens + media query); sw.js 3.42 → 3.70 kB gz (+0.28 kB — longer doc comments); total 769.1 → 770.2 kB gz (+1.1 kB, 1.28 MB headroom under 2 MB). **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + post-build SRI/CSP + sw post-pass; new BUILD_VERSION embeds in sw.js), `cargo workspace` clean (no Rust touched). **Open follow-ups**: bootstrap-referral auto-send (notes above), real timeline polish (avatars / read receipts / typing indicators — separate sprint after we have design tokens for messaging widgets), SVG icon set replacing text-only nav (~6 routes worth of icons would be a pleasant pass but not blocking).
- **1.18e** ✅ Web tech-debt sweep — three contained polish items before moving to mobile. **(1) DevProbe hidden from sidebar**: the `/dev` route stays reachable by URL for the maintainer's self-debugging (renders raw 8-byte pubkey fingerprints + KeyPackage byte counts via `loadWasm` + `new wasm.Identity` + `new wasm.Group`) but the navbar link is gone — end-users won't wander into a smoke-surface page. Comment in `AppShell.tsx` documents the deliberate split. **(2) `Chat.tsx` folds inline base64url decode into shared helper**: the per-component `replace(/-/g, "+").replace(/_/g, "/")` + padding pattern from 1.16e5 is now `base64UrlDecodeNoPad(params.id)` — the helper landed in 1.18c alongside its encode-side sibling. Eliminates duplicate logic across `/chat/:id` and `/i/:slug`; both call sites converge on one tested decoder. **(3) Post-auth deep-link return for `/i/:slug`**: anonymous joiners who click "Sign up" / "Log in" from the invite landing previously lost the original URL — had to re-paste / re-click after auth. New `apps/web/src/auth/return-url.ts` carries the convention: `buildAuthUrlWithReturn(authPath, returnPath)` encodes `?return=<urlencoded-path>` into the auth-form link; `readReturnUrl(location)` validates the query (own-origin path only — defense against open-redirect via `?return=https://evil.com`) and falls back to `/` for absent / malformed / unsafe inputs. Wired into three sites: `NeedsAuthPanel` in `Invite.tsx` builds both buttons with the current `/i/:slug` as return, `Signup.tsx::onAcknowledge` (post-recovery-phrase) navigates to `readReturnUrl(location)` instead of unconditional `paths.home`, `Login.tsx::onSubmit` (post-OPAQUE success) does the same. **Threat model** documented in the helper: refuses `//` (protocol-relative URL) + `/\` (legacy IE parser edge) + anything not starting with `/` (off-origin redirect). Silent fallback over visible error — a malformed `?return=` is usually a stale link, not an attack we want to surface to the legitimate user. **Bundle delta**: app JS 34.4 → 34.4 kB gz (+0.02 kB, rounding noise — the three changes are tiny ESM additions); total 769.1 → 769.1 kB gz unchanged. **Gates**: `npm run typecheck` clean, `npm run build` clean, `cargo workspace check / clippy / fmt / test` all clean (no Rust touched).
- **1.18 (mobile)** ✅ iOS + Android invite-link flow. Shipped through the 1.14e7–e11b and 1.15e6–e11b sub-sprints alongside the host-app skeletons: capability generate / revoke / consume on both platforms, Universal Link / App Link deep-link routing into `InviteLandingScreen`, auto-Accept on `bootstrap_referral=true` invites (1.9b4 — iOS / Android already auto-accept every invite by construction), and the 1.18d2b-list Active Invites panel.
- **1.19** ✅ Apps: 1×1 chat — encrypted text + attachments via the storage service. **All sub-items (1.19a/b/c1-c5) shipped on all three clients — encrypted text bodies + attachment refs (encrypt locally → PUT to services/storage → ship the AttachmentRef wire bytes inline in the MLS application payload → recipient downloads + decrypts on demand), per-platform composer with picker / drag-drop (web only) / inline image previews (web; iOS+Android via the Open chip), the polish-layer per-file upload-progress indicator (web/iOS/Android), and MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES enforcement at both compose and receive sides.**
  - **1.19a** ✅ `lakko-attachments` crate — per-attachment encryption + application-message JSON wire envelope. **`encrypt_attachment(plaintext) -> EncryptedAttachment { ciphertext, key, nonce, blob_id }`**: fresh random 32-byte CEK + 12-byte nonce via `rand::OsRng`, ChaCha20-Poly1305 seal with AAD empty, SHA-256 over ciphertext → 32-byte content-addressed `blob_id`. `decrypt_attachment(ciphertext, &key, &nonce) -> plaintext` is the inverse. The CEK lives in `lakko_crypto::aead::AeadKey` (zeroize-on-drop); the in-function local `key_bytes` array is explicitly zeroized after move into the wrapper. **Wire shape**: `AttachmentRef { blob_id, key, nonce, mime?, size, name? }` — all byte fields base64-encoded standard alphabet with padding (mirror of the auth + discovery wire conventions); plaintext `size` (not ciphertext size) for UI surfacing. `from_encrypted(&enc, plaintext_size, mime, name)` constructs from an `EncryptedAttachment`; `decode_blob_id()` / `decode_key()` / `decode_nonce()` are the inverse boundary helpers (all return `AttachmentError::WireFormat` on bad base64 or wrong length). **Envelope**: `Envelope { v: 1, text, attachments }` is the canonical MLS Application message body — `compose(text, refs) -> Vec<u8>` serialises to JSON; `parse(bytes) -> (text, attachments)` deserialises with three fallback layers: (i) JSON v1 → `(text, attachments)`; (ii) JSON with unknown `v` → `("[unsupported v$N message]", [])` (legacy clients see a placeholder, not a mis-render); (iii) non-JSON bytes → bare UTF-8 lossy decode + empty attachments (forward-compat with pre-1.19 chat messages + simple test harnesses). `#[serde(default)]` on every `Envelope` field tolerates future v1.x additions (`reply_to`, `edit_of`, …) without bumping the wire version. **Hard ceiling**: `MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES = 50 MB` mirrors the `services/storage` blob-PUT ceiling; oversize plaintext fails at `encrypt_attachment` with `AttachmentError::TooLarge { got, max }` so the UI surfaces a clean "file too large" instead of round-tripping a doomed PUT. **Threat-model note in module doc**: per-attachment fresh keys (not MLS-exporter-derived) chosen because blobs are content-addressed and the same byte sequence needs to remain readable across MLS epoch advances + new-member adds — matches the message-key paradigm of `InviteCapability` (1.18a) + the 1×1 contact-request envelope (ADR-0005 §13). **Gates**: 11/11 unit tests (round-trip encrypt + decrypt, fresh-CEK-per-call distinct blob_ids, tampered-ciphertext rejection, oversize plaintext rejection, ref wire round-trip, envelope text-only round-trip, envelope omits-empty-attachments serialisation, envelope-with-attachments round-trip, bare-UTF-8 fallback, unknown-version placeholder, unknown-field tolerance), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo test --workspace` 0 failed. **What 1.19a does NOT ship**: UniFFI exposure (1.19b — wraps `encrypt_attachment` / `decrypt_attachment` / `compose` / `parse` for Swift + Kotlin), wasm-bindgen exposure (same 1.19b), platform-side storage HTTP clients for `PUT/GET /blob/<hex>` (1.19c — three small files: Swift URLSession, Kotlin Ktor, TS fetch), file-picker UI (`UIDocumentPicker` / `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` / `<input type=file>`), in-chat attachment rendering (image preview, MIME-icon fallback for non-image), upload progress indicators, thumbnail generation (post-MVP polish).
  - **1.19b** ✅ UniFFI + wasm-bindgen exposure of `lakko-attachments` primitives — every platform-side attachment send/receive path now has the encrypt/decrypt/compose/parse surface in its native idiom. **`crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/attachments.rs`** — four top-level `#[uniffi::export]` free functions (`encrypt_attachment` / `decrypt_attachment` / `compose_envelope` / `parse_envelope`) returning three new flat `#[uniffi::Record]` types (`EncryptedAttachment`, `AttachmentRef`, `ParsedEnvelope`) with `Vec<u8>` byte fields. **No opaque handles** — the inner `lakko_crypto::aead::AeadKey` wrapper (zeroize-on-drop) cannot survive the UniFFI boundary, so the FFI surface returns raw `Vec<u8>` per field and the caller manages lifetime; documented in the module doc-comment + cross-referenced from the `EncryptedAttachment` record. **Base64 hidden behind the FFI** — `AttachmentRef` carries raw bytes at the boundary, base64 conversion happens inside `compose_envelope` (encode) and `parse_envelope` (decode). Wrong-length byte fields rejected at `compose_envelope` with `FfiError::Attachment("blob_id length 16 != 32")` so a misbehaving caller fails fast before JSON serialisation. **`parse_envelope` never throws** — every malformed input collapses into one of the documented fallback paths (legacy bare-UTF-8 → text-only, unknown-version → `"[unsupported v$N message]"`, malformed individual refs → skipped silently while preserving the rest). **`FfiError::Attachment(String)`** variant added next to the existing `Auth` / `Mnemonic` / `Pow` / `Discovery` / `Contacts` / `Crypto` shapes; same elision posture (wrong-length, AEAD-tag-mismatch, oversize-plaintext, malformed-base64 collapse into one variant). **`crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/attachments.rs`** — wasm-bindgen mirror with three handle classes (`EncryptedAttachment`, `AttachmentRef`, `ParsedEnvelope`) + an `EnvelopeBuilder` for the multi-attachment compose path (wasm-bindgen does not export `Vec<NonCopyType>` across the JS boundary, so the builder pattern — `new builder(text)`, `builder.add(ref)*`, `builder.build()` — sidesteps the limitation cleanly). Getters return `Uint8Array` (TS `.d.ts` is auto-generated by wasm-bindgen as `Uint8Array`). `composeEnvelopeText(text)` convenience exists for the text-only case. `parseEnvelope(bytes)` returns a `ParsedEnvelope` with `text` getter + `attachmentCount` getter + an `attachment(i)` indexed accessor (since `Vec<AttachmentRef>` can't round-trip directly). **`js_name` attrs** spell every method in JS-idiomatic camelCase (`encryptAttachment` / `decryptAttachment` / `composeEnvelopeText` / `parseEnvelope` / `blobId` / `attachmentCount` / etc.); the Rust side keeps `snake_case`. **Why ChaCha20-Poly1305 via wasm and not WebCrypto** — documented in the module doc: `crypto.subtle` shipped AES-GCM in 2014 but never picked up ChaCha20-Poly1305; going through `lakko-attachments` keeps the algorithm identical across iOS (CryptoKit ChaCha20), Android (Bouncy Castle / Tink), and Web (the wasm wrapper). The 12-byte nonce, 32-byte CEK, and 16-byte tag layout is byte-identical everywhere — ciphertext encrypted on iOS decrypts on Web. **Gates**: `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 47/47 (39 prior + 8 new — encrypt/decrypt round-trip through FFI shape, wrong-length key/nonce rejection, compose/parse text-only round-trip, compose/parse with-attachment round-trip, wrong-length blob_id rejection, legacy-UTF-8 fallback, unknown-version placeholder); `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 52/52 (50 prior + 4 new — happy-path round-trips only; error-path tests are skipped per the documented "JsError::new panics on non-wasm targets" policy, error coverage stays in `lakko-attachments` + the apps/web wasm-bindgen-test suite); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-ffi-wasm` clean. **`*+*-at-line-start` clippy doc-list gotcha**: a wrapped-line continuation that began with `+ 32-byte CEK +` was mis-parsed as a markdown bullet-list item by `clippy::doc_lazy_continuation`; reworded to "The 12-byte nonce, 32-byte CEK, and 16-byte tag layout" to avoid the false-positive. **What 1.19b does NOT ship**: storage HTTP clients (`PUT /blob/<hex>` + `GET /blob/<hex>`) on the three platforms — those are small mechanical wrappers (Swift URLSession, Kotlin Ktor, TS fetch) but each platform has its own auth-header / progress-callback idiom, batched into 1.19c. UI integration (file pickers, attachment rendering, image previews) is also 1.19c. The bindings emitted into `apps/{ios,android}/.../uniffi/` via `just regen-bindings` will pick up the new types automatically next time the apps build; `apps/web/wasm/` regenerates on the next `npm run dev` / `npm run build` (wasm-pack invocation is part of vite's build pipeline).
  - **1.19c1** ✅ Storage HTTP clients on Swift / Kotlin / TS — thin `PUT/GET/HEAD /blob/<sha256_hex>` wrappers around `lakko-svc-storage` for every platform-side attachment send/receive path. **`apps/web/src/storage/client.ts`** — fetch-based `StorageClient.putBlob(blobId, ciphertext)` / `getBlob(blobId)` / `headBlob(blobId)`. `PUT` body is `application/octet-stream` (no JSON wrapping, no base64 inflation); accepts both 200 (idempotent re-upload) and 201 (first upload) as success. `GET` returns `Uint8Array | null` (null on 404); `HEAD` returns `Content-Length | null`. Vite dev-proxy entry added: `/api/storage` → `http://127.0.0.1:8083`. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Storage/StorageClient.swift`** — URLSession-based mirror with `async throws` shape, `Data?` returns for `getBlob` / `Int?` for `headBlob`. Same default-base-URL convention as `DsClient` (loopback `http://localhost:8083`). **`apps/android/.../storage/StorageClient.kt`** — Ktor + OkHttp mirror, `AutoCloseable`, `MockEngine`-friendly engine override. Android-emulator loopback alias `http://10.0.2.2:8083`. **TS-typing gotcha**: `fetch()`'s `BodyInit` type rejects `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` (newer TS distinguishes from `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>`); fixed via `ciphertext.slice()` — same trick `ds/client.ts` already uses on its publish path. **Tests**: 13 new host-side Android tests via `MockEngine` (lowercase-hex URL property, octet-stream content-type, 200 / 201 both success, 404 → null on GET / HEAD, hash-mismatch → `StorageHttpError(400, …)`, transport failure → `StorageNetworkError`, four fail-fast arg-validation gates). iOS uses `xcodegen`'s `sources: Lakko/` glob so the new file is picked up on the next `xcodegen generate`; web `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Gates**: `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean, `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean. **What 1.19c1 does NOT ship**: file-picker UI on the three platforms (1.19c2 web `<input type=file>`, 1.19c3 iOS `PhotosPicker`/`UIDocumentPickerViewController`, 1.19c4 Android `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`); in-chat attachment rendering (image preview, MIME-icon fallback for non-image); upload progress indicators; thumbnail generation. All deferred to the per-platform UI sub-sprints.
  - **1.19c2** ✅ Web attachment UI — file picker + send/receive + image preview wired into the 1×1 chat surface. **`apps/web/src/chat/attachments.ts`** — UI-layer bridge between three Phase-1.19 layers (browser `File` ↔ wasm `encryptAttachment`/`decryptAttachment`/`EnvelopeBuilder`/`parseEnvelope` ↔ `StorageClient` from 1.19c1). Public surface: `encryptAndUploadFile(file)` (reads bytes → wasm encrypt → storage PUT → returns `AttachmentRefBytes`), `downloadAndDecryptRef(ref)` (storage GET → wasm decrypt), `composeEnvelope(text, refs)` (wraps the `EnvelopeBuilder` builder-pattern dance), `parseEnvelope(bytes)` (wraps the wasm `parseEnvelope` index-based accessor pattern; collapses wasm `AttachmentRef` handles into plain-data `AttachmentRefBytes`), `formatAttachmentSize`, `isInlinePreviewable`, `saveDecryptedBytes`. **`AttachmentRefBytes` over wasm handles**: JS code passing refs around (component state, optimistic-bubble bookkeeping) wants plain `Uint8Array` + primitive fields rather than wasm handles with allocating getters; the converter pair is tiny and the handles are freed eagerly to keep peak memory bounded on long composer sessions. **`apps/web/src/chat/client.ts` rewire**: `IncomingChatEvent.application` carries `{ text, attachments[] }` from the JSON envelope; `plaintext` field preserved as a `text` alias for transitional call-site compatibility. `toEvent` is now async (resolves the wasm-cached promise on first call, synchronous thereafter); legacy pre-1.19 plain-UTF-8 messages collapse to `{ text: <utf-8 lossy>, attachments: [] }` via the wasm parser's documented fallback paths — existing bubbles render unchanged. **`apps/web/src/routes/Chat.tsx` composer**: new `pendingAttachments` signal staged below the text field; 📎 button drives a `<input type="file" multiple>` whose `onChange` runs `encryptAndUploadFile` sequentially (one-at-a-time so a fat upload doesn't fan out N parallel PUTs). Per-chip × removes a staged attachment; sequence aborts on first failure to surface a friendly error. **Composer send**: empty-and-empty rejected; text-only / attachment-only / both all valid. Outbound bubble carries `attachments` so the optimistic chips appear instantly. **Bubble render**: `<AttachmentChips>` strip below text. Each chip shows `name · size` plus actions: "Save" (download + decrypt + browser save dialog) on every chip, "Open" (inline-preview in a new tab via `URL.createObjectURL`) on images only (`image/jpeg|png|webp|gif`; SVG deliberately excluded — attacker-supplied SVG can carry JS). Cached object URLs persist across renders. **MIME sniff**: browser-provided `File.type` (empty string ⇒ `undefined` so the wire ref's optional field stays empty). **CSP unchanged**: `window.open(blob:url)` opens a new top-level browsing context governed by its own document CSP, not the parent's; `<a download>` save path is not CSP-gated. Inline `<img src="blob:">` would need a CSP loosen but we don't ship that surface yet. **Build delta**: WASM bundle 742→752 kB gz (+10 kB for `lakko-attachments` + 1.19b exposure picked up in the production wasm-pack output), app JS 24.6→39.6 kB gz (+15 kB for the chat-attachments bridge + storage client + composer UI). Total ~800 kB gz, comfortably under the < 2 MB Phase-1 ceiling. **Gates**: `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + post-build SRI/CSP injection + service worker manifest all green), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean. **What 1.19c2 does NOT ship**: iOS UI (1.19c3 — `PhotosPicker` / `UIDocumentPickerViewController`), Android UI (1.19c4 — `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT`), inline-image-thumbnail rendering inside the bubble itself (currently only "Open" in a new tab — `<img src="blob:">` inline preview would need a CSP `img-src` widening + a "tap-to-load" gesture for unsolicited images; deferred), drag-drop file picker (`<input type=file>` only; drag-drop is a one-screen polish item), upload-progress indicators (uploads are fast enough on local dev that the "Encrypting + uploading…" string suffices; real progress bar lands when production storage shows real latency).
  - **1.19c3** ✅ iOS attachment UI — PhotosPicker + file importer + send/receive + image preview wired into the per-thread `ChatScreen`. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/AttachmentBridge.swift`** — Swift mirror of the web `chat/attachments.ts` (1.19c2). Three flows: `encryptAndUpload(bytes:mime:name:storage:)` (bytes → UniFFI `encryptAttachment` → `StorageClient.putBlob` → returns a `LakkoCore.AttachmentRef`), `downloadAndDecrypt(ref:storage:)` (storage GET → UniFFI `decryptAttachment` → plaintext `Data`), `composeEnvelopeBytes(text:attachments:)` / `parseEnvelopeBytes(_:)` wrap the UniFFI free functions. Plus `formatSize`, `isInlinePreviewable`, `hex` UI helpers. Strict 50 MB ceiling mirrors `crates/lakko-attachments` + `services/storage`. **Naming conflict workaround**: hand-rolled `Lakko.parseEnvelope(_ bytes: Data) -> Lakko.ParsedEnvelope` (Contacts/Envelope.swift) co-exists with UniFFI's `LakkoCore.parseEnvelope(bytes: Data) -> LakkoCore.ParsedEnvelope`; Swift overload resolution disambiguates by argument label (`_` vs `bytes:`) and Records are unambiguous in pattern-match context. The bridge uses the UniFFI variant exclusively. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatClient.swift`**: `IncomingChatEvent.application` now carries `(messageId, text, attachments, createdAt)` — `plaintext` field renamed to `text` (no transitional alias on iOS because `Equatable` synthesis can't backfill the duplicate); `toEvent(...)` routes the application bytes through `AttachmentBridge.parseEnvelopeBytes` so legacy pre-1.19 plain-UTF-8 messages collapse cleanly via the parser's documented fallback paths. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/ChatRepository.swift`**: `ChatMessage` gains an `attachments: [AttachmentRef]` field (default `[]` keeps every existing call site source-compatible); `sendMessage(groupIdHex:plaintext:attachments:)` builds the envelope before `chatClient.sendMessage` — empty `attachments` ⇒ pure-text envelope; empty-and-empty rejected upstream by the view model. `GroupRecord.appendOutgoing` / `applyPollResult` plumb the attachments through to the in-memory timeline. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Chat/ChatViewModel.swift`**: new `@Published pendingAttachments: [AttachmentRef]` + `uploading: Bool`. `onAttachmentPicked(bytes:mime:name:)` async drives `AttachmentBridge.encryptAndUpload`, appends on success; sequential — the screen calls it once per picked item from a `Task { … }` so the upload-state flag stays honest. `onSendClicked` reads the staged refs, hands them to the repository, clears the tray on success, leaves on failure (so the user can retry without re-picking). `onRemovePendingAttachment(at:)` drops a chip. **`apps/ios/Lakko/UI/Chat/ChatScreen.swift`**: paperclip button next to the text field opens a `.confirmationDialog` ("Photo Library" / "File" / "Cancel"). Photo path uses iOS 17 `PhotosPicker` (multi-select, `matching: .images`) routed through a `photoPickerOpenRequested` `@State` (workaround for the modifier lacking a programmatic show-trigger); resolves each `PhotosPickerItem` to `(bytes, mime, name)` via `loadTransferable(type: Data.self)` + magic-byte image-format sniff (JPEG / PNG / GIF / WebP) for the MIME, synthetic `photo-<unixtime>.{jpg,png}` filename. File path uses `.fileImporter(allowedContentTypes: [.data], allowsMultipleSelection: true)` with security-scoped resource access; MIME from `UTType(filenameExtension:).preferredMIMEType`, filename from `url.lastPathComponent`. **Pending-attachments tray**: horizontal `ScrollView` of chips above the text field; per-chip × dismisses; "Encrypting…" spinner chip while `uploading` is true. **Bubble chips**: refactored `MessageRow.bubble` from a single `Text` into a `VStack` that conditionally renders the text + an attachment-chip column. New `AttachmentChip` private view: shows MIME-appropriate icon (`photo` for image MIMEs, `doc` otherwise) + name + size, plus a two-step Save (resolve via download/decrypt → write to temp file → hand to `ShareLink(item: URL)`) and — only for `isInlinePreviewable` MIMEs — an Open button that routes resolved bytes into a screen-level `previewImageData` `@State` driving an `.sheet` with a native `Image(uiImage:)` viewer. **Why the two-step Save**: `ShareLink` wants a transferable up front; for a downloaded blob the canonical iOS share-sheet shape is "Save to Files" via a `URL` to a temp file. First tap triggers the download, second tap hosts the `ShareLink`. Subsequent renders skip the resolve. **Equatable note**: `ChatMessage` + `IncomingChatEvent` synthesise `Equatable` over `[AttachmentRef]` — UniFFI 0.28 emits Swift Record structs with `Equatable, Hashable` extensions auto-generated, so synthesis works without a Rust-side `PartialEq` derive. Kotlin side: `data class` does the same job automatically. **Bindings refreshed**: `just regen-bindings` produces `target/bindings/kotlin/uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` carrying the new `data class AttachmentRef` / `EncryptedAttachment` / `ParsedEnvelope` types; the Android side picks them up automatically via the gitignored mirror under `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/kotlin/uniffi/`. iOS side regens via `just build-ios` at the next xcodebuild — `lakko_ffi_uniffi.swift` is gitignored (build artefact) per Phase-1.14b convention. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `just regen-bindings` clean. iOS / Android cannot be xcodebuild / gradle-tested from the host CLI in this environment (no xcodebuild / no gradle wrapper), so syntax correctness verified by careful read-through against the existing UniFFI / SwiftUI patterns + auto-formatting convention. **What 1.19c3 does NOT ship**: Android UI (1.19c4 — `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` + Compose chips + Coil image preview); inline-image thumbnails directly inside the bubble (current preview opens a sheet — inline `Image` thumbnails would need image-aspect-ratio bookkeeping and a lazy-load hook); upload-progress indicators (`URLSession.upload` doesn't surface progress without a delegate; the "Encrypting…" chip suffices for typical mobile upload sizes); thumbnail generation pre-send (sends full-resolution JPEG / PNG straight from PhotosPicker — fine for chat, would want a downscale step for a 50 MP camera dump but that's a polish item).
  - **1.19c4** ✅ Android attachment UI — `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` + send/receive + image preview wired into the per-thread `ChatScreen` Compose surface. **`apps/android/.../chat/AttachmentBridge.kt`** — Kotlin mirror of `AttachmentBridge.swift` (1.19c3). Three flows: `encryptAndUpload(bytes:mime:name:storage:)` (bytes → UniFFI `encryptAttachment` → `StorageClient.putBlob` → returns a `uniffi.lakko_ffi_uniffi.AttachmentRef`), `downloadAndDecrypt(ref:storage:)` (storage GET → UniFFI `decryptAttachment` → `ByteArray`), `composeEnvelopeBytes(text:attachments:)` / `parseEnvelopeBytes(_:)` wrap the UniFFI free functions. `ParsedEnvelopeView` plain-data type decouples the receive path from the UniFFI generated `ParsedEnvelope` so call sites don't pull the FFI namespace. Plus `formatSize`, `isInlinePreviewable`, `hex` UI helpers. Strict 50 MB ceiling mirrors `crates/lakko-attachments` + `services/storage`. Errors discriminated via `AttachmentBridgeException` sealed class (TooLarge / Encryption / Decryption / Upload / Download / Missing). **Import-rename trick**: `composeEnvelope` / `parseEnvelope` / `encryptAttachment` / `decryptAttachment` UniFFI free functions imported as `ffiComposeEnvelope` etc. via `import … as …` so the wrapper methods on `AttachmentBridge` can keep the same names without recursive self-references. **`apps/android/.../chat/ChatClient.kt`**: `IncomingChatEvent.Application` now carries `(messageId, text, attachments, plaintext, createdAt)` — `text` is the parsed envelope text, `attachments` the parsed refs, `plaintext` kept as a transitional alias of `text` so existing call sites keep working. `toEvent(...)` routes the application bytes through `AttachmentBridge.parseEnvelopeBytes` so legacy pre-1.19 plain-UTF-8 messages collapse cleanly via the parser's documented fallback paths. **`apps/android/.../chat/ChatRepository.kt`**: `ChatMessage` gains an `attachments: List<AttachmentRef>` field (default `emptyList()` keeps every existing call site source-compatible); `sendMessage(groupIdHex:plaintext:attachments:)` builds the envelope before `chatClient.sendMessage` — empty `attachments` ⇒ pure-text envelope; empty-and-empty rejected upstream by the view model. `GroupRecord.appendOutgoing` / `applyPollResult` plumb the attachments through to the in-memory timeline. **`apps/android/.../ui/chat/ChatViewModel.kt`**: `ComposerState` + `ChatScreenUiState` gain `pendingAttachments: List<AttachmentRef>` + `uploading: Boolean`. `onAttachmentPicked(bytes:mime:name:)` launches a coroutine that drives `AttachmentBridge.encryptAndUpload`, appends on success; `onSendClicked` reads the staged refs, hands them to the repository, clears the tray on success, leaves on failure (so the user can retry without re-picking). `onRemovePendingAttachment(at:)` drops a chip. **`apps/android/.../ui/chat/ChatScreen.kt`**: paperclip `TextButton` drives a `rememberLauncherForActivityResult(OpenMultipleDocuments())` launcher with MIME pattern `*/*` (covers both photos and arbitrary files through one entry point — keeps the surface compact for 1.19c4; a separate `PickVisualMedia`-driven photo route can be folded in later as a polish item). Per-pick URI is resolved off the main thread via `Dispatchers.IO` to raw bytes + MIME (`ContentResolver.getType`) + display name (`OpenableColumns.DISPLAY_NAME`) by `readPickedUri`; then handed to `viewModel.onAttachmentPicked`. **Pending-attachments tray**: horizontal-scrolling `Row` of `AssistChip` instances above the text field; each chip shows `name · size` with a × trailing icon to drop it. Plus an inline `CircularProgressIndicator` + "Encrypting…" label while `uploading` is true. **Bubble chips**: refactored `Bubble` from a single `Text` into a `Column` rendering the optional text + an optional `AttachmentChipRow` list. Each chip shows `name · size` plus a `Save` action (every chip) and an `Open` action (image MIMEs only). Save: downloads + decrypts off the main thread, writes the plaintext to `cacheDir/shared-attachments/<name>` and fires `Intent.createChooser(ACTION_SEND)` so the user routes to Files / Drive / Gmail / Telegram / etc. via the standard share sheet. Open: same download/decrypt path, then `BitmapFactory.decodeByteArray` off the main thread, surfacing the result through a screen-level `previewImage: ImageBitmap?` state that drives an `AlertDialog` carrying a Compose `Image`. **FileProvider plumbing**: `AndroidManifest.xml` gains a `<provider>` declaration scoped to `${applicationId}.fileprovider` (resolves to `me.lakko.dev.fileprovider` for the debug build) with `grantUriPermissions="true"`; new `res/xml/file_paths.xml` exposes the cache-dir subtree `shared-attachments/` as the only path. Android 7+ blocks `file://` URIs in `EXTRA_STREAM`; FileProvider's `content://` URI satisfies the OS, and the per-Activity grant carried by the chooser intent expires when the receiving app's process is killed — no explicit revoke needed. Cache-dir is the right backing store because the OS reclaims when storage runs low, matching the "transient share" semantic perfectly. **No new Gradle dep**: `androidx.core:core` (FileProvider's home) lands transitively through `activity-compose → activity-ktx → core-ktx`. No explicit version-catalog entry needed. **Equatable**: `ChatMessage` is a `data class` over `[AttachmentRef]` — UniFFI 0.28 emits Kotlin Records as `data class`es with auto-derived `equals`/`hashCode` over the byte fields, so the composite equality works without any manual override. **Bindings already in place**: 1.19c3's `just regen-bindings` pass laid down `data class AttachmentRef` / `EncryptedAttachment` / `ParsedEnvelope` in `apps/android/lakko-core/src/main/kotlin/uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi/lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt`. Android picks them up at the next `:app:assembleDebug`. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean. Android `:app:assembleDebug` not runnable from this CLI (no gradle wrapper / no Android SDK); syntax verified against the existing AndroidX picker / FileProvider / Compose Material3 idioms + the auto-formatting convention. **Closes 1.19c**: all four sub-sprints (storage HTTP clients on three platforms + per-platform UI for Web / iOS / Android) shipped. The whole 1.19 line (`lakko-attachments` crate → UniFFI + wasm exposure → storage HTTP clients → per-platform UI integration) is now feature-complete; what's missing is dogfood-driven polish (drag-drop on Web, photo-picker route on Android, upload-progress on all three, thumbnail generation pre-send for the camera-dump case), which lands when real users surface real friction. **What 1.19c4 does NOT ship**: dedicated photo-picker route (`PickVisualMedia` contract) — `ACTION_OPEN_DOCUMENT` covers the surface for 1.19c4; can be added as a polish item if photo-specific UX (Photos library vs Files browser) becomes friction; drag-drop file picker; upload-progress indicators (typical mobile upload sizes don't surface friction without one); thumbnail generation pre-send (sends full-resolution from the picker — fine for chat, would want a downscale step for a 50 MP camera dump but that's a polish item).
- **1.20** ✅ Apps: push notifications — APNs (iOS) / FCM (Android) / Web Push VAPID (browsers); encrypted envelopes everywhere. **All sub-items (1.20a/b/c-c2/d-d2/e) shipped — services/push-gw with three pluggable providers (APNs / FCM / Web Push), per-platform device-token registration + key rotation handshake against /push-gw, opaque wake envelopes that do NOT carry decryptable content (just a routing pseudonym + optional cursor pointer; the client wakes up + polls the mailbox over its own E2EE stack), iOS UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate routing + 1.20c2 UNNotificationServiceExtension for thread-grouping on the lock screen, Android FirebaseMessagingService + 1.20d2 notification-tap deep-linking into the matching chat thread, and Web SW push handler. Provider key provisioning (real APNs auth key / FCM service account / VAPID keypair) is operator-side work — services boot with empty key slots in dev and skip the actual fan-out, the wire-level integration is gated by the wake-envelope unit tests rather than by reachability of the upstream providers.**
  - **1.20a** ✅ Client-side push wire audit + Service Worker `push` / `notificationclick` handlers. **`apps/web/public/sw.js`**: replaced the 1.16f2 no-op `push` stub with a real `parseWakeBody(event.data)` → JSON decode (matches `WakeBody { v, d, g?, p? }` from `services/push-gw/src/providers/webpush.rs`) → fan-out to open tabs via `clients.postMessage({type: "LAKKO_PUSH_WAKE", wake})` → `showNotification("Lakko", { body: "New message", tag: groupHex, data: { wake } })`. **Content-free preview**: the SW deliberately does NOT attempt to decrypt the MLS message itself — doing so would need the device key + cached MLS group state + the WASM glue, running inside a ~30 s SW budget. Generic "New message" suffices; foreground tabs handle the real preview via the postMessage wake. **`notificationclick` handler**: closes the OS notification, attempts to focus an existing tab (URL.startsWith(origin)), falls back to `clients.openWindow("/?wake=<groupHex>")` so the SPA's deep-link reader can route to the relevant chat. **API_PREFIXES extension**: `/api/storage` + `/api/push` added so neither path gets stale-cached (mirror of the 1.19c1 / 1.20b proxy additions). **Vite proxy**: `/api/push` → `127.0.0.1:8085` matching `services/push-gw`'s default bind. **Audit finding**: server-side `/push/register`, `/push/unregister`, `/push/notify` plus all four `Provider` implementations (APNs / FCM / WebPush / UnifiedPush) shipped in 1.6b — the client side just needed the registration plumbing + SW handler, not new server endpoints. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` + `npm run build` all clean.
  - **1.20b** ✅ Web push subscribe orchestrator wired into the auth flow. **`apps/web/src/push/client.ts`** — `PushClient` HTTP wrapper for `/push/register` + `/push/unregister` + `/pow/challenge`, mirror shape of the Auth + Discovery clients (typed errors `PushClientArgError` / `PushHttpError` / `PushNetworkError`, snake_case `ProviderKind` matching `services/push-gw/src/provider.rs`'s serde rename). **`apps/web/src/push/device-id.ts`** — per-device 32-byte push pseudonym, generated fresh on first subscribe via `crypto.getRandomValues`, persisted in `localStorage["lakko.push.device.v1"]`. Fresh-random (not identity-derived) keeps the server from correlating identity uploads with push registrations; `forgetDevice` clears it alongside the rest of the per-device material. **`apps/web/src/push/subscribe.ts`** — `subscribeToPush()` orchestrator joins three layers: `navigator.serviceWorker.ready` → `Notification.requestPermission` → `PushManager.subscribe({userVisibleOnly: true, applicationServerKey: VAPID_PUBLIC})` → `PushClient.register(devicePseudonym, "web_push", subscriptionJson)`. Idempotent via `getSubscription()` reuse — re-running on a returning user gets the same `PushSubscription` and just re-UPSERTs the server row. Returns `null` (no throw) for the four expected no-go conditions: no build-time VAPID key, no SW support, no PushManager, permission denied. **`unsubscribeFromPush()`** — server-side `/push/unregister` + browser-side `subscription.unsubscribe()` + `clearDevicePushId()`. Best-effort on every leg. **AuthContext wire**: `signup` / `login` / `recover` each fire `void subscribeToPush().catch(…)` after the state flip (non-blocking — auth succeeds even if push fails). `logout` fires `void unsubscribeFromPush().catch(…)`. `forgetDevice` `await`s `unsubscribeFromPush()` to honour its "leave NOTHING per-account behind" contract. **VAPID key plumbing**: build-time `VITE_LAKKO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY_B64URL`. Missing key ⇒ orchestrator returns `null`, app keeps working without push (right posture for any contributor not actively iterating on push). New `apps/web/.env.example` documents the toggle + the `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys` recipe; workspace `.env.example` cross-links to the apps/web file. **TS-typing gotcha**: `PushManager.subscribe`'s `applicationServerKey` rejected `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` in TS 5.6 (same `BodyInit` problem the 1.19c1 storage client hit); fixed via `.slice()` to reify a fresh `ArrayBuffer`. **Build delta**: app JS 39.6→40.6 kB gz (+1 kB for PushClient + subscribe + device-id), WASM unchanged at 752 kB gz. Total ~800 kB gz, comfortably under the < 2 MB Phase-1 ceiling. **Gates**: `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + SRI/CSP injection + service worker manifest), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean. **What 1.20b does NOT ship**: iOS UI (1.20c — `UNUserNotificationCenter` registration + APNs token capture + `UNNotificationServiceExtension` for the wake decrypt path); Android UI (1.20d — `FirebaseMessagingService` + FCM token capture); server-side per-group device-pseudonym fan-out (peers' clients calling `/push/notify` with the right pseudonym when they publish to a group — needs a DS-side fan-out registry or a client-side address book of group-member-device-pseudonyms; deferred sub-sprint of its own).
  - **1.20c** ✅ iOS APNs registration + AppDelegate adaptor + AuthRepository wire-in. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Push/PushClient.swift`** — URLSession + async/await HTTP wrapper for `/push/register` + `/push/unregister` + `/pow/challenge`. Mirror shape of the auth + discovery clients (typed `PushClientError.{argument,http,network}`). `Provider` enum with `rawValue` mapped to the snake_case wire form the server expects (`apns`, `fcm`, `web_push`, `unified_push`). Lowercase-hex device-pseudonym encoding helper to keep one canonical form across the iOS client + the server's `validate_hex` check. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Push/DevicePushId.swift`** — per-device 32-byte push pseudonym persisted in the iOS Keychain. `kSecAttrService = "me.lakko.push"`, `kSecAttrAccount = "device-id-v1"`, `kSecAttrAccessible = kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlock` (APNs callbacks fire pre-unlock on cold boot). Fresh `SecRandomCopyBytes(32)` on first call; subsequent calls return the cached bytes. `kSecAttrSynchronizable` left at default `false` — iPad alongside iPhone generates an independent pseudonym; each registers its own token row server-side. Race-safe `errSecDuplicateItem` → `SecItemUpdate` fallback. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Push/PushSubscriber.swift`** — `@MainActor`-isolated orchestrator joining three layers: `UNUserNotificationCenter.requestAuthorization([.alert, .badge, .sound])` → `UIApplication.registerForRemoteNotifications` + parked `CheckedThrowingContinuation` → `PushClient.register(devicePseudonym, .apns, tokenBytes)`. The continuation is the actor-isolated bridge from the UIKit AppDelegate callback (which is the OS's only path for token delivery) into the async/await surface. At most one register in flight at a time — a second call while one is parked surfaces `PushSubscribeError.concurrentSubscribe`. `unsubscribe()` runs the server `/push/unregister` (best-effort, log+continue) + `DevicePushId.clear()`. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Push/PushAppDelegate.swift`** — tiny `NSObject` `UIApplicationDelegate` adaptor that forwards `application(_:didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken:)` + `application(_:didFailToRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithError:)` into `PushSubscriber.shared` via `Task { @MainActor in … }`. Also claims the `UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate` so foreground pushes surface as banners (`[.banner, .sound, .badge]`) instead of being silently dropped. **Wired into `LakkoApp` via `@UIApplicationDelegateAdaptor(PushAppDelegate.self)`** — SwiftUI's `App` protocol doesn't surface the APNs callbacks; the adaptor is Apple's blessed pattern for SwiftUI-only apps that need UIKit lifecycle hooks. **AuthRepository wire**: `signup` / `login` / `recover` each fire `Task { await Self.subscribePushBestEffort(context: "…") }` after the state flip — best-effort with errors logged via `NSLog`, never thrown past the auth flow (right posture: a user without push permission still gets a working session). `logout` fires `Task { await PushSubscriber.shared.unsubscribe() }`. `forgetDevice` also fires `unsubscribe()`; the local `clear()` inside the subscriber runs regardless of the server leg outcome so the Keychain pseudonym is always wiped. **Entitlements**: `apps/ios/project.yml` `aps-environment: development` added — `registerForRemoteNotifications` fails immediately without it ("no valid 'aps-environment' entitlement string found"). Production builds will flip to `production` via a release-only entitlement override; for local dev the sandbox endpoint is the right route. **No iOS extension target yet**: the `UNNotificationServiceExtension` that decrypts MLS messages before the OS displays them is deferred to a 1.20c2 sub-sprint. The extension would need a separate xcodegen target, an App Group for shared Keychain access, and a wasm-pack output target other than the regular `LakkoCore.xcframework` (extensions can't link the full `LakkoCore` because of memory + bundle-size limits on the extension process). For 1.20c the OS displays a generic alert title from the APNs payload; the SPA-style decrypt happens when the user taps and the main app comes to foreground. **Gates**: `swiftc -parse` on every new + edited Swift file clean (full xcodebuild not runnable from this CLI without a checked-in `xcodeproj` — xcodegen would generate one but isn't installed); `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. **What 1.20c does NOT ship**: Android (1.20d), `UNNotificationServiceExtension` for MLS-decrypt-before-display (1.20c2 follow-up), foreground deep-link routing from `userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)` to a specific chat (1.20c2 — needs a `PushIntake` analog of the existing `InviteIntake`), silent push for background MLS catch-up (needs `UIBackgroundModes` + the extension target; deferred).
  - **1.20c2** ✅ iOS `UNNotificationServiceExtension` — APNs alert shape + extension target + App Group entitlement. Lock-screen notifications now group per conversation (instead of piling under one "Lakko" bucket). **APNs provider switch** (`services/push-gw/src/providers/apns.rs`): the payload moves from a silent push (`aps.content-available: 1` only) to an alert push with `aps.alert.{title, body}` + `mutable-content: 1` + `thread-id: <group_hex>`. `mutable-content: 1` tells iOS to invoke the NSE before display; `thread-id` is the OS's notification-grouping key (extension can override but having it pre-populated means grouping still works when the NSE is unavailable — disabled in Settings, killed under memory pressure, etc.). Body copy stays a generic placeholder `"New message"` — the NSE can rewrite it post-mutation; foreground / no-NSE paths see the same fallback the existing 1.20c code uses. Test rename: `aps_body_serializes_silent_push_shape` → `aps_body_serializes_nse_alert_shape` + a new `aps_body_omits_thread_id_when_group_absent` regression. 25/25 push-gw tests. **New extension target** (`apps/ios/LakkoNotificationService/`): `NotificationService.swift` subclasses `UNNotificationServiceExtension`, reads `userInfo["g"]` for the group hex, sets `bestAttemptContent.threadIdentifier` + a `subtitle` of `"in <first-8-of-hex>"` so simultaneous conversations stay visually distinct on the lock screen. Mandatory `serviceExtensionTimeWillExpire` fallback delivers the unmodified `bestAttemptContent` when the 30 s budget runs out — same posture as Apple's canonical sample code (skipping the contentHandler is far worse than a generic preview). `Info.plist` declares the `com.apple.usernotifications.service` extension point with `$(PRODUCT_MODULE_NAME).NotificationService` as the principal class. **`project.yml` updates**: new `LakkoNotificationService` target with `type: app-extension`, bundle id `me.lakko.dev.LakkoNotificationService` (Apple requires a strict child of the host app), iOS 17 deployment floor matching the host; main `Lakko` target carries the extension as a `target: LakkoNotificationService` dependency (auto-embedded in `PlugIns/` via xcodegen's default `embed: true` for app-extension deps). **App Group entitlement** `group.me.lakko.dev.shared` added on both targets (host + extension) — required for the 1.20c2b decrypt path to read shared MLS state; not used yet in 1.20c2 itself but provisioned now so the App Store / signing infrastructure is set up once. Explicit `LakkoNotificationService.entitlements` committed alongside the auto-generated xcodegen output for reproducible-build pipeline + reviewer readability. **What 1.20c2 deliberately does NOT do**: MLS-decrypt-before-display. That requires (1) a slim `lakko-ffi-extension` xcframework — the full `LakkoCore.xcframework` blows past the extension's 30 MB memory budget; (2) migration of the MLS provider's persistence backend from per-bundle Keychain to App-Group-shared file storage (mirror writes from main app → extension reads); (3) canonical Identity bridging through the App Group keychain.
