All three are weeks of work; deferred to **1.20c2b**. In the meantime, the lock-screen grouping the `threadIdentifier` flip gives us is real user value — a chat that gets 5 messages in a minute shows as one stack instead of five separate banners. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p lakko-svc-push-gw` 25/25; `swiftc -parse` on `NotificationService.swift` clean. xcodebuild full-build not runnable from this CLI (xcodegen-generated `.xcodeproj` is gitignored per Phase-1.14b convention).
  - **1.20d** ✅ Android FCM registration + `FirebaseMessagingService` + `EncryptedSharedPreferences` pseudonym + AuthRepository wire-in. Closes 1.20. **No `google-services` Gradle plugin**: applying `com.google.gms:google-services` would require a checked-in `google-services.json` at build time. Instead Firebase is initialised programmatically at runtime via `FirebaseOptions.Builder()` populated from four `BuildConfig.FCM_*` string fields generated from Gradle properties (`lakko.fcm.projectId`, `lakko.fcm.applicationId`, `lakko.fcm.apiKey`, `lakko.fcm.senderId`). All four empty (the default) ⇒ `PushSubscriber` short-circuits and returns `null` — rest of the app keeps working without push. **`apps/android/.../push/PushClient.kt`** — Ktor + OkHttp mirror of `PushClient.swift` (1.20c) / `client.ts` (1.20b). `@Serializable` `Provider` enum with `@SerialName("apns"|"fcm"|"web_push"|"unified_push")` matching the server's snake_case `ProviderKind` rename. Typed `PushHttpError` / `PushNetworkError`. **`DevicePushId.kt`** — `EncryptedSharedPreferences` (AES-256-GCM value encryption, AES-256-SIV key encryption, master key in Android Keystore) wrapping a 32-byte pseudonym. Mirror of the iOS Keychain posture: at-rest-encrypted with hardware-backed keys; an attacker with raw disk access cannot read it. `SecureRandom().nextBytes(32)` on first call. **`PushSubscriber.kt`** — orchestrator: `buildFirebaseOptionsOrNull()` reads `BuildConfig.FCM_*` (returns `null` when any empty) → `ensureFirebaseInitialised` calls `FirebaseApp.initializeApp(context, options, "lakko")` (idempotent — looks up the named app first, inits only if absent) → `FirebaseMessaging.getInstance(firebaseApp).token.await()` (via `kotlinx-coroutines-play-services`'s `Task<T>.await()`) → `PushClient.register(.FCM, token.toByteArray(UTF_8))`. Every failure mode logs via `android.util.Log.w` and returns `null`; the caller treats `null` as "push not available". **`LakkoMessagingService.kt`** — `FirebaseMessagingService` subclass for the two callbacks: `onNewToken` re-registers via a `SupervisorJob`-rooted `Dispatchers.IO` scope (the FMS process lives only briefly for each invocation; we fire-and-forget and let the SDK's wakelock keep us alive). `onMessageReceived` parses the wake-body JSON from `RemoteMessage.data` (key `v == "1"`, `d`, `g?`, `p?` — matching `services/push-gw/src/providers/fcm.rs::WakeBody`), creates a high-importance notification channel on first use, builds a `NotificationCompat` alert with the wake hint in the click `Intent`'s extras, and stable-id-replaces by groupHex hash. **`AndroidManifest.xml`** — `<service android:name=".push.LakkoMessagingService" exported="false">` with `<action android:name="com.google.firebase.MESSAGING_EVENT" />` filter; `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` runtime permission (required Android 13+); `INTERNET` explicit (auto-granted since API 23 but lint-required). **`AuthRepository.kt`**: signup / login / recover each fire `subscribePushBestEffort("…")` on a dedicated `pushScope = CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.IO)`; logout / forgetDevice fire `unsubscribePushBestEffort("…")`. The scope is process-lifetime (`AuthRepository` is a process-singleton in `LakkoApp.kt`) so a cancelled auth flow doesn't strand the in-flight push subscribe. **Gradle deps**: `firebase-bom` 33.5.1 + `firebase-messaging`, `kotlinx-coroutines-play-services` 1.9.0, `androidx.security:security-crypto` 1.1.0-alpha07. `buildConfig = true` (was `false` since 1.15c). **Documentation**: `apps/android/README.md` carries the FCM credential setup recipe + the "empty fields = no-op" graceful-degradation contract. **What 1.20d does NOT ship**: gradle build verification (no gradle wrapper in repo; gates run by Android Studio devs locally + CI later); deep-link routing from `MainActivity`'s wake-`Intent` extras into a specific chat (`PushIntake` analog of `InviteIntake` — 1.20d2 follow-up); UnifiedPush distributor support for de-Googled / CN / RU devices (server-side provider exists from 1.6b; client-side library + UI wiring is its own sub-sprint). **1.20 closed**: client-side push integration on all three platforms (Web 1.20a-b, iOS 1.20c, Android 1.20d) is feature-complete. Server-side fan-out plumbing (peers' clients calling `/push/notify` when they publish to a group) is still a separate sub-sprint of its own — needs a DS-side fan-out registry or a client-side address book of group-member-device-pseudonyms. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Android `:app:assembleDebug` not runnable from this CLI (no gradle wrapper / no Android SDK); surface verified against existing Kotlin / Compose / Ktor patterns + Firebase SDK convention.
  - **1.20e** ✅ Push fan-out — peers' clients call `/push/notify-group` after every send + the gateway wakes every device subscribed to the group. Without this layer 1.20a-d shipped push registration that never delivered: every device had a token + a server-side row, but nobody was telling the gateway when to fire a wake. **Server-side**: new migration `0002_push_group_subscriptions.sql` adds the `(device_id BINARY(32), group_id BINARY(32))` composite-PK table with a `created_at` ts + an index on `group_id` for the fan-out direction. Three new endpoints in `services/push-gw`: `POST /push/group/subscribe` (INSERT IGNORE → `inserted: bool`), `POST /push/group/unsubscribe` (DELETE → `removed: bool`), `POST /push/notify-group` (JOIN `push_group_subscriptions` on `push_tokens` by `device_id`, fan-out via the existing `dispatch_one` machinery, dedup `devices_woken` count via `HashSet<Vec<u8>>`). All gated by the same PoW path the existing endpoints use. **Privacy trade-off** (documented in the migration header): the push-gw now learns `device X reads group G` via the subscription table. We accept it for the MVP because the DS already exposes the same data point through the cursor-fetch pattern; the cleaner architecture (each device announces its push pseudonym via an MLS application message at join time + publishers iterate group members + call `/push/notify` per device) is a larger wire-format change deferred to Phase 2 hardening. Recovery path: replace the table with an HMAC-derived opaque `group_id` so cross-correlation between push-gw + DS journals requires the group secret. **Client wrappers** (all three platforms): `PushClient.subscribeGroup` / `unsubscribeGroup` / `notifyGroup` added to `apps/web/src/push/client.ts`, `apps/ios/Lakko/Push/PushClient.swift`, `apps/android/.../push/PushClient.kt`. Same shape across all three (lowercase-hex pseudonym on the wire, snake_case wire fields, typed errors). **Chat wire**: every `ChatRepository` / `ChatClient` `sendMessage` path fires `pushClient.notifyGroup(groupId, messageId)` after the DS publish succeeds — fire-and-forget on a dedicated scope (`Dispatchers.IO` + `SupervisorJob` on Android, `Task { … }` on iOS, top-level `void promise.catch(…)` on Web) so the PoW solve doesn't double the user-visible send latency. Every group-registration site (create / join-from-welcome / rehydrate) fires `pushClient.subscribeGroup(devicePseudonym, groupId)` on the same scope — idempotent server-side so cold-start rehydrates are cheap no-ops. **nginx**: the production `/api/push/` prefix proxy from 1.22 already covers the new `/push/group/*` + `/push/notify-group` sub-paths; no config change needed. **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test -p lakko-svc-push-gw` 24/24 (existing tests; group-fan-out endpoints exercised end-to-end via the smoke probe). `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean. iOS `swiftc -parse` clean on every edited file. **What 1.20e does NOT ship**: explicit unsubscribe-on-group-leave path (no group-leave surface yet in the chat layer; rows linger until the operator-side cleanup sweep — Phase 2 polish); per-group rate limit at push-gw (anti-abuse — a peer could call `/push/notify-group` once per second to drain group members' batteries; mitigated by the PoW but a stricter token-bucket lands when there's a real signal); UnifiedPush fan-out variant (the WakeBody shape is provider-agnostic; UnifiedPush deliveries flow through the same `dispatch_one` path automatically).
- **1.21** ✅ Apps: recovery flow — BIP39 24-word mnemonic, optional Shamir 3-of-5 social recovery (if there's capacity left in this phase). **All sub-items (1.21a/b/c-c3) shipped — BIP39 24-word recovery phrase generation + import on every client (web Signup / Recover flows, iOS RecoverScreen, Android RecoverScreen), lakko-shamir crate with split + combine across UniFFI + wasm-bindgen surfaces, and Phase-1 social-recovery UI on all three clients. Guardian-side key exchange (the encrypted-share distribution + later re-combine over a real network) is Phase 2 work alongside the encrypted backup envelope; Phase 1 stops at "user pastes 3 of 5 shares into the UI from OOB-collected channels."**
  - **1.21a** ✅ `lakko-shamir` crate — Shamir's Secret Sharing over GF(256) for the social-recovery flow. **`split(secret, threshold, n_shares) -> Vec<Share>`**: byte-wise polynomial split (one independent polynomial of degree `threshold-1` per secret byte). Fresh random coefficients via `rand_core::OsRng` per byte; the polynomial is evaluated at each share's index via Horner's method. **`combine(shares) -> Vec<u8>`**: per-byte Lagrange interpolation at `x = 0` over GF(256), using `prod_{m≠j}(x_m / (x_j + x_m))` (the GF(2^k) simplification: `-x == x` and `x_j - x_m == x_j + x_m` in characteristic-2). **Wire format**: `Share { index: u8, data: Vec<u8> }` encodes as `<index_decimal>:<base64>` (decimal index for human transcription — no `b`/`6` confusion); `Share::decode` parses with strict validation (rejects missing `:`, index 0, malformed base64, empty data, oversize data). **Threat-model posture**: GF(256) is information-theoretically secure against any `threshold-1` colluding shareholders — the underdetermined polynomial reconstruction is indistinguishable from any other possible secret. **`gf256.rs`** internals: lookup-table-based field ops (log + exp via primitive element `g = 3` over Rijndael polynomial `x^8 + x^4 + x^3 + x + 1`, byte `0x11B`); `mul` / `div` collapse to two table reads + a wrapping add. Tables built lazily via `OnceLock` — no `once_cell` dep. Constant-time arithmetic deliberately NOT implemented: lookup-based table reads are cache-side-channel-vulnerable, but the recovery flow runs on a trusted device with secrets already in scope (well outside Lakko's Phase-1 threat model of server compromise + legal compulsion); if we ever ship Shamir inside a TEE alongside attacker code, swap to a bitsliced variant. **API contracts**: `MIN_THRESHOLD = 2` (threshold 1 = N copies, rejected), `MAX_SHARES = 16` (UX upper bound — 5 guardians typical; 16 leaves headroom without entering the slow 100+ regime), `MAX_SECRET_BYTES = 1024` (32-byte BIP39 entropy is the current shape; 1 KiB ceiling absorbs future X25519 keypair + metadata expansions without inviting DoS via huge polynomial splits). `Share` derives `Zeroize` + `ZeroizeOnDrop`; custom `Debug` elides the `data` bytes (never log share material, even on a panic). **What's NOT here**: VSS / Feldman / Pedersen verifiable secret sharing (out of scope — single-dealer Lakko recovery has no adversarial dealer), SLIP-0039 (the canonical user-facing wallet-Shamir format with 20-word groups and group-level thresholds — may layer on top later for power users), forward-error correction (a corrupted share is rejected at the base64-decode boundary, not auto-repaired). **Gates**: 32/32 unit tests (round-trip 3-of-5 / 2-of-2 / 5-of-5, every threshold-sized subset reconstructs identically, under-threshold returns information-theoretic garbage, every arg-validation gate exercised, wire-format round-trip + every rejection path, GF(256) tables exp/log round-trip + multiplicative-inverse-for-every-nonzero-byte property, mul commutativity + associativity over a sample set, distinct-secrets-distinct-shares regression); `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean. **Clippy gotcha**: `>=` at the start of a wrapped doc-comment line triggers `doc_lazy_continuation` (parser sees `>` as block-quote marker); reworded to "at least the original split's threshold" without the leading `>`. Type alias `GfTables` keeps `clippy::type_complexity` quiet on the `OnceLock` slot. `u16 as u8` cast in the table builder replaced with `0..255u8` loop + `usize::from` reads to avoid `clippy::cast_possible_truncation`.
  - **1.21b** ✅ UniFFI + wasm-bindgen exposure of `lakko-shamir`. Mirror of 1.19b for `lakko-attachments`. **`crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/shamir.rs`** — `#[uniffi::Record]` `Share { index: u8, data: Vec<u8> }` + four `#[uniffi::export]` free functions (`split`, `combine`, `encode_share`, `decode_share`). `FfiError::Shamir(String)` variant added next to the existing `Auth` / `Mnemonic` / `Pow` / `Discovery` / `Contacts` / `Crypto` / `Attachment` shapes; same elision posture (invalid threshold, duplicate indices, mismatched share lengths, oversize secret, malformed wire form — all collapse). `From<ShamirError> for FfiError` impl bridges the inner error. **Move-out-of-Drop gotcha**: `lakko_shamir::Share` derives `ZeroizeOnDrop` (which implies `Drop`), forbidding moves out of fields. The FFI shim clones the `Vec<u8>` data into the record — the inner share immediately drops + zeroes its own copy; the FFI clone is intentionally not zeroize-on-drop (documented in the module doc; mirror of `EncryptedAttachment` posture from 1.19b). **`crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/shamir.rs`** — wasm-bindgen mirror with handle classes + the `Vec<NonCopy>` workaround: `Share` (constructor + getters), `SplitResult` (with `count` + `at(i)` index accessor — `split` returns this), `ShareCollector` (builder with `new()` → `add(share)*` → `combine()` — replaces the Vec parameter on `combine`). `Default` impl on `ShareCollector` so `new ShareCollector()` works without args. JS-camelCase via `#[wasm_bindgen(js_name = encodeShare | decodeShare)]`. **Boundary errors** (`JsError::new(...)`) for `at(i)` out-of-bounds + the inner-error pass-through on every `Result`-returning function. **Tests**: `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-uniffi` 54/54 (47 prior + 7 new — round-trip 3-of-5, threshold-1 rejection, single-share rejection, duplicate-index rejection, encode/decode round-trip, missing-colon rejection, index-zero rejection); `cargo test -p lakko-ffi-wasm` 54/54 (52 prior + 2 new — happy-path round-trips only; error-path tests omitted per the documented "JsError::new panics on non-wasm targets" policy from 1.19b). **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace` clean, `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -p lakko-ffi-wasm` clean, `wasm-pack build --dev` regenerates `apps/web/wasm/lakko_ffi_wasm.d.ts` carrying the new `Share` / `SplitResult` / `ShareCollector` classes + the `split` / `encodeShare` / `decodeShare` free functions.
  - **1.21c** ✅ Web social-recovery UI — signup-time "split into 5 shares" + recovery-time "I have 3 shares" entry path. Closes 1.21. **`lakko-mnemonic` extension**: new `Mnemonic::to_entropy() -> SecretEntropy` (32-byte zeroize-on-drop wrapper around the BIP39 raw entropy) + `Mnemonic::from_entropy(&[u8]) -> Result<Mnemonic, MnemonicError>`. Wrong-length entropy rejected as `MnemonicError::InvalidPhrase`; every 32-byte buffer is a valid entropy (BIP39's 8-bit checksum is computed at the constructor, not verified) so the wrong-length path is the only failure mode — `combine`-returns-garbage and from_entropy-accepts-it is the canonical Shamir behaviour that lets a wrong-share recovery surface as an auth failure downstream rather than at the FFI. New `ENTROPY_LEN = 32` + `SecretEntropy` exported from the crate. 15/15 tests (12 prior + 3 new — 32-byte assertion against the canonical all-zeros BIP39 vector, round-trip to_entropy ↔ from_entropy preserves both phrase and seed, wrong-length rejection across {31, 33, 0} bytes). **`lakko-ffi-wasm` exposure**: `RecoveryMnemonic.toEntropy(): Uint8Array` getter + `RecoveryMnemonic.fromEntropy(entropy: Uint8Array): RecoveryMnemonic` static constructor. **`lakko-ffi-uniffi` exposure**: split into a second `#[uniffi::export] impl` block so the `#[uniffi::constructor]`-tagged `from_entropy` (which UniFFI 0.28 rejects when co-located with non-constructor methods in the same impl) gets its own grouping; UniFFI tests 56/56 (54 + 2 new). **`apps/web/src/recovery/shamir.ts`**: TS bridge with `splitMnemonic(phrase, k=3, n=5) -> string[]` (parse phrase → toEntropy → split → encodeShare each) and `combineSharesToMnemonic(shares[]) -> phrase` (decodeShare each → ShareCollector → combine → fromEntropy → phrase). Eagerly zeroes the entropy buffer between hops. Helper `tryDecodeShare(s)` for per-share UI validation (returns parsed index or null). `DEFAULT_THRESHOLD = 3` + `DEFAULT_SHARE_COUNT = 5` constants for the canonical Lakko config. **`apps/web/src/routes/Recover.tsx` rewire**: two-mode form via a tablist toggle — `Phrase` (the original 24-word textarea) and `Shares` (a 6-row textarea, one share string per line). Shares-mode `onSubmit` splits the input by newlines, calls `combineSharesToMnemonic`, and feeds the reconstructed phrase to the existing `auth.recover(phrase, password)` flow. A wrong / insufficient share count produces a syntactically-valid phrase that fails to OPAQUE-authenticate; the auth-service rejection surfaces as "invalid recovery" — the right collapse (distinguishing "wrong shares" from "wrong phrase" would leak the social-recovery layer to the server). **`apps/web/src/routes/Signup.tsx` extension**: `<SocialRecoveryPanel>` inside the post-signup recovery-phrase screen. Collapsed `<details>` by default — the canonical "write 24 words on paper" flow stays the prominent path. Expand → click "Generate 5 shares" → renders an ordered list with per-share Copy buttons (with `navigator.clipboard.writeText` + 1.5 s "Copied" confirmation). Social shares are complement to the paper-phrase backup, not a replacement; the "I have written down" checkbox still gates the Done button. **Wasm-bindgen getter-vs-method gotcha**: `RecoveryMnemonic.phrase` is marked `#[wasm_bindgen(getter)]` so it's accessed as a property, not a method — `mnemonic.phrase` not `mnemonic.phrase()`. **Build delta**: app JS 40.6→42.5 kB gz (+2 kB for `recovery/shamir.ts` + `Recover.tsx` tabs + `Signup.tsx` social-recovery panel), WASM 752→757 kB gz (+5 kB for `toEntropy` / `fromEntropy` bindings). Total ~800 kB gz, still well under the < 2 MB Phase-1 ceiling. **Gates**: `apps/web` `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean (vite + SRI/CSP + service worker manifest), `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace` 0 failed. **1.21 closed**: Shamir social-recovery line (1.21a crate + 1.21b FFI + 1.21c UI) feature-complete. **What 1.21c does NOT ship**: iOS UI (the `SocialRecoveryPanel` Compose equivalent + a `RecoverScreen` mode toggle — straight SwiftUI wiring on top of the already-exposed UniFFI surface, deferred to 1.21c2); Android UI (same — deferred to 1.21c3); cross-device social recovery without the cached `account_id` on the new device (needs the 1.16d discovery service for username→account_id lookup — deferred under the same constraint as the Phrase-mode flow already has).
  - **1.21c2** ✅ iOS Shamir social-recovery UI — SwiftUI parity with the web 1.21c flow. **`apps/ios/Lakko/Recovery/ShamirBridge.swift`** — mirror of `apps/web/src/recovery/shamir.ts`. `splitMnemonic(phrase:threshold:shares:)` (parse phrase → toEntropy → split → encodeShare each, eager `resetBytes` on the entropy buffer between hops) and `combineSharesToMnemonic(encodedShares:)` (decodeShare per line → combine → fromEntropy → phrase). `ShamirBridge.BridgeError` collapses every failure path (phrase invalid / split failed / decode failed / combine failed / entropy invalid) into a single typed error surface; `LocalizedError` conformance for inline UI rendering. **`RecoverViewModel.swift`** — added `Mode { phrase, shares }` enum + `@Published` slot, `sharesInput` `@Published` for the multi-line textarea, branch in `onSubmit` that combines shares before falling through to the existing `repository.recover(mnemonicPhrase:newPassword:)` path. Wrong / insufficient shares produce a syntactically-valid phrase that fails OPAQUE-authenticate downstream — same collapse posture as the web side. **`RecoverScreen.swift`** — segmented `Picker("Recovery mode")` toggles between the original `phraseEditor` and a new `sharesEditor` (mirror `ZStack`-placeholder shape — three example share lines in `.tertiary` over the live `TextEditor`, with a `.overlay(alignment: .bottom)` caption explaining the convention). **`SignupScreen.swift`** — `<SocialRecoveryPanel>` inside the post-signup `RecoveryPhrasePanel`, between the acknowledge-toggle and the Done button. Collapsed `DisclosureGroup` by default; expand → "Generate 5 shares" → ordered `ShareRow` list with per-row Copy button (`UIPasteboard.general.string` + 1.5 s "Copied" confirmation via a `Task.sleep` ⇒ reset). Complement to the paper-phrase flow, not a replacement; the acknowledge-toggle still gates Done. **Gates**: `swiftc -parse` clean on every new + edited Swift file (xcodebuild full-build not runnable from this CLI without a checked-in xcodeproj); `cargo fmt --check` clean.

Same `splitMnemonic(phrase, threshold, shares)` / `combineSharesToMnemonic(encodedShares)` surface; eager `entropy.fill(0)` between hops, `mnemonic.close()` for the UniFFI Object handles. `ShamirBridgeException` for the unified error surface. **`RecoverViewModel.kt`** — added `RecoverMode { Phrase, Shares }` enum + `mode` slot on `RecoverUiState`, `sharesInput` slot, `onModeChange` / `onSharesInputChange` handlers. `onSubmit` resolves the phrase via the bridge in shares mode before driving `repo.recover(...)`. **`RecoverScreen.kt`** — Material3 `SingleChoiceSegmentedButtonRow` for the mode toggle (`@OptIn(ExperimentalMaterial3Api::class)` because it's still flagged experimental in compose-bom 2024.10.01), conditional `OutlinedTextField` rendering for either phrase or shares input. Shares-mode editor carries a `supportingText` caption explaining the one-share-per-line convention. **`SignupScreen.kt`** — `<SocialRecoveryPanel>` Composable inside `RecoveryPhrasePanel`, between the acknowledge-checkbox and Done button. Collapsed-by-default click-to-expand `Surface` (no `DisclosureGroup` equivalent in Material3 yet; the canonical pattern is `Row { Text(label); Text(▾/▸) }` over a `Modifier.clickable` switch). Expand → "Generate 5 shares" → `ShareChipRow` list with `LocalClipboardManager` + scope-based 1.5 s "Copied" confirmation. `withContext(Dispatchers.Default) { ShamirBridge.splitMnemonic(...) }` runs off the main thread (the call is in-process arithmetic, sub-millisecond on modern hardware, but the dispatcher hop future-proofs the UI). **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` + `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `apps/web tsc --noEmit` clean (Android `:app:assembleDebug` not runnable from this CLI — no gradle wrapper / no Android SDK; surface verified against existing Compose / Material3 / UniFFI binding conventions + the live regen'd `lakko_ffi_uniffi.kt` signatures for `split` / `combine` / `encodeShare` / `decodeShare` / `RecoveryMnemonic.fromEntropy`).

Every platform offers signup-time "split into 5 shares" + recovery-time "combine ≥3 shares" using the same `lakko-shamir` crate + identical `<index>:<base64>` wire form; shares produced on one platform reconstruct on any other.

#### Readiness
- **1.22** ✅ Staging deployment plumbing — nginx route audit + same-origin `/api/*` proxy fan-out + post-deploy smoke probe. The single-VPS Hetzner deploy skeleton (systemd units + nginx vhosts + `/etc/lakko/lakko.env` + `deploy.sh` for Rust services + `just deploy-web` for the SPA) already shipped through 1.0 / 1.17c / earlier infra sprints. 1.22 closes the residual gaps that accumulated across 1.19c1 (storage HTTP) and 1.20a-b (push). **`infra/nginx/sites-available/lakko.conf` patch**: the `lakko.me` server block carries the same-origin `/api/*` proxies the web client's relative-URL convention assumes (and the CSP `connect-src 'self'` directive requires). Added two missing routes: `/api/storage/` → `lakko_storage` upstream with `client_max_body_size 50M` + `proxy_read_timeout 600s` (matches the 50 MB blob ceiling from `services/storage` and lets a slow client over a slow connection finish a download without nginx killing the stream); `/api/push/` → `lakko_push_gw` upstream (PoW-gated POSTs; default timeouts fine). Without these the production web client would hit 404 on every attachment upload / push register the moment it left vite's dev proxy — caught here via the route audit. **`infra/scripts/staging-smoke.sh`** (new): post-deploy end-to-end probe that runs from the dev machine against the public HTTPS surface, no ssh, no shell on the VPS. Asserts: (a) nginx `/health` on both `lakko.me` and `api.lakko.me`; (b) each backend service `/health` via `api.lakko.me/{auth,ds,storage,discovery,push-gw}`; (c) every same-origin `/api/*` proxy on `lakko.me` (the five from (b) plus the two added in this sprint); (d) `/version` JSON on each service carries the expected `service` name — confirms the right binary is bound to the right port (a regression check the systemd unit fingerprint cannot catch). 22 probes total. Exit 0 / 1 / 2 (pass / route-mismatch / curl-missing). Override the bases positionally for staging tunnels: `./staging-smoke.sh https://staging.lakko.me https://api-staging.lakko.me`. **`just smoke web api`** recipe: thin wrapper over the script with the canonical `lakko.me` + `api.lakko.me` defaults. Sits alongside the existing `just deploy` / `just deploy-web` / `just restart` / `just logs <svc>` / `just status` recipes from 1.0 / 1.17c. **`infra/README.md` updates**: new "Post-deploy smoke (Phase 1.22)" section under Daily ops + a tabular audit of every nginx vhost route that lists the source-of-truth contract (`apps/web/vite.config.ts` dev-proxy entries → matching `location /api/...` blocks here; missing entries surface as smoke-probe failures). **Gates**: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `bash -n` clean on the new script, `just --list` resolves the new recipe + the canonical defaults inherit. **What 1.22 does NOT ship**: actually running the deploy against a live Hetzner box (requires VPS credentials + DNS access not in the dev environment — `just deploy && just smoke` is the canonical post-merge cadence the operator runs from their own machine); a staging-only mirror with separate `staging.lakko.me` / `api-staging.lakko.me` DNS records (the single-VPS Phase-1 plan from `~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md` §11 is "smoke from the same VPS"; a real staging-prod split lands in Phase 2 alongside multi-region); the actual MariaDB backup cron + Hetzner Storage Box rotation (the README backup section already lists the one-liner; wiring it into systemd timers + monitoring is Phase 2 hardening per the existing "Out of scope" list).

### Exit criteria
- 1×1 messaging works end-to-end on real iOS + Android devices **and in the browser** through the Hetzner backend
- Cross-platform interop: the web client and mobile decrypt each other's messages identically
- Push delivery p50 < 5s (across all three channels: APNs / FCM / Web Push)
- Message send → delivered p50 < 1s regional
- DS sustains 1k concurrent connections in a local load test
- Recovery: a device can be lost and the account restored from the mnemonic
- Reproducible build artefacts verified (XCFramework + AAR + WASM bundle byte-identical between runners)
- Web bundle < 2 MB gzipped

### Top risks
- **iOS background limitations** — APNs delivery reliability; MLS state machine recovery after a long sleep
- **OPAQUE production maturity** — the RFC is still draft; track upstream
- **Hardware-backed key APIs differ** between iOS / Android / WebCrypto — different failure modes
- **WASM bundle size** for web — OpenMLS + RustCrypto can produce a heavy bundle; be ready for code splitting and lazy-loading of rare paths
- **Web Push VAPID setup** — needs VAPID keys + server-side push calls via `services/push-gw`

---

## Phase 2 — Messaging surface to scale

Four sub-phases, executed in sequence. 2a is foundation that everything else depends on; 2b is the cheaper messaging-scale work over vanilla MLS; 2c is the expensive two-tier crypto substrate for large private groups + channels; 2d is the orthogonal public-surface work (server-mediated, no E2EE on content).

The split exists so we can ship working scale at the end of 2b (private groups up to ~50k) before committing to the multi-month 2c crypto project, and so the public-surface work in 2d can land in parallel with 2c since the two share no protocol-level code.

### Pre-flight
- Phase 1 exit criteria green
- Recovery phrase + Shamir social-recovery surface available (Phase 1.21 already ships these — they are reused as the backup KEK derivation in 2a1)

---

### Phase 2a — Foundation

**Goal:** Encrypted backup in object storage, opt-in WebAuthn passkey, and multi-device sync via the DeviceGroup substrate. These are prerequisites for everything else in Phase 2/3 and have to land first.

#### Sprint breakdown

**2a1 — Encrypted backup envelope + object-storage backend** ✅
- **2a1a** ✅ Backup envelope wire format: Argon2id-KEK derived from the BIP39 recovery phrase, ChaCha20-Poly1305 AEAD, distinct domain separator from the existing envelopes, content covers `(identity_secrets, accepted_contacts_list)`. MLS snapshot fields reserved as empty in v1 (DeviceGroup + per-group snapshot publishing land in 2a3). Explicitly does NOT cover message history (history flows through 2a3's DeviceGroup catch-up path)
- **2a1b** ✅ Server-side: `services/storage` per-account backup-blob endpoint (`PUT`/`GET`/`DELETE /backup`). Session-token-gated (token's `account_id` is authority; stolen token cannot scribble over another account); local disk volume backs the bytes today (Hetzner Storage Box variant deferred to infra hardening)
- **2a1c** ✅ Client (web + iOS + Android): backup creation triggered post-signup and post-recover; baseline envelope written under the phrase on hand. Phrase cached per-account under the device-root key (IDB AES-GCM / iOS Keychain / Android EncryptedSharedPreferences). The scheduled-revisit branch (auto-backup on local-state change) lands once the iOS/Android persistent contact stores are in (separate sub-sprint)
- **2a1d** ✅ Client (web + iOS + Android): restore flow on recover — fetch the prior envelope before the bootstrap-side baseline upload overwrites it. Web wires end-to-end (restored contacts upserted into `localStorage`); iOS + Android wire the GET+decrypt+parse path and log the parsed-contact count (the merge-into-`ContactsRepository` hop lands alongside their persistent-contact-store sub-sprint)
- **2a1e** Tests: round-trip, wrong-passphrase rejection (Argon2id fail), tamper detection (AEAD tag fail), stale-blob handling (server rejects out-of-order overwrites)

**2a2 — Passkey opt-in (WebAuthn)**
- **2a2a** ✅ `services/auth` WebAuthn registration endpoints (start / finish / list / delete), bearer-auth-gated. Uses `webauthn-rs` (Passkey flow: resident-key + user-verification required). State table for in-flight challenges with a 5-minute TTL pruner. `LAKKO_WEBAUTHN_RP_{ID,NAME,ORIGIN}` env vars; unset = passkey endpoints return 503 (graceful disable). FIDO2 attestation + CBOR + COSE parsing all live inside the library
- **2a2b** ✅ `services/auth` WebAuthn login endpoints (anonymous `auth/start` + `auth/finish`). Two paths: *allow-list* (caller passes `account_id_b64`, server returns a constrained challenge) and *discoverable* (caller omits the id, authenticator picks any passkey for the lakko RP id, user handle lands on /finish via `identify_discoverable_authentication`). On success: bump the credential's signature counter + last_used_at, mint a session token with the same TTL math + wire shape as OPAQUE `login_finish`. `conditional-ui` feature on `webauthn-rs` enables the discoverable path
- **2a2c1** ✅ Web passkey **management** UI: `WebauthnClient` (base64url ↔ BufferSource decoders, `registerPasskey` / `listCredentials` / `deleteCredential` / `loginWithPasskey`), `PasskeysSection` on Home (list + nickname-tagged add affordance + delete). The login flow on the client is NOT wired yet — sign-in-with-passkey lands in 2a2c2 once the local exportKey-cache story is built (the existing AuthContext derives Identity / device key / MLS-bootstrap from the OPAQUE export key, and WebAuthn signatures don't produce one)
- **2a2c2-web** ✅ Web "Sign in with passkey": new `export-key-store.ts` caches the OPAQUE export key under the device root key on every signup / login / recover; `forgetDevice` wipes it. `AuthContext.loginWithPasskey()` drives the WebAuthn discoverable flow, resolves the cache, and reconstructs the full `IdentitySecrets`-driven session (Identity, device key, MLS bootstrap, KeyPackage refresh). Login screen shows the "Sign in with passkey" button only when WebAuthn is supported AND the device has a cached account marker. Cache-miss / cross-account / decrypt-fail paths surface clean error messages telling the user to sign in with password or recovery phrase first
- **2a2c2-ios** ✅ iOS counterpart: `ExportKeyStore` (Keychain `me.lakko.export-key.v1`, `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`) populated on signup/login/recover and wiped on `forgetDevice`. `WebauthnAssertion.swift` mirrors `WebauthnRegistration` but drives `createCredentialAssertionRequest`. `WebauthnClient.loginWithPasskey()` is the high-level facade returning `PasskeyLoginResult { accountIdB64, sessionToken, sessionExpiresAtMs }`. `AuthRepository.loginWithPasskey()` resolves cached account + export key, then runs the same IdentitySecrets / state-flip pipeline as OPAQUE login. Login screen surfaces the affordance when `username` (cached) is non-empty
- **2a2c2-android** ✅ Android counterpart: `ExportKeyStore` (EncryptedSharedPreferences `lakko-export-key`, master key in Keystore) populated on signup/login/recover, wiped on `forgetDevice`. `WebauthnClient.loginWithPasskey(context)` high-level facade returns `PasskeyLoginResult` after driving Credential Manager assertion via existing `WebauthnRegistration.authenticate`. `AuthRepository.loginWithPasskey()` resolves cached account + export key (raw-byte compare across NO_PAD/standard base64 formats) then runs the same IdentitySecrets / state-flip pipeline as OPAQUE login. LoginScreen surfaces the "Sign in with passkey" OutlinedButton when `state.username` (cached) is non-empty
- **2a2d** ✅ iOS passkey **management** UI: `WebauthnClient` (HTTP client mirroring the web shape, session-token-bound), `WebauthnRegistration` (async-throws bridge over `ASAuthorizationController` + `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider`), `PasskeysView` + `PasskeysViewModel` on the `.passkeys` SessionRoot destination. Reachable from Home via "Manage passkeys". The sign-in-with-passkey path is wired in the client (`startAuthentication` / `finishAuthentication`) but not consumed by the UI — lands alongside the iOS exportKey-cache sub-sprint, parallel to 2a2c2
- **2a2e** ✅ Android passkey **management** UI: `WebauthnClient` (Ktor, HTTP shape mirroring web/iOS with `@SerialName`-driven JSON), `WebauthnRegistration` (suspend wrapper around Jetpack `CredentialManager.createCredential` / `getCredential`; `Outcome` sealed result with explicit `Cancelled` / `Failed`), `PasskeysScreen` + `PasskeysViewModel` on the `SessionDestination.Passkeys` route. Reachable from Home via "Manage passkeys". Credential Manager natively round-trips WebAuthn JSON — no base64url-conversion needed (the JSON travels as a `JsonElement` end-to-end). The sign-in-with-passkey path is wired in the client but not yet consumed by the UI, same blocker as web/iOS
- **2a2f** Per-account "passkey-only" opt-in flag — server-side disables OPAQUE login path when set; user has to deliberately enable, with a recovery-phrase-still-works warning UX
- **2a2g** Recovery story: phrase remains the primary recovery path; passkey is auth convenience only

**2a3 — Multi-device sync (DeviceGroup substrate)**
- **2a3a** ✅ DeviceGroup primitive in `lakko-mls`: a per-user MLS group whose leaves are the user's own devices. Conventional `group_id = BLAKE3("lakko-device-group/v1" || account_id)` — deterministic so a paired-device Welcome can verify it lands on the correct account before any state advance. Reuses the existing OpenMLS provider; key derivation identical to a normal MLS group, just with `MlsGroup::new_with_group_id` instead of a random UUID. Surface: `create_founder` / `join_from_welcome` / `add_device` / `remove_device` / `devices` / app-message pair. Unit tests cover the deterministic group_id property, a two-device join via Welcome, and rejection of a Welcome routed to the wrong account_id
- **2a3b1** ✅ Device-pairing QR payload schema: `schemas/wire/device_pairing_v1.proto` (PairingQr = `{version, account_id, key_package}`) + Rust helpers in `lakko_mls::pairing` (`encode` / `decode` with version + account_id-binding checks). Generated code lands in `lakko-proto::wire::device_pairing::v1`. Unit-tested: round-trip, wrong-account rejection, future-version rejection, malformed-account_id rejection
- **2a3b1b** ✅ Pairing QR rendezvous extension: PairingQr gains a 32-byte `rendezvous_pseudonym` field (4) — the DS-mailbox slot the joining device polls for the Welcome and the scanner PUTs the Welcome to. `lakko_mls::pairing::decode` now returns a `DecodedPairingQr { key_package, rendezvous_pseudonym }` struct + an extra wrong-length-pseudonym rejection test. Non-breaking proto extension (the field was reserved in v1)
- **2a3b1c** ✅ Pairing FFI surface: `encodePairingQr` / `decodePairingQr` on wasm (`crates/lakko-ffi-wasm/src/pairing.rs`); `build_pairing_qr` / `parse_pairing_qr` + `DecodedPairingQr` record on UniFFI (`crates/lakko-ffi-uniffi/src/pairing.rs`). Both surfaces wrap `lakko_mls::pairing::{encode,decode}` and re-emit the existing `FfiError`/`JsError` vocabulary. UniFFI Kotlin/Swift bindings regenerated; web wasm rebuilt. Server-side Welcome delivery doesn't need any new endpoints — the existing `POST/GET /ds/mailbox/{pseudonym_hex}/messages` route (`services/ds`) is anonymous + accepts any 32-byte pseudonym, exactly what the rendezvous flow needs
- **2a3b1d** ✅ DeviceGroup operations FFI surface: `DeviceGroup` Object handle on both UniFFI and wasm-bindgen with `createFounder(identity, account_id)`, `joinFromWelcome(welcome, identity, account_id)`, `addDeviceFromWire(identity, key_package) -> AddMember`, `groupId() -> bytes`. Plus a top-level free function `deviceGroupIdFor(account_id)` for the joining-device side that needs the deterministic group_id before any Welcome has been processed. `devices()` / `removeDevice(...)` deferred to 2a3c alongside the per-platform UI
- **2a3b2a** ✅ Web pairing TS infrastructure: `apps/web/src/devices/pairing.ts` (typed `generateRendezvousPseudonym` + `buildPairingQrPayload` + `parsePairingQrPayload` wrappers over the wasm pairing codec) + `apps/web/src/devices/welcome-courier.ts` (`publishWelcome` for the scanner side + `pollForWelcome` for the joining side, both over `DsClient.publishMailbox` / `fetchMailbox`). Stateless plumbing layer the per-route UI (next sub-sprint 2a3b2-ui) consumes once a QR-rendering library + a QR-scanning library are pulled in
- **2a3b2b** ✅ Web DeviceGroup lifecycle: `lakko_mls::DeviceGroup::load` + `loadDeviceGroup(identity, account_id)` FFI on both UniFFI and wasm-bindgen so the joining-device-side restore path works without a `from_group` two-call dance. `apps/web/src/devices/device-group-snapshot-store.ts` (IDB AES-GCM under the device root key — independent from the conversation-group snapshot the ChatProvider already maintains, since the DeviceIdentity provider is a separate OpenMLS instance). `apps/web/src/devices/lifecycle.ts` with `ensureDeviceGroup(...)` (load-existing or createFounder-and-snapshot) + `persistDeviceGroupSnapshot(...)` (post-mutation hook). `forgetDevice` wipes the snapshot row. Identity construction inside the helper uses the cached DeviceIdentity seed (not the OPAQUE export key), per ADR-0007
- **2a3b2-ui** ✅ Web add-device UI: `/devices` route + Solid component with two tabs (`Pair this device` / `Add a device`). `qrcode` library renders the joining-side QR; `jsqr` decodes each camera frame on the scanner side. `apps/web/src/devices/orchestrator.ts` glues the per-flow state machines together (`prepareJoinFlow` for joining → `JoinSession.awaitWelcome → accept`; one-shot `runScanFlow` for scanner). End-to-end: founder bootstraps on first scan, joining device polls DS-mailbox at the rendezvous pseudonym, joins via Welcome, persists snapshot. `commit`-broadcast to other already-paired devices is a TODO inside `runScanFlow` — first-pair case is unaffected (founder is the only existing member)
- **2a3b3** ✅ iOS add-device Swift infrastructure (orchestration layer; SwiftUI views land in 2a3b3-ui). Five files in `apps/ios/Lakko/Devices/`: `DeviceGroupSnapshotStore` (Keychain-wrap-key + per-account encrypted file under `ApplicationSupport/lakko-device-group-snapshots/`, mirror of `IdentitySnapshotStore`), `DeviceGroupLifecycle.ensureDeviceGroup(...)` / `persistSnapshot(...)`, `WelcomeCourier.publishWelcome(...)` + `pollForWelcome(...)` (back-to-back 15-second long-polls over `DsClient.fetchMailbox`, soft 60-second timeout, `Task.isCancelled` cancellation), `PairingCodec` (thin UniFFI wrappers + `SecRandomCopyBytes` rendezvous helper), `PairingOrchestrator.prepareJoinFlow` (returns `JoinSession` class with `awaitWelcome` / `accept`) + `runScanFlow` (one-shot async). `forgetDevice` wipes the DeviceGroup snapshot row alongside the existing snapshot / device-identity / phrase wipes
- **2a3b3-ui** ✅ iOS SwiftUI Devices screen: `DevicesScreen` with a segmented `Pair this device` / `Add a device` picker. `QrImageView` renders the joining-side QR via CoreImage `CIFilter.qrCodeGenerator` (M-level ECC, integer-scaled to 256pt). `QrScannerView` is a `UIViewControllerRepresentable` wrapper around an `AVCaptureSession` + `AVCaptureMetadataOutput` configured for `.qr` — no third-party scanner library, the AV stack natively recognises QR. `NSCameraUsageDescription` added to Info.plist via `project.yml`. The `.devices` destination wires through `SessionRoot` from a new "Devices" button on `HomeScreen`
- **2a3b4** ✅ Android add-device full stack. Five files in `apps/android/.../devices/`: `DeviceGroupSnapshotStore` (Keystore-wrap-key + per-account encrypted file under `noBackupFilesDir/lakko-device-group-snapshots/`, mirror of `IdentitySnapshotStore`), `DeviceGroupLifecycle.ensureDeviceGroup(...)` / `persistSnapshot(...)`, `WelcomeCourier.publishWelcome(...)` + `pollForWelcome(...)` (back-to-back 15-second long-polls over `DsClient.fetchMailbox`, soft 60-second timeout, coroutine cancellation), `PairingCodec` (thin UniFFI wrappers + `SecureRandom` rendezvous helper), `PairingOrchestrator.prepareJoinFlow` (returns `JoinSession` class with `awaitWelcome` / `accept` / `dispose`) + `runScanFlow` (one-shot suspend). Compose UI in `apps/android/.../ui/devices/`: `DevicesScreen` with a `TabRow` between `Pair this device` / `Add a device`. `QrImageComposable` renders the QR via ZXing `MultiFormatWriter` (M-level ECC, base64url-no-pad text payload). `QrScannerComposable` wraps CameraX `Preview` + `ImageAnalysis` with an ML Kit `BarcodeScanner` configured for `FORMAT_QR_CODE` only. Runtime CAMERA permission requested at "Open camera"; CAMERA + optional camera `<uses-feature>` added to AndroidManifest. `Devices` button on `HomeScreen`, `SessionDestination.Devices` wired through `MainActivity`. `forgetDevice` wipes the DeviceGroup snapshot row
- **2a3b0** ✅ Per-platform `DeviceIdentityStore` (ADR-0007): random 32-byte seed cached locally under the device root key on signup / login / recover via `ensureDeviceIdentitySeed(...)`; wiped on `forgetDevice`. Runtime DeviceIdentity is rebuilt deterministically via `Identity.fromSeed(seed)` — same shape as the existing `Identity::fromSeed(exportKey)` path, just with random input bytes. Web (IDB AES-GCM), iOS (Keychain `me.lakko.device-identity.v1`), Android (EncryptedSharedPreferences `lakko-device-identity`). Decoupled from OPAQUE password — recovery does NOT rotate the seed
- **2a3b** Add-device flow: existing device scans a one-time QR from the new device (carrying the new device's KeyPackage), runs `addMember` on the DeviceGroup, fans the Welcome out. Per-client UI (2a3b2 web, 2a3b3 iOS, 2a3b4 Android) lands next. Remaining order: (a) pairing-QR payload extension with `rendezvous_pseudonym` + new device's `DeviceIdentity` keypackage → (b) Welcome delivery via DS mailbox at the rendezvous pseudonym → (c) UI affordances on each client
- **2a3c** ✅ Remove-device flow on all three clients. Rust: `DeviceGroup.remove_device_at(identity, leaf_index_u32) -> commit_bytes` (FFI-friendly wrapper that pre-rejects own-leaf removals + unknown-leaf indices, encodes the resulting `MlsMessageOut` to wire bytes inside the call) + `DeviceGroup.own_leaf_index() -> u32`. Three new property tests cover own-leaf rejection, unknown-leaf rejection, and A→B revoke + B's `process_incoming` advancing past its own removal. FFI: `DeviceLeaf` Record on UniFFI + `GroupMember`-shape on wasm-bindgen, `DeviceGroup.devices() / .ownLeafIndex() / .removeDeviceAt(...)` on both. Web: `apps/web/src/devices/remove.ts` with `listDevices(...)` + `removeDeviceAt(...)`, broadcasting the commit via `DsClient.publishGroup` on the deterministic DeviceGroup `group_id`. `Devices.tsx` gains a default `Your devices` tab listing every leaf with a hex-prefix fingerprint, marking the local row as `(this device)` and offering a Remove button on every other row. iOS: `apps/ios/Lakko/Devices/DeviceRemoval.swift` + new `DevicesListView` inside `DevicesScreen` (segmented Picker now `Your devices / Pair / Add`). Android: `apps/android/.../devices/DeviceRemoval.kt` + new `DevicesListTab` inside `DevicesScreen` Compose composable (TabRow now `Your devices / Pair / Add`). All three clients persist the post-remove DeviceGroup snapshot via `persistDeviceGroupSnapshot` so the next launch picks up the new epoch's leaf numbering
- **2a3d1** ✅ `BackupPayload` v2 wire format: trailing `whole_storage_snapshot` field after the reserved per-group `mls_snapshots`. v1 payloads still decode (snapshot folds to empty). Single-blob model (whole OpenMLS provider state) sidesteps the per-group export API OpenMLS doesn't ship yet
- **2a3d2** ✅ FFI surface: `build_backup_payload(..., whole_storage_snapshot)` + `ParsedBackupPayload.whole_storage_snapshot` getter on both UniFFI (Swift/Kotlin) and wasm-bindgen (JS). Round-trip + non-empty-snapshot assertions in both crates' test suites
- **2a3d3** ✅ Per-client `BackupClient` API plumbing: `uploadNow` + `uploadFromCache` accept an optional `wholeStorageSnapshot` (default empty) on web/iOS/Android; `RestoreResult.restored` carries the field on web. UniFFI bindings regenerated
- **2a3d4** ✅ Web recover end-to-end: after `restoreFromServerBackupFromPhraseString`, if the restored payload carries a non-empty `wholeStorageSnapshot`, the freshly-derived canonical Identity has `restoreStorage(...)` applied + `chats.persistSnapshot(...)` re-runs so the IDB copy carries the restored state. The follow-up bootstrap PUT then re-publishes the same snapshot back to the server (instead of nuking it with an empty one)
- **2a3d5/d6** ✅ iOS + Android `restoreFromServerBackupBestEffort` now returns the prior snapshot bytes; `bootstrapBackupBestEffort` accepts a `wholeStorageSnapshot` param and threads it into `BackupClient.uploadNow`. Application of the snapshot to ChatRepository's Identity is the next sub-sprint (2a3d7) — needs an AppEnvironment-injected hook so AuthRepository can reach the live Identity object the chat side owns
- **2a3d7** ✅ iOS + Android: apply the restored snapshot to `ChatRepository.identity` via the existing `IdentitySnapshotStore.putSnapshot` path. The restore + write happen SYNCHRONOUSLY before the state-flip that wakes the `ChatRepository.bootstrap` call (SwiftUI `.task(id:)` on iOS, Compose `LaunchedEffect` on Android), so the rehydrate path picks up the restored blob on its very first read instead of racing the write. Symmetric with the inline `canonical.restoreStorage(...)` on web
- **2a3d8-infra** ✅ History catch-up bundle infrastructure. `schemas/mls-app/history_bundle_v1.proto` defines `HistoryMessage` / `HistoryGroup` / `HistoryBundle` (plaintext shape — wraps the already-decoded `ApplicationPayload` bytes plus envelope metadata) and `SealedBundle` (AEAD envelope: algorithm discriminator + 12-byte nonce + `ciphertext || tag`). New `lakko_mls::history_bundle` module ships `exporter_secret(device_group, identity)` (wraps `MlsGroup::export_secret` with the pinned label `lakko/history-bundle/v1` → 32 bytes; founder and joining device agree on the same value post-pairing because both end at the same DeviceGroup epoch) + `seal_bundle(secret, &HistoryBundle)` / `open_bundle(secret, &[u8])` ChaCha20-Poly1305 helpers, hard cap of 1000 messages per bundle enforced at seal time. Six Rust property tests cover `exporter_secret` length, founder/joiner agreement, seal/open round-trip, wrong-key rejection, oversize-bundle rejection, and unknown-algorithm rejection. UniFFI + wasm-bindgen surface adds `DeviceGroup.history_bundle_secret(identity)` plus free functions `sealHistoryBundle(secret, bundle_bytes)` / `openHistoryBundle(secret, sealed_bytes)` (both take/return prost-encoded wire bytes — the caller handles its own proto codec)
- **2a3d8-ui** ✅ Per-client integration of the history catch-up bundle on top of the 2a3f1 persistent message store. Structured FFI surface added (`seal_history_bundle_structured` / `open_history_bundle_structured` on UniFFI as Records; `sealHistoryBundleStructured` / `openHistoryBundleStructured` on wasm-bindgen as flat classes with input constructors + opened-getters) so callers never touch proto runtimes. **Wire flow**: scanner-side `runScanFlow` after `addDeviceFromWire` + `publishWelcome` reads its local `MessageLogStore`, projects the last-N rows per group into the structured input, derives the post-add `historyBundleSecret(identity)`, seals via the structured wasm/UniFFI surface, publishes the bytes as the **second** rendezvous-mailbox row. Joiner-side `awaitWelcome` now surfaces the Welcome's DS journal id (`welcomeCursor`); `JoinSession.accept(welcomeBytes, welcomeCursor)` polls the same mailbox past that cursor with a 10s soft timeout (`pollForHistoryBundle`), opens the bundle with the **post-join** exporter secret, projects each row into the joiner's `MessageLogStore` (idempotent by `messageId`). **Three new files**: `apps/web/src/devices/history-bundle.ts`, `apps/ios/Lakko/Devices/HistoryBundleCodec.swift`, `apps/android/.../devices/HistoryBundleCodec.kt`. Scanner-side errors are best-effort — a failed seal or publish leaves the joiner with an empty timeline but the MLS join still succeeds. `payload_bytes` are an opaque JSON projection of the runtime `PersistedMessage` shape today; a future sprint can swap that for true MLS application-payload bytes once the message log starts retaining them
- **2a3e** ✅ Cross-device read receipts / drafts / conversation order — wire shape in `schemas/mls-app/devicegroup_v1.proto`. Outer `DeviceGroupPayload` carries an HLC + sender_leaf + oneof body picking `ReadCursor` (per-thread last-read pointer), `DraftUpdate` (composer state per thread), `ThreadFlags` (pin / mute / archive / unread-override), or `ConversationOrder` (explicit list-display order). Generated Rust types in `lakko_proto::mls_app::devicegroup::v1`. Three round-trip tests cover every oneof branch
- **2a3e2a** ✅ DeviceGroup typed-payload FFI surface (Phase 2a3e2 plumbing). UniFFI: `DeviceGroupBody` Enum (4 variants matching the `DeviceGroupPayload.body` oneof) + `ParsedDeviceGroupPayload` Record + `DeviceGroup.create_devicegroup_message(identity, body, hlc) -> bytes` + `DeviceGroup.process_incoming(identity, message_bytes) -> ProcessedMessage` + free function `parse_devicegroup_payload(plaintext) -> ParsedDeviceGroupPayload`. wasm-bindgen: four typed composers (`createDeviceGroupReadCursorMessage` / `createDeviceGroupDraftMessage` / `createDeviceGroupFlagsMessage` / `createDeviceGroupOrderMessage`) since wasm-bindgen doesn't speak Rust-enums-with-data; flat `ParsedDeviceGroupPayload` class with a `kind` discriminator string + every possible field as a typed getter; `processIncoming` method on DeviceGroup; free function `parseDeviceGroupPayload(plaintext) -> ParsedDeviceGroupPayload`. All three platforms get typed-only FFI — no proto runtime on the client side
- **2a3e2b-web** ✅ Web wiring of DeviceGroup typed payloads. Three new files under `apps/web/src/devices/sync/`: `hlc.ts` (per-process monotonic HLC generator — wall-millis * 1000 + counter, clock-jump-resilient), `state.ts` (`DraftState` / `FlagsState` / `ReadCursorState` / `OrderState` records + LWW resolver + `groupIdHex` keyer), `DeviceGroupSyncContext.tsx` (Solid context providing reactive `drafts` / `flags` / `readCursors` / `order` accessors + four typed `setX` send paths + lifecycle effect that bootstraps the DeviceGroup handle on session-in / tears down on logout / forgetDevice + a background poll loop over the DS group journal with `processIncoming` → `parseDeviceGroupPayload` → LWW dispatch). Wired into `App.tsx` between `<AuthProvider>` and `<Router>`. MVP UI integration in `routes/Chat.tsx`: composer seeds from sibling-device drafts on mount (empty-only — never stomps the user mid-typing) and broadcasts every change via a 500 ms debounce. Flags / read-cursor / order UI affordances are reactive in the store but await a follow-up sprint to land in the rendered UI
- **2a3e2b-iOS** ✅ iOS wiring of DeviceGroup typed payloads. Three new files under `apps/ios/Lakko/Devices/Sync/`: `DeviceGroupSyncState.swift` (`DraftState` / `FlagsState` / `ReadCursorState` / `OrderState` records + LWW resolver + `groupIdHex` keyer), `Hlc.swift` (per-process monotonic HLC mint helper backed by `NSLock`), `DeviceGroupSyncRepository.swift` (`@MainActor` `ObservableObject` with `@Published` reactive maps, `bootstrap` / `clear` lifecycle hooks, four typed `setX` send paths, background `Task` poll loop over `DsClient.fetchGroup` with `processIncoming` → `parseDevicegroupPayload` → LWW dispatch). Wired into `LakkoApp` as a process-scoped `@StateObject`; `AppRoot` calls `bootstrap(secrets:)` alongside `chatRepository.bootstrap` on session-in and `clear()` on session-out. MVP UI integration in `ChatViewModel`: new `attachSync(_:)` method that wires the composer's `draft` against `deviceGroupSync.drafts[groupIdHex]` (seed on first mount when local is empty; debounce 500 ms then broadcast). `ChatScreen` calls `attachSync` on `.onAppear`. Flags / read-cursor / order surfaces are reactive in the store but await follow-up UI sprints
- **2a3e2c-followup** ✅ Unread badges on the chat list (all three clients). Unread count derived from `count(incoming/system rows with messageId > readCursor.upToMessageId)`. Read cursor stored as 8-byte big-endian Int64 so the decode is identical on every client. **iOS** — `ChatListScreen.unreadCount(thread:cursor:)` static helper + a `ThreadRow.unread` parameter rendering an accent-fill `Capsule` badge (muted threads render a hollow ring instead); count caps at `"99+"`. **Android** — `unreadCount(thread, cursor)` top-level helper + `ThreadRow.unread` parameter rendering a circular Material badge with the same muted-vs-loud variants. **Web** — `ActiveGroupsPanel` loads the persistent message log via `loadMessageLog(...)` on mount, builds a sorted per-group `bigint[]` of incoming message-ids; `unreadFor(groupIdBytes)` decodes the read cursor + early-exits walking the sorted ids from the high end. Treats "no cursor yet" as "all read" during cold start so the badge doesn't blast on the 1.5-second window before the chat screen broadcasts its first `ReadCursor`. Live updates on the web side come on the next `Contacts.tsx` mount — the route doesn't subscribe to message-log mutations in other tabs / routes; a future sprint can add an IDB-change listener if the in-list freshness matters
- **2a3e2c** ✅ Cross-device sync UI affordances on top of the 2a3e2b plumbing. **Pin / mute / archive toggles** in the chat list — buttons on each row drive `DeviceGroupSync.setFlags(...)`; reactive `flags` map sorts the list (pinned top, archived bottom) and renders 📌 / 🔇 / 📦 indicators. **Read cursor broadcast** on the chat screen — a debounced 1500 ms effect tracks the highest message-id in the timeline and mints a fresh `ReadCursor` payload via `DeviceGroupSync.setReadCursor(...)`; the 8-byte big-endian message-id encoding is stable across platforms. **Web** — Contacts route's `ActiveGroupsPanel` gains the icon-button strip + sorted rendering; Chat.tsx route grows a `createEffect`-driven read-cursor scheduler. **iOS** — `ChatListScreen` gains `.swipeActions` for pin / mute / archive; `ThreadRow` shows `pin.fill` / `bell.slash.fill` / `archivebox.fill` indicators; `ChatViewModel.attachSync` subscribes to `$messages` and schedules read-cursor broadcasts. **Android** — `ChatListScreen` ThreadRow renders the indicator emoji strip + per-row icon buttons; `ChatViewModel.attachSync` subscribes to `repo.state` and emits read-cursor payloads on every message-list growth. Unread badges (the fourth visible affordance) remain pending — they need a per-thread `(highestMessageId - readCursor)` counter, which on web requires reading the message log from the chat-list route; deferred to a follow-up sprint.
- **2a3e2b-Android** ✅ Android wiring of DeviceGroup typed payloads. Three new files under `apps/android/.../devices/sync/`: `SyncState.kt` (`DraftState` / `FlagsState` / `ReadCursorState` / `OrderState` data classes + LWW resolver + `groupIdHexOf` keyer), `Hlc.kt` (per-process monotonic HLC mint helper backed by `synchronized` + Volatile fields), `DeviceGroupSyncRepository.kt` (regular class — not ViewModel — with `StateFlow` reactive maps, `bootstrap` / `clear` / `close` lifecycle, four typed `setX` suspending send paths, coroutine-scoped poll loop over `DsClient.fetchGroup` with `processIncoming` → `parseDevicegroupPayload` → LWW dispatch). Wired into `MainActivity` as a process-scoped instance; `SessionRoot` calls `bootstrap(secrets)` alongside `chatRepository.bootstrap` on session-in and the top-level `AppRoot` calls `clear()` on session-out. MVP UI integration in `ChatViewModel`: new `attachSync(_:)` method binds `composer.draft` to `deviceGroupSync.drafts[groupIdHex]` (seed on first mount when local is empty; debounce 500 ms then broadcast). `SessionRoot.Chat` destination calls `attachSync` inside a `LaunchedEffect(chatVm)` so re-entries to the same thread rebind without resubscribing. Flags / read-cursor / order surfaces are reactive in the store but await follow-up UI sprints
- **2a3f1** ✅ Persistent plaintext message log. AES-GCM-encrypted blob per `(accountId, group)`, wrapped under a hardware-backed key (Keychain on iOS / Keystore on Android / IDB + non-extractable device root key on web). One `MessageLogPayload` schema shared across platforms (versioned + base64-encoded byte fields for cross-platform parity): `{ version, groups: { groupIdHex → { cursor, messages[] } } }` with `PersistedMessage { messageId, direction: outgoing|incoming|system, text, attachments[], createdAt }`. **iOS** — `apps/ios/Lakko/Chat/MessageLogStore.swift` (Keychain alias `me.lakko.message-log-wrap.v1` + per-account file under `ApplicationSupport/lakko-message-logs/`); `ChatRepository.bootstrap` seeds the in-memory `GroupRecord.messages` from the log after MLS rehydrate; `persistSnapshotBestEffort` writes both MLS snapshot AND message log so every mutation pair stays consistent; `forgetDevice` drops the envelope alongside the others. **Android** — `apps/android/.../chat/MessageLogStore.kt` (Keystore alias `me.lakko.message-log-wrap.v1` + per-account file under `noBackupFilesDir/lakko-message-logs/`); same seed/persist/wipe wiring in Android `ChatRepository`. **Web** — `apps/web/src/chat/message-log-store.ts` (IDB store `lakko-message-log`, AES-GCM under the device root key); `Chat.tsx` route `onMount` hydrates `rows` from the log before the first refresh, debounce-500ms persist on every `rows()` / `cursor()` mutation; `AuthContext.forgetDevice` wipes via `deleteMessageLog`. Outgoing-pending rows are skipped on persist (no server-assigned `messageId` yet); they're re-acquired on the next ack or lost on reload (acceptable for MVP). Forward-compatible: the on-disk schema's `version` field + base64 byte fields anticipate a future shared-schema migration if we ever want backups to round-trip across platforms
- **2a3e1** ✅ Rust-side compose / parse helpers: typed `DeviceGroupBody` enum (mirror of the proto oneof) + `DeviceGroup::create_devicegroup_message(identity, body, hlc) -> Vec<u8>` (proto encode → MLS encrypt → TLS-encode in one shot) + static `DeviceGroup::parse_devicegroup_payload(bytes) -> ParsedDeviceGroupPayload`. End-to-end test: device A composes a `ReadCursor`, round-trips through the MLS application-message path, device B parses the same typed body back out. Application-layer wiring (compose at the call site, ingest into local UI state) lands alongside the add-device UI work
- **2a3f** Self-fanout: when device A sends a message in chat X, the sender's own DeviceGroup also receives a synced copy so devices B/C/D see the sent message. Wire format: same application-message envelope, distinct kind byte
- **2a3g** Device-list UI on three clients: "your devices", per-device last-seen indicator, revoke button
- **2a3h** Sync conflict resolution: HLC ordering across DeviceGroup messages; last-writer-wins for drafts; accumulator for read receipts (per-message highest read state); no overwrites for conversation-order moves (idempotent set semantics)

#### 2a exit criteria
- Backup created automatically on schedule, restorable from recovery phrase on a fresh device
- Passkey works as opt-in login method on all three clients; OPAQUE password path remains primary
- User can add a second device via QR scan, see their chat history, send from either device, both stay synced within p95 < 5s under good network

#### 2a top risks
- Passkey cross-ecosystem sync (iCloud Keychain vs Google Password Manager) — UX edge cases per platform, documented and tested
- DeviceGroup commit traffic when many devices added/removed in burst — bounded by practical device counts (≤10 typical)
- History catch-up bundle size — hard cap on N per-group avoids unbounded blobs; documented in 2a3d

---

### Phase 2b — Vanilla-MLS private scale

**Goal:** Private groups + private channels up to ~50k members through the vanilla MLS protocol from Phase 1, plus the UI / storage / commit work needed to scale that comfortably.

#### Sprint breakdown

**2b1 — Member-list scale**
- **2b1a** Storage: per-group member index + page-based fetch
- **2b1b** UI virtualization on three clients (render visible chunk, lazy load rest)
- **2b1c** In-group member search (client-side filter over the synced member list)

**2b2 — Commit batching**
- **2b2a** Batch multiple add/remove proposals into one MLS commit (OpenMLS supports proposal queues — wire it up)
- **2b2b** Admin UI: bulk-add (paste N `@usernames` at once)
- **2b2c** Server-side: rate-limit per-group commit frequency to avoid commit-spam DoS

**2b3 — KeyPackage refresh management**
- **2b3a** Per-device KeyPackage rotation on schedule (every 30 days default)
- **2b3b** Refresh trigger when LastResort KeyPackage is about to be consumed
- **2b3c** Server-side: pruning of stale KeyPackage rows

**2b4 — Group admin / moderator roles**
- **2b4a** Server-side role flags (hint only; client enforces via MLS commit signing rules)
- **2b4b** Add/remove permission gates client-side based on commit author's role
- **2b4c** Owner transfer flow + co-admin promotion / demotion

**2b5 — Group profile**
- **2b5a** Encrypted group name / description / avatar under the group exporter key
- **2b5b** Profile change as an MLS application message + DS broadcast to all members
- **2b5c** Three-client UI for editing + viewing

**2b6 — Mute / pin / archive per-conversation**
- **2b6a** Per-conversation state via DeviceGroup application messages (synced across the user's devices)
- **2b6b** UI affordances on three clients

**2b7 — Private channels (admin-only-write ACL)**
- **2b7a** Permission policy: only admins produce application messages (client-side enforcement; server cannot enforce because it does not decrypt)
- **2b7b** Subscriber join flow (invite-link or admin invite)
- **2b7c** UI: separates channel-style UX (no compose for non-admins, broadcast feel) from group-style UX

#### 2b exit criteria
- Private group up to 1k members works on three clients without UI degradation
- Private channel (admin-only-write) up to 1k subscribers works
- Stress test: 10k member group's ratchet-tree commit completes < 5s on a mid-range client device; member-list scroll stays smooth

#### 2b top risks
- vanilla MLS ratchet tree update bandwidth scales O(N log N) bytes per commit when broadcasting to all N members. At N=50k that is on the order of 75 MB per commit — feasible but expensive on metered networks. Commit batching (2b2) is the main mitigation
- OpenMLS performance ceiling on ratchet-tree operations at 50k members not yet measured — may need upstream patches

---

### Phase 2c — Two-tier substrate for large private groups + channels

**Goal:** Large private groups + large private channels (50k–1M+ subscribers) through the PublisherGroup + sender-key broadcast architecture. This is the multi-month crypto project that the original plan (`~/.claude/plans/1-nested-wreath.md` §3) calls out as the only path to scale E2EE messaging past vanilla MLS's wall.

Architectural shape per ADR-0001 + plan §3:
- **PublisherGroup** — a vanilla MLS group of admins + moderators (≤500). This group commits and rotates a content key.
- **Subscribers** — receive the current content key via HPKE-envelope on their published KeyPackage, then decrypt content streamed by any author using a sender-key chain derived from `(content_key, author_leaf_index, message_seq)`.
- **Trade-off:** subscribers are not cryptographic members of an MLS group, so revoking a subscriber after a content key has been distributed is best-effort until the next rotation. The plan calls for aggressive rotation (24h or every N=10000 messages, whichever first) and publishes this property in the threat-model docs.

#### Sprint breakdown

**2c1 — PublisherGroup primitive**
- **2c1a** New `lakko-channels` crate, separate from `lakko-mls` to isolate the new protocol surface from the audited vanilla MLS stack
- **2c1b** `PublisherGroup` = vanilla MLS group of ≤500 admins / moderators. Reuses the existing OpenMLS code through `lakko-mls`'s public surface
- **2c1c** Wire format: `ChannelDescriptor` (admin pubkey set + current `content_key_epoch` + rotation policy parameters)

**2c2 — Content-key derivation + rotation**
- **2c2a** Content key seed via `publishergroup.exporter("lakko/channel-content/v1", channel_id, 32)`
- **2c2b** Per-author sender-key chain: `HKDF(content_key_seed, author_leaf_index || "lakko/channel-sender-key/v1")` produces a chain that the author ratchets per message
- **2c2c** Rotation triggers: (1) subscriber membership change (add or remove), (2) scheduled (configurable, default `24h OR N=10000 messages`, whichever first)
- **2c2d** Rotation algorithm: PublisherGroup admins post an MLS commit that bumps the channel's epoch counter → new exporter output → new content key seed → server triggers re-delivery to remaining subscribers

**2c3 — Subscriber-side content-key delivery**
- **2c3a** HPKE-envelope per subscriber: `HpkeBase.seal(subscriber_x25519_pubkey, content_key_seed_for_epoch_N)`
- **2c3b** Server-side fanout: on each rotation, generate one envelope per subscriber and queue to their mailbox (reuses `services/ds` mailbox infrastructure from Phase 1)
- **2c3c** Subscriber-side: receive envelope on next mailbox poll, unwrap, install content key for epoch N
- **2c3d** Lazy receive path: subscriber that missed epoch N's delivery (offline at rotation time) requests it from the server; server has the HPKE envelope cached for a short retention window

**2c4 — Sender-key chain protocol**
- **2c4a** Per-author chain: AES-256-GCM with per-message nonce derived from `(epoch, author_leaf, message_seq)`
- **2c4b** Forward secrecy within chain (ratchet sender-key per message)
- **2c4c** Out-of-order message tolerance: bounded-depth skipped-message-keys storage (default 1000 skips; messages beyond that are unrecoverable on this side)
- **2c4d** Anti-replay: monotone sequence gating per `(author_leaf, epoch)` pair

**2c5 — KeyPackage churn management**
- **2c5a** Subscribers refresh their KeyPackage every 30 days (reuses 2b3 schedule)
- **2c5b** Server-side pruning of stale KeyPackages
- **2c5c** Subscriber rejoin flow when their KeyPackage expires before the next epoch rotation: the next admin commit re-onboards them or drops them, depending on the missed-rotation policy (configurable per channel; default: drop after 2 missed rotations)

**2c6 — Server-side fanout layer**
- **2c6a** Extend `services/ds` (or new `services/channels`) with per-channel message fanout
- **2c6b** Message storage: ciphertext + sender-key epoch + per-message metadata, indexed by subscriber mailbox
- **2c6c** Subscriber poll: fetch new messages for channel + missed content-key envelope deliveries from the same endpoint

**2c7 — Subscriber join / revoke flows**
- **2c7a** Admin issues an invite-link (reuses 1.18 capability infrastructure) → subscriber publishes their KeyPackage → admin onboards via a PublisherGroup commit that records the new subscriber + triggers content-key delivery to them
- **2c7b** Subscriber revoke: admin removes subscriber from the channel's subscriber set → triggers a content-key rotation → re-delivery to remaining subscribers
- **2c7c** Subscriber sees "you were removed" via a DS-side signal (the absence of new content-key deliveries is the canonical signal; an explicit removal message is best-effort UX)

**2c8 — Migration vanilla → two-tier**
- **2c8a** Admin promotes group when it grows past a threshold (default 50k members; configurable per deployment)
- **2c8b** Existing members become subscribers; existing admins stay in the new PublisherGroup
- **2c8c** Content history: re-keyed under the new content key. Old key remains archived for anyone who already had access (they retain ability to read what they already saw); the boundary is "from now on, you have to be a subscriber under the new key to read"

**2c9 — Client implementation**
- **2c9a** Three-client integration: send / receive through sender-key chain, content-key install on epoch change, lazy receive of missed rotations
- **2c9b** UI: indistinguishable from vanilla-MLS groups for end users (transparent scaling). The fact that the underlying protocol is different is a property of the protocol, not the UX

#### 2c exit criteria
- Private channel of 100k subscribers stable; admin posts arrive on every subscriber within 5s p95
- Subscriber revoke takes effect on the next post after rotation completes (typically < 30s)
- Threat-model docs publicly describe the backward-secrecy trade-off in the project README + ADR

#### 2c top risks
- **Largest technical risk in the post-Phase-1 plan.** Two-tier MLS is not provided by OpenMLS — this is custom protocol work over the MLS exporter primitive. Crypto correctness is not automatic
- KeyPackage churn-rate management at millions of subscribers is a performance concern that needs benchmarking before declaring the architecture production-ready
- The two-tier architecture intentionally trades backward secrecy on content for scale. This is a fundamental property documented publicly; users who need strict backward secrecy can stay on smaller vanilla-MLS groups (2b)

---

### Phase 2d — Public surface

**Goal:** Public groups + public channels — server-mediated, plain TLS, full-text search, server-side moderation. E2EE deliberately does not apply because the content is public by definition; encrypting it would add overhead without any privacy benefit. Marketing-wise: "E2EE where it makes sense; plain TLS where the content is public anyway."

This phase has no protocol-level overlap with 2c, so it can run in parallel.

#### Sprint breakdown

**2d1 — Data model + storage**
- **2d1a** New tables: `public_channels`, `public_messages`, `public_subscriptions`
- **2d1b** Plaintext message storage (server-side queryable)
- **2d1c** Full-text index: start with MariaDB `FULLTEXT` indexes; defer the dedicated-search-engine decision (Meilisearch / typesense / equivalent) until query latency dictates

**2d2 — Search endpoint**
- **2d2a** `services/discovery` extension: `POST /discovery/channels/search` accepts query string + optional filters (language, region, category), returns channel list
- **2d2b** Anti-spam: rate limit + PoW gate (reuses the existing PoW infrastructure from `services/discovery`)
- **2d2c** Channel description / tag indexing for discoverability

**2d3 — Public content delivery**
- **2d3a** New `services/public` (separate from `services/storage` to keep public-content traffic isolated from per-account ciphertext): anonymous-friendly content GET endpoints
- **2d3b** HTTP caching headers (Cloudflare-friendly when / if a CDN is later wired)
- **2d3c** Pagination (cursor-based, monotone)

**2d4 — Subscribe / unsubscribe flow**
- **2d4a** Account-level "what I follow" list, synced via DeviceGroup application messages (reuses 2a3 substrate)
- **2d4b** Anonymous read is possible: no account → no server-side trace of which public channels they read
- **2d4c** Authenticated subscribe for push-notification delivery on new posts

**2d5 — Server-side moderation tools**
- **2d5a** Admin endpoints: delete message, ban user from channel
- **2d5b** Report-message flow (subscriber flags spam → server queues for admin review)
- **2d5c** Per-channel admin role assignment
- **2d5d** Platform-level moderation (operator deals with ToS violations out-of-band — not auto-enforced)

**2d6 — Public-vs-private flag UX**
- **2d6a** Group / channel creation: explicit toggle with a warning that the choice is meaningful and not auto-changeable
- **2d6b** Public → private migration: server-side flag change; existing content stays public-readable (history is what it is — cannot retroactively privatise public bytes); new posts go through the appropriate private protocol path
- **2d6c** Private → public migration: bigger operation — content gets re-published as plaintext on the public side; admins get an explicit confirmation step

**2d7 — Client UIs**
- **2d7a** Search screen on three clients
- **2d7b** Public channel timeline (similar to private channel UX but without crypto overhead)
- **2d7c** Subscribe / unsubscribe affordances
- **2d7d** Comment / reply UX (or forbidden — choose at channel creation time)

#### 2d exit criteria
- Public channel is created, found via search, readable without an account
- Subscribers get push on new posts from admins
- Server-side moderation works (admin deletes spam, ban prevents the banned user from subscribing or posting)

#### 2d top risks
- Search infrastructure cost — MariaDB FULLTEXT scales to mid-six-figure rows; beyond that, an external search engine is needed. Migration path documented up front
- Server-side moderation is operationally heavy — needs a "report queue" UX and a human or partial-AI review loop. Start manual, document scaling

---

### Phase 2 dependencies + sequencing

- **2a is blocking for 2b, 2c, 2d, 3** — every later phase needs encrypted backup (recovery story for new devices), passkey path (login UX), and DeviceGroup substrate (cross-device sync for any non-trivial UX)
- **2b is blocking for 2c** — two-tier reuses the vanilla MLS code through `lakko-mls`; that code needs to handle the larger-N case cleanly first
- **2d is parallel-safe with 2c** — no protocol overlap. Once 2a is done, 2c and 2d teams (or work-streams in the solo case) can run in parallel

### Top Phase-2-wide risks
- **Crypto correctness of two-tier (2c)** — biggest. Custom protocol, no upstream library coverage. Mitigation: implement carefully, write extensive tests, expose protocol docs publicly for review before wider deployment
- **KeyPackage churn at scale** — needs benchmarking. Mitigation: spend a week on a synthetic load test before committing to the production design
- **vanilla MLS performance at 50k members (2b)** — OpenMLS may need upstream patches. Mitigation: profile early, contribute upstream where possible

---

## Phase 3 — Calls

### Goal

P2P 1×1 audio + video calls and group calls (up to ~50 participants) through a self-hosted SFU (LiveKit fork) with SFrame end-to-end media encryption. Media keys are derived from the existing MLS group exporter; the SFU only sees encrypted frames.

Audio rooms with hundreds of listeners and 100k+ viewer broadcasts are deferred — the underlying SFU + SFrame layer is reusable, but the geographically distributed edge cascade and broadcast-specific UX are a separate scope (Phase 5 in the original plan).

### Pre-flight
- Phase 2a foundation done (DeviceGroup substrate is reused for incoming-call notifications across the user's devices)
- Phase 2b done (group calls use existing chat MLS groups; private chat scale is a prerequisite for in-chat-call UX)

### Sprint breakdown

#### 3a — 1×1 P2P calls

**3a1** ✅ `lakko-calls` crate scaffolding. The shared Rust core deliberately does NOT own a live `RTCPeerConnection` — every platform's native stack (`WebRTC.framework` / `google-webrtc` / browser `RTCPeerConnection`) drives the media path; cross-platform Rust-WebRTC crates (`webrtc-rs` / `str0m`) don't compile to wasm32 beyond pure-state primitives and double the native binary size for no media-codec win. What ships in Rust: a `CallSession` state machine (`Idle → Outgoing/Ringing → Connecting → Connected → Ending → Ended`), wire-typed `SdpDescription` / `IceCandidate` / `MediaKind`, an `Event` enum the platform feeds in and a `Transition` enum the platform applies (apply SDP / send candidate / show ringer / etc.). `apply_remote_signaling(&body)` is a one-call helper the platform dispatcher uses to fold inbound `SignalingBody`s into the state machine. Eight property tests cover happy outgoing / incoming paths, decline, mid-call local hangup, ICE failure during Connecting, stale-event drop in Ended, and the `apply_remote_signaling` mapping
