**3a2** ICE infrastructure: STUN (public bootstrapped + self-hosted backup) + TURN-over-TLS (self-hosted `coturn` on the same VPS as the SFU)
**3a3** ✅ Signaling: `CallSignalingPayload` proto (`schemas/mls-app/call_signaling_v1.proto`) with `CallOffer` / `CallAnswer` / `IceCandidate` / `CallEnd` oneof branches; each variant carries a 16-byte `call_id` minted by the offer side. Rust codec in `crates/lakko-calls/src/signaling.rs` exposes a typed `SignalingBody` enum + `compose_signaling_payload` / `parse_signaling_payload` free functions; caller wraps the plaintext via `Group::create_application_message` so signaling rides the existing chat MLS group as application messages on the Phase 1 DS journal — no new transport. Seven property tests cover each variant round-trip, unknown-end-reason forward-compat (folds to `HungUp`), wrong call_id length rejection, and the media_flags bitmap codec
**3a4** ✅ Media-key derivation: `mls_exporter("lakko/call/v1", call_id, 32)` → SFrame seed. Shipped in `crates/lakko-calls/src/sframe.rs`: `CallKey::derive(group, identity, call_id)` wraps the MLS exporter; `CallKey::participant_key(sender_leaf)` derives per-participant `ParticipantKey` via HKDF-SHA-256 over `(seed, info = "lakko/sframe/v1/key" || sender_leaf_be)`; nonce salt derived in parallel via `"lakko/sframe/v1/nonce" || sender_leaf_be`. `ParticipantKey.seal(aad, plaintext) -> (frame_counter, ciphertext)` increments the 32-bit counter; `ParticipantKey.open(aad, ct, frame_counter)` accepts the explicit counter from the SFrame header. Nonce composition is RFC 8439 §2.4 / RFC 9605 §4.4 (salt XOR counter-in-low-4-bytes). Seven property tests cover key derivation, per-leaf differentiation, counter monotonicity, wrong-counter rejection, wrong-AAD rejection, and fingerprint stability. Frame-counter exhaustion surfaces as `Error::CounterExhausted` — caller triggers a fresh MLS commit to re-key. FFI surface added (Phase 3a4 follow-up): UniFFI gets `FfiCallSignalingBody` Enum + `FfiSframeParticipantKey` Record + free functions `compose_call_signaling` / `parse_call_signaling` / `sframe_derive_call_key` / `sframe_derive_participant_key`; wasm-bindgen gets four typed composer free functions + flat `ParsedCallSignaling` class with kind discriminator + `SframeParticipantKey` getter class. Stateless seal/open shape — the platform maintains the frame counter, so cross-FFI state passing isn't needed
**3a5** SFrame (RFC 9605): per-frame AEAD encryption (AES-128-GCM with hardware AES-NI / ARM Crypto), key rotation on participant Add / Remove with 1s overlap
**3a6** Audio: Opus 32-64 kbps + DTX + FEC
**3a7** Video: AV1-SVC (preferred) / VP9-SVC / H.264 fallback (Apple legacy)
**3a8** ✅ Web client UI MVP. Audio-only 1×1 P2P, plain DTLS-SRTP (no SFrame in P2P — that ships once an SFU sits between peers in 3b). New `apps/web/src/calls/call-session.ts`: `CallSession` class wraps `RTCPeerConnection` + manages the local mic track + the trickle-ICE flow; `onOutboundSignaling` callback emits `composeCall*Signaling` bytes for the chat group's DS journal; `onRemoteStream` callback binds the remote audio to a hidden `<audio>` element. Chat client side: `IncomingChatEvent.callSignaling` variant added; `toEvent` discriminates by the first proto byte (call signaling tags 0x52 / 0x5A / 0x62 / 0x6A) vs the chat envelope's leading `{` (0x7B) — cheap unambiguous split, false positives surface as proto-decode failures downstream. `Chat.tsx` route gains a `📞 Call` button in the header + a sticky overlay rendering the current call state (`outgoing` / `ringing` / `connecting` / `connected` / `ending` / `done`) with Accept / Decline / Hang up affordances. Public Google STUN servers as the bootstrap ICE list — replaced by self-hosted in Phase 3a2. iOS / Android UI surfaces (3a9 / 3a10) follow
**3a9** ✅ iOS call surface (scaffolding — real `WebRTC.framework` integration is the 3a9-followup once the pod / SwiftPM artefact is added to the project). New `apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/CallSession.swift`: `@MainActor ObservableObject` mirroring the web `CallSession` shape — `@Published state` (idle / outgoing / ringing / connecting / connected / ending / done), `dial` / `acceptCall` / `declineCall` / `hangUp` methods that compose outbound signaling via the UniFFI `composeCallSignaling` free function, `applyIncomingSignaling(body)` for the inbound path. SDP bodies are placeholders today; the receiver-side WebRTC stack accepts them but produces no audio until the WebRTC pod lands. `ChatClient.IncomingChatEvent` gains a `.callSignaling(messageId, payloadBytes, createdAt)` case + the same first-byte discriminator (0x52/0x5A/0x62/0x6A vs `{` 0x7B) the web client uses. `ChatRepository.publishApplicationMessage` exposes the MLS-wrap + DS-publish path without the chat-envelope composer + timeline append so signaling rides out-of-band. A `callSignalingSink: ((String, Data) -> Void)?` closure on the repository routes inbound bytes to the per-thread `ChatViewModel`. `ChatViewModel.activeCall` is the published session; `ChatScreen.swift` adds a `phone.fill` toolbar button + a sticky `callOverlay` with state-driven background colour + Accept / Decline / Hang up affordances. iOS↔web signaling round-trips end-to-end; media path waits on the pod
**3a9-old** iOS UI: CallKit + WebRTC iOS pod (`GoogleWebRTC` or Apple's `WebRTC.framework`), PushKit for wake-up, Live Activities for in-progress indicator — full media-path integration follow-up
**3a10** ✅ Android call surface (scaffolding — real `org.webrtc:google-webrtc` pod integration is the 3a10-followup). New `apps/android/.../calls/CallSession.kt` mirrors the iOS / web shapes: regular class with `StateFlow<WebCallState>` lifecycle, `dial` / `acceptCall` / `declineCall` / `hangUp` methods composing outbound signaling through the UniFFI `composeCallSignaling` Kotlin binding, `applyIncomingSignaling(body)` for the inbound path. `ChatClient.IncomingChatEvent.CallSignaling` data class added; `toEvent` discriminates by the first proto byte (same 0x52 / 0x5A / 0x62 / 0x6A vs 0x7B test the web + iOS sides use). `ChatRepository.publishApplicationMessage` exposes the MLS-wrap + DS-publish path without the chat-envelope composer + timeline append. `callSignalingSink: ((String, ByteArray) -> Unit)?` on the repository routes inbound bytes to the per-thread `ChatViewModel`. `ChatViewModel.activeCall` is a `StateFlow<CallSession?>`; `ChatScreen.kt` adds a `📞 Call` button in the header + a sticky `CallOverlay` Compose surface with state-driven background colour (Material Orange for ringing / Green for connected / Grey for done) + Accept / Decline / Hang up / Dismiss affordances. Android↔iOS↔web signaling round-trips end-to-end; media path waits on the WebRTC pod
**3a10-old** Android UI: ConnectionService + WebRTC Android pod, MediaSession for controls, ForegroundService for long-running calls — full media-path integration follow-up
**3a11** ✅ Call quality metrics — Rust core + cross-platform FFI. New `crates/lakko-calls/src/quality.rs` ships a simplified ITU-T G.107 E-model: `QualitySample { rtt_ms, jitter_ms, loss_pct, codec }` in → `QualityScore { r_factor, mos, level }` out via `estimate_quality(sample)`. Codec hint (`AudioCodec::{Opus, Other}`) selects the loss-impairment curve: Opus gets `Ie=0, Bpl=25` (treated as transparent at 32 kbps+ with FEC absorbing 5–10% loss); non-Opus baseline is `Ie=0, Bpl=4.3` per G.113 Appendix I (no-FEC G.711-like). Delay impairment uses the 177.3 ms knee from G.107 §A.2.1. R→MOS mapping is the standard cubic with `MOS ∈ [1.0, 4.5]` clamp. `QualityLevel { Excellent ≥4.3 / Good ≥4.0 / Fair ≥3.6 / Poor ≥3.1 / Bad }` is a cheap UX-bucket. Companion `recommend_bitrate(current_bps, sample) -> u32` is a step-controller: `loss > 5%` decrements by ×0.8 (clamped to `OPUS_MIN_BITRATE_BPS = 8 kbps`), `loss < 1% AND mos > 4.0` increments by ×1.1 (clamped to `OPUS_MAX_BITRATE_BPS = 510 kbps`), otherwise hold. Caller-side normalisation: out-of-range `current_bps` is clamped before any step. 15 property tests cover ideal-Opus-is-Excellent, MOS-bounds across full loss sweep (0..100%), MOS-monotonic in loss / RTT / jitter, Opus-resilience-vs-Other across loss range, severe-loss-is-Bad, high-delay-degrades, bitrate decrease / increase / hold / floor-clamp / ceiling-clamp / out-of-range-normalisation. **Crate stays wall-clock-free and stats-agnostic** — the platform layer samples `RTCStatsReport` (web) / `RTCStatistics` (iOS) / `RTCStatsCollector` (Android) on its own cadence and feeds `QualitySample` instances in. **Multi-sample smoothing** via the new `QualitySampler` — stateful ring buffer of the N most-recent samples (default `DEFAULT_SAMPLE_WINDOW = 5`, capped at `MAX_SAMPLE_WINDOW = 60`). On every `record(sample)` call it returns a smoothed `QualityScore`: **median** R-factor / MOS (more robust than mean against a transient packet-loss burst), **majority-vote** level (ties broken by picking the higher / better bucket — anti-flicker UX preference). `latest()` returns the current smoothed score without pushing; `clear()` resets the window on call end / SFU handoff. Wall-clock-free — no age tracking. 11 new tests cover empty-state, default-window, window-size-clamping, single-sample-passthrough, majority-vote on 3-of-5, tie-prefers-higher-bucket, median-resists-single-spike, ring-rotation-drops-oldest, clear, window-1-disables-smoothing, and window-bounded-not-history. **FFI surfaces**: UniFFI gets `FfiAudioCodec` / `FfiQualityLevel` Enums + `FfiQualitySample` / `FfiQualityScore` Records + `estimate_call_quality` / `recommend_call_bitrate` free fns + the new stateful `FfiCallQualitySampler` Object (constructor `new(window_size)` + methods `record(sample) -> FfiQualityScore` / `latest() -> Option<FfiQualityScore>` / `clear()` / `window_size()` / `count()`) plus the `ffi_default_quality_sample_window` / `ffi_max_quality_sample_window` constants. Wasm-bindgen mirror as `CallQualityScore` flat class (rFactor / mos / level getters) + `estimateCallQuality(rtt_ms, jitter_ms, loss_pct, codec)` / `recommendCallBitrate(current_bps, ...)` free functions + the stateful `CallQualitySampler` class (constructor + `record(rtt_ms, jitter_ms, loss_pct, codec) -> CallQualityScore` / `latest() -> CallQualityScore | undefined` / `clear()` / `windowSize` / `count` getters) + `defaultQualitySampleWindow` / `maxQualitySampleWindow` free fns. `codec` accepts `"opus"` / `"Opus"` / `"OPUS"`; anything else maps to `Other`. Total lakko-calls test count: 43 → 69.

#### 3b — SFU deployment

**3b1** ✅ LiveKit fork strategy. Captured in [`docs/adr/0008-livekit-fork-strategy.md`](adr/0008-livekit-fork-strategy.md) as the operational extension of ADR-0003. Fork ONLY `livekit/server` + `livekit/protocol` — not the client SDKs (clients drive native `RTCPeerConnection` directly, see 3a1), not `egress` / `ingress` (recording is deferred per 3c8), not the orchestration layer. Upstream-tracking shape: a `services/sfu/UPSTREAM.md` pin (commit SHA + minor version) + `services/sfu/lakko-patches/` directory holding `git format-patch`-style diffs that re-apply on rebase, with a quarterly rebase cadence. SFrame integration: delete LiveKit's `participant_key` flow entirely — clients derive per-participant keys from the MLS exporter independently per 3a4 / 3c4, and the SFU never sees or routes a media key. SFU sees only the JWT (`account_id`, `call_id`, `role`, `expires_at`, signed by `services/auth`) + encrypted SFrame frames; no plaintext-media branch in the codebase post-fork. Diff target < 800 lines net so quarterly rebases stay cheap. Sub-sprint decomposition: 3b1a fork tree setup + UPSTREAM.md pin, 3b1b strip plaintext-media path + delete `participant_key`, 3b1c JWT validation against `services/auth` JWKS endpoint, 3b1d call-control RPC surface. Single-VPS deployment plan: SFU + coturn co-resident on a Hetzner CCX VPS, separate from the messaging VPS, sharing only the auth JWKS
**3b1a** ✅ Fork tree scaffolding. New `services/sfu/` directory with the layout the rest of 3b builds against: `README.md` (entry point + sprint status table), `UPSTREAM.md` (currently-unpinned pin metadata + how-to-read-as-auditor block), `PLAYBOOK.md` (exact commands for initial vendor, patch addition, quarterly rebase, lakko-additions/), `lakko-patches/README.md` (filename convention `NNN-<sprint>-<desc>.patch`, patch shape, re-apply procedure, "patches that need to die" tracking table), `lakko-additions/README.md` (separate Go module shape with `replace` directive against `../upstream/livekit/`, what-lives-here-vs-in-a-patch decision matrix, JWT verifier as the 3b1c load-bearing example), `.gitignore` (Go build artefacts). The actual upstream vendor (a single dedicated commit landing ~150k lines of Go) is the maintainer's separate operational step per `PLAYBOOK.md` §1 — not part of this sprint. CI gates documented but stubbed pending 3b1b (every commit touching `upstream/` must also touch `UPSTREAM.md`, every modified upstream file carries a `LAKKO:` marker comment, `just sfu-verify-clean` applies every patch + builds). Cross-links: `docs/adr/0008` (rationale), `docs/security/calls.md` §"What the SFU sees vs what the client owns" (threat-model anchor for what the fork's `participant_key` deletion buys)
**3b2** SFU deployment: separate Hetzner CCX-tier VPS (media bandwidth heavy). 20 TB monthly transfer allowance baseline
**3b3** TURN deployment: coturn config alongside the SFU, TURN-over-TLS on port 443
**3b4** ⚙️ SFU auth — Rust side complete (issuance + client-side verify); Go-side SFU validation (3b1c) pending vendor pull. **Crypto + wire shape** lives in a new shared `crates/lakko-call-jwt/` crate (same pattern as `lakko-auth-token`): `CallJwtClaims` / `CallRole` / `CallSigningKey`, `mint_jwt`, `parse_unverified`, `verify_jwt`, plus the JWKS surface (`Jwk` / `Jwks` / `parse_jwks` / `verify_with_jwks` resolving `kid` against a JWK Set). Hand-rolled JWS Compact — no `jsonwebtoken` dep, sign/verify ride on `lakko-crypto::signing`. Claim shape per ADR-0008: `iss = "lakko-auth"`, `aud = "lakko-sfu"`, `sub = account_id_hex`, `call_id` hex, `role ∈ {participant, publisher, subscriber}`, `iat`, `exp = iat + 3600`. **Server side** in `services/auth/src/call_jwt.rs` is now slim — only the two HTTP handlers + `load_or_init_call_signing_key`: `POST /call/jwt` (bearer-auth via the same `webauthn::require_session` middleware passkey routes use; takes `{call_id, role}`, returns `{jwt, expires_at_unix_secs}`); `GET /.well-known/jwks.json` (anonymous — publishes the public key as OKP/Ed25519 JWK per RFC 8037). Migration `0007_call_jwt_signing.sql` adds a single-row `call_jwt_signing_key` table; first boot generates the seed via `INSERT IGNORE` (race-safe), every subsequent boot reads back deterministically. `kid` = first 8 bytes of BLAKE3(public_key) hex (16 chars), stable across restarts, bumps only on rotation. **Client side** via FFI: UniFFI gets `FfiCallRole` Enum + `FfiCallJwtClaims` Record + free fns `parse_call_jwt_unverified(jwt) -> FfiCallJwtClaims` and `verify_call_jwt_with_jwks(jwks_doc_bytes, jwt, now_unix_secs) -> FfiCallJwtClaims`; wasm-bindgen mirror as `ParsedCallJwt` flat class + `parseCallJwtUnverified` / `verifyCallJwtWithJwks`. The platform owns the HTTP path — `POST /call/jwt` and the JWKS fetch are plain JSON over the existing transport, no FFI HTTP client needed. **Tests**: 14 in `lakko-call-jwt` (the original 9 moved from auth + 5 new: JWKS round-trip through JSON, kid-resolution across a multi-key JWKS doc during rotation, no-matching-kid rejection, JWK wrong-kty rejection, parse-unverified shape) + 7 server-side (1 smoke + 6 HTTP integration covering happy path, role default, missing bearer 401, wrong-secret bearer 401, malformed call_id 400 per-shape, mint→JWKS→verify full chain) driving the real axum router through `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot`. **Client transports** mirror the same shape on three platforms:

- **Web** (`apps/web/src/calls/call-jwt-client.ts`) — `CallJwtClient` over `globalThis.fetch`; wasm-bindgen `parseCallJwtUnverified` / `verifyCallJwtWithJwks` for the parse + verify path; `mintCallId()` via `crypto.getRandomValues`. Mirrors `WebauthnClient` (constructor takes wasm + baseUrl, `setSessionToken` rebinds the bearer).
- **iOS** (`apps/ios/Lakko/Calls/CallJwtClient.swift`) — `CallJwtClient` over `URLSession`; UniFFI `parseCallJwtUnverified` / `verifyCallJwtWithJwks` + `FfiCallJwtClaims` / `FfiCallRole`. Typed errors `CallJwtClientError.{argument, http, network, verify}`. `MintCallJwtResult` + `ParsedCallJwt` Sendable structs. `mintCallId()` via `SecRandomCopyBytes`.
- **Android** (`apps/android/app/src/main/kotlin/me/lakko/app/calls/CallJwtClient.kt`) — `CallJwtClient` over Ktor `HttpClient(OkHttp)`; same UniFFI surface via the `uniffi.lakko_ffi_uniffi.*` Kotlin bindings. `MintCallJwtResult` + `ParsedCallJwt` data classes. `mintCallId()` via `SecureRandom`. Default `baseUrl` is `http://10.0.2.2:8081` for emulator dev.

Each client exposes the same four methods (`mint(callId, role)` / `fetchJwks(forceRefresh?)` / `parseUnverified(jwt)` / `verifyAgainstJwks(jwt, jwks, nowUnixSecs?)`) plus the convenience `verifyJwt(jwt, nowUnixSecs?)` that auto-fetches + auto-retries on key-rotation. `setSessionToken(token | null)` rebinds the bearer. A single audit pass covers the wire shape across all three transports.

**JWKS caching** is inlined into every transport — `fetchJwks` returns an in-memory-cached blob if it's within `jwksTtlMs` (default 24 h per ADR-0008), or fetches fresh + caches. `clearJwksCache()` is explicit. The convenience `verifyJwt(jwt, nowUnixSecs?)` chains `fetchJwks → verifyAgainstJwks` and on a `no JWK matches header.kid` failure — the canonical rotation signal — automatically invalidates the cache, refetches once with `forceRefresh = true`, and retries the verify exactly once. Any second failure surfaces verbatim. Callers don't have to remember the TTL, don't have to handle the rotation case, and can't accidentally accept a stale JWKS doc past its TTL window. Cache lifetime is the client instance — every signed-in session gets a fresh cache (no cross-account leakage). **Audit**: invariants C-15..C-23 in [`docs/security/calls.md`](security/calls.md) — includes JWKS multi-key resolution + JWK algorithm-confusion defence. SFU-side validation (Go, 3b1c) still gated on vendor pull; deployment (3b2) + monitoring (3b5) follow
**3b5** Monitoring: SFU CPU + bandwidth + dropped-stream count → Prometheus exposition → simple Grafana dashboard
**3b6** Cost analysis: per-user-minute bandwidth estimate, scale projection to inform when a second SFU VPS is needed

#### 3c — Group calls

**3c1** ✅ Call MLS-group primitive (Rust scaffolding). Shipped in `crates/lakko-calls/src/group_call.rs`: `GroupCallScope { InChat, LinkBased }` discriminates the two topologies; `GroupCallSession` is the per-call ledger holding the `CallKey` seed, the own-leaf `u32`, a `BTreeMap<u32, Participant>` of joined / invited leaves, and a `needs_rekey` flag that flips on every membership change (set_joined toggle or forget_participant). Public API: `create(scope, call_id, group, identity)` derives the seed from `mls_exporter("lakko/call/v1", call_id, 32)` per 3a4; `refresh_call_key(group, identity)` re-derives after an MLS epoch advance (the chat group commit that opens the call also rotates the call key); `set_joined` / `forget_participant` track the on-call subset (a proper subset of MLS membership — being in the chat group doesn't mean being on the call); `own_participant_key()` / `remote_participant_key(leaf)` derive `ParticipantKey` via `CallKey::participant_key` per 3a4; `joined_participants` / `total_participants` / `joined_count` surface the on-call state; `needs_rekey()` + `ack_rekey()` are the rotation handshake the SFU-side caller (3b1 + 3c5) drives. Six property tests cover create / set_joined-toggles-rekey / forget_participant / per-leaf key differentiation / refresh-after-epoch-advance / BTreeMap iteration order. Live `RTCPeerConnection` instances stay platform-owned per 3a1; the ledger is a state shape, not a media object. Group-call signaling wire shape lands in 3c2-3c3
**3c2** ✅ In-chat group call wire shape. Extended `schemas/mls-app/call_signaling_v1.proto` with three call-control variants (`GroupCallStart` field 14, `GroupCallJoin` field 15, `GroupCallLeave` field 16; reserved 17–31). The split is deliberate: in SFU-based group calls per-peer SDP / ICE rides participant ↔ SFU directly through LiveKit signaling, never through MLS — what rides MLS is call-control only (announce + roster updates). Keeps the chat DS journal small and the SFU blind to chat membership. Rust side in `crates/lakko-calls/src/signaling.rs`: added `SignalingBody::GroupStart` / `GroupJoin` / `GroupLeave` variants + `SignalingBody::is_group_control()` helper for client dispatchers. `GroupCallSession::apply_remote_signaling(&body) -> SignalingOutcome` (new enum: `Ignored / Announced / Joined { leaf_index } / Left { leaf_index }`) folds inbound bodies into the ledger and discriminates against 1×1 bodies that leak into the group dispatcher. The 1×1 `CallSession::apply_remote_signaling` silently drops group-control variants. FFI surfaces extended: UniFFI `FfiCallSignalingBody` enum gains `GroupStart` / `GroupJoin` / `GroupLeave` records; wasm-bindgen gets three new typed composer fns (`composeGroupCallStartSignaling` / `composeGroupCallJoinSignaling` / `composeGroupCallLeaveSignaling`) + `ParsedCallSignaling` gains `kind = "groupStart" | "groupJoin" | "groupLeave"` plus `initiatorLeaf` / `leafIndex` / `joinedAtMs` getters. Seven new property tests (4 in `signaling.rs`: round-trip per group variant + `is_group_control` discrimination; 3 in `group_call.rs`: routes-each-body + ignores-1×1 + ignores-wrong-call-id) + extended `lakko-proto::call_signaling_roundtrip` to cover the three new prost message types. Group-call media keying + per-pair SFrame plumbing follow in 3c4 / 3c5
**3c3** ✅ Link-based group call — wire codec + joiner-side MLS orchestration + three-platform FFI shipped (V1 single-joiner); server-side blob storage is the remaining 3c3-server follow-up. New `crates/lakko-contacts/src/call_invite.rs` ships a sibling of `InviteCapability` for `/call/<slug>` links: `CallInviteCapability { owner_signature_pubkey, owner_account_id, call_id, expiry_ms, max_uses, media: CallInviteMedia { audio, video }, one_time_key_package, label }`, signed by the owner's Ed25519. Wire format is a manual codec frozen at v1, mirroring `invite.rs`'s shape: schema-version byte + Ed25519 pubkey + 16-byte UUIDv7 + 16-byte `call_id` + u64be `expiry_ms` + u32be `max_uses` + media-flags byte + u32be `key_package_len` + KP bytes + u16be `label_len` + label + 64-byte Ed25519 signature over everything that came before. Hard caps: `MAX_KEY_PACKAGE_LEN = 4 KiB`, `MAX_LABEL_LEN = 64` (re-using contact-invite's constant). Sentinels `CALL_NEVER_EXPIRES` / `CALL_SINGLE_USE` (= 0). The owner mints `call_id` via `crypto.getRandomValues` / `SecRandomCopyBytes` / `SecureRandom.nextBytes`; every joiner sees the same value so the `GroupCallSession::LinkBased` ledger (3c1) shares membership across all joiners. 13 property tests in the crate cover round-trip, audio-only-media variant, never-expires-is-always-valid, expired-after-expiry, tampered-payload-rejected (signature invalid), truncated-wire-rejected, unsupported-schema-version-rejected, empty-KP-rejected-at-compose, oversize-KP-rejected-at-compose, oversize-label-rejected-at-compose, cross-signing-key-spliced-pubkey-rejected (attacker-substitution shape), media-byte-codec-stable, unknown-media-bits-forward-compat. FFI surfaces: **UniFFI** gets `FfiCallInviteMedia` Record + `compose_call_invite_capability` free fn + `CallInviteCapability` Object with constructor `decode_and_verify(wire)` + getters (`owner_signature_pubkey`, `owner_account_id`, `call_id`, `expiry_ms`, `max_uses`, `media`, `one_time_key_package`, `label`, `is_valid_now(now_ms)`). **wasm-bindgen** mirror as `composeCallInviteCapability(owner_signing_seed, owner_account_id, call_id, expiry_ms, max_uses, media_audio, media_video, one_time_key_package, label)` free fn + `CallInviteCapability` class with `decodeAndVerify(wire)` constructor + `ownerSignaturePubkey` / `ownerAccountId` / `callId` / `expiryMs` / `maxUses` / `mediaAudio` / `mediaVideo` / `oneTimeKeyPackage` / `label` getters + `isValidNow(now_ms)`. The wire codec doesn't talk MLS — it carries the KP as opaque bytes minted via `Identity::create_key_package`. **Joiner-side orchestration** in new `crates/lakko-calls/src/link_invite.rs`: `accept_call_invite(&capability, &joiner_identity) -> AcceptedCallInvite { group, session, welcome_for_owner }` — joiner founds a fresh MLS group, adds the owner via the embedded KP, builds a `GroupCallSession { scope: LinkBased, call_id, ... }` keyed by `capability.call_id`, and returns the Welcome bytes the joiner ferries to the owner over the platform's existing transport (typically the stranger-inbox routed by `capability.owner_account_id`). Owner's side runs `Group::join_from_welcome` + `GroupCallSession::create(LinkBased, capability.call_id, ...)` and arrives at a byte-identical `CallKey` seed (cross-party MLS exporter agreement per 3c4 invariant C-1). Four property tests in `link_invite.rs`: (1) `accept_invite_round_trips_via_welcome` — Alice mints invite → Bob accepts → both build sessions → seal/open frame between them works (full C-1, C-2 hold across the bootstrap path). (2) `accept_invite_with_corrupted_kp_rejects` — KP byte flipped mid-payload → MLS validate refuses → `Error::Crypto` surfaced. (3) `accept_invite_uses_link_based_scope` — scope discriminator preserved end-to-end. (4) `joiner_is_founder_at_leaf_0` — first-accepted-joiner founds the group per ROADMAP contract; owner lands at leaf 1 after Welcome. V1 scope is **single-joiner** (the embedded KP is one-time); multi-joiner support needs server-side KP refresh + `/call-invite/{blob_id}` rendezvous endpoint (3c3-server follow-up, deferred). lakko-calls test count: 69 → 73; lakko-contacts: 73 → 89 (+13 codec + +3 owner_fingerprint). **`accept_call_invite` FFI** shipped on all three platforms: UniFFI exposes it as a `pub fn accept_call_invite(capability: &CallInviteCapability, joiner_identity: &Identity) -> Result<Arc<AcceptedCallInvite>, FfiError>` free fn that returns a uniffi::Object wrapping the new `Group` handle + flat `welcome_for_owner` / `call_id` / `own_leaf_index` / `call_key_seed` getters. wasm-bindgen mirrors as `acceptCallInvite(capability, joinerIdentity) -> AcceptedCallInvite` returning a class with `group()` one-shot move + `welcomeForOwner` / `callId` / `ownLeafIndex` / `callKeySeed` getters. The 32-byte `call_key_seed` feeds straight into the existing `sframe_derive_participant_key` / `sframeDeriveParticipantKey` FFI fns — platforms don't need to hold a GroupCallSession handle to derive per-leaf SFrame keys. **Anti-phishing UX**: `CallInviteCapability::owner_fingerprint()` returns a Signal-style 71-char safety number derived from the owner's Ed25519 pubkey (reuses `lakko_contacts::safety::safety_number(pk, pk)`). Joiner UI shows it before Accept so the human cross-checks the digits against what the owner shared OOB — defends against link-substitution attacks where someone swaps a real `/c/<slug>` URL for one pointing at their own account. Exposed in both UniFFI (`owner_fingerprint() -> String`) and wasm-bindgen (`ownerFingerprint` getter on `CallInviteCapability`). Three new property tests: `owner_fingerprint_is_deterministic_per_pubkey`, `different_owners_have_different_fingerprints`, `owner_fingerprint_matches_safety_number_format`. Audit anchor: C-28. Pattern parallels how 1.18a's `InviteCapability` shipped before 1.18d's server-side enforcement landed
**3c4** ✅ Per-participant SFrame keys end-to-end. The math itself shipped in 3a4 (`CallKey::participant_key(sender_leaf)` via HKDF-SHA-256 over `(exporter_seed, info = "lakko/sframe/v1/key" || sender_leaf_be)` + parallel nonce-salt derivation under `"lakko/sframe/v1/nonce"`); 3c4 proves the cross-party shape by exercising **two and three independent `GroupCallSession` instances** sharing only the MLS group + call_id — no shared seed, no shortcut. Four integration tests in `crates/lakko-calls/src/group_call.rs::tests`: (1) **`cross_party_seal_open_round_trips`** — A and B build separate `GroupCallSession`s from their own `Group` handles; both agree on the call_key seed byte-for-byte, A→B and B→A frames seal/open via `own_participant_key` and `remote_participant_key(sender_leaf)`. (2) **`cross_party_round_trip_survives_epoch_advance`** — after A adds C and B processes the commit, both call `refresh_call_key`; the new seed differs from the old, fresh frames still round-trip, and frames sealed under the OLD seed cannot be opened with the NEW key (seed-boundary forward secrecy — independent of the 1s SFrame overlap window that lives in the platform). (3) **`three_party_full_mesh_seal_open`** — A, B, C each seal under own key; the other two open via the sender's leaf index. Exercises the "everyone derives everyone else's key" invariant the SFU forwarding path depends on. (4) **`cross_party_wrong_aad_rejects`** — AEAD binding holds across the cross-party path (SFrame header forms the AAD in production). The math primitive's 7 unit tests in `sframe.rs` stay as the per-primitive contract; 3c4 wires them through the full ledger + MLS exporter
**3c5** ✅ Mid-call Add / Remove + 1-second overlap window (Rust core). `GroupCallSession` extended with `previous_call_key: Option<CallKey>` retained across `refresh_call_key` — when the MLS epoch advances, the prior seed moves to the previous-slot rather than being dropped. New `try_open_with_overlap(sender_leaf, aad, ct, frame_counter)` tries the current seed's per-participant key first; on AEAD rejection falls back to the previous seed's key, absorbing in-flight frames sealed under the OLD key that land after the rotation (RTT, jitter, reorder). Caller invokes `drop_previous_call_key()` when its own 1-second timer fires — at that point seed-boundary forward secrecy is fully in effect. The crate stays **wall-clock-free**: the platform owns the timer, the ledger owns the keys. Chained rotation contract: only the **most-recent previous** epoch is retained (single overlap slot), which assumes ≤ 1 commit per second per call — matches the SFU's commit debouncing. Four new property tests: (1) `overlap_window_accepts_stale_frame_until_dropped` — stale frame decodes during overlap, fresh frame decodes always, stale stops decoding after drop. (2) `no_overlap_window_collapses_to_current_only` — outside an overlap window the fallback path is skipped; tampered frames reject with a "no overlap window active" hint to differentiate the failure mode for platform-side debugging. (3) `chained_rotations_retain_only_most_recent_previous` — three epochs (0 → 1 → 2): epoch-0 frame decodes during epoch-1 overlap, epoch-1 frame decodes during epoch-2 overlap, but epoch-0 frame no longer decodes after epoch-2 displaces epoch-1 in the slot. (4) `drop_previous_call_key_is_idempotent`. FFI surface: UniFFI gets `sframe_open_with_overlap(current_seed, previous_seed: Option, sender_leaf, aad, ciphertext, frame_counter)` — seed-flat shape so platform code keeps both seeds in its keychain/SE store and ferries them in per-frame; wasm-bindgen mirror as `sframeOpenWithOverlap(currentCallKeySeed, previousCallKeySeed | null, ...)`. The wall-clock 1-second overlap policy itself lives in the platform layer (iOS / Android / Web call sessions) and gets wired alongside the WebRTC pod integration in 3a9 / 3a10 followups
**3c6** UI: gallery view (grid of participants), screen-share, mute controls, participant list, raise-hand. Three-client implementation
**3c7** Bandwidth adaptation: simulcast / SVC layers, dynamic resolution per participant view based on viewport / pin state
**3c8** Recording — deferred. The default is no recording. If it lands later, the recording client is a regular participant with its own MLS leaf, so the recorded stream is end-to-end encrypted just like any other consumer

#### 3d — Real-network testing + polish

**3d1** Cross-continent latency test (Hetzner EU SFU ↔ US + Asia clients) — measure baseline numbers, document them in the SFU README
**3d2** Packet loss tolerance (simulated via Linux `netem`) — verify Opus FEC + SVC adaptation hold up
**3d3** A / B benchmark vs Zoom / Telegram / WhatsApp on p50 / p95 latency and audio MOS — set realistic expectations in the project README
**3d4** Background-CPU constraints on iOS — verify the PushKit + CallKit + ForegroundService stack handles 30+ minute calls without OS kill

### Exit criteria
- 1×1 audio + video call works end-to-end on Web + iOS + Android
- Group call up to 10 participants stable in a 30-minute session through the self-hosted SFU
- Group call mid-call Add / Remove of a participant — no audible lag in the > 1s key-rotation overlap window
- Audio p50 latency < 100 ms regional (P2P) / < 150 ms (via SFU)
- 1×1 video — call sustains 30 fps at 720p baseline; up-tier to 60 fps at 1080p when bandwidth allows
- E2EE is strict — no silent fallback (including media in group calls). If SFrame keys cannot be established, the call does not start

### Out of scope for Phase 3
- Audio rooms with hundreds of listeners (broadcast pattern — share lifts with the 2c two-tier substrate; defer until / unless demand)
- 100k+ viewer broadcasts (Phase 5 — needs multi-region edge cascade)
- Recording (deferred — separate scope decision, opt-in if it ever lands)

### Top risks
- **iOS background CPU limits** during long calls — Apple's enforcement can be inconsistent; needs careful PushKit + CallKit handling
- **AV1 hardware decode** absent on older devices — H.264 fallback covers the long-tail
- **TURN abuse** — self-hosted coturn needs rate limits + auth tokens scoped to active calls
- **CallKit / ConnectionService UX divergence** — incoming-call presentation differs between iOS and Android; document acceptable per-platform UX
- **SFU bandwidth ceiling** — a single CCX-tier VPS gives ~60 Mbps sustained; ~30 concurrent 4-person video calls. Scale up to a second SFU VPS when sustained utilisation crosses 70%

---

Phase 4 (channels / two-tier crypto / medium broadcasts) folded into
Phase 2c (large private groups + channels via PublisherGroup +
sender-key) and Phase 2d (public channels). The original
"Phase 4 = channels" framing pre-dated the split between private-
with-scale (needs E2EE, lives in 2c) and public-by-design (no E2EE
on content, lives in 2d). The two-tier substrate ships in 2c; the
public broadcast surface ships in 2d. 100k+ viewer broadcasts with
geographically distributed edge cascade remain a Phase 5 concern.

## Phase 5 — Anti-censorship + Scale

### Goal

Anti-censorship transport stack (for users in RU / IR / CN) + multi-region edge cascade for broadcasts of 100k+ concurrent viewers + watchOS + localization.

### Pre-flight
- Phase 4 exit criteria green

### Sprint breakdown

#### Pluggable transports (see ADR-0004)
- **5.0** REALITY (xtls/REALITY) in lakko-transport — TLS-in-TLS hiding behind a foreign certificate
- **5.1** MASQUE / CONNECT-UDP (RFC 9298) — tunnels QUIC inside HTTP/3
- **5.2** Shadowsocks-2022 (AEAD-2022)
- **5.3** VLESS + XTLS-Vision
- **5.4** WebTransport for the web client
- **5.5** obfs4 — legacy compatibility

#### Bootstrap rendezvous
- **5.6** ECH (RFC 9460/9525) on edge endpoints
- **5.7** `services/transport-rendezvous` — produces signed bootstrap configs (Ed25519, JSON: endpoints + working PT configs)
- **5.8** DoH-resolved (Cloudflare 1.1.1.1, Google, Quad9, Mullvad) rotating pool of innocuous-looking domains
- **5.9** TUF (The Update Framework) signed transport configs — new transports as **data**, not code
- **5.10** Auto-discovery: happy-eyeballs racing, top-2 working ones kept warm, budget ≤10s cold start

#### Mobile transport integration
- **5.11** iOS: pluggable transports embedded in URLSession + QUIC (not via NEPacketTunnelProvider)
- **5.12** Android: VpnService available optionally, default — in-app

#### Multi-region broadcast cascade (for 100k+ viewer broadcasts)
- **5.13** `services/edge` extension — origin SFU → edge SFU cascade with region-affinity routing
- **5.14** Edge nodes deployed in several Hetzner regions (Helsinki / Falkenstein / Nuremberg / Ashburn) or multi-CDN
- **5.15** Late-joiner flow for cascade broadcasts
- **5.16** Load testing: 100k+ concurrent viewers spread across regions

#### watchOS
- **5.17** `apps/watchos` — thin presenter, paired secure channel with the phone

#### Localization
- **5.18** Translation pipeline (Crowdin or self-hosted) for all clients
- **5.19** 10+ languages (ru / en / fa / zh-CN / ar / es / pt / de / fr / ja / ko / uk / …)
- **5.20** RTL layouts

#### Polish
- **5.21** Onboarding flow (minimum friction, secure-by-default)
- **5.22** Accessibility (full VoiceOver / TalkBack support)
- **5.23** Animations, transitions

### Exit criteria
- Successful connections from ≥5 censorship-emulating environments (GFW / TSPU / IR DPI / corporate firewall) in lab
- Broadcast 100k+ concurrent viewers stable through cascade
- WatchOS app works
- Localization for 10+ languages with native review
- Auto-discovery of transports picks a working one in ≤10 s cold start

### Top risks
- **Censorship arms race** — needs fast TUF-signed transport rollouts (new transports as data, not code)
- **iOS Network Extension limitations** restrict anti-censorship — fallback to in-app transports
- **Hetzner multi-region** — all Hetzner DCs are in EU; for US / APAC users we either accept latency or onboard other providers
- **DPRK-style allowlist-only internet** — service unavailable there; **abandon plan**: we do not engineer for it

---

## Post-Phase-5 (out of scope of this roadmap)

After Phase 5 it is normal product evolution. Technical directions worth tracking:

- **Federation** between Lakko instances
- **Post-quantum** — MLS extensions for ML-KEM are being worked on at IETF; migrate when stable and available in OpenMLS
- **libcrux replacement** — switch to a formally verified crypto provider
- **OpenMLS upstream contributions** — reserve capacity for upstream
- **SDK split** — separate `lakko-mls` / `lakko-crypto` / `lakko-proto` under Apache 2.0 into a sub-repo if the industry asks for an embedded MLS implementation; thanks to the CLA this is possible

---

## Things to keep in mind

- **The roadmap is not a calendar** — a sprint = an atomic deliverable, not a time slot
- **Phase N+1 does not start** until Phase N's exit criteria are green — the only hard rule
- **The document is updated** — after each phase closes, we add a `### Retrospective` section with what went as expected vs what did not
